  - **2 — the deliberate stale-version arrangements.** `payload=2`, `pinned=1`, `live=1`: `baseline-stale-decision-version.test.ts` and the `promotionProbe('stale-version')` inside the enforcement register's own WP-16 probe map — **RPH-GOV-003's evidence**.
- **SO RPH-GOV-003'S TWO TESTS CONSTRUCT THEIR SCENARIO USING THE DEFECT REG-F-014 RECORDS.** The rule is *"an approval of Architecture version 2 does not approve Architecture version 3"*; the only way these arrangements make a decision bind a version the subject is not at is to have the approver **state a version that is not true** — the caller-supplied-fact bug, used as a fixture device. The refusal they prove is real. The premise they prove it from is a forgery.
- **AND THE HONEST ARRANGEMENT DOES NOT EXIST FOR A PWU.** Censused every `bumpSemanticVersion: true` and every explicit `newSemanticVersion` write: exactly three aggregates can ever move their semantic version — **INTENT** (`ReviseIntent`), **DECOMPOSITION_CONTRACT** (`ReviseDecomposition`) and **PROFESSIONAL_WORK_ARCHITECTURE** (the authoring commands, §10.1 L1379). **A PWU's semanticVersion is set to 1 at creation and no command ever changes it.** Both tests use a PWU subject. So for their subject the rule's antecedent — the subject moving on after approval — is **unreachable by construction**, which is why the forgery was needed to produce it at all.
- **THE OBVIOUS FIX IS WRONG, and this is recorded because it was implemented before it was rejected.** The natural remedy is symmetric with REG-F-014's first instance: stop trusting the caller, DERIVE the versions from the store at approval. Implemented, it passed 2086 tests and broke exactly the two sites above — and it is still wrong. **Deriving at approval time makes the engine perform the very laundering the rule forbids:** an approver who reviewed v1 and approves after the subject silently moved to v2 would be RECORDED as approving v2. The caller-supplied number would be replaced by an engine-supplied one that is equally untrue to what was reviewed. The distinction that saved this — a fact the engine OWNS should be derived, a fact it cannot compute should be refused — is right for `authority` and wrong here, because the version that matters is **the one that was reviewed**, not the one that is current.
- **WHAT THE FIX SHOULD BE.** The Decision's `subjectSemanticVersions` is set at PROPOSAL and is **immutable**: `approveDecision` must not write it at all. A payload disagreeing with the pin is refused (for subjects the pin covers — leaving the 11 above untouched). Staleness is then caught where the rule places it, at PROMOTION, by `decisionAuthorizesVersions` comparing the immutable pin against the subject's current version. No new check at approval is needed.
- **AND THE TESTS MUST MOVE TO A SUBJECT THAT VERSIONS.** With the forgery closed, the two arrangements can only be built over an INTENT, a DECOMPOSITION_CONTRACT or a PWA — approve at v1, move the subject, then promote. That is a materially better test than the one it replaces: it exercises the sequence the rule actually describes instead of asserting it from a false premise. It is not free: the WP-16 `promotionProbe` is shared by **four** rules (RPH-BAS-003/004/006 and RPH-GOV-003), so changing its subject touches all four probes.
- **Disposition:** ~~**Recorded, not fixed**~~ — **FIXED 2026-08-03**, in the increment after the one that filed it. The pause was deliberate and is left on the record: a first fix was written, measured, and withdrawn on its own merits, and the correct one changes a governance authority path and re-bases four register probes.
  - **The pin is immutable.** `approveDecision` no longer writes `subjectSemanticVersions`. It records what existed when the decision was **proposed** — what the approver reviewed.
  - **The payload keeps its meaning through a check, not a write.** An approval stating a version that is not the pin is REFUSED. Scoped to subjects the pin covers, so the 11 "subject does not exist" divergences are untouched and the separate question they raise stays separate.
  - **THE TWO PRODUCTION CHANGES ARE NOT INDEPENDENTLY OBSERVABLE, and the tests say so** rather than letting a reader assume otherwise. Once a disagreeing payload is refused, no accepted command distinguishes "keeps the pin" from "writes the payload" — they agree by construction. The single discriminating case is a subject the pin does NOT cover: the old code recorded the caller's claim for an object the engine had never seen; the new code records nothing. That is the third test in `decision-version-pin.test.ts`, and restoring the overwrite reddens **only** it.
  - **Both stale-version arrangements are re-based onto a subject that can actually move.** The decision now names the **INTENT** as a co-subject beside the baselined PWU, and the intent is genuinely revised after approval (`ReviseIntent`, semanticVersion 1 → 2). Each asserts the subject really moved, so neither can pass over an unchanged world. **This is the first time RPH-GOV-003's refusal has been proved from a true premise.**
  - **Three mutants, each fired and each on exactly its predicted test:** neutralise the payload-vs-pin refusal → the disagreement test only; restore the overwrite → the ghost-subject test only; neutralise `decisionAuthorizesVersions`' arm → **both** RPH-GOV-003 tests, which is the proof that re-basing the arrangement did not cost it its sensitivity to the rule it exists to prove.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `handlers/governance.ts` (`approveDecision`, and the shared approval precondition), `baseline-stale-decision-version.test.ts`, `execrem-wp16-enforcement-observed.test.ts`'s `promotionProbe`, and the new `decision-version-pin.test.ts`. Canon is unchanged and correct. **Status:** CLOSED. This was REG-F-014's second instance; **three stand** — `ReshapePwu.triggeringObjectId`, `detectedConflicts`, and `authorityDecisionId`, which is never resolved to a real Decision.

### REG-F-018 — Every number in the generated contracts is unconstrained, while the engine's hashing layer accepts only integers — and one of them crashed `dispatch`
- **Date:** 2026-08-04 (found while closing REG-F-012's payload dimension; the crash path traced, the schema gap counted) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a contract admitting values a downstream invariant rejects, with a live crash on one field.
- **Statement:** `contentHash`'s canonicalization is explicit that **numbers MUST be finite integers** — *"the domain models quantities as integers or strings; a float in hashed content is a modeling smell and is rejected loudly, per the Constitution's 'fail loud on invariants' rule"* — and it throws `CanonicalJsonError` otherwise. Every object state is hashed (`contentHash(nextState)`, `kit.ts`). Yet **32 generated contract fields are bare `z.number()`** — 22 in `messages.ts`, 10 in `objects.ts` — and **not one** carries `.int()`.
- **THE CONSTRAINED SCHEMA ALREADY EXISTS AND IS ALREADY NAMED FOR THIS.** `common.ts` defines `NonNegativeIntSchema = z.number().int().nonnegative()` and re-exports it as **`SemanticVersionSchema`**, `RevisionSchema`, `SchemaVersionSchema`. `envelopes.ts` uses it. The generated surface does not: fields literally called `approvedSemanticVersion`, `expectedSemanticVersion`, `policySemanticVersion`, `priorSemanticVersion`, `newSemanticVersion`, `subjectSemanticVersions` are bare `z.number()`. This is the REG-F-010 shape once more — **ratified material present, and unread** — with the twist that here the unread material is a schema rather than a column.
- **AND IT IS NOT INERT.** `recordArtifact` copies `p.byteSize` **straight into persisted state**, and both `RecordArtifactPayloadSchema.byteSize` and `ArtifactSchema.byteSize` are bare `z.number().optional()`. So `RecordArtifact` with `byteSize: 1.5` passed Zod, reached `contentHash(args.nextState)`, and threw a `CanonicalJsonError` **out of `Engine.dispatch`** — a throw where the method's whole contract is a typed, classified `CommandResult`. That is REG-F-011's crash class, on a field nothing guards. Most of the other 31 are shielded by accident rather than design: the version fields sit behind exact-equality checks (`ApproveIntent` refuses unless `approvedSemanticVersion` equals the current version, and `1.5` never will), which refuses the float for a reason that has nothing to do with hashability.
- **ALREADY NEUTRALIZED, THOUGH NOT BY THIS ENTRY.** REG-F-012's hashability gate (`command-bus.ts` step 0c) refuses an uncanonicalizable payload before any handler runs, so the crash became a **classified refusal** in that commit. ~~What remains is the divergence itself: the contracts still promise to accept values the engine cannot store, and a caller learns this from a runtime refusal rather than from the schema.~~ The divergence itself closed in the next one — see the disposition.
- **Disposition:** ~~Recorded, not fixed, with the cost stated rather than the work quietly deferred. `messages.ts` / `objects.ts` are **generated** from `packages/rph-contracts/vocab/*.json` by `bun run gen`, so the fix is a vocab change plus a regeneration — and the committed generated files are known to be **stale against their generators**, so a regeneration lands a large unrelated diff that must be isolated in its own commit to stay reviewable.~~ **FIXED 2026-08-04, in the next increment.** Struck rather than deleted, because **both halves of that deferral were wrong and I wrote them an hour earlier**:
  - **THE MERGE TARGET WAS WRONG.** The vocab says `"type": "number"`; the **generator** decides the Zod expression (`gen-messages.ts` / `gen-objects.ts`, `if (t === 'number') return 'z.number()'`). So the fix is **two constants in two generators**, not an edit to twenty-four vocab fields — which is also why it cannot be forgotten per-field, and why a new number-typed field inherits the constraint for free.
  - **THE COST WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS A CLAIM I NEVER MEASURED.** "The committed generated files are stale against their generators" came from an **old plan document**, repeated on trust. Measured: `bun run gen` on a clean tree produces **zero** changes. There was no drift, no large unrelated diff, and no reason to defer. This is the register's own `absence of evidence` lesson inverted — a *presence* asserted from a record instead of from a run.
  - **THE FIX.** One `INT` constant per generator, then `bun run gen`. Twenty-two payload fields, ten object fields and six emitted JSON schemas changed; **zero bare `z.number()` remain**, and the full suite passed unchanged — no fixture anywhere used a fractional number.
  - **THE `.int()` IS INVISIBLE AT THE BUS, which is the trap.** Step 0c hashes the payload **before** payload validation runs, so the hashability gate answers first and the schema is never consulted. Revert the mapping and **no engine test reddens.** So the constraint got its own tests: `number-integrity.test.ts` asserts the *agreement* — for the same value, the schema's verdict and `contentHash`'s verdict now match — rather than just the presence of `.int()`, which would still pass if `contentHash` were later loosened.
  - **A STANDING GATE, `verif/contract-number-census.test.ts`**, with an empty `FRACTIONAL_BY_DESIGN` allowlist: no generated schema may admit a non-integer. Mutants: revert the generator mapping → **five** tests redden across both files; blind the detector → **only the CONTROL** reddens, the two census assertions passing vacuously, which is the whole reason the CONTROL is there. And the CONTROL earned itself once more before that — the first draft of the object fixture omitted four envelope fields, and the CONTROL is what caught a fixture that would have made every refusal above it pass for the wrong reason.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `packages/rph-contracts/src/gen/{gen-messages,gen-objects}.ts` (the mapping), the regenerated `messages.ts` / `objects.ts` / `schemas/objects/*.json`, `packages/rph-contracts/src/number-integrity.test.ts`, and `verif/contract-number-census.test.ts`. The `contentHash` header is unchanged and correct; it stated the requirement all along. **Status:** **CLOSED.**

### REG-F-019 — A rule family was exempted from the conformance gate while four of its six rules had named, running, passing checks
- **Date:** 2026-08-04 (found by auditing the last two `DEFERRABLE_PREFIXES` entries, the set REG-F-013 named as the manifest's most dangerous data) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a certification claiming **less** than the repository proves.
- **Statement:** `conformance-manifest.ts` recorded `RPH-FIX` as `status: 'DEFERRED'`, note *"field-service fixture replay (expected-events.jsonl) — the M13 Reference Undertaking replay harness"*, and listed `RPH-FIX` in `DEFERRABLE_PREFIXES`. Both the harness and the fixture that note describes as future work **exist and run**:
  - `packages/rph-engine/src/replay.ts` — `runConformance` adds checks under the literal ids **`RPH-FIX-001`**, **`RPH-FIX-002`**, **`RPH-FIX-003a/b/c`** and **`RPH-FIX-006`**.
  - `packages/rph-engine/fixtures/expected-events.jsonl` — the 72-event trace, present.
  - `replay.test.ts` asserts them green, including by id (`runConformance(events).checks.find(c => c.id === 'RPH-FIX-002')`). **Eleven tests, passing.**
- **THIS IS AN UNDERCLAIM, AND THAT DIRECTION WAS INVISIBLE BY CONSTRUCTION.** REG-F-013 caught `RPH-CMP` here because its *rationale* named a different concern. The enforcement register's overclaim gate catches a family certifying **more** than it proves — that is how RPH-PWU-010, RPH-PWU-007, RPH-CON-004 and RPH-PER-012 were found. **Nothing catches a family certifying less**, for exactly the reason the manifest's own comment gives: an exempt family makes no claim to check. The instrument had one blind direction and this finding is what was sitting in it.
- **THE PRECISION IS THE ASYMMETRY, checked in both directions rather than assumed by association.**
  - **Four of six are discharged**: FIX-001 (contiguous seq), FIX-002 (every trace event resolves to a registered contract), FIX-003a/b/c (both baselines promoted; trace ends AUTHORITATIVE + `PwuBaselined`; a Behavior PWU SATISFIED but not baselined), FIX-006 (the deferred offline capability stays represented).
  - **Two are genuinely outstanding, and the reason is structural rather than a gap in effort.** FIX-004 (architecture obligations allocated to child PWUs) and FIX-005 (Multi-Tenancy Constraint → Architecture PWU → Tenant Isolation Artifact → Claim → Assessment) are **object-graph** assertions, and the fixture is an **event trace of five fields** (`seq`/`event`/`aggregate`/`phase`/`label`) carrying no obligations, artifacts or claims. Measured: of 27 distinct labels in the fixture, exactly **one** mentions tenancy (`"Tenant isolation criteria"`, an observation at seq 48). The corpus **does** state both (§25 *"a second chain should demonstrate a constraint"*; §25 Test 4 *"decomposition coverage"*), so the material exists — the route is the one `replay-conformance.test.ts` already takes, asserting against the **engine-driven graph** rather than enriching a hand-transcribed trace. Named work, not a missing capability.
  - **FIX-004 CLOSED, and the vacuity was the whole job.** `runGraphConformance` (`packages/rph-engine/src/graph-conformance.ts`) asserts it over the store `driveReferenceUndertaking` builds. **Writing the check first would have certified the rule on an empty set**: every PWU was proposed with `obligationIds: []` and every decomposition with no `obligationAllocations`, so a universally quantified test would have ranged over NOTHING and passed — the shape this register has recorded repeatedly (a floor test that arranged no floor; a guard that could not see its own subject). So the drive now **authors** three MANDATORY architecture obligations — corpus §25 Test 3's three traceability targets, Multi-Tenancy / Data Architecture / Integration — and allocates them, and the check treats an empty population as a **FAILURE** whose detail reads `VACUOUS`. Proved by mutant: remove the authored obligations and both the rule test and its non-vacuity test redden with that word. Four controls, each with its own arrangement: an empty graph fails; an obligation allocated to a **non-child** is reported unallocated (else `allocatedTo: ['anything']` would satisfy it and the word "child" would carry no weight); a **retained** obligation is not counted unallocated (DEC-3's equation is *allocated + retained + already satisfied*); and the population is asserted non-empty by name.
  - ~~**FIX-005 is deliberately ABSENT from the new module rather than present-and-failing.**~~ **CLOSED in the next increment**, on exactly the inventory this bullet named — `AssertConstraint` + the Multi-Tenancy PWU's `constraintIds`, a Tenant Isolation artifact via `RecordArtifact.producingPwuId`, a claim whose `subjectObjectIds` names that artifact, reaching the assessment the drive already runs. All four references existed in the ratified model, as checked. The refusal to ship a hard-coded `false` check stands as written: it would have been permanent noise that trains a reader to ignore the report.
  - **THE CHECK WALKS THE CHAIN, because the rule says the fixture EXPOSES the trace.** Five objects each existing independently would satisfy an existence check and expose nothing — you could not get from the constraint to the assessment. So every hop reads the FORWARD field naming the next object (`constraintIds` → `producingPwuId` → `subjectObjectIds` → `claimIds`) and the walk fails at the first link that does not resolve, reporting `BROKEN: <hop>`. **Each hop has its own mutant**, breaking exactly that link and no other, plus an empty-graph control that resolves `0/5`.
  - **AND CLOSING IT PRODUCED TWO FINDINGS OF ITS OWN, which is the argument for doing the authoring rather than asserting over what was already there.** Recording the drive's first ever artifact exposed **REG-F-020** (a defect register that read CLEARED because the event had never been in its population). And the three chain ids, placed first in `REFERENCE_UNDERTAKING`, turned a Constraint, an Artifact and a Claim into "PWUs" in two test suites — because `PWU_IDS` there was derived by **EXCLUDING** `intentId` rather than by matching the `pwu_` prefix. The chain now has its own constant **and** both derivations are positive, so neither mistake alone can reproduce it.
  - **`RPH-E2E`'s deferral is LEGITIMATE and stays.** No `RPH-E2E` rule id appears in any check, test, or source file in the repository; the seven scenarios are genuinely unbuilt. Two families sat in that set under one sentence and only one of them belonged.
- **THE REGISTER'S OWN SCOPE PARAGRAPH WAS WRONG IN THE SAME TWO WAYS, and it is the paragraph that exists to prevent exactly this.** It said both families are *"legitimately DEFERRED … with named work packages"* — half true. And it said reaching them *"needs a different instrument (a replay harness that can assert over a recorded event log), not another row in this file"* — the diagnosis is right and **the tense is wrong**: that instrument is not needed, it EXISTS, and it had been discharging four of these rules the whole time the paragraph called them unreached. Both struck and corrected in place. *"Out of reach by THIS instrument"* is a claim about the register; it does not license a claim about the repository, and this paragraph made the second while asserting the first.
- **Disposition:** **FIXED.** `RPH-FIX` → `PARTIAL` with a `testFile` and a note naming which four are asserted and what the other two need; `DEFERRABLE_PREFIXES` reduced to `RPH-E2E` alone.
  - **A STANDING GATE FOR THE BLIND DIRECTION, `verif/deferral-honesty.test.ts`**: no rule named by a **passing** check may be certified `DEFERRED`. It **runs the harness** rather than reading its note — a note can say anything; a passing check is a fact. Derived from the harness's own output, so it cannot go stale the way a written list does.
  - **Red first**: the gate was written before the manifest was touched and failed on the live defect, naming `RPH-FIX-001/002/003/006`, while both controls passed. Mutants: certify any of the four `DEFERRED` again → the derived assertion **and** the by-name assertion redden; break `ruleIdOf` → the resolution CONTROL reddens (ids that match no rule would otherwise pass silently); empty the harness's checks → the non-empty CONTROL reddens.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `conformance-manifest.ts` (the status, the note, `DEFERRABLE_PREFIXES`), `enforcement-register.ts`'s scope paragraph, `verif/deferral-honesty.test.ts`, and — for the two graph rules — `packages/rph-engine/src/{graph-conformance.ts,reference-undertaking.ts}`. Canon is unchanged and correct. **Status:** **CLOSED.** All six RPH-FIX rules are asserted by name across the two oracles, and `RPH-FIX` is `COVERED` (the M12 COVERED ledger records the 34 → 40 move and why). `RPH-E2E` remains the only deferrable family, on a deferral checked in both directions.

### REG-F-020 — A defect register read CLEARED because the drive it measures had never once exercised the event
- **Date:** 2026-08-04 (found by closing RPH-FIX-005, which made the Reference Undertaking record its first artifact) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — an emitted event whose payload its own ratified contract rejects, plus the reason nothing noticed.
- **Statement:** `recordArtifact` emitted the raw command payload as the `ArtifactRecorded` event payload. `ArtifactRecordedPayloadSchema` is a `z.strictObject` that does **not** declare `artifactId` — the artifact's identity is the event's `aggregateId`, and repeating it in the payload is the *"carries keys the strict schema rejects outright"* half of the class `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts` was built to catch. Measured verbatim: `ArtifactRecorded || (root): Unrecognized key: "artifactId"`.
- **WHY THE REGISTER SAID CLEARED, which is the finding rather than the one-key defect.** That test drives the Reference Undertaking and validates **every event the drive emits** against its declared shape. Its register comment reads *"THE REGISTER IS NOW CLEARED — every event this system emits conforms to its own declared shape"*, down from twelve, each fixed deliberately. The claim is true **as written** and reads as more than it is: **the drive had never recorded an artifact**, so `ArtifactRecorded` was never in the population. The register was cleared over the events that happened to be exercised, and the sentence does not say so.
- **THE PREMISE GUARD EXISTS AND IS TOO WEAK TO CATCH THIS.** The same file carries `PREMISE: the reference undertaking exercises a broad slice of the event catalog` — `types >= 20`, `checked > 200`. That is a **breadth floor**, not a coverage claim: it stops the sweep passing on a drive that emitted almost nothing, and says nothing about *which* types are missing. So the guard was present, correct, and silent, which is the more interesting shape than an absent one.
- **THE MOMENT IT BECAME VISIBLE IS THE EVIDENCE.** Closing RPH-FIX-005 required the drive to author the corpus §25 chain, including a Tenant Isolation Model via `RecordArtifact`. That single new dispatch — the drive's **first ever artifact** — grew the register an entry on the next run. Nothing about the handler changed; only the population did.
- **Disposition:** **FIXED.** `recordArtifact` now supplies the DECLARED shape via `eventPayload` rather than falling through to the raw command payload, and the register is empty again — this time over a population that includes the event. The `kit.ts` `eventPayload` seam already existed for exactly this (its own comment: *"the command payload is NOT the event payload"*); `recordArtifact` had simply never used it.
  - ~~**WHAT IS NOT DONE, named rather than implied:** the register is still only as strong as the drive's coverage.~~ **DONE in the next increment, and the class was far larger than the instance.** Surveyed by instrumenting the commit path and running the whole suite: **19,581 event commits, 89 distinct event types, and TWENTY-ONE types emitting a payload their own declared shape rejects.** The drive-scoped register saw **one** of them. A census of never-emitted types was the plan; measuring first showed the better instrument was a *total* ledger over what the suite actually emits.
  - **`verif/emitted-event-guard.ts`** wraps `commit` on both shipped StorageAdapters and fails the run on any nonconforming event type not in a **shrink-only** ledger. Declared in `vitest.projects.ts` beside the unread-refusal guard, for the same reason: a new package must not be able to opt out by omission.
  - **THE LEDGER'S REASONS ARE THE MEASURED VIOLATIONS, not authored rationales.** Twenty-one hand-written justifications produced in one sitting would be exactly the bulk fabrication this programme refuses; the Zod issue string is the fact. They fall in the two classes the drive-scoped register had already named at twelve — the event carries the RAW COMMAND PAYLOAD while its shape describes the RESULTING STATE (`status`, `authorizationStatus`, `workLifecycleState`), or it carries KEYS the strict shape rejects. **Neither class was ever closed; it was closed for the events one drive emits.**
  - **THE DISCRIMINATOR RESTS ON ANOTHER FINDING.** `rph-persistence`'s own tests commit SYNTHETIC events straight to the adapter to exercise atomicity — `{ eventType: 'IntentCaptured', payload: { note } }` — and counting those would put two non-defects in a defect ledger. The guard therefore checks only events carrying a `commandId`, which is sound rather than convenient: `makeEvent` sets it on every path and `Engine.dispatch` REFUSES a command without one (**REG-F-011's identity guard**). A handler-produced event cannot lack it.
  - **Proved to fire, and to fail the run:** deleting one ledger row (`PwaCreated`) makes the suite exit **1** naming that type; restoring it returns exit 0. A guard whose failure does not fail the build is decorative, and this programme has already been burned once by a harness that reported nothing.
  - **THREE LIMITS, stated in the file rather than discovered later:** 43 declared types are still emitted by nothing and remain unmeasured; the guard **cannot see a stale ledger row** (vitest runs each project in its own worker, so no worker sees the whole catalog — closing that needs cross-worker aggregation, named rather than pretended away); and it says nothing about events with no declared shape, deliberately, since conflating "no contract" with "contradicts its contract" would hide both.
  - **The drive-scoped test is kept and is not superseded** — it asserts something the ledger cannot, that the canonical reference undertaking emits nothing nonconforming end to end. Its "CLEARED" sentence is corrected in place to say which population it means.
- **TWENTY OF THE TWENTY-ONE ARE REAL, AND THE TWENTY-FIRST IS NOT — a correction made by DUMPING the payloads instead of reading the handlers.** `PwuMarkedReady` looked like the same defect: its ledger row said `workLifecycleState: Invalid option`. `markPwuReady` **already supplies a conforming `eventPayload`**, deriving `workLifecycleState` from the committed next state, so the obvious reading was wrong. The nonconforming commit is `event-gate.test.ts`'s **negative arm**, which emits `{total: 'garbage'}` on purpose to prove that gate's scope (a ratified event is refused; an unratified authored one is allowed). It carries a real `commandId` and is indistinguishable from handler output at the commit path. It is now filed in a **separate** `DELIBERATE_TEST_FIXTURES` list — a defect ledger holding a non-defect sends someone to fix a handler that is correct.
- **SIX ARE FIXED, FIFTEEN REMAIN.** `ArtifactRecorded` (the instance above), then `PwaCreated`, `PwaPublished`, `UndertakingCreated`, `PwuTypeDefined` and `PwuTypeRedefined` — each given the DECLARED shape through `kit.ts`'s existing `eventPayload` seam, and each verified by deleting its ledger row and re-running. The remaining fifteen are all the same class: the handler emits the raw command payload while the declared shape describes the resulting state (`status`, `authorizationStatus`, `waiverDecisionId`, `parentWorkUnitId`, …), measured payload by measured payload.
- **AND THE GUARD ITSELF ALMOST WENT SILENT, which is the sharper lesson.** Mid-construction, a probe edit left a literal newline inside a string literal; the setup module stopped parsing; and **three consecutive runs reported "no violations"** that were really "no guard". I read those as evidence and nearly concluded the ledger was stale. What broke the tie was asking the guard how many events it had *inspected* — so it now carries a **liveness counter**, and `verif/emitted-event-guard.test.ts` asserts the counter moves for a real dispatch (disable the prototype patch and that test reddens). A second assertion checks the guard is declared as a setup file for **every** project, since the liveness test alone only proves the mechanism, not the installation. This is the third time in this session a *harness* rather than the code under test was the thing lying — and the first two were caught the same way, by noticing a report that could not distinguish "found nothing" from "ran nothing".
- **TWENTY OF THE TWENTY ARE NOW FIXED BUT ONE, and the exception is the most valuable thing the pass produced.** The remaining fifteen were taken in one orchestrated pass — five agents, one handler file each so no two could collide, each followed by an independent adversarial verifier told to REFUTE rather than confirm. **Fourteen fixed, one REFUSED, zero blocking problems, seventeen concerns.**
  - **`WaiverRequested` CLOSED in the next increment — the refusal was right about the DANGER and wrong about the CONSTRAINT.** The danger is exactly as reported and is why the naive fix must never be made. But the refusal called widening `WaiverRequestedPayloadSchema` *"editing ratified-material"*, and **it is not ratified**: its vocab entry is annotated `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED` — *"DOC-007 schematizes NO interface for this, so these fields were AUTHORED, not derived … Do NOT treat this sourceSection as proof the shape is ratified. Ratification pending."* The shape is **ours**, the command declares those five fields REQUIRED, and the projection needs three of them. **A shape we authored that cannot carry what its only consumer requires is the defect** — the event was the thing that was wrong, not the handler and not the reader. The vocab now carries `waivedPolicyId`, `waivedCriterionId`, `waivedFindingIds`, `compensatingControls`, `reviewConditions` and optional `expiresAt`; the handler emits the full declared shape, with `decisionType` and `status` — which the shape always required and the raw command payload never carried — read off the committed next state. **Nothing is dropped and the ledger is now EMPTY.**
  - **AND THE BLIND SPOT THAT MADE IT DANGEROUS IS NOW CLOSED, which matters more than the fix.** `packages/rph-engine/src/waiver-attachment-live.test.ts` drives a REAL `RequestWaiver` against two assessments under DIFFERENT policies and folds the REAL event log. Proved by mutant: make the handler stop emitting `waivedPolicyId` and the hand-built projection test passes **22 of 22** while this control fails **all three**. That is the precise asymmetry the verifier identified — a fixture that supplies the field the handler no longer sends can never see the handler stop sending it.
  - ~~**`WaiverRequested` IS REFUSED, AND THE REFUSAL IS CORRECT.**~~ *(the refusal as filed, kept because its reasoning was sound and its one wrong premise is the instructive part)* Emitting its declared shape would drop `waivedPolicyId`, `waivedCriterionId` and `waivedFindingIds` — and `foldWaiverRequested` in `packages/rph-projections/src/assurance-view.ts` reads `waivedPolicyId` as its **attachment predicate**. It is a pure fold over the event log with no store handle, so the `WaiverDetail` on the Decision object (where the data does live) is unreachable to it. Conforming the payload would make `waivedPolicyId` undefined at every real waiver, which takes the projection's permissive `waivedPolicyId === undefined ||` branch: **every waiver would attach to every assessment whose subjects it intersects, under any policy** — and it would stay GREEN, because that projection's own "a waiver for a DIFFERENT policy does NOT attach" test builds its events by hand. The live path is real (`apps/rph-demo/.../undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts` builds this view from `readAllEvents()`). Closing it means either widening the ratified shape or teaching the projection to read the Decision object — a two-package change that must land in one commit. **Left in the ledger with the reason written at the emit site.**
  - **THE DOMINANT CONCERN WAS THE SAME IN ALL FIVE GROUPS AND IT WAS RIGHT: the fixes shipped UNPROTECTED.** One verifier proved it by reverting its fix and running the whole suite green. That is precisely what the ledger shrink answers, which is why the agents were forbidden to touch it — the rows come out **centrally, after verification**, so the shrink is checked rather than asserted by the same edit that needs checking. Verified after shrinking: reverting the `ExecutionTerminated` fix now **fails the run (exit 1)**.
  - **THREE FALSE STATEMENTS WERE CAUGHT AND CORRECTED, none of which a test could see.** `RPH-BAS-007`'s register row asserted in the present tense that `supersedeBaseline` "supplies neither `mutate` nor `eventPayload`" and emits "the RAW COMMAND PAYLOAD" — all three now false, struck in place with the disposition deliberately unchanged. A new source comment **misquoted the vocab note it cited as its justification** (`RECOMPOSING->RECOMPOSED`; the actual note is `SATISFIED->RECOMPOSING`, which belongs to a different field) — the conclusion survives, the quote did not. And a comment claiming the blocking-observation derivation reads "as `governance.ts`'s baseline gate reads it" invited a parity reading it does not earn: the severity SET is shared, the surrounding predicate is not (`governance.ts` also filters on `disposition`), and whether a WAIVED observation should still block a DECOMPOSITION is a real question now stated rather than silently decided.
  - **NAMED RESIDUES, not swept:** `TacticalChangeApplied` no longer writes `rationale` into the governed stream (the declared shape has no home for it); ~~`GrantWaiver` declares `effectiveAt` REQUIRED while the handler overwrites it with the command timestamp~~ **(CLOSED — below)**; ~~`DecompositionRejected.blockingObservationIds` is structurally satisfied and **empty on every live path**~~ **(CLOSED — below)**; ~~`RecompositionCompleted` emits a `parentCompletionClaimId` without comparing it to the one the command required~~ **(CLOSED — below)**. Each is real, none is a regression introduced here, and each is a separate deliberate change.
  - **`parentCompletionClaimId` CLOSED — a required field nothing read, turned into a PRECONDITION rather than deleted.** `CompleteRecomposition` declares it REQUIRED and `completeRecomposition` never referenced `p.parentCompletionClaimId` at all: the event takes the claim from the CONTRACT. That is the `GrantWaiver.effectiveAt` shape a second time — a contract inviting a caller to believe they set something — and **deleting the field was available** (the command is UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED) **and is the weaker answer.** The caller naming the claim it believes it is settling is a meaningful assertion, and checking it catches a caller completing the **wrong contract** — a real mistake at a real seam, since the recomposition-contract id and the claim id are different ids and a caller that has muddled them was otherwise told it succeeded. So it refuses on disagreement (the REG-F-017 medicine) rather than silently preferring one side; the event still records the contract's claim, now provably the same value. **Zero blast radius** — every existing caller already names the right claim, measured by the suite passing unchanged. Mutant: neutralise the guard and only the refusal test reddens, the control staying green.
  - **THE TWO ANSWERS ARE DIFFERENT ON PURPOSE, and the difference is the useful part.** `GrantWaiver.effectiveAt` was **deleted** and this one is **checked**, from the same diagnosis. A caller-supplied *timestamp* for a governance act is something the engine must not honour at all — honouring it permits backdating. A caller-supplied *identity of the thing being settled* is something the engine should honour by **verifying**, because the caller is the only party that knows what it meant to complete. "A required field nothing reads" names the defect; it does not settle the remedy.
  - **`blockingObservationIds` CLOSED — the derivation was sound and NOTHING EXERCISED IT.** The verifier reported it *"empty on every live path"*, and the sharper reading is that this was a statement about the SUITE, not about the code: the filter loads each cited observation and keeps only blocking severities, and it can plainly return non-empty. What was true is that **no test ever drove it** — repo-wide, exactly one test passed `observationIds` to `ValidateDecomposition`, and with `CONDITIONALLY_VALID`, which never reaches the rejection path. So the severity filter, the unresolvable-id rule, and the *"not every cited observation is a blocker"* distinction were all unproven. `decomposition-blocking-observations.test.ts` now arranges a real BLOCKING and a real MATERIAL observation against a real assessment and rejects a real decomposition citing both. Mutant: neutralise the filter (copy `observationIds` wholesale) and two of the four redden. **A derivation with a careful comment and no arrangement is the shape this programme keeps finding — the comment is not the evidence.**
  - **`TacticalChangeApplied.rationale` CLOSED as a RECORDED GAP, not a fix.** The command declares `rationale` REQUIRED; the event's declared home for it is `TacticalChangeRequested`, which **nothing emits**. So the argued justification for a tactical change is recorded nowhere, and the fix disclosed that rather than smuggling the field onto an event whose shape excludes it. Counted and pinned by the REG-F-021 census — see the REQUEST-half measurement there, where the tempting generalisation turned out to be false.
  - **`GrantWaiver.effectiveAt` CLOSED — a REQUIRED field the engine discarded, removed from the contract rather than honoured.** The command declared it required; `grantWaiver`'s `extraMutate` wrote `command.issuedAt` over it. **Every caller supplied a value that did nothing** — nine test sites and, more to the point, both demo routes, which sent `hostNow()` through a UI action. Two readings were possible and **the handler already had the safer one**: the moment a waiver became effective is a fact about the ACT, and honouring a caller-supplied value would permit a waiver to be **BACKDATED** — the manufacture-a-governance-fact shape REG-F-014 records, on a governance object whose whole purpose is to excuse a control. The ratified sibling settles it: **`ApproveDecision` (DOC-007 §22.1) declares no `effectiveAt` either** and lets the engine stamp it. So the authored command shape was the defect, it is removed, and the vocab entry records why. The EVENT payload still carries `effectiveAt`, read off committed state; the demo's behaviour is unchanged because its `issuedAt` came from the same clock. **A contract that requires a field the engine refuses to honour is worse than one that omits it** — it invites a caller to believe they set something.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/{artifact,pwa-authoring,governance,execution,intent,decomposition,runtime-binding}.ts` (twenty fixes), `verif/emitted-event-guard.ts` + `verif/emitted-event-guard.test.ts` + `vitest.projects.ts`, the scope correction in `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts`, and the `RPH-BAS-007` correction in `enforcement-register.ts`. The vocab and every declared schema are unchanged and were correct throughout — **no contract was widened to make a handler pass.** **Status:** **CLOSED.** All twenty-one nonconforming event types conform, the defect ledger is **EMPTY**, and the gate that found them stands with its own liveness control. What remains OPEN is not this finding: 43 declared event types are still emitted by nothing and therefore unmeasured, and four named residues (below) are separate deliberate changes. The one contract that moved was **authored, not ratified**, and it moved because its only consumer could not read what it omitted.

### REG-F-021 — A command declares which event it emits and emits a different one, so a ratified lifecycle moment is never recorded
- **Date:** 2026-08-04 (found by censusing the event surface after REG-F-020 closed) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — the vocabulary's command→event binding does not describe what the engine does.
- **Statement:** `RequestAssuranceAssessment` is bound in `vocab/m3-commands-events.json` to **`AssuranceAssessmentRequested`**. `requestAssuranceAssessment` emits **`AssuranceAssessmentStarted`**. So the REQUESTED moment — a declared event with its own payload contract — is **never recorded by anything**, and the STARTED moment is recorded by a command the table says produces something else.
  - **THE CORPUS EXPECTS BOTH.** The §26 worked trace has `AssuranceAssessmentRequested` at **seq 31**, and `expected-events.jsonl` transcribes it. The fixture agrees with the corpus, the engine disagrees with both, and the replay oracle compares them only by TYPE COVERAGE — so this sat inside a harness explicitly built to catch this class after the `PwuMarkedReady` / `PwuStateChanged` drift Increment 23 found by hand. **Same shape, one lifecycle over.**
- **THE CENSUS THAT FOUND IT, because the number is the finding.** Three surfaces claim to answer "which events exist": **DECLARED** (132 payload contracts in `EVENTS`), **BOUND** (84, the vocab's command→event table), **EMITTED** (89, measured by instrumenting the commit path across the whole suite). They disagree three ways:
  - **6 EMITTED but not BOUND** — `AssuranceAssessmentStarted`, `AssuranceAssessmentEscalated`, `AssuranceIndependenceViolated`, `DecompositionRejected`, `RecompositionConflictDetected`, `RecompositionFailed`. Not a defect to fix by deletion: a kernel that CHOOSES its outcome event (`eventType: evaluation.event`) cannot be expressed as a static command→event row. What is wrong is that the table reads as though it were total.
  - **1 BOUND but never EMITTED** — the finding above.
  - **42 NEITHER bound nor emitted** — declared payload contracts nothing binds and nothing produces. Mostly surface for milestones that are not built, and **that is not a defect to force to zero**; it is a number that should only fall.
- **AND IT IS NOT A NAMING QUESTION AT ALL — THE LIFECYCLE IS RATIFIED AND THE ENGINE SKIPS THREE STATES OF IT.** This entry first recorded the fix as "a lifecycle question for DOC-004, not a rename", i.e. as something needing a ratification. **That was a claim about my search, and it did not survive checking.** The lifecycle is already ratified, in `rph-domain/src/transitions.data.ts`:
  - `REQUESTED → EVIDENCE_PENDING` — trigger *"AssuranceAssessmentRequested; claims instantiated, evidence requirements evaluated"*
  - `EVIDENCE_PENDING → READY` — guard ***"all required evidence present and admissible per §6.2"***
  - `READY → ASSESSING` — trigger *"selectAssuranceEvaluator (AssuranceEvaluatorSelected) then beginAssuranceAssessment (AssuranceAssessmentStarted)"*
  
  `requestAssuranceAssessment` creates the assessment **already in `ASSESSING`**, with `startedAt` set, emitting the event that belongs to the LAST arrow. **Measured: `REQUESTED`, `EVIDENCE_PENDING` and `READY` are written by NO production line.** Three ratified states no assessment ever occupies; two of three arrows never taken. The unemitted event is the visible tip of that, not the whole of it.
  - **STATED FAIRLY: the admissibility guard is not simply absent.** `admitEvidence` runs the ratified `evidenceAdmissibility` rule (RPH-EVD-007, its own enforcement probe), so evidence IS checked — at the ADMISSION command rather than at the `EVIDENCE_PENDING → READY` arrow. What the collapse costs is the ORDERING the machine specifies: nothing requires the admitted evidence to be in place *before* the assessment is in ASSESSING.
  - **AND THE CODE ALREADY KNEW, with the record one item stale.** `completeAssuranceAssessment` carries the note that DOC-004 §32's `selectAssuranceEvaluator`, `recordCriterionResult`, `submitEvidenceForAssessment` and `beginAssuranceAssessment` "do not exist — 4 of §32's 13", and that their absence is why the evaluator is smuggled through the verdict. **`submitEvidenceForAssessment` HAS since been built and registered**, so the count is THREE, and the record had gone stale in the *safe* direction — claiming less capability than exists. Corrected in place. The two that remain missing are exactly the commands the skipped arrows run through.
- **Disposition:** **RECORDED with a standing gate, not fixed.** `verif/event-surface-census.test.ts` holds the three event surfaces against each other and now also pins the three unoccupied lifecycle states, so neither can drift silently — the day a handler writes `REQUESTED`, that assertion reddens and the pin comes out. **Not fixed** because restoring the arrows means building `selectAssuranceEvaluator` and `beginAssuranceAssessment` and re-sequencing every caller that today requests-and-starts in one dispatch; that is a substantial domain increment, not a rename. Renaming the emitted event to match the binding would make the census green and **destroy** the STARTED moment, which the assurance view actually reads.
  - **THE PINNED MEASUREMENT IS A SNAPSHOT AND SAYS SO.** A live count would need every test to run in one worker, which is exactly the cross-worker aggregation `emitted-event-guard.ts` documents itself as unable to do. The pin was **re-measured against current code** before it landed — the first draft was hand-assembled from an earlier survey and wrongly listed `PwuSatisfied` and `PwuBaselined` as emitted (nothing emits them) while omitting `ConstraintAsserted`, which this session's own §25 chain had started emitting an hour earlier. A hand-copied measurement is not a measurement.
- **THE SAME SHAPE LANDED TWICE INDEPENDENTLY, AND THE OBVIOUS GENERALISATION IS FALSE.** REG-F-020's `TacticalChangeApplied` fix could not carry its command's **REQUIRED** `rationale`: the declared home for that argument is `TacticalChangeRequested`, and **nothing emits it** — so the justification for a tactical change is recorded nowhere in the governed stream, which the fix disclosed rather than smuggling the field onto an event whose shape excludes it. With this entry's `AssuranceAssessmentRequested`, that is two REQUEST events missing in a row, which invited the reading *"the engine records the act and not the asking."* **Measured, it is not true:** of five declared `*Requested` events, **two ARE emitted** (`RuntimeBindingRequested`, `WaiverRequested`) and **three are not** (`AssuranceAssessmentRequested`, `ClarificationRequested`, `TacticalChangeRequested`). The unevenness is the fact; the sweeping version was a guess, and it is recorded here as a guess that did not survive its own measurement. The census pins the three so the number may only fall.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `verif/event-surface-census.test.ts` (the gate). The vocab binding and the handler both stand until the lifecycle question is answered; **neither is edited to make the other look right.** **Status:** OPEN — gated and counted; the REQUESTED moment remains unrecorded, and with it the argued justification for three acts.
- **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — the engine now runs the ratified §30 lifecycle.** Five increments, each with its own gate; `AssuranceAssessmentRequested` is emitted, `AssuranceEvidenceRequired` and `AssuranceEvaluatorSelected` exist and fire, and the census's bound-but-unemitted list is **empty** for the first time. The corpus's own §26 worked trace expects the REQUESTED event at seq 31 and the engine finally produces it. **Status: CLOSED**, with one residual named below that is NOT a defect of this work but a state it made reachable.
  - **INCREMENT 0 — THE CONTRACT HAD TO MOVE BEFORE THE MACHINE, and the obvious order fails on the first dispatch.** `startedAt` was a REQUIRED `z.string()` and `kit.ts` validates produced state on every write, so an assessment created in `REQUESTED` — which by definition has not started — could not be persisted **at all**. And it was a **consistency correction, not a relaxation**: `m1-object-fields` openItems[6] retains six DOC-002-only fields *"(optional) per union rule"*, and **five of the six were emitted optional while `startedAt` alone was emitted required.** The guarantee **moved rather than lapsing** — optional at the schema, mandatory at the state that implies it, checked at `commitState`, expressed as the EXEMPT set so a state added later defaults to REQUIRING it.
  - **INCREMENTS 1+2 — THE CODEBASE REFUSED THE INCREMENT BOUNDARY, and it was right.** The roadmap split "declare the contracts" from "build the handlers". `command-dispatch-census.test.ts` rejected the interval: its `UNDRIVEN` allowance is **empty by design** — *"an entry is a claim that a ratified command should stay unobserved, which needs an argument stronger than 'nobody got to it'"* — and mine would have been *"not yet, by plan"*, which is that argument wearing a schedule. **A real rule, discovered by being refused: EVENTS may be declared ahead of their emitters (the census pins the gap and names each one's exit increment); COMMANDS may not.**
  - **AND THE UNEMITTED EVENT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN EMITTED** — the stronger explanation, found when the REG-F-020 ledger refused a test fixture that tried. Its AUTHORED payload REQUIRED `evaluator` (chosen two arrows later) and `disposition` (the outcome, produced at the very end). **An event announcing that an assessment had been REQUESTED was required to carry who would assess it and how it turned out.** Its own annotation prescribed `disposition: 'PENDING'` — not a member of `AssuranceDisposition`. **Unsatisfiable, not merely unwired.** Corrected on the standing rule for authored shapes: −`evaluator`, −`disposition`, +`assessmentId`, +`subjectSemanticVersions` (DOC-004 invariant 2).
  - **INCREMENT 3 — AND THE GUARD I FIRST WROTE MADE GATE A UNREACHABLE.** Keying the `EVIDENCE_PENDING → READY` arrow on ALL declared requirements means you can never be `ASSESSING` with anything outstanding, so the completion-time refusal could never fire. **Two guards that cannot both matter is one guard and a decoration.** Caught by RPH-ASR-002's probe becoming unconstructible — REG-F-017's shape. The fix is what §6.1 says: `requiredForDispositions: 'ALL'` is evidence needed to **judge** (a precondition of assessing); `SATISFIED_ONLY` gates a positive **conclusion**. An assessor may examine work and REJECT it without ever seeing evidence only a SATISFIED verdict would need. Complementary, not redundant.
  - **THE BLAST RADIUS WAS 34 FILES, NOT FOUR CALLERS.** The roadmap said *"four production callers, plus fixtures"* — it was **135 failing tests across 34 files**. Four production sites took one added dispatch each; the rest were drives asserting a lifecycle that genuinely changed. Recorded because the estimate was wrong in a predictable direction: a caller count measures who ISSUES the command, and what breaks is everything that ASSERTS the resulting state.
  - **INCREMENT 4 — AND THE ONE GOVERNANCE GUARANTEE THIS BUYS.** `driveAssessmentToAssessing` writes the §30 sequence once (dispatch-agnostic: the demo route hands it a collector so its batch keeps all-or-nothing semantics). It does **not** reduce traffic and says so in the file. The canonical drive now SELECTS its evaluator: **WHO assessed becomes a committed act with its own event**, instead of arriving inside the verdict as `validatorResult.executionProvenance.evaluator` — the assessor's identity as a field of the answer they produced. The rest of the increment is ordering fidelity; this is a guarantee that did not exist.
  - **WHAT REQUESTED DOES AND DOES NOT MEAN NOW.** `EVIDENCE_PENDING` and `READY` are occupied — an assessment genuinely waits in them. **`REQUESTED` is CROSSED, not occupied**, and that is faithful rather than unfinished: §30 puts the request and the requirement evaluation under ONE trigger (*"AssuranceAssessmentRequested; claims instantiated, evidence requirements evaluated, missing evidence requested"*), so both events commit atomically and the assessment lands beyond it. Occupying it would need a second dispatch the corpus does not name.
  - **RESIDUAL R-1 — CLOSED 2026-08-05, AND ALL THREE DISPOSITIONS OF IT WERE WRONG, INCLUDING THE CLOSING ONE.** It was first recorded as *"the machinery is NAMED by the corpus and not wired to this state"*; then RE-DISPOSITIONED to a ratification gap on the measurement that `CANCELLED` has no in-arrow anywhere; then CLOSED on the account below. **All three missed the arrow, and the third also misplaced where it was lost.** DOC-004 §30's *"Alternate transitions"* block declares SIX arrows, and the sixth — **`ANY ACTIVE → CANCELLED`** — did not reach the machine. So the arrow was RATIFIED the whole time: **DELIVERY, not authoring** — the same shape as the `initialState` correction the same day.
    - **⚠ THE CLOSING ACCOUNT IS SUPERSEDED BY REG-F-025 (same day, hours later). It read:** *"five were transcribed into `m2-transitions.json` and the sixth was dropped, because its from-state is QUANTIFIED and a quantifier is not a row."* **The row WAS transcribed** — spelled `ANY_ACTIVE`, present since `19a1b20f`, note and all. It was discarded one layer lower by `gen-transitions.ts`, which recognises `Any active` with a **space**. Kept in place rather than rewritten: the wrong account is the more instructive half, and it is exactly the error that asking the general form found.
    - **"ACTIVE" resolves to the four states before an outcome** (REQUESTED, EVIDENCE_PENDING, READY, ASSESSING) — the same partition `assessmentHasConcluded` uses for baseline promotion, derived independently there and agreeing here. Post-outcome change is what `INVALIDATED` and `WAIVER_EXPIRED` are for, and §32's `invalidateAssuranceAssessment` invalidates a **VERDICT**, which a cancelled assessment never reached.
    - **IT OVERRULED AN AUTHORED JUDGEMENT OF MINE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF LOOKING.** The design proposed cancellation from the three PRE-ASSESSING states only, reasoning that an assessment being judged should reach a disposition since `INCONCLUSIVE` exists for *"could not decide"*. **The ratified rule is broader than the rule I was about to author**, and it wins. A test asserts the ratified breadth so the narrower authored version cannot creep back.
    - **ONE OF EACH, SAID SEPARATELY:** the TRANSITION is delivered from the corpus; the COMMAND and EVENT names (`CancelAssuranceAssessment` / `AssuranceAssessmentCancelled`) are **AUTHORED**, since §32 names no cancel command and §31 no cancel event. It would be easy to present the whole increment as ratified because the arrow is, or as invented because the names are.
    - **AND MY OWN INCREMENT-0 INVARIANT CAUGHT IT.** The first cancel of a stalled assessment was REFUSED: `CANCELLED` was not in the `startedAt` exempt set, so an assessment cancelled BEFORE it began could not be persisted. The guard built in increment 0 refused the increment that needed it — the exempt set doing its job, since a terminal state newly reachable from a pre-start state is exactly the change that should have to come back and say so.
  - **The superseded disposition, kept for the record:**
  - **RESIDUAL R-1 — RE-DISPOSITIONED 2026-08-05: it is NOT an implementation task, it is a ratification gap.** The note below said the machinery was *"NAMED by the corpus and not wired to this state"*. **Measured: `CANCELLED` has NO in-arrow anywhere in the ratified machine** (REG-F-023), and `INVALIDATED` is reachable only from `SATISFIED` / `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED`. So there is no ratified arrow to wire — closing a stalled assessment requires the corpus to DECLARE one. Moved to REG-E-024. The original note stands as written:
  - **RESIDUAL R-1 (OPEN, and NOT a defect of this work): an assessment stalled in `EVIDENCE_PENDING` cannot be closed by any command.** `completeAssuranceAssessment` requires `ASSESSING`, and EVIDENCE_PENDING's only ratified exit is getting the evidence — so an assessment that CANNOT gather what it requires has no way to be closed at all. §30 declares `CANCELLED` and `INVALIDATED` and §32 declares `invalidateAssuranceAssessment`, so the machinery is **NAMED by the corpus and not wired to this state**. Writing an arrow to make a test pass would be inventing a governance fact. Asserted as the engine's ACTUAL behaviour in `assurance-independence.test.ts`, with instructions to replace rather than widen. Unreachable in production today (no policy declares evidence — REG-F-022) and live the day that changes.
  - **RESIDUAL R-2 (OPEN): the census compares its three surfaces as SETS**, so "bound" can never mean "bound to the RIGHT command". During increment 1 `AssuranceAssessmentStarted` looked correct there while still being fired by the wrong command. The gate that actually watched it was a per-aggregate event count in `assessment-ready-arrow.test.ts`. Recorded so the census's green is never read as the stronger claim.
- **NOTATION 2026-08-04 — the design landed, and three of its four "sponsor decisions" were measurable questions I had not measured.** `docs/_working/DESIGN-assessment-lifecycle-restoration.md` now carries the increment's design. Recording what the design got wrong before it was checked, because each error is a shape this register has seen before:
  - **A RULING EXTENDED TO AN EVENT IT DOES NOT NAME.** The design claimed both new events (`AssuranceEvidenceRequired`, `AssuranceEvaluatorSelected`) sit under DOC-004 §31's *"ratified names … schematized nowhere … a schema-and-wiring task, **not** a ratification decision"* ruling. **They do not.** That paragraph's subject is the §38 "missing evidence" field and its *"Both events"* is `AssuranceEvidenceRequired` + **`AssuranceEvidenceReceived`**. `AssuranceEvaluatorSelected` appears **nowhere** in it — its four occurrences in DOC-004 are the §31 list, the composition bullet, and the §32 list twice. Both events are ratified NAMES; **only one carries the ruling.** The other is authored on the ordinary standing grant and must say so rather than borrowing a blessing that does not name it.
  - **A LIST READ AS CARRYING A RULE IT DOES NOT STATE.** The design claimed a fused request-and-begin command would *contradict* §32's ratified command list. §32's entire normative content after the names is *"Every mutation must enforce: policy version; object semantic version; independence; authorization; expected revision."* **No exhaustiveness clause, no dispatch-granularity rule** — and §31's own annotation rules the other way on exactly this granularity question, holding that a coarser set which "spans" finer steps **composes** rather than conflicts. The true objection is weaker and is the one that stands: a fusion leaves a §32-named command **unbuilt** *and* invents a shape the corpus does not name. Both, not one instead of the other.
  - **"NO READER" ASSERTED WHILE A VALIDATOR WAS READING IT ON EVERY WRITE — and this one changes the build order.** The design called moving `startedAt` *"a free correction today"* on the measured ground that no production reader consumes it. True of its **value**: no view, projection, query or demo route names it, and the demo's assessment recency ordering keys on `updatedAt`. **But it is a REQUIRED `z.string()` on `AssuranceAssessmentSchema`, and `kit.ts` validates produced state on every write under an explicit "never persist an object that is not a valid domain object" contract.** An assessment created in `REQUESTED` — which by definition has not started — **cannot be persisted at all** until that field is relaxed to optional. Every `RequestAssuranceAssessment` would be rejected `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`, the floor would stop recording, and `PublishPwa` would stay blocked. **A validator is a reader**, and "no reader consumes it" was the wrong question — the writer's own schema was the binding constraint. The contract relaxation must land **before** the first state-machine change, not alongside it.
  - **HOW THEY WERE CAUGHT, and it was not by a test.** Five load-bearing claims were handed to independent verifiers instructed to **refute** rather than confirm, to default to "refuted" on ambiguity, and to report true-but-narrower as refuted with corrected wording; absence claims had to be searched in **both** directions — the identifier *and* the content it would carry. **Two claims stood and were widened; three needed narrowing.** **None of the three would have surfaced as a failing test** — two would have shipped as prose, and the third would have shown up as every assessment write failing on day one of implementation.
  - **NOTATION 2026-08-04 (increments 0-2 landed) — AND THE UNEMITTED EVENT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN EMITTED.** This entry recorded `AssuranceAssessmentRequested` as bound and produced by nothing, and read that as wiring never done. **There is a second reason, and it is stronger: the event was UNEMITTABLE.** Its AUTHORED payload REQUIRED `evaluator` — chosen two arrows later by `selectAssuranceEvaluator`, whose entire purpose is to record that choice — and `disposition`, the assessment's OUTCOME, produced at the very end by `completeAssuranceAssessment`. **An event announcing that an assessment has been REQUESTED was required to carry who would assess it and how it turned out.** With the REG-F-020 event gate live, any conformant emitter was impossible.
    - **AND THE ANNOTATION PRESCRIBED A VALUE ITS OWN TYPE REJECTS.** The `disposition` field's note read `=PENDING`. `PENDING` is **not** a member of `AssuranceDisposition` (SATISFIED, CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED, REJECTED, INCONCLUSIVE, WAIVED, ESCALATED). The shape was **unsatisfiable**, not merely awkward — nobody could have filled it correctly.
    - **CORRECTED, NOT WORKED AROUND**, on the standing rule this programme already applies to authored shapes: *an authored shape that cannot carry what its moment can know is the defect, and authored shapes are ours to correct.* Delta: **−`evaluator`, −`disposition`** (neither knowable at request time); **+`assessmentId`** (every sibling assurance event names its aggregate); **+`subjectSemanticVersions`** (DOC-004 invariant 2 — *"every assessment identifies its subject semantic version"* — the request is where that binding is made, and a subject recorded without its version is the exact gap Increment 10b established the floor cannot work without).
    - **FOUND BY A GUARD, ON MY OWN FIXTURE.** Not by reading the schema: the REG-F-020 emitted-event ledger refused a test fixture that emitted `AssuranceAssessmentRequested`, which is the first time anything in this repository had tried to emit it at all.
  - **AND THE CODEBASE CORRECTED THE ROADMAP'S INCREMENT BOUNDARY.** The roadmap split "declare the contracts" (1) from "build the handlers" (2). `verif/command-dispatch-census.test.ts` refused the interval: a declared, routable command that no test drives is exactly what it exists to catch, and its `UNDRIVEN` allowance is **empty by design** — *"an entry is a claim that a ratified command should stay unobserved, which needs an argument stronger than 'nobody got to it'."* **So EVENTS may be declared ahead of their emitters (the census pins that gap and names each one's exit increment) but COMMANDS may not.** A real rule of this codebase, discovered by being refused, and the two increments landed together.
  - **AND THE INCREMENT IS HONESTLY SMALLER THAN IT LOOKS.** Restoring the ordering does **not** close a soundness hole. Admissibility-at-admission (RPH-EVD-007) is real and non-vacuous; the completion-time backstop (Gate A) is real logic reading an empty set on every shipped policy — **REG-F-022**. What the collapse costs is the **ordering**, and that is what restoring it buys.

### REG-F-022 — Twelve of twelve ratified policies declare which evidence they require; the seeding drops the field, so the gate that enforces it reads an empty set on every policy that ships
- **Date:** 2026-08-04 (found while trying to settle an open decision in the REG-F-021 lifecycle design — the question dissolved and this was underneath it) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — ratified governance content is authored, is well-typed, and never reaches the runtime.
- **Statement:** The PWA ontology's `SeedPolicy` carries **`requiredEvidenceTypes`**, and **12 of 12** catalog policies declare it (the policies are ratified transcriptions; **this field on them is self-declared AUTHORED** — see the correction below). `seedAdditivePolicies` (`seed-workbench.ts`) maps **eleven** fields into the `CreateAssurancePolicy` payload and **that one is not among them**. The whole repository mentions the name in exactly three files — the type declaration, the data, and the vocab — and **nothing reads it**.
- **MEASURED LIVE, from objects read back out of a seeded engine rather than from the source literals:**
  ```
  POLICIES WITH NON-EMPTY requiredEvidence ON THE OBJECT:  0 of 15
  ONTOLOGY seedPolicies DECLARING requiredEvidenceTypes:  12 of 12
  ```
- **⚠ THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT MEASUREMENT WAS UNSOUND, AND I FILED IT ANYWAY. RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THIS REGISTER'S OWN RECURRING DEFECT, COMMITTED BY ME, IN THE ENTRY THAT NAMES IT.** The "0 of 15" above was first produced by a throwaway probe that read `getObject(engine, id)?.state`. **`getObject` returns the state itself, not a wrapper** — so the probe read `state.state`, got `undefined`, and `?? []` turned it into an empty array **for every policy**. A blind reader cannot report anything but zero. **The number was right; the instrument was not, and nothing in the probe could tell those apart.** Compounding it, the probe was deleted after reading, so the load-bearing count in this entry had **no surviving source** and could not be re-run.
  - **WHAT CAUGHT IT:** promoting the probe into a standing gate with a CONTROL — a policy created through the real command path *carrying* an `EvidenceRequirement`, asserted to read back non-empty. The control **reddened immediately**; the finding's own assertion stayed green, because a blind reader and a real gap produce the identical result. That is the whole argument for controls having their own failure mode.
  - **NOW VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED:** `verif/policy-evidence-requirement-census.test.ts` derives both counts from live objects with a reader proven able to see a non-empty value. **Mutation-checked:** mapping the field into `seedAdditivePolicies` reddens the census (naming all 12 catalog policies) and the site assertion, while the CONTROL stays green — so the gate fires on the right limb. The conclusion stands; it now stands on a measurement instead of a coincidence.
- **WHAT IT COSTS — a control that reads an empty set is a control that cannot fail.** `completeAssuranceAssessment` carries **Gate A**: it refuses a `SATISFIED` or `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` disposition while a policy's mandatory evidence is unmet, computing required-minus-received exactly as the §38 view does. Real logic, with a test that proves it fires. But it reads `policy.requiredEvidence`, and that array is `[]` on every policy this product can produce — so on shipped policies it evaluates an empty set and admits everything. **The test that proves it works constructs its own policy with `requiredEvidence` explicitly; nothing shipped exercises it.** Same family as REG-F-015 and REG-F-016: the check is not absent, its *population* is.
  - **And §38's "missing evidence" is therefore always empty** — the field DOC-004 §31 calls "ratified and sourceable in principle", sourced from a set that is always `[]`.
- **THE VACUITY IS THE WHOLE PRODUCTION SURFACE, NOT JUST THE SEEDED CATALOG.** Re-checked against every production creation path: `seedFloorPolicies`, `seedAdditivePolicies`, the reference undertaking's bespoke policy, the demo undertaking's policy, the PWA Designer's `createPolicy`/`editPolicy`, and the agent broker (whose `CreatePolicyInput` has no such member). **None can supply `requiredEvidence`.** The one non-test line that puts the field on a `CreateAssurancePolicy` payload is the **supersede** action, which copies the predecessor's value — always `[]` — so it propagates emptiness rather than introducing content. A version-bump e2e asserts the field survives supersede; **it compares `[]` to `[]`.**
  - **Causally precise:** the handler's `requiredEvidence: p.requiredEvidence ?? []` is the DEFAULTING, not the cause. **No production caller supplies the field**, so changing the default would change nothing. Recorded because the defaulting line is the one a reader lands on first and it is the wrong place to fix.
- **IT IS WIDER THAN THE POLICIES.** Adversarial re-checking found the same field declared a second time, on the ontology's **`PwuTemplate`** type, with **14 further carriers** among the templates — equally unread. **26 authored declarations, none consumed.**
- **CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY, SAME DAY — THE DROPPED FIELD IS SELF-DECLARED *AUTHORED*, NOT RATIFIED, AND THAT CHANGES THE REMEDIATION.** This entry first said "12 of 12 **ratified** catalog policies declare which evidence they require". The **policies** are ratified and machine-checked transcriptions; **the field is not.** Every one of the 12 `sourceSection` notes in `ontology.data.ts` lists `requiredEvidenceTypes` explicitly under **"AUTHORED (no ratified source)"**, next to `rationale`, `evaluatorRole` and `failureSeverity`. So the dropped values were **an authored guess, not ratified intent**, and any plan framed as *"restore the dropped ratified requirement"* is mis-scoped. The finding stands — authored governance content silently dropped, with a gate reading the empty result — but the fix is **author-and-ratify from scratch**, not **re-connect a wire**.
- **NOT A ONE-LINE FIX, WHICH IS WHY IT IS FILED SEPARATELY RATHER THAN FOLDED INTO REG-F-021.** `EvidenceRequirementSchema` is a **`z.strictObject` of nine fields**: `id`, `evidenceType`, `description`, `purpose`, `cardinality`, `admissibilityRules`, `freshnessRule?`, `requiredForDispositions`, `mayBeWaived`. **The ontology carries one of the nine, as `readonly string[]`** — so no name-to-name mapping was ever available, and the eight remaining fields per requirement must be authored and ratified. Every value it does carry is a valid `EvidenceType` (`SOURCE`, `ARTIFACT`, `TRACE`, `ANALYSIS`, `TEST_RESULT`, `MEASUREMENT`, `OBSERVATION`, `REVIEW`, `APPROVAL` — all nine enum members are used), so it is well-typed; it is simply not enough to constitute a requirement. **Inventing the other eight to close a number is the disease this programme treats.**
  - **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-05 — IT IS NINE OF NINE, NOT EIGHT.** The line above says *"the ontology carries one of the nine"*, which reads as one field **delivered** and eight missing. Measured: `requiredEvidenceTypes` is listed under **`AUTHORED (no ratified source)`** in **every** policy's own `sourceSection` — including `pol_intent_fidelity`, one of only two policies that even has a corpus "Required evidence" section. So **zero of the nine are delivered**; the one field present is authored too, and closing this by construction means authoring on top of authoring. The ruling above is unchanged and better supported.
  - **⚠ THE FOLLOWING BULLET IS FALSE AND IS RETAINED STRUCK, BECAUSE THE ERROR IS THE FINDING — see REG-F-030.** ~~**AND THE CORPUS GROUNDS LESS THAN THE FIELD COUNT SUGGESTS.** Only **2 of 12** policies have a required-evidence section at all (§15.5, §16.4), and both give a **prose list of item names** — no ids, no `evidenceType`, no cardinality, no disposition-gating, no waivability. Ten policies have no textual basis whatsoever.~~ **CORRECTED: it is ELEVEN of twelve, and 89 items — the heading is `Required evidence` in §15/§16 and plain `Evidence` from §17 on, so the "2" was a fact about the search string.** Measurement and proposal: `docs/_working/DESIGN-evidence-requirements.md` (v2); raised as **REG-E-026**, ratified and closed 2026-08-05.
  - **THE LOAD-BEARING FIELD IS `requiredForDispositions`, AND IT IS NOT A FORMATTING CHOICE.** `ALL` gates *assessing* — every assessment would sit in `EVIDENCE_PENDING`, the canonical drive submits no evidence, and **the product would ship unable to complete an assessment**. `SATISFIED_ONLY` gates a positive *conclusion* — Gate A becomes real and the lifecycle still flows. The corpus states neither, so the two readings differ by whether the system can assess at all.
- **THE FIELD IS INVISIBLE PAST THE PACKAGE BOUNDARY, WHICH IS WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT.** `EngineSeedPolicy` (`engine.ts`) — the engine's own port type for an ontology policy — **does not declare `requiredEvidenceTypes` at all**. So the seeding code could not have read it even by accident, and no type error was ever available to report the drop. `validateOntology`'s `checkSeedPolicies` reads only `criteria`, `independenceRequirement` and `failureSeverity`, so the ontology validator does not check it either. **A field can be authored, well-typed at its source, carried through codegen, and structurally unreachable by every consumer — with no gate anywhere reporting it.**
- **IT IS REG-F-001, STILL ALIVE, ON A FIELD NOBODY CHECKED.** The founding finding of this register reads: *"Seeded policy objects existed that the runtime never read … Docs described a governing layer the code did not perform — the motivating evidence for the anti-vacuity clause."* The wiring programme closed that at the level of whole objects and whole rules. **This is the same defect one field down**: the policy object IS read, the gate that reads it IS wired, and the specific authored content that would give the gate anything to say is dropped in the seeding. **A layer can be de-vacuified object-by-object and still be hollow field-by-field**, and nothing in the existing gates measures that — which is the generalisable lesson and the reason this is filed rather than quietly patched.
- **NOTATION 2026-08-05 — A SECOND INSTANCE, FOUND BY ASKING THE GENERAL QUESTION, AND A GATE SO THERE IS NO THIRD.** REG-F-022 was found by chasing one field. Asking what the ontology AUTHORS versus what the seeding DELIVERS — derived by intercepting the real seeder's dispatches, never by reading either side's source — found a second: **`appliesToPwuKinds`**. Twelve catalog policies declare which PWU kinds they apply to; `seedAdditivePolicies` names it nowhere, and **nothing in the repository reads it**. Every catalog policy is instead seeded with the identical wholesale scope `applicableObjectTypes: ['PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT']`.
  - **AND THE FIELD THAT REPLACES IT IS NOT READ EITHER.** `applicableObjectTypes` is stored on the policy object and consulted by **no applicability decision anywhere**. So policy SCOPE is authored, dropped, and then not consulted — the assurance catalog has no operative notion of which policy applies to what.
  - **I WROTE THE FALSE EXEMPTION MYSELF, AND THE FILE'S OWN NEXT TEST IS WHY IT DID NOT SURVIVE.** `appliesToPwuKinds` was first entered in the gate's `ACCOUNTED_FOR` table with the reasoning *"the value is not silently lost — it is overridden by a broader scope"*. That was asserted, not checked; checking it took one grep. **A list of exemptions is exactly how a census rots into an allowlist**, which is why the gate carries a companion assertion that no exemption may name a field that IS delivered, and every exemption must name a field the ontology actually authors. The rot was mine and it was caught in the same commit.
  - **THE GATE:** `packages/rph-engine/src/ontology-delivery-census.test.ts`. Every authored ontology key must be **delivered verbatim** or **named in `ACCOUNTED_FOR` with the route its content takes** — *"not yet" is not a route*. Four rows are legitimately accounted for (`findingTypes` + `findingAnnotations` + `failureSeverity` are TRANSFORMED into `findingDefinitions`; `sourceSection` is ontology-internal provenance). **Mutation-checked:** silently dropping `evaluatorRole` from the seeding makes the census name it. So the next dropped field is reported rather than discovered a year later by someone chasing a different bug.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `seed-workbench.ts` + the ontology, once the requirement content is authored. **Status:** **CLOSED 2026-08-05** (REG-E-026 ratified; see its Section E disposition). The content is authored from DOC-004's own 89 items and delivered; the census pin at zero is DELETED per its own instruction rather than widened, and replaced by a positive census of what arrives. **Two things this entry got wrong are corrected by REG-F-030 and recorded there rather than silently amended here:** the corpus grounds ELEVEN of twelve policies, not two, and the remediation was therefore not the "author-and-ratify from scratch" this entry called for — it was overwhelmingly TRANSCRIPTION. **Sequencing:** does **not** block REG-F-021's lifecycle restoration, and that restoration must **not** assume this is fixed — the `EVIDENCE_PENDING → READY` guard is written against `requiredEvidence` either way, passes vacuously today, and starts biting the day this lands. Restoring the lifecycle on a silently-empty required set would produce a lifecycle that *looks* like it gates evidence and does not.

### REG-F-023 — Five declared states no arrow can reach, one of them an entire machine — and a sixth group that was my own miscount
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (asked immediately after REG-F-021 closed: how many OTHER states are occupied by nothing) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — ratified state vocabulary that the ratified transition table cannot enter.
- **Statement:** Walking every machine in `transitions.data.ts` and asking which declared states are neither the initial state nor the target of any declared transition yields **ELEVEN**, across four machines. **Two of those machines declare states and NO transitions at all** — not partially wired, *names*.
  - **`AggregateAssuranceDisposition` — SIX states, ZERO arrows.** `SATISFIED`, `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED`, `REJECTED`, `INCONCLUSIVE`, `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED`, `UNASSESSED`. This is the axis that would roll assurance up **across an aggregate** — so the thing declared and unrunnable is the answer to *"is this body of work assured overall"*.
  - **`ValidatorRegistryEntry.status` — THREE states, ZERO arrows.** `ACTIVE`, `DEGRADED`, `DISABLED`. Validator health (§22's DEGRADED / DISABLED) is declared and unrunnable: **the assurance system cannot record that one of its own validators is failing.**
  - **`AssuranceAssessment.state.CANCELLED`** — declared **TERMINAL** with no in-arrow. See the disposition of REG-F-021's residual R-1 below; this is that residual's real shape.
  - **`PWU.assuranceState.UNASSESSED`** — the state meaning *"assurance applies and has not happened yet"*. The axis starts at `NOT_REQUIRED`, and no arrow enters `UNASSESSED`, so **a PWU that NEEDS assurance can never be marked as awaiting it.**
