- **A TERMINAL STATE WITH NO IN-ARROW IS THE WORST SHAPE** — an ENDING nothing can end at. Exactly one qualifies (`CANCELLED`). `PWU.assuranceState.NOT_REQUIRED` is terminal-and-initial, which is a coherent modelling choice (start there, never return) and is deliberately NOT counted; a check that conflated them would report a defect where there is a design.
- **THE FIRST MEASUREMENT UNDER-COUNTED, IN THE COMFORTABLE DIRECTION.** A regex over the source found **five**. Deriving from `getMachine` found **eleven** — the pattern had run past the end of one machine block into the next, at one point reporting **zero transitions for a machine that has fifteen**. Recorded because "less wrong than you thought" is the hardest instrument failure to notice, and because it is the same lesson as REG-F-022's blind reader one day earlier: **an instrument that can silently see nothing must be able to prove it sees something.** The gate now derives from the runtime machines and carries a CONTROL asserting the machines are loaded and carry arrows.
- **IT IS REG-F-001'S FAMILY AGAIN, ONE LEVEL UP FROM REG-F-022.** The founding finding was seeded objects the runtime never read. REG-F-022 was an authored FIELD no consumer could reach. This is a declared STATE VOCABULARY the transition table cannot enter — *"docs described a governing layer the code did not perform"*, measured at a third altitude. A reviewer choosing among dispositions, or a designer relying on a state existing, is reading a promise the machine cannot keep.
- **CORRECTED 2026-08-05, SAME DAY — THE COUNT IS FIVE, NOT ELEVEN, AND THE ERROR WAS A CLAIM ABOUT MY SEARCH.** Six of the eleven belonged to **`AggregateAssuranceDisposition`**, which **JAN-CMDPRE-SPEC-001 §2 had already ruled** *"a computed disposition rollup with no transitions — not a state machine; out of transition-legality scope"*. A computed disposition has no in-arrows because nothing MOVES it: its states are outcomes DERIVED from what they summarise. Counting them as unreachable is a category error. **I had searched the transition table and this register, and not the spec that interprets them** — so a number I had just finished deriving carefully from runtime data was still wrong, because I checked the instrument and not the POPULATION. The count went 5 (regex, under-counted) → 11 (derived, over-scoped) → **5** (derived, prior ruling honoured).
  - **AND THE EXCLUSION CANNOT BE DERIVED — WHICH IS ITSELF THE FINDING'S SHARPEST PART.** `ValidatorRegistryEntry.status` has the **identical structural signature**: zero transitions, `initialState: undefined`, no terminal states. The same §2 catalogues it **as a machine**, with a transition table that is simply empty. Nothing in the data separates the two; one was ruled a computed disposition and the other an unbuilt machine **by judgement**. The gate therefore CITES the ruling rather than inferring it, and asserts the identical signature explicitly so no later reader "simplifies" it into a rule that would silently swallow the real finding.
- **THE SURVIVING FIVE**, each still a declared state no arrow can enter: `AssuranceAssessment.state.CANCELLED` (declared TERMINAL — an ending nothing can end at), `PWU.assuranceState.UNASSESSED`, and `ValidatorRegistryEntry.status`'s ACTIVE / DEGRADED / DISABLED.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `packages/rph-domain/src/state-reachability.test.ts` (the standing gate; the set may SHRINK and may not GROW without an argument). The remediation is NOT repository work: each entry is either an arrow the corpus must declare or a state it should retire, which is **ratification**, not wiring. **Status:** **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — the unreachable set is ZERO**, the first time since this entry was filed. The remediation was ratification, exactly as this entry said: `ANY ACTIVE → CANCELLED` turned out to be DECLARED all along and missing only its trigger (REG-F-037); `AggregateAssuranceDisposition` was never a machine (REG-F-035); `PWU.assuranceState.UNASSESSED` is the declared initial state; and `ValidatorRegistryEntry.status`'s three — the last group — got their arrows by amending §35 (REG-F-038). **⚠ AND THE GATE'S OWN CONTROL HAD TO BE REBUILT TO SURVIVE THE FIX**: it asserted `unreachableStates() > 0` against the live machine set, so it was guaranteed to fail the day the last defect was cured — a control whose witness is the defect it guards against. It now supplies its own synthetic witness. The set may not GROW without an argument.

---

### REG-F-024 — The de minimis floor is scoped to one object type, so enforcing ratified applicability would stop it governing the work it exists to govern
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found by BUILDING the §5.1 applicability check and running it, not by reading) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a disclosed provisional narrowing becomes load-bearing the moment the ratified rule is enforced.
- **Statement:** DOC-004 §5.1 ratifies an `ApplicabilityRule` and §5.2 the outcomes a determination yields. Neither was ever consulted: `applicability` was hardcoded `{}` on every policy. Increments 3-4 schematized §5.1 and DELIVERED the authored scope. Wiring the determination as a precondition on `RequestAssuranceAssessment` — the only moment the question can be asked — **refused 54 drives**, and the canonical reference undertaking failed at step **#47**, requesting an assessment of an **EVIDENCE object** under `floor.schema-invariant`.
- **⚠ THE DIAGNOSIS BELOW IS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED 2026-08-05 (same entry, kept in place). It read: the mismatch is not the drive's fault because `seedFloorPolicies` scopes all three floor policies to `PROFESSIONAL_WORK_ARCHITECTURE`, *"and its own comment says why: the single-value `applicableObjectTypes` limitation is §16-unresolved; the plane-agnostic array is a later reconciliation"*.** The narrow scope was real and the 54 refusals were real. **The explanation was not, in three ways, and I wrote all three:**
  - **(a) There is no single-value limitation and there never was.** The field is `z.array(ProfessionalWorkObjectTypeSchema)` — a required array of the ratified 22-value enum. The vocab entry mentions no §16 and no limitation; its only note records the DOC-002 → DOC-007 rename from `targetTypes`. **The array was available the whole time.**
  - **(b) The comment was wrong when WRITTEN, not stale.** `git log -S` places it in `3162083a`, *the same commit* as the seeding it describes. It never described a constraint that had since been lifted.
  - **(c) The test fixture had been doing it right all along.** `__tests__/floor-fixtures.ts` seeds three object types. Somebody needed the array, used it, and it worked — so the suite passed against a floor scoped more honestly than the one that shipped.
  - **I took a CODE COMMENT as evidence of a CONTRACT CONSTRAINT and never read the schema** — while writing the entry that was supposed to be the careful account. Same error as REG-F-026 group (b)'s note, one artifact over, and it is what made a five-minute correction look like a ratification blocker for a week. **The floor is UNIVERSAL by design (§8.4)**; it was scoped narrowly by a limitation that did not exist.
- **THE ENFORCEMENT WAS WRITTEN, RUN, AND DELIBERATELY WITHDRAWN.** Enforcing today would turn a disclosed provisional narrowing into a hard refusal, and stop the floor governing exactly the work it exists to govern. **The green suite that followed would be a system that had stopped assessing, not one that had started checking** — which is the same trade REG-F-015 and REG-F-016 record from the other direction. Enforcing bad scope is worse than not enforcing.
- **WHAT LANDED INSTEAD:** the `policyApplicability` kernel (pure, in `rph-domain`) with its own tests, declared **DEFERRED** in the dead-kernel census with this reason. So the reconciliation has an instrument waiting rather than a function nobody has exercised, and the census cannot forget it — an uncalled kernel export is exactly what that gate watches.
  - **THE KERNEL'S CONTESTABLE JUDGEMENT IS ON ITS OWN LINE, ASSERTED.** §5.1's `expression` is a `PolicyExpression`, a type the corpus NAMES and DEFINES NOWHERE; `ObjectTypeCondition` likewise. A rule carrying either states a condition this engine cannot evaluate, and it yields **`REQUIRES_HUMAN_DETERMINATION`** (a §5.2 outcome, not an invented one) which is **PERMITTED and disclosed**. Refusing on an undecidable condition blocks real work; ignoring it lets the undecidable pass as decided. Permitting-and-surfacing is the third option and it is a judgement, so it is a separate census row and a separate assertion rather than buried in a branch.
  - **AND I MADE THE OPPOSITE MISTAKE AT THE CALL SITE FIRST.** The wiring read `objectType: String(subj?.objectType ?? '')`, so a subject the store could not resolve became the empty string, matched no condition, and was reported **NOT_APPLICABLE** — a DECIDED NEGATIVE derived from having no information, committed at the call site of the kernel built to prevent exactly that.
- **THE SCOPE IS FIXED (2026-08-05), AND §16 ITEM 23 SAYS SO ITSELF.** Item 23 *is* open and *is* about this floor — but what it leaves unresolved is **material-boundary identity/classification**, not which object types a policy may name, and its own instruction settles the direction verbatim: **"Never interpret the missing wire shape as permission to omit or hide the floor."** A narrow scope citing the unresolved wire shape is exactly that reading. §8.4 scopes the floor by *"every material professional transformation"* and enumerates no object types at all. `seedFloorPolicies` now **derives** `applicableObjectTypes` from `ProfessionalWorkObjectTypeSchema.options` — derived rather than listed, because a hand-written list rots in the **forbidden** direction, silently exempting the next object type added. The fixture's own three-type list is the proof: it was already missing `EVIDENCE`, which is what step #47 tripped on. Design note: `docs/_working/DESIGN-floor-declared-scope.md`.
  - **THE REAL SHAPE WAS DECLARED-vs-ENFORCED, AND IT WAS INVISIBLE FROM EITHER SIDE.** `floor-gate.ts` — the §8.4 step-4 protected-transition gate — **never reads `applicableObjectTypes` or `applicability` at all**; it requires the `FLOOR_POLICY_IDS` triple outright. So the floor was **already universal where it bites and narrow only where it is described.** Reading either file alone showed something coherent. `floor-declared-scope.test.ts` now holds declared ⊇ enforced (one-sided on purpose, per item 23) and is **mutation-verified**: restoring the old single-type declaration reddens three of its five assertions, naming `EVIDENCE`.
  - **AND BROADENING REQUIRES NOTHING NEW**, which is what made it safe: the gate's requirement set is independent of applicability, and `applicabilityPermitsAssessment` refuses only on `NOT_APPLICABLE`. Broadening **permits strictly more and requires strictly nothing more**. The 54 refusals were a false declaration, not a scope conflict.
  - **⚠ AND THE FIX COVERED ONE OF TWO WRITERS — FOUND 2026-08-05 BY MULTI-AGENT REVIEW, FIXED IMMEDIATELY.** The floor policies are created in **two** places: `seedFloorPolicies` (corrected) and `driveReferenceUndertaking`'s standalone path, which creates the same three itself because `satisfyFloor` cites them and nothing else seeds them there. That second copy kept `['PROFESSIONAL_WORK_ARCHITECTURE']`. **`floor-declared-scope.test.ts` could not see it, because the gate called only `seedFloorPolicies`** — so the instrument written to stop the floor being scoped away from the work it governs asserted over one of the two places that scope is set, and reported green. On the path it could not see, `satisfyFloor`'s subjects are **EVIDENCE** objects: the exact case that produced the original step-#47 refusal, still live one commit after being declared closed.
  - **A gate that covers one of two writers is not a gate; it is the first writer's own test wearing the word "declared."** Both sites derive now, and the gate is parameterised over the creation paths so that adding a third writer without adding it there is itself the visible omission. **Mutation-verified:** reverting only the standalone site reddens three assertions on that path and leaves the other path green.
- **Merge target:** Repository — the floor's declared scope and its gate landed. **Status:** **CLOSED 2026-08-06** (re-measurement + adversarial refutation). Both blockers this entry names are gone: REG-F-028's vocabulary divergence and REG-F-029 are closed, the §5.1 applicability determination is **wired and live** as a precondition on `RequestAssuranceAssessment`, and the floor's declared scope **derives from `ProfessionalWorkObjectTypeSchema.options` at BOTH production creation sites**. Every load-bearing claim was re-derived rather than quoted, and a refuter specifically hunted for the defect having MOVED, for a gate passing on an empty population, and for a fix covering only this entry's example — and found none.
  - **⚠ THE ONLY THING THAT WAS STILL OPEN WAS THIS LINE.** `DESIGN-floor-declared-scope.md` item **D-4** said, verbatim, *"Correct the record in place — REG-F-024's entry, `seed-workbench.ts`'s comment, and `assurance.ts`'s withdrawal comment"*. Both CODE comments were corrected on the day; **the REGISTER entry was the one D-4 item never done**, so a finding fixed weeks ago went on reading OPEN, *"on a NEW and correct blocker"*, long after that blocker closed. **A remediation plan that lists the record as an item and then skips it leaves the record asserting a defect the code no longer has** — the inverse of every other finding here, and just as misleading.

### REG-F-028 — The catalog scopes twelve policies by a PWU-kind vocabulary the seeded work does not speak, so delivered scope still matches nothing
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found by wiring REG-F-024's precondition once the floor's scope was fixed — it got past #47 and refused at #56) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — authored governance content that reaches the runtime and cannot bind.
- **Statement:** REG-F-022's second instance **delivered** `appliesToPwuKinds`: twelve catalog policies declare which PWU kinds they govern, the seeding had dropped the field, and it now reaches `applicability.pwuKindConditions` where the §5.1 kernel reads it. That closed the delivery question and revealed a better one — **a field can reach the runtime and still match nothing, because it names a vocabulary the runtime does not speak.** The catalog says `INTENT_AND_PRODUCT_DEFINITION`; `seed-workbench.ts` gives its PWU Type the kind `INTENT_DEFINITION`.
- **MEASURED, NOT ESTIMATED.** **Six of the nine** seeded kinds appear in no catalog policy (`ARCHITECTURE`, `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN`, `BASELINE_PROMOTION`, `INTEGRATED_VALIDATION`, `INTENT_DEFINITION`, `PRODUCT_BEHAVIOR`); **eleven of the fourteen** catalog kinds bind to nothing seeded. The overlap is three. So a kind-scoped policy silently governs nothing for two-thirds of the work the workbench creates, however exactly its `objectType` matches.
- **NOTHING FAILED, BECAUSE NOTHING COULD.** `pwuKind` is a free `z.string()` with **no ratified enum** on either side to hold the names honest, and `pwuKindConditions` is `z.array(z.string())`. Both spellings validate; neither side can detect the other. It surfaced only when the applicability precondition was run against the reference drive — the same shape as REG-F-025, where a build step and a vocab file were each individually correct.
- **THE DIRECTION IS SETTLED BY THE CORPUS, NOT BY WHICH ARTIFACT LOOKS MORE OFFICIAL.** The Field Service Management Reference Undertaking writes `"pwuKind": "INTENT_AND_PRODUCT_DEFINITION"`. **The catalog agrees with the corpus; the seeder does not** — so the repository's seeded PWU Types are the drift, and the fix is a vocabulary migration of the seeder. Not an edit to the catalog, and **emphatically not a mapping table invented to make two names meet in the middle**, which is the shape this register has rejected before as an exemption list that rots into an allowlist.
- **WHAT LANDED:** `pwu-kind-vocabulary-census.test.ts`, deriving BOTH sides from their real sources — the catalog from the ontology, the seeded kinds from the committed **event log** and materialized objects, never from `seed-workbench.ts`'s constants (a census reading the seeder's own list would agree with it by construction and could not have seen this). It pins the divergence at its measured size so the number may only fall, asserts the three agreeing kinds as proof the mechanism works where names match, and asserts the corpus-side name so the migration's direction cannot quietly reverse.
- **MIGRATED THE SAME DAY, AND THE MAPPING WAS DERIVED RATHER THAN CHOSEN — SIX DIVERGENCES DOWN TO ONE.** The five that closed were **abbreviations of names the seeder already carried in full**: every PWU Type's `name` was already the corpus's prose (`'Architecture Definition'`, `'Integrated Product Validation'`, `'Intent & Product Definition'`), and only the UPPER_SNAKE `kind` had been shortened. So the migration was snake-casing the name each type already had — no mapping table, no judgement. The ontology specification draws the tree in prose and UPPER_SNAKE of its eight nodes **is** the catalog's vocabulary, which is what settles the direction properly (my first pass asserted "the catalog agrees with the corpus" on the strength of **one** name found by grep; ten of the fourteen appear in the corpus as prose, not as tokens, so the token search had been the wrong instrument).
  - **⚠ THE ONE THAT REMAINS — AND MY ACCOUNT OF IT WAS FALSE, CORRECTED 2026-08-05 (multi-agent review).** It read: *"`ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN` appears zero times in the ontology specification — the repository invented a generic child of Architecture Definition. The corpus defines a real one at §15, Architecture Decision"*, and proposed renaming to it. **The measurement was true and the inference was false.** Zero hits in the ontology *specification* is a fact about **one file**. The corpus names this type repeatedly: the *PWA Designer and Undertaking Workbench — Reference Demonstration* lists **"Custom Architecture Concern"** among Architecture Definition's permitted children and defines the type with inherited purpose *"Define a coherent architecture concern contributing to Architecture Definition."* — which `seed-workbench.ts` carries **almost verbatim** (*"A generic architecture concern contributing to Architecture Definition"*). The Field Service Management Reference Undertaking puts "Architecture Concern" in its §32 trace. **The seeded type is TRANSCRIBED, not invented, and the proposed rename would have swapped one corpus concept for a different one.** Searching a single document and concluding about the corpus is the absence-of-evidence error this register already names; committed here, in the entry that was reporting a vocabulary correction.
    - **WHAT IS GENUINELY ABSENT is a POLICY scoped to this kind** — absent from the dataset *and* the corpus. That is REG-F-029's blocking question. It is also **not unique**: the dataset scopes policies to the same 14 kinds it has `pwuTemplates` rows for, while those templates' `candidateChildren` name ~62 kinds. **The catalog governs depth-1 work areas and says nothing about depth-2**, so renaming the five concerns to their exact corpus child kinds would not give them a policy either.
- **AND THE MIGRATION EXPOSED A DEFECT OF ITS OWN, WHICH IS THE SAME SHAPE AS EVERYTHING ELSE THIS WEEK.** `compatibility-view.ts` maps PWU kind → legacy milestone and ends `?? 'INTAKE'`. That default is CORRECT — `pwuKind` is a free string and inventing a later phase for an unknown kind would be worse — but it cannot distinguish *"a kind we never knew"* from *"a kind we knew, renamed underneath us."* The rename staled five keys at once and **four of the five silently degraded to INTAKE**; a single test asserting one specific milestone is the only reason it surfaced. The map is corrected, and the census now asserts that **every seeded kind has an explicit entry**, so the fallback covers only the case it was written for.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED but for the disclosed `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN` question, which is an authoring decision and is recorded as one.

### REG-F-029 — The canonical drive assesses ten of thirteen PWUs under a policy the ratified catalog says does not govern them
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found by the applicability precondition's THIRD run, once REG-F-024's and REG-F-028's causes were fixed) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a repository fixture claiming governance the corpus does not grant.
- **Statement:** `driveReferenceUndertaking`'s `earnAssurance` cites **one** policy for every PWU it assesses. When the workbench seeds it, that policy is the catalog's `pol_fitness_for_purpose`, scoped to `INTEGRATED_PRODUCT_VALIDATION` / `PRODUCT_REALIZATION` / `PRODUCT_BASELINE_PROMOTION`.
  - **⚠ THE NUMBERS IN THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS ENTRY WERE BOTH WRONG, CORRECTED 2026-08-05 (multi-agent review, verified against the code).** It said *"three of the drive's thirteen PWUs are governed by it."* The drive **PROPOSES** thirteen PWUs and **ASSESSES eight** — `earnAssurance` is reachable only through `driveToSatisfied` / `driveToConditional`, called eight times (intent definition, behaviour definition, architecture definition, and five architecture concerns). And **ZERO of the eight are governed**: the policy's three kinds belong to the root, validation and promotion — three of the five PWUs the drive **never assesses**. The assessed population and the policy's scope are **perfectly disjoint**, so it is 8 of 8 refused, not 10 of 13. I derived "thirteen" from the proposal list and never checked which of them reach an assessment.
  - **AND "the ratified catalog scopes" OVERSTATES THE PROVENANCE.** All twelve catalog policies label `appliesToPwuKinds` **`AUTHORED (no ratified source)`** in their own `sourceSection`. DOC-004 §25.2 reads *"Required during integrated product validation and before product baseline promotion"* — two **situations**, not three kinds, and `PRODUCT_REALIZATION` has no footing there at all. Under this repository's own census vocabulary the ontology **authors** these scopes and does not **deliver** them, so a blocking precondition built on them needs that settled first (see the elicitation note below).
- **IT IS NOT A SPELLING OR A SCOPE MISTAKE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS FILED SEPARATELY.** REG-F-024 was a declaration narrower than its enforcement; REG-F-028 was two vocabularies for one concept. Both were the repository disagreeing with itself. This one is the fixture asserting governance the catalog does not grant.
  - **⚠ AND THE PROPOSED FIX WAS WRONG TOO, CORRECTED 2026-08-05.** It said *"the seeded PWU Types already declare, in their own `policies` field, which policies govern them. The information to do it correctly is present and unused."* **That holds for two of the eight assessed PWUs.** `PT_BEHAVIOR` and `PT_CONCERN` declare **no `policies` key at all`; only `PT_INTENT` and `PT_ARCH` do. Worse, `PWU_TYPES[].policies` is itself a hand-written **third copy** — a strict subset of the ontology's per-kind `pwuTemplates[].defaultPolicyIds` for three kinds and absent for five. Building the selector on it would enshrine exactly the rot this week has been about. The declaration must first be **derived** from `defaultPolicyIds`, which is the move `seedAdditivePolicies` already made for `seedPolicies`.
  - **THE SHAPE THE FIX HAS TO TAKE**, from the review: select per PWU as **DECLARED ∩ DETERMINED** — the declaration join `buildApplicablePolicies` already computes for §38, intersected with `policyApplicability` run over the policy objects in the store. That makes the precondition pass **by construction rather than by coincidence**, because the drive and the handler call the same function on the same data. It also implies assessing the whole resulting **set**, not one of it: citing 1 of 4 applicable policies would leave three §38 rows reading `assessed: false` on a PWU the drive calls SATISFIED — the same class of defect as this finding.
- **THE INSTRUMENT HAS NOW BEEN WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN DIAGNOSIS TWICE AND RIGHT ABOUT THERE BEING SOMETHING WRONG THREE TIMES OUT OF THREE.** Each withdrawal of the precondition was recorded as "now for a true reason", and twice that claim was itself mistaken. Worth stating plainly rather than presenting the third as obviously final: what is durable is that running the check keeps surfacing real defects, not that any particular account of the blocker has been right.
- **THE BLOCKING AUTHORING QUESTION IS ANSWERED (2026-08-05, standing grant) — AND THE ANSWER WAS READ OFF A RATIFIED TRACE, NOT INVENTED.** *What governs an `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN`?* The corpus **names the type** (see the correction in REG-F-028) and named **no policy** for it, so the five seeded concerns declared nothing and could not be assessed at all once §5.1 is enforced. DOC-006 §32.2 routes a concern's assurance **by name**: *"Scheduling and Dispatch Architecture Concern → PRODUCES → Architecture Artifact → SUPPORTS → **Architecture Coverage Claim** → VERIFIED_BY → **Architecture Assurance Assessment**"*. The catalog's `pol_architecture_coverage` is named **"Architecture Coverage"** and evaluates `COVERAGE` claims; the concern's own corpus purpose is *"contributing to Architecture Definition"*, the kind that policy already governed. **What is AUTHORED is the membership; the routing is delivered.** Recorded in full in the policy's own `sourceSection` rather than in a new field — see the census note below for why that distinction cost a round.
  - **THE MINIMAL TEMPLATE ROW.** `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN` gained a `pwuTemplates` row carrying only `pwuKind`, `isRoot`, the corpus's **verbatim** purpose, `defaultPolicyIds` and its provenance. It **deliberately omits** `inputs`, `outputArtifactTypes`, `completionClaims`, `requiredEvidenceTypes` and `candidateChildren` — every one would be invented, all five are optional, and the row exists to close a policy gap, not to describe work the corpus has not described.
  - **AND THE HAND-WRITTEN THIRD COPY IS GONE.** `PWU_TYPES[].policies` — four lists maintained by hand — is replaced by `policiesForKind`, reading `pwuTemplates[].defaultPolicyIds` and **throwing** on a kind the ontology does not describe. The copy had drifted exactly as such copies do: a strict subset for three kinds, absent for five. **Declarations went from 4 of 9 types to 9 of 9**, and `PRODUCT_BEHAVIOR_DEFINITION` — which declared nothing — now declares three.
  - **THE GATE I WROTE FOR THIS WAS VACUOUS FOR TWENTY MINUTES.** It read `appliesToPwuKinds` off the stored policy — an **ontology** field name, where the delivered form is `applicability.pwuKindConditions` — so the lookup was `undefined` for every policy and `if (kinds && …)` skipped the whole check. **It passed while measuring nothing**: the blind reader again, inside a gate written to catch that class, minutes after writing it. It now reads the delivered form, treats an absent `applicability` as a **reported** problem, and asserts the real requirement — DECLARED ∩ APPLICABLE is **non-empty** for every seeded type. Mutation-verified: removing the authored membership reddens it, naming the Architecture Concern.
  - **DECLARED-BUT-INAPPLICABLE IS PINNED, NOT ZEROED.** The ontology carries four such pairs across fourteen templates (one on a seeded kind: `PRODUCT_REALIZATION` declares `pol_baseline_promotion`, which scopes itself to `PRODUCT_BASELINE_PROMOTION`). That is **exactly what DECLARED ∩ DETERMINED exists to drop**, so it is recorded and held rather than edited away — removing it would be an authoring act on the catalog's authored scope lists, on top of the one already made.
- **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — AND THE §5.1 DETERMINATION IS NOW ENFORCED, WHICH IS WHAT THE WHOLE SERIES WAS FOR.** `earnAssurance` selects **DECLARED ∩ DETERMINED** per PWU and assesses the resulting **SET**: DECLARED is the same PWU-plus-PWU-Type join §38's `buildApplicablePolicies` computes, DETERMINED runs `policyApplicability` over the policy objects in the store. The drive and the handler therefore call the **same function on the same data**, so the precondition passes **by construction rather than by coincidence** — a drive that merely happened to agree would drift the first time either side moved. An empty selection **throws** rather than returning `[]`: a PWU nothing can assess must not reach a disposition.
  - **THE SET, NOT ONE OF IT.** Citing 1 of 4 applicable policies would leave three §38 rows reading `assessed: false` on a PWU the drive calls SATISFIED — a required-but-unassessed gap, this finding's own defect class. The PWU's assurance axis still hops **once** (it records that the work is under assessment, not how many assessments are open), and each assessment carries a ValidatorResult naming **its own** policy, since a verdict attributed to criteria it was not measured against is a false record.
  - **AND IT SURFACED A SECOND DEFECT UNDERNEATH, WHICH IS THE SHAPE OF THIS ENTIRE WEEK.** With the right policies selected, two of them refused: `pol_intent_completeness` and `pol_assumption_disclosure` reached **INDEPENDENCE_VIOLATION**. Both require `DIFFERENT_INVOCATION` — the **weaker** requirement — while the `DIFFERENT_AGENT` policies passed. `checkIndependence`'s `differs()` requires the compared field present on **both** sides, so `DIFFERENT_INVOCATION` **fails closed on absent data**, correctly: independence you cannot demonstrate is independence you do not have. The drive had never recorded an invocation identity because no policy it used had needed one. Fixed by recording a fact already true — producing and assessing are separate acts, issued as separate commands by separate parties (`executionInstanceId` → `invocationId`).
  - **THE DEAD-KERNEL CENSUS PRODUCED ITS GOOD FAILURE.** `policyApplicability` and `applicabilityPermitsAssessment` were declared DEAD/DEFERRED; wiring them tripped the census's *"DECLARED DEAD BUT NOW CALLED"* branch — the failure a prose list could never raise. Their rows are deleted, **and it is worth recording that their deferral reason cited the blocker that turned out to be false** (REG-F-024's non-existent schema limitation) and stood unexamined for as long as the row did. **A deferral reason is an assertion like any other, and nothing was checking that one.**
  - **THE ENFORCEMENT HAS ITS OWN PREDICTED RED.** A green suite cannot distinguish an enforced check that never fires from an unenforced one, so `assurance-applicability-precondition.test.ts` asserts the refusal **happens** (a kind-scoped policy refuses a subject of another kind, leaving **no** assessment behind), plus three controls: the same request **succeeds** when the kind matches, an **unrestricted** policy still assesses anything, and `REQUIRES_HUMAN_DETERMINATION` is **permitted** — with a fifth proving an **unresolvable subject is never refused as NOT_APPLICABLE**, the mistake once made at this exact call site.
- **THE CLOSURE WAS REVIEWED ADVERSARIALLY (25 agents) AND THE HEADLINE JUDGEMENT OF THE KERNEL WAS REFUTED — FIXED 2026-08-05.** The review's own nominated defect (that `objectType` is absent from stored PWU state, which would make the whole check inert) was **refuted**: the field is written by `newEnvelope` and by `proposePwu`, survives the round-trip, and the refusal fires through a real dispatch. But it found something worse in the kernel I had defended:
  - **`policyApplicability` returned `REQUIRES_HUMAN_DETERMINATION` for any `expression`, on the stated ground that it is *"a type the corpus NAMES and DEFINES NOWHERE."* THE CORPUS DEFINES IT.** DOC-007 §18 ratifies the full **eight-op `ApplicabilityExpression` grammar** (`ALL/ANY/NOT/EQUALS/IN/CONTAINS/EXISTS/RISK_AT_LEAST`); ratified item **C-9 — recorded in THIS register** — states *"the PolicyExpression grammar is unified with the ApplicabilityExpression op set (one DSL)"*; and `rph-assurance/src/applicability.ts::evaluateApplicability` has implemented and unit-tested it since **before the kernel was written**. I searched for the string `PolicyExpression`, found only references, and concluded the CONCEPT was undefined. **Searching one name and concluding about the corpus — the absence-of-evidence error, third instance today, with the refutation sitting in the register the finding was being written into.**
  - **AND THE ARM WAS FAIL-OPEN, NOT MERELY CAUTIOUS.** Because `REQUIRES_HUMAN_DETERMINATION` is PERMITTED and the expression arm ran last, no input could change the decision: a policy scoping itself by expression could never be refused. The most precisely ratified arm of §5.1 was the one arm the kernel could not act on.
  - **FIXED BY INJECTION, because the boundary forbids the obvious import** — `rph-assurance` depends on `rph-domain`, so the kernel cannot import the evaluator. `policyApplicability` now takes an optional `evaluate`, supplied by both call sites (handler and drive, so expressions decide the same way on both sides). **The undecidable outcome survives but is now EARNED**: without an evaluator it states a true fact about the call ("none was supplied") instead of a false one about the corpus. A throwing evaluator yields `REQUIRES_HUMAN_DETERMINATION`, never `NOT_APPLICABLE` — a malformed expression is undecidable, not unsatisfied. An expression can only **narrow**: it is consulted last and never re-admits a subject the other conditions excluded.
- **THE REVIEW'S REMAINING FINDINGS ARE RECORDED AND NOT YET CLOSED**, because each is a separate defect rather than a flaw in this mechanism, and closing them by assertion is what this series has been correcting: (a) **CLOSED 2026-08-05** — `buildApplicablePolicies` is **pure over id arrays**: it never loads a policy, so it could not apply §5.1 or the ACTIVE filter and reported every DECLARED policy as governing. My claim that *"the drive and the read model cannot give different answers"* was **false** — they could, because only one of them was answering the question. Concretely, the root PWU's §38 view listed `pol_baseline_promotion` as applicable-and-unassessed while the engine's determination says `NOT_APPLICABLE` for that exact pair. **The fix is NOT to drop the row.** Guide §8.4 is explicit — *"required, inherited, deferred, waived, and **inapplicable** … coverage are explainable; gaps are never silent"* — so a policy declared and then determined not to govern is precisely a thing that must be **explainable**. The caller may now supply `outcomeByPolicy` and the row REPORTS `applicable` + `applicabilityOutcome`; the demo's undertaking loader supplies it from `selectGoverningPolicies`, the same determination the engine enforces. **When no determination is supplied both fields stay `undefined`**: defaulting to `true` would restate the original defect and `false` would hide real coverage, so *"nobody asked"* is now a state this view can express rather than one it silently resolves; (b) **CLOSED 2026-08-05** — the DETERMINED half excluded nothing on either drive path: V8 branch coverage put every exclusion arm at **count = 0** (15/15 and 8/8 selections returned true), so stubbing `policyApplicability` to always-permit would not have changed either drive. *"Passes by construction"* was a claim about a branch nothing had executed. The selector is now a module (`governing-policies.ts`) rather than a closure inside a 400-command drive, it **returns** its exclusions instead of discarding them, and `governing-policies.test.ts` exercises each arm **on the real seeded workbench**: the ROOT PWU declares `pol_baseline_promotion`, which scopes itself to `PRODUCT_BASELINE_PROMOTION`, so it is excluded with reason `NOT_APPLICABLE` — the ontology contradiction that had been in the data all along and had never once passed through the selector, because the drive never assesses the root. A control asserts an assessed PWU excludes **nothing**, so the exclusion is a property of the data rather than of the selector; a third case exercises **ABSENT**; a fourth proves the empty selection is a loud failure; (c) **CLOSED 2026-08-05** — `EditAssurancePolicy` accepted `applicability` and dropped it (present on the payload, absent from `EDITABLE_PATCH_FIELDS`), while `applicableObjectTypes` **was** patchable. So narrowing the declared types moved the fallback and left the field the enforced §5.1 determination PREFERS pinned at creation: **REG-F-024's defect, re-openable by anyone holding an EditAssurancePolicy** — and an edit changing only the rule compared nothing, looked like a no-op, and was rejected, so the field could not be edited by any route. Fixed by making it patchable and by `reconcileApplicability`: an explicit rule wins outright; a bare `applicableObjectTypes` edit carries the rule's object-type arm with it and **preserves every other arm** (kind conditions, tags, a §18 expression), because those were authored separately and a type edit does not speak to them — overwriting the whole rule would turn a narrowing edit into a silent broadening, the worse direction.
  - **AND THE FIX TRIPPED ANOTHER GATE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS WORTH RECORDING.** `AssurancePolicyEdited` is a `strictObject` that **did not declare `applicability`**, though the command did and `AssurancePolicyCreated` does — an asymmetry introduced when §5.1 scope was first delivered and **invisible while the field was unpatchable**, since the handler never put it in an edit event. Making it patchable exposed it instantly via REG-F-020's emitted-event ledger, which refused a payload the event's own shape rejects. Without that, the governed stream would have recorded every part of a scope edit **except the field a live refusal reads.** (d) baseline promotion cites **1 of 3 / 1 of 4** assessments, so "the SET, not one of it" stops one layer short; (e) **CLOSED 2026-08-05** — observations were recorded against the PRIMARY assessment only, while EVERY assessment's ValidatorResult carried the same observations: a peer's verdict asserted findings the peer had no observation objects for. Not merely untidy — `completeAssuranceAssessment` loads observations by `assessmentId === this assessment` for **two** gates (§10.3 foreclosure and the escalation rule), so on every peer both ran over an **EMPTY population**, and a peer could reach SATISFIED while carrying a finding its own policy forbids that disposition for. An `ASSURANCE_OBSERVATION` is keyed by `assessmentId`, so a finding four assessments each report is four observations — one per judgement that reports it. **THE DRIVE CANNOT DEMONSTRATE THIS AND THAT IS SAID RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER:** the only PWU it gives observations to is an architecture concern, whose kind is governed by exactly ONE policy, so N = 1 and the primary IS the whole set. The proof is a handler-level test — a peer with an open BLOCKING finding is REFUSED a SATISFIED disposition, with a control that an observation on a DIFFERENT assessment does **not** foreclose it (observations are keyed by assessment on purpose; a gate that pooled them would refuse verdicts nobody's evidence supports).
  - **AND TWO FIXTURES WERE THE REASON IT LOOKED FINE.** The first draft of that test passed a `dispositionRules` the fixture silently dropped, so **Gate C never ran and the test reported a pass from a gate that was not there** — the same shape as the vacuous gate three commits earlier. Extending `seedPolicy` exposed that `DispositionRule.condition` is REQUIRED (`z.unknown()`, nonoptional), which the first version also omitted. **REG-F-015's unread-refusal guard caught the arrangement dispatches too**, which is why every arrangement in that file now asserts its own acceptance: an arrangement whose command was refused is an arrangement that did not happen.
- **(d) CLOSED 2026-08-05** — baseline promotion's `requiredAssessmentIds` cited **1 of 3 and 1 of 4**, because `earnAssurance` and `driveToSatisfied` returned only the PRIMARY assessment id. So at the one moment DOC-004 §24 asks a promotion to carry its required assessments, the gate verified a **single policy's verdict** and took the other governing policies on trust — "the SET, not one of it" stopping one layer short of the gate that matters most. Both now return the whole set; the two seeded baselines carry **3** and **4**, pinned, with a **control** asserting those counts equal what `selectGoverningPolicies` says governs each subject — otherwise the promotion could cite a set unrelated to the determination that produced it. The satisfied-hop `supportingObjectIds` cite them all too: `rejectUnbackedDisposition` needs one, and naming every assessment that permits the hop is both stronger and truer.
  - **THE FIRST PROBE OF THIS REPORTED ZERO FOR BOTH**, because it read the COMMAND's field name (`requiredAssessmentIds`) off the EVENT, which carries `assuranceAssessmentIds`. A blind reader again — the fourth this session — caught only because "zero" was too strong a result to accept without looking. **The pinned assertion reads the field the event actually has.**
- **ALL FIVE ADVERSARIAL-REVIEW FINDINGS ARE NOW CLOSED.** What the review changed is worth stating plainly: of its five, **none** was the defect I had nominated when commissioning it, **one** (the expression arm) refuted the headline judgement of a kernel I had defended in this register, and **two** ((b), (e)) were gates that reported green while measuring nothing.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED for the mechanism and the expression arm; five reviewed findings above are OPEN and separately owned. The standalone drive still selects exactly one policy per PWU (its declaration is written on the PWU itself rather than falling back inside the selector, because a fallback cannot tell *"declared nothing"* from *"declared this"*), so the 332-event replay pin does not move.

### REG-F-025 — A ratified arrow was written in the vocab and deleted by the build, in silence — and my own account of how it was lost was wrong
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found by asking the general form of REG-F-021's residual R-1, hours after closing it) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a generator silently discards ratified input, so two artifacts that are each correct read alone disagree about what the engine does.
- **Statement:** `gen-transitions.ts` expands quantified from-states (`Any active`, `Any non-baselined`, …) into concrete edges. A row whose from-state matches **no** recognised spelling and **no** declared state fell through two `continue`/filter paths and was discarded with **no error, no warning, and no line in the output**. Same for a row whose `to` is not a declared state. Nothing anywhere compared the vocab against the machine, so a row could be authored, reviewed, cited and lost, and every review of either artifact alone would show it present.
- **THE CLOSURE I WROTE THIS MORNING GOT THE MECHANISM WRONG, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** REG-F-021 residual R-1 was closed on the account that DOC-004 §30's `ANY ACTIVE → CANCELLED` had been **lost in transcription** — *"a quantifier is not a row"*. **It was not.** The row was transcribed, in the right machine, spelled **`ANY_ACTIVE`**, carrying a note stating its own intended expansion — and it had been in `m2-transitions.json` since the workbench arrived in this repository (`19a1b20f`). `expandFrom` recognises `Any active` **with a space**; `ANY_ACTIVE` matched nothing and was filtered out. **One underscore, for the lifetime of the repository.** The corpus said it, the vocab carried it, and the build dropped it — in the one place nobody looked, between two artifacts each individually defensible.
  - **PROOF THE ROW WAS INERT, NOT MERELY SUSPECTED SO:** deleting it regenerates `transitions.data.ts` **byte-identical**. A row that changes nothing when removed was doing nothing while present.
  - **AND IT IS THE SAME LESSON ONE LEVEL DOWN.** R-1's own closure said the loss happened at the corpus→vocab seam. The audit that followed found corpus→vocab **CLEAN**: every quantified arrow in the ratified corpus is transcribed, or belongs to a lower-authority doc that conflicts with the authority the vocab cites (the migration doc's §11.1 `ANY ACTIVE STATE → ESCALATED` against core-model §8.2's `EVIDENCE_PENDING → ESCALATED`; the vocab followed §8.2, and narrower is the safe direction). The defective seam was the next one along — which is what "ask the general form" is for, and why the general form must be asked about the **answer** and not only the question.
- **THE OBVIOUS FIX WAS THE WRONG FIX, AND THE SILENT DROP WAS THE ONLY THING KEEPING THIS MACHINE HONEST.** Teaching `expandFrom` to accept `ANY_ACTIVE` would have "restored" the arrow — and minted three the corpus does not ratify. `expandFrom` implements *"any active"* as `states MINUS terminalStates`, i.e. it treats **active** and **non-terminal** as the same claim. They are not: "active" says work is still under way, "non-terminal" says the machine can still leave. They coincide in every machine where this quantifier currently lands, and they **part company in `AssuranceAssessment.state`**, whose `SATISFIED`, `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` and `WAIVED` are non-terminal **verdicts** (reachable onward via `INVALIDATED` / `WAIVER_EXPIRED`). The widened matcher would have produced `SATISFIED → CANCELLED` — cancelling an assessment that has already concluded, which §30 does not ratify and which R-1's own test explicitly forbids. **The bug was load-bearing.** Resolution: four LITERAL rows, the quantifier row deleted, and the conflation named at `expandFrom` so the next person to widen it reads why first.
- **WHAT LANDED:** (1) `buildLegalTransitions` now **THROWS** on any row that produces no edge — the Constitution's fail-loud rule applied to a build step, which had been exempt from it by omission. (2) A row that genuinely is not an arrow says so **in the row**, via `unrepresentable` carrying the ruling — per-row so the reason is read next to the thing it exempts, and it cannot grow without someone writing one. The six `AggregateAssuranceDisposition` rows take it, citing JAN-CMDPRE-SPEC-001 §2's exclusion by name (nothing MOVES a computed rollup). (3) `transition-row-landing.test.ts`, which is the only gate here that reads **two** artifacts — it has to, because the defect *is* the gap between them.
  - **THE GATE DELIBERATELY DOES NOT RE-RUN `expandFrom`.** A checker that recomputes the generator's answer agrees with it by construction and would have passed on `19a1b20f`. Landing is detected instead by a **fingerprint** the generator copies verbatim onto every edge it emits — the row's `trigger`. Its blind spot is stated in the file rather than discovered later: five `(machine, to, trigger)` keys are shared by more than one row, so an arm-level check covers the compound case and the generator's throw covers the rest. **Two instruments with different failure modes; neither trusted alone.**
  - **BOTH SIDES OF THE FIX WERE SHOWN TO FIRE.** The gate was run RED first and named exactly seven rows (the arrow plus the six rollup rows). The generator's new refusal was run against the **pre-fix** vocab and threw on `ANY_ACTIVE → CANCELLED` by name — a build step that had accepted that file for the lifetime of the repository now refuses it. The staleness control is the mirror: an `unrepresentable` row that *does* land fails too.
  - **THE CONTROL CAUGHT ITSELF.** The first draft of "the detector says `true` for a row that landed" selected the row by `to === 'CANCELLED'` and got the **first** such row — the dropped `ANY_ACTIVE` one — so the control asserted *landed* about the defect itself. It failed, which is the only reason the selection was corrected to key on the from-state.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED. The R-1 account is corrected in place (register above, `m2-transitions.json` note, `assessment-cancellation.test.ts` header) rather than rewritten, because the wrong account is the more instructive half.

### REG-F-026 — Seven payload fields declare an enum and ship as unvalidated strings, because the generator answers a broken reference with silence
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found by applying REG-F-025's question to the OTHER generators the same day) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a declared constraint that the emitted contract does not carry.
- **Statement:** `gen-messages.ts` resolved a field's `enumRef` against the emitted enum set and, on a miss, returned `z.string()` under the comment *"never invent enum values"*. Not inventing values is right; **dropping the constraint and reporting nothing is not.** Seven command/event payload fields — including `AssuranceAssessmentCompleted.disposition` and both observation `severity` fields — declare an enum and validate **any string whatsoever**. Unlike REG-F-025 these are not lost declarations but lost *guarantees*: the schema is the only thing standing between a governed payload and an arbitrary value.
- **THE BROKEN REFERENCE SHADOWS A WORKING ONE, WHICH IS THE SHARPEST PART.** `zodExpr`'s `enumRef` branch returns unconditionally. `AssuranceAssessmentCompleted.disposition` declares its five values **twice** — once as `enumRef: AssessmentDispositionSchema` (which does not exist) and once as the inline literal union `'SATISFIED' | 'CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED' | …`, which `zodLiteralExpr` would have compiled into a real `z.enum`. **The unusable declaration beat the usable one**, and the field shipped unconstrained with its own values written three lines away. The same function already carries a recorded resolution-order defect (`ARRAY DETECTION MUST COME FIRST`); this is that class again, one branch over.
- **AND THE SISTER GENERATOR NEVER HAD THE BUG, WHICH IS WHY ALL SEVEN ARE IN M3.** `gen-objects.ts` emits the `enumRef` **verbatim** as an identifier, so an unresolvable one becomes an import of a nonexistent symbol — a compile error at the next build. **The identical mistake is fatal in one generator and silent in the other**, so it accumulated only where it could hide. M1 has none; M3 has seven. That is not a difference in care between the two vocabularies, and reading it as one would have sent the fix to the wrong place.
- **THE SEVEN ARE THREE DIFFERENT PROBLEMS, AND CONFLATING THEM WOULD CLOSE ONE BY BREAKING ANOTHER.** Each field now carries its group in its own `enumRefUnresolved` reason:
  - **(a) The enum exists under another identifier — 4 fields, resolvable.** `ObservationSeverity` is emitted as `AssuranceSeverity` (×2; `enums.ts`'s own doc comment on that enum names `AssuranceObservation.severity (ObservationSeverity)`, so the mapping is documented rather than guessed). `RecommendedControlAction` → `ControlAction`, pending a value read. And **`AssessmentDisposition` → `AssuranceDispositionRecommendation`, NOT `AssuranceDisposition`** — the name points at the six-value enum (it carries `WAIVED`); the five-value one matches the field's own inline union exactly. **Resolved by VALUES; the name was the trap.**
  - **(b) No enum exists — 1 field.** `ValidateDecomposition.disposition`'s note ratifies three values (`VALID | CONDITIONALLY_VALID | INVALID`) that are a **subset** of `DecompositionContractStatus`'s six. Pointing at that enum would **admit `DRAFT`, `UNDER_REVIEW` and `SUPERSEDED` as validation dispositions** — a fix that constrains the field and corrupts its meaning.
  - **(c) The corpus and the runtime disagree — 2 fields, DO NOT CONSTRAIN.** DOC-004 §36.2 gives a **prose** taxonomy of seven execution-failure kinds and no enum was ever minted from it. The only `failureClass` value in flight is **`TRANSIENT`**, from the retry-capability work, which §36.2 does not contain. Constraining to the ratified taxonomy refuses live data; constraining to the live vocabulary ratifies an unratified one. **Recorded as the open question it is** rather than resolved by whichever side was nearer to hand.
- **WHAT LANDED, AND WHAT DELIBERATELY DID NOT.** The generator now **THROWS** on an unresolvable ref unless the field declares `enumRefUnresolved` with a reason (the same per-row escape hatch as REG-F-025, so both generators refuse the same way). All seven are annotated, and **regenerating changed the emitted schemas by nothing at all** — the hole is now *declared*, not yet closed. Narrowing a contract that currently accepts anything can refuse data already in flight, and (c) shows that is not hypothetical, so the corrections are a separate increment with their own value checks.
- **THE CENSUS THAT PINS IT READS THE RUNTIME SCHEMA OBJECTS.** Its first draft rebuilt the generator's lookup from `canonical-vocabulary.json`'s enum NAMES — the wrong set, since the generator regexes the GENERATED `enums.ts` — and reported **twelve** degradations, **all twelve false**: `RiskConsequenceSchema` was on the list while `WorkRiskProfileSchema.consequence` is, and always was, exactly that. Asking the schema object what it *is* avoids both failure modes at once — an instrument that re-derives the thing it checks agrees by construction, and one that re-derives it wrong invents work. **Caught by checking the artifact before believing the probe.**
  - **RATCHET, WITH A DEMONSTRATED RED.** The seven are pinned by name and may only fall; a stale annotation (declared unresolvable, schema constrained) fails too. Verified by mutation — deleting one annotation reddened both the silence check and the classification check — and by the generator's own refusal run against the **pre-annotation** vocab.
- **GROUP (a) CLOSED THE SAME DAY — AND IT WAS THREE, NOT FOUR.** `RecordAssuranceObservation.severity`, `AssuranceObservationRecorded.severity` and `AssuranceAssessmentCompleted.disposition` now carry real enums; the census ratchet fell **7 → 4**. Each was confirmed by **values against the object model**, never by name:
  - **severity → `AssuranceSeverity`:** M1's `ASSURANCE_OBSERVATION.severity` already binds `AssuranceSeveritySchema`, and these are the command and event that write that very field. The mapping is read off the object model, not off the resemblance between "ObservationSeverity" and "AssuranceSeverity".
  - **disposition → `AssuranceDispositionRecommendation`, confirmed three independent ways:** the field's own inline union names exactly those five; the handler's hand-written `DISPOSITIONS` guard admits exactly those five and rejects the command otherwise; and M1's `ValidatorResult.dispositionRecommendation` + `DispositionRule.disposition` already bind that schema. **NOT `AssuranceDisposition`** — the enum the NAME points at, which carries a sixth value (`WAIVED`) this event cannot take, since completion reads `validatorResult.dispositionRecommendation` and a waiver is a different command. The census now asserts the emitted VALUES and that `WAIVED` is absent, because "constrained" was never the requirement — constrained to the ratified set was.
  - **AND THE FOURTH DID NOT SURVIVE ITS OWN CHECK, WHICH IS THE POINT OF RUNNING ONE.** `AssuranceAssessmentRejected.recommendedControlAction` was filed as resolvable to `ControlAction`. It is not: M1 models the concept as `ControlActionRecommendation[]` — a helper the corpus **defines nowhere** (its single vocab field is literally named `(undefined)` with type `—`, emitted as `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())`) — and the event has **no producer anywhere in the repository**. Binding the scalar field to a bare action code would assert a shape the object model contradicts, to constrain an event nothing emits. It stays annotated, with the reason sharpened by what the check found.
  - **ONE COPY OF THE LIST IS GONE.** The handler's `DISPOSITIONS` is now derived from `AssuranceDispositionRecommendationSchema.options`. It had been a third restatement of the same five values alongside the schema and the vocab field — **which is how the enum came to be misnamed in the vocab without any copy noticing: each was individually right, and none was the source.**
- **GROUP (b) CLOSED THE SAME DAY — AND NOT BY QUOTING ITS OWN NOTE.** `ValidateDecomposition.disposition` now admits exactly `VALID | CONDITIONALLY_VALID | INVALID`; the ratchet falls **4 → 3**. The obvious close was to quote the three values the field's vocab note already carried. **That would have ratified a note by promoting it:** the command's `sourceSection` says UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED in as many words — *"DOC-007 schematizes NO interface for this, so these fields were AUTHORED, not derived … Do NOT treat this sourceSection as proof the shape is ratified"* — so those values were somebody's reading, which is the exact species of unchecked transcription this whole series has been about.
  - **DERIVED FROM A RATIFIED ARTIFACT INSTEAD.** The six `DecompositionContract.status` states are VERBATIM core-model §13.1, and the machine's only arrows out of `UNDER_REVIEW` — the state this command acts on — go to those three. **A validation disposition is exactly the set of outcomes the machine can reach from the state being validated.** The note turned out to be right; it simply was not evidence. `decomposition-disposition-derivation.test.ts` now checks the emitted set against the machine, so the two cannot drift, and **mutation-verified**: widening the union to admit `SUPERSEDED` reddens three of its four assertions.
  - **AND THE NEAREST RATIFIED ENUM WAS AGAIN THE WRONG ONE** — `DecompositionContractStatus` carries these three values *plus* `DRAFT`, `UNDER_REVIEW` and `SUPERSEDED`, so binding it would have constrained the field and corrupted its meaning. The same trap as group (a)'s `AssuranceDisposition`, in a different costume, and the test asserts the three non-outcomes are refused rather than merely trusting the union's length.
  - Emitted as a **literal union, not a new canonical enum**, on purpose: minting one would add an authored narrowing to the ratified enum surface that `enums.test.ts` holds to the canonical vocabulary. The field consequently leaves the enumRef census entirely, which is why the ratchet moves by one while the field becomes *more* constrained, not less.
- **GROUP (c) RAISED TO THE SPONSOR AS REG-E-025.** It is a ratification, and the picture is worse than "two vocabularies": the field is **inert** (`execution.ts` only passes it through; nothing reads it), `TRANSIENT` has **no production producer** and appears only in test fixtures, and §36 closes with a rule nobody has met — *"Each failure class must map to permitted control actions."* Adopting either vocabulary makes that unbuilt mapping's domain, and adopting `TRANSIENT` would orphan the rule outright since it is not a §36 class.
- **Merge target:** Repository — the fail-loud generator, the annotations, the census, and groups (a) and (b) landed. **Status:** **CLOSED 2026-08-05** — group (c) closed (REG-E-025 ratified: `ExecutionFailureClass` minted from DOC-002 §36.2, all FOUR `failureClass` fields constrained — two of them were below the census's floor and invisible to it — and the §36 control-action mapping built and enforced). **Group (d) closed the same day, and its blocker was never real** — the entry said `ControlActionRecommendation` "is a shape the corpus has never defined", reasoning from the type NAME and an M1 row named `(undefined)`, while DOC-004 §33's worked example shows the field carrying bare `ControlAction` strings (REG-F-036). Ratchet 7 → 4 → 3 → 1 → **ZERO**.

### REG-F-027 — The binding table attributes an arrow to an event that does not drive it, because a resolver that could not dissent laundered the disagreement — as its own comment said it would
- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found in the same generator sweep as REG-F-026) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a generated declaration that contradicts both the vocab it is generated from and the engine it describes.
- **Statement:** `gen-messages.ts` aligned every binding row's `eventType` to its command's `emitsEvent` by **overwriting** it. `emitsEvent` is 1:1; the corpus is not. DOC-004 §30 puts the request and its requirement evaluation under ONE trigger, so `RequestAssuranceAssessment` commits **two** governed facts atomically (kit's `alsoEvents`) and has **two** binding rows. The second — `AssuranceEvidenceRequired` driving `REQUESTED → EVIDENCE_PENDING` — was rewritten to say `AssuranceAssessmentRequested`, an event that does not drive that arrow. The generated table then carried two rows identical in command and event, distinguishable only by machine/from/to, so the duplicate did not even read as one.
- **THE COMMENT ON THAT LINE ALREADY NAMED THE HAZARD, IN THESE WORDS: *"a resolver that cannot dissent is a resolver that launders whichever side it was pointed at."*** It was written after the overwrite erased a real contradiction (`MarkPwuReady → PwuStateChanged` in the command entry vs `→ PwuMarkedReady` in the table; the command entry was the wrong side, and an *earlier* comment had invented a "display alias" theory to explain the drift away). **The hazard was diagnosed, written down, and the behaviour left in place — so it laundered again.** A documented hazard is not a mitigated one, and this is the second finding in two days where the record already contained the answer.
- **AND THE TEST THAT WATCHED IT WAS UNFALSIFIABLE.** `messages.test.ts` asserted `expect(binding.eventType).toBe(cmd[ct].emitsEvent)` — **the exact identity the generator forced.** It could not fail for any vocab, and it stayed green throughout. It also used `.find()`, reading only the FIRST row per command, so the wrong row was doubly invisible. This is the `a-control-that-cannot-fail` shape at the contract layer.
- **THE VOCAB WAS RIGHT THE WHOLE TIME, AND SAID SO.** The dropped row carries its own note: *"So ONE command produces TWO events … Recorded HERE, unemitted, so the census NAMES the gap instead of hiding it."* The row was authored **specifically to prevent** a gap being hidden, and the generator hid it. `verif/event-surface-census.test.ts` was never fooled, because it reads the VOCAB's bindings rather than the generated table — which is how the two artifacts could disagree for months without either looking wrong alone.
- **THE FIX MAKES THE STEP CHECK INSTEAD OF REWRITE.** Commands may declare `alsoEmitsEvents`; a binding may name any event its command actually commits, and naming anything else **THROWS**. This *keeps* the MarkPwuReady catch — a genuine drift is still not in the declared set — while removing its silence. Verified by running the generator against the vocab **before** the declaration was added: it refused, naming the row. The regenerated table now attributes `REQUESTED → EVIDENCE_PENDING` to `AssuranceEvidenceRequired`, which is what the engine emits.
  - The registry entry gained `alsoEmitsEvents` too, because a runtime table saying a command emits one event while it emits two is the same false declaration one layer over.
  - The test now checks EVERY row against the declared set (plus a control proving the membership test can say no, and that the primary event is still bound somewhere). **Mutation-verified:** deleting the declaration from the generated registry reddens both.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED. Blast radius was contained: `emitsEvent` had exactly one consumer, the unfalsifiable test, so the defect was a false *declaration* rather than a runtime misbehaviour — which is precisely why nothing caught it.

### REG-F-030 — I reported that ten of twelve policies have no ratified evidence list. Nine of those ten have one. The heading changes at §17, and I searched for the heading

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found while executing the ratification of REG-E-026, which had been raised *on this measurement*) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD_DIVERGES — a load-bearing measurement in this register, made by me, that was a statement about my search rather than about the corpus.
- **Statement:** REG-F-022 and REG-E-026 both record: *"Only **2 of 12** policies have a required-evidence section at all (§15.5, §16.4) … Ten policies have no textual basis whatsoever."* **It is 11 of 12, and 89 items.** I grepped RPH-DOC-004 for the heading `Required evidence`, found exactly two occurrences, and reported that as a fact about the catalog. From §17 onward the heading is plain **`Evidence`** — §17.4, §18.4, §19.4, §21.4, §22.4, §23.4, §24.4, §25.4, §26.4. Only §20 POL-CONSTRAINT-PROPAGATION genuinely has none.
- **THE RULING IT PRODUCED WAS THEREFORE WRONG, AND IT WAS A RULING NOT TO ACT.** REG-E-026's default read *"NOTHING IS INVENTED … the vacuity is disclosed rather than papered over"*, and the design note's P-1 was *"Do not land invented requirements."* Both followed **validly** from the false measurement — which is the point worth keeping. A sound inference from a bad measurement is not a smaller error than a bad inference; it is the same error with a clean audit trail, and it is harder to spot precisely because the reasoning survives inspection.
- **IT IS `feedback_absence_of_evidence` FOR THE EIGHTH RECORDED TIME, AND THE WARNING WAS IN A FILE I HAD EDITED.** `doc004-conformance.test.ts`'s `subsection()` carries this, in these words: *"BY TITLE, never by number. Subsection numbering is NOT uniform across the twelve policies … Assuming a number is the single most reliable way to read the wrong subsection and report it as ratified."* I obeyed that rule and committed **its mirror image**: I assumed the **title** was uniform. The habit that catches one axis does not generalise on its own.
- **THE GENERAL FORM, which is the reason this is a finding and not a correction:** **any lookup key can be non-uniform, not just the one that burned you last time.** A search for a **label** establishes where the label is — never where the **content** is. To claim content is ABSENT, the search must be over content, or over an enumeration derived from **structure** (here: "every `# N. Policy` section, then ask what subsections it has"). This is the same shape as REG-F-022's blind reader and the regex state-count: *the instrument answered a narrower question than the one the entry asked, and nothing in the answer said so.*
- **THE GATE:** `doc004-conformance.test.ts` now walks the twelve sections structure-first and asserts the split (2 `Required evidence` / 9 `Evidence` / 1 neither) and the per-policy item counts summing to 89 — with a CONTROL that the parser returns real text, because a parser yielding 89 empty strings would satisfy every count. **Mutation-checked:** deleting a §15.5 bullet reddens the counts; retitling §21.4 to `Required evidence` reddens the split.
- **AND THE HEADING TURNED OUT TO BE LOAD-BEARING, NOT NOISE.** DOC-004 §3.1 declares **both** `requiredEvidence` and `optionalEvidence?`. The two sections that say *"Required evidence"* map to the field that GATES; the nine that say *"Evidence"* map to the one that does not. So the distinction whose absence caused the error is the same distinction that decides which of 89 items can withhold a verdict.
- **Merge target:** This register (both entries corrected in place) + `docs/_working/DESIGN-evidence-requirements.md` (v2, with v1's reasoning struck rather than deleted). **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-031 — `cardinality` was declared and unread, so a requirement satisfied by zero instances would have withheld the verdict it permits

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (found while landing REG-E-026, before it could ship) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a ratified field carried, well-typed, and consulted by nothing.
- **Statement:** DOC-004 §6.1 gives every `EvidenceRequirement` a `cardinality`, one value of which is `ZERO_OR_MORE` — zero instances **satisfy** the requirement. Every "outstanding evidence" computation in `assurance.ts` worked on **ids alone**: required MINUS received, in the `EVIDENCE_PENDING → READY` arrow, in `submitEvidenceForAssessment`, in **Gate A**, and (via the event) in §38's `missingEvidence`. A `ZERO_OR_MORE` requirement was therefore treated as unmet until something arrived — **the exact inverse of what it declares.**
- **IT WAS INVISIBLE BECAUSE THE FIELD HAD NEVER HELD A VALUE.** While every shipped policy carried `requiredEvidence: []` (REG-F-022), no cardinality was ever read, so no cardinality could be misread. **The defect arrived with the fix** — and would have shipped inside it.
- **A CONCRETE VICTIM, not a hypothetical:** §15.5's *"prior intent version **where applicable**"*. An intent with no predecessor would have been permanently unable to reach a SATISFIED fidelity verdict, for want of a version the corpus itself says need not exist.
- **THE GENERALISABLE LESSON, and the reason this is filed separately from REG-F-022 rather than folded into it:** **delivering authored content is not the end of the work.** REG-F-022 is "a policy object is read, the gate that reads it is wired, and the specific content is dropped". This is one layer further in: the content arrives, and a **field of that content** is unread. *A layer can be de-vacuified object-by-object, then field-by-field, and still be hollow one field deeper.* Landing 89 requirements while inverting the meaning of one of their nine fields would have been worse than the vacuum it replaced — a gate that looks enforced and enforces the opposite.
- **THE FIX:** one shared predicate (`demandsAnInstance`, fail-closed on an absent or unrecognised cardinality) used at all three sites, so they cannot disagree — the same reason `driveAssessmentToAssessing` exists. **Gate:** `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/evidence-cardinality.test.ts` — two requirements differing ONLY in cardinality, both `SATISFIED_ONLY`, so a pass cannot come from the disposition filter. **Mutation-checked:** reverting either filter reddens the `ZERO_OR_MORE` arm while the CONTROL (an `AT_LEAST_ONE` sibling still refusing SATISFIED) stays green — which is what makes the green mean "exempt" rather than "nothing is enforced".
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-032 — The adversarial review of REG-E-025/026: ten findings, and the two worst were a premise that emptied the gate it was defending and a refusal I added without telling the read-model

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (65-agent adversarial review of the two ratified authoring acts, `4801c889` + `ac134b6a`) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES / RECORD_DIVERGES — defects in content authored the same day, found by attacking it rather than by re-reading it.
- **Method, because the result depends on it:** five independent lenses over the authored content (tier split, cardinality, classification, purpose prose, failure taxonomy), each finding then attacked by TWO verifiers with different jobs — one instructed to REFUTE from primary sources and default to refuted, one asked only whether anything *wrong would happen*. A finding survived only on two non-refutations. **Thirty findings raised, TWENTY refuted, TEN confirmed.** The 2:1 refutation rate is the useful number: a panel that confirmed everything would be measuring nothing.
- **THE WORST ONE IS A RULE THAT DESTROYED ITSELF, AND I HAD ARGUED FOR IT AT LENGTH.** The `CONTINGENT` cardinality rule exempted an item when *"the system has no object type that can produce it"* — used to make `EV-16-02` (ambiguity catalog) and `EV-16-04` (stakeholder catalog) `ZERO_OR_MORE`, because no `AMBIGUITY` or `STAKEHOLDER` object type exists. **The premise is false and was false 200 lines from where I wrote it.** `cardinality` counts EVIDENCE instances, and an Evidence object needs no same-named object type — `contentReference` is an open record. There is no `NON_GOAL` type either, yet `EV-16-05` gates, and the reference undertaking evidences it by pointing at the INTENT. **Applied consistently the rule would also have voided `EV-15-01`, `EV-16-05` and `EV-16-06` — it emptied the gate rather than discriminating within it**, which is the exact failure the whole exercise exists to prevent, committed inside the fix for it.
  - **WHAT REPLACED IT IS THE CORPUS, NOT A BETTER GUESS.** DOC-002 §6.2's intent transition matrix discriminates where I could not: *"| UNDER_DISCOVERY | Create provisional intent | PROVISIONAL | Objective and **known ambiguities recorded** |"* is a GUARD on the only path to the state these policies assess — so `EV-16-02` **gates** — while the matrix names stakeholders in no guard at all, so `EV-16-04` stays contingent on its surviving textual ground. The contingent set is four, not five.
  - **AND FIXING IT MADE THE NEXT FINDING FIRE.** With `EV-16-02` gating, the reference undertaking's shortfall branch — previously dead — became live and refused the Intent Baseline promotion. That is how the two findings turned out to be one.
- **A CONTROL THAT COULD NOT FAIL, BUILT THE SAME DAY I FILED REG-F-031 ABOUT CONTROLS THAT CANNOT FAIL.** `earnAssurance`'s evidence-shortfall branch recorded a MATERIAL observation and downgraded the verdict to `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` — and was **unreachable**, because the contingent set had been sized to exactly what the fixture can produce. Dead code wearing the costume of a governance control, which would have reported *"the fixture falls short"* as a feature while never firing. It is now a LOUD FAILURE: the drive claims it can evidence every gating requirement and says so by throwing if it cannot, checked on every seed run. A downgrade is a decision, and a fixture driver must not settle a governance question by lowering its own verdict.
- **F-29, SIXTH INSTANCE, CREATED BY THE COMMIT THAT ADDED THE REFUSAL.** The §36 retry refusal was added to the engine and **not** to the read-model, so the execution tab kept offering `retry` on a step whose failure class forbids it. *"No affordance the engine would reject"* — broken again, one work package after I wrote the entry about the fifth instance. **The generalisable half: adding an engine refusal is STRUCTURALLY INCOMPLETE until the read-model has it, and nothing in the type system says so.** Fixed with the established split — one kernel fold (`lastFailureClassFrom`) called by both, the decision in `isPermittedForFailure`, and the withheld affordance carrying its own reason so a vanishing button still explains itself.
- **A SECOND COPY OF A RATIFIED SET.** `EXECUTION_FAILURE_CONTROL_ACTIONS.RETRY_EXHAUSTION` was authored from scratch as four actions while `RETRY_EXHAUSTION_ACTIONS` — RPH-EXE-008's ratified five, in the same package — had existed all along. My version silently dropped `REJECT`. The fix is not "add REJECT": the row now **is** the constant, because two constants naming one governed set is the drifting twin REG-F-029 review finding (c) caught, and I had just written a register entry about it.
- **THREE TESTS WHOSE NAMES OVERPROMISED**, all mine, all from the same day: one asserted the enum where its own comment said it must assert the four payload schemas (it would have stayed green if all four reverted to `string`); one cited *"a ratified §37 ControlAction"* while checking DOC-004 §11's different 23-value list; one recorded that a behaviour was *"not reachable from here"* when the discriminating sequence uses only legal arrows. **The third is the one to keep:** *"untestable" is a claim about the search for a test*, and writing it into a comment is how a gap becomes permanent, because the next reader believes it.
- **TWO MIS-CITED CLAIM NUMBERS** in authored `purpose` prose (`EV-17-05` cited §17.3 claim 6 for *prohibited* work where the ratified text says *irreversible*; `EV-15-07` cited IF-02 for a constraint change, which is IF-03), and **every policy's `sourceSection` carried the provenance paragraph twice** — the second copy a superseded draft stating a cardinality rule the same object's data violates, from running the authoring script a second time after amending it.
- **WHAT THE REFUTATIONS ARE WORTH SAYING TOO.** Twenty findings died, and the pattern in them is instructive: most measured something true and then inferred a defect the corpus does not support — a classification called wrong against a semantics DOC-004 §6.1 never states, a `purpose` attacked for citing a claim it cites correctly, `optionalEvidence` called "unreachable" when ungated is what the corpus's own heading makes it. **Being right about the measurement and wrong about the consequence is the dominant failure mode of review, and it is the same shape as REG-F-030** — a sound inference from a premise nobody checked.
- **Merge target:** Repository — all ten landed. **Status:** CLOSED. **Mutation-checked:** removing the read-model limb reddens both refusal assertions while all three over-refusal guards stay green; the payload-schema test's CONTROL caught that two of its four refusals had been rejecting a stray field rather than the value.

### REG-F-033 — REG-F-022's other half is five fields, not one, and the census that exists to find them walked only half the ontology

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 (measured while picking up REG-F-022's own "14 further carriers" notation) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — authored governance content that reaches no consumer, in the object type the existing census does not walk.
- **⚠ THE ENTRY OPENS WITH MY OWN MIS-QUOTED NUMBER, BECAUSE THAT IS THE FINDING'S FIRST LESSON.** REG-F-022's notation says the field is declared *"a second time, on the ontology's `PwuTemplate` type, with **14 further carriers**"*. I proposed this work quoting that figure — **from the register, without re-measuring.** Measured: **seven authored keys, five of which reach nothing, across 64 declarations.** `requiredEvidenceTypes` is one of five. The estimate was low by ~4.5×.
  - **THIRD TIME IN TWO DAYS THAT A REPEATED NUMBER WAS WRONG** (REG-F-030's corpus count; REG-F-032's twenty refuted review measurements). **A count in a register entry is evidence that someone once measured something. It is not evidence that it is still true, or that it was ever complete.** Re-measure on the way in — especially when the number is what justifies the work.
- **Statement:** `pwuTemplates` authors ten keys. Three are delivered verbatim (`pwuKind`, `purpose`, `isRoot`), one is TRANSFORMED (`defaultPolicyIds` → `requiredAssurancePolicyIds` via `policiesForKind`), one is ontology-internal (`sourceSection`). **Five reach nothing: `candidateChildren` (8 carriers), `completionClaims` (14), `inputs` (14), `outputArtifactTypes` (14), `requiredEvidenceTypes` (14).**
- **AND THE INSTRUMENT BUILT TO FIND EXACTLY THIS COULD NOT SEE THEM.** `ontology-delivery-census.test.ts` — created *for* REG-F-022, under the heading "of everything the ontology AUTHORS, what does the seeding DELIVER?" — walked `seedPolicies` **only**. Sixty-four declarations sat outside it. This is the same shape REG-E-025 produced the same day, when two `failureClass` fields turned out to be invisible to the enumRef census because they declared no enum: **a field is not safe because the census is quiet about it; it may simply be below the census's floor.** The engine's port makes it structural, precisely as it did for policies — `EngineOntology.pwuTemplates` is declared `{ pwuKind, isRoot }`, two members, so no type error was ever available for the other eight.
- **ONE OF THE FIVE IS NOT MERELY UNREAD — IT CONTRADICTS THE TREE THAT SHIPS.** `permittedChildren` comes from a **hand-written `PWU_TYPES` list** in `seed-workbench.ts`, not from the ontology.
