The root agrees (7 = 7). **`ARCHITECTURE_DEFINITION` does not:** the ontology authors **ten** candidate children and the seed ships **one** — `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN` — **which is not among the ten.** Six further templates author candidates (8, 8, 1, 9, 10, 10) and ship none. `policiesForKind`'s own comment says these lists *"used to be written by hand on `PWU_TYPES` — a THIRD copy of content the ontology already carries"*; the policy list was fixed, **the composition list is the copy that was not**, and it is the one that disagrees.
- **WHY IT IS PINNED AND NOT WIRED, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE ENTRY.** **62 distinct candidate child kinds are authored; 50 have no `pwuTemplate` row at all.** A kind with no template has no `defaultPolicyIds`, and `requireGoverningPolicies` THROWS on an undescribed kind (REG-F-029) — so deriving `permittedChildren` from `candidateChildren` would publish a PWA offering **50 child types that cannot be assessed and cannot be driven**, trading an inert field for 50 unusable affordances. It is REG-F-028's finding one level up: *a PWU-kind vocabulary the seeded work does not speak*, in a third place. **And which composition structure the reference PWA publishes is a governance decision, not a wiring one** — the field is even named *candidate* children. Selecting all 62 by construction would be an authoring act performed by a `.map()`.
- **THE GATE:** the census now walks `pwuTemplates` too, pinning the five as a KNOWN GAP with its declaration count (64), asserting the `candidateChildren` divergence by name, and checking its two accounting rows rather than trusting them. **Mutation-checked BOTH WAYS, and the second matters more:** delivering one of the five reddens the pin (that is the fix landing), *and* adding a `TEMPLATE_ACCOUNTED_FOR` row for one of them **also** reddens it — because the pin is an exact list rather than a ceiling, the gap cannot be closed by exemption. That is the direct answer to this census's own recorded history of carrying a false exemption.
- **NARROWED 2026-08-06 (sponsor direction: deepen the tree; give the fields consumers).** **THE FIELDS DID NOT NEED CONSUMERS BUILT — THEY NEEDED WIRING**, which is REG-F-022's shape a third time. Four of the five already had ratified homes on `PWU_TYPE` that `DefinePwuType` already accepted, every one optional and unpopulated: `inputs`→`requiredInputs`, `outputArtifactTypes`→`requiredOutputs`, `completionClaims`→`completionRule` (TRANSFORMED as a conjunction, every claim retained), `candidateChildren`→`permittedChildren`. This entry was right that they reached nothing; what it could not say is that **they had somewhere to reach all along**.
  - **THE TREE IS NOW DERIVED, NOT HAND-WRITTEN.** `policiesForKind`'s comment had already named the disease — *"a THIRD copy of content the ontology already carries"* — and fixed the POLICY list while leaving the COMPOSITION list, which is exactly the copy that drifted into contradicting the ontology. Only a kind→id map stays hand-assigned, because ids are stable identity. **9 published types → 14; two levels → three.**
  - **THE ENGINE REFUSED THE FIRST ATTEMPT, CORRECTLY.** Publishing all fifteen templates failed `ValidatePwa`: `ARCHITECTURE_DECISION` is an ORPHAN — described by the ontology and named in NO `candidateChildren` list. The design note had said *"recorded rather than parented by guess"*; §11.6 enforced precisely that, independently of the note that predicted it.
  - **DEEPENING EXPOSED A TWIN.** Three new kinds declared a policy whose `appliesToPwuKinds` did not name them, and **in all three the PARENT was listed and the CHILD was not** — that list was authored against the NINE PUBLISHED kinds, so the six unpublished templates were outside its field of view. **Widened, never narrowed:** dropping the policy from the template would silently exempt work from assurance, REG-F-024's forbidden direction.
  - **AND A ROUTE I ASSERTED ONLY HALF HELD.** I accounted for `requiredEvidenceTypes` as SUPERSEDED by the policies' §6.1 requirements — then checked: **6 of 14 templates declare an evidence type NONE of their own policies require**, nearly the rate REG-E-026 measured on the policy side (6 of 12). Pinned with the exact map rather than deleting the values, because editing authored governance data to make a gate pass is the inversion this register exists to prevent.
- **Merge target:** Repository — the census extension, the derived tree and the four deliveries landed. **Status:** OPEN, GATED AND COUNTED — and the remainder is smaller and sharper: **50 candidate kinds with no template**, `ARCHITECTURE_DECISION` described but parented by nobody, and the 6-of-14 unsupported evidence types. The contradiction is closed and the unexplained list is empty. Design notes: `docs/_working/DESIGN-template-delivery-census.md`, `docs/_working/DESIGN-deeper-tree-and-template-consumers.md`.

### REG-F-034 — The §36 mapping had no basis field, so one ratified row and six authored ones were indistinguishable — and a row changed membership with nothing able to notice

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD_DIVERGES — an authored governance table asserting no provenance, in a repository whose neighbouring artifact solved exactly that.
- **Statement:** `EXECUTION_FAILURE_CONTROL_ACTIONS` shipped as seven rows of authored judgement with the reasoning in **comments**. During the same session the `TOOL_FAILURE` row acquired `REQUEST_WAIVER`. Both gates caught the **spelling** — §37 says `WAIVE`, the contract uses DOC-004 §11's `REQUEST_WAIVER` — and **nothing caught, or could have caught, that the row's membership had changed at all**, because no structure distinguished *an action the corpus requires* from *an action someone added*.
- **THE GENERAL FORM, and the reason this is a finding rather than a note about one value:** **an authored governance table with no basis field is indistinguishable from a ratified one, and its comments cannot fail.** I initially responded by flagging the single value to the sponsor. That is the wrong shape of answer — the next edit to any of the seven rows has the identical problem, and flagging values one at a time does not scale past the one I happened to notice.
- **THE PRECEDENT WAS ONE ARTIFACT OVER AND I DID NOT REACH FOR IT.** `FindingAnnotation` already carries `severityBasis` / `severityQuote` / `severityRationale` with three locks: an **anti-laundering** check (a RATIFIED claim must quote words verbatim in the ratified corpus), **its inverse** (an AUTHORED row may carry NO quote, so authority cannot be implied decoratively), and **the ratio pinned as a number**. That machinery was built, argued for over four adversarial rounds, and sitting unused twenty metres away.
- **MEASURED: ONE ROW OF SEVEN IS RATIFIED.** RPH-EXE-008 states the exhaustion remedy set verbatim — *"the controller must not issue a fourth retry. It must select: change tactic; replan; escalate; reject; abandon."* The other six are mine. Before this field, all seven looked alike to any reader.
- **AND IT SETTLED THE FLAGGED VALUE BETTER THAN A RULING WOULD HAVE.** §36's rule governs *"permitted **control actions**"* — the controller's, which DOC-002 §37 enumerates as **eighteen**, against the contract enum's twenty-three. So a mapping could reach for a §11 action §37 never gave the controller. **Measured: it does not** — every mapped action is in §37, *including* `REQUEST_WAIVER`, which is §37's `WAIVE` under the ratified rename. So the concern was never "is this action allowed" but "who chose it, on what basis" — which the basis field now answers, permanently and for every row. The containment is asserted with the **rename named explicitly rather than normalized away**, because hiding it inside a normalization would be the laundering the lock exists to prevent.
- **THE FIX ALSO REMOVED A TWIN BEFORE IT EXISTED.** `EXECUTION_FAILURE_MAPPING` is the single source and `EXECUTION_FAILURE_CONTROL_ACTIONS` is **derived** from it, rather than the provenance being maintained beside the data it describes — the drifting-twin shape this register has now caught three times, once in this same file.
- **Mutation-checked on both locks, and the second is the one that matters:** relabelling an AUTHORED row `RATIFIED` with a **plausible fabricated quote** reddens the anti-laundering check *and* the pinned ratio; giving an AUTHORED row a **genuine corpus quote** — which the anti-laundering check alone would happily accept — reddens the inverse lock. Decorative authority is exactly how an authored judgement acquires standing nobody granted it.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED. *The governance question it exposes stays open and is now auditable rather than flagged:* whether `REQUEST_WAIVER` belongs on `TOOL_FAILURE` is a sponsor call, the row says `AUTHORED`, and it carries a rationale written to be rejected. Design note: `docs/_working/DESIGN-failure-mapping-provenance.md`.

### REG-F-035 — REG-E-024(b) asked which arrows the aggregate disposition lacks. It lacks arrows because it is a fold, and its rule has been ratified in DOC-004 §28.2 the whole time

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD_DIVERGES — an elicitation item whose framing withheld work that was never blocked.
- **Statement:** REG-E-024(b) reads *"`AggregateAssuranceDisposition` — six states, no arrows: is the aggregate roll-up axis intended and unbuilt, or superseded?"* **Both branches presuppose a state machine, and it is not one.** It has no arrows because it is a **computed reduction** — which this repository already knew: `gen-transitions.ts` describes such rows as *"not an arrow at all (a computed reduction, e.g. `AggregateAssuranceDisposition`'s …)"*, and `state-reachability.test.ts` **excludes** it from the reachability census for precisely that reason. Asking which arrows it lacks is like asking which arrows `sum()` lacks.
- **AND THE RULE IS RATIFIED IN FULL.** DOC-004 **§28.2** states it as an ordered decision table — *critical rejection → REJECTED; blocking rejection → REJECTED; required assessment missing → EVIDENCE_REQUIRED or UNASSESSED; inconclusive → INCONCLUSIVE; conditional → CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED; all satisfied → SATISFIED* — closing with *"This must not be reduced to a numerical average."* **§28.1** supplies the invariant: *"An aggregate assurance state must preserve the strictest unresolved disposition relevant to the work."* **The document order IS the strictness order**, which is what makes §28.1 executable rather than aspirational.
- **⚠ IT COST ME REAL WORK THE SAME DAY.** While landing REG-E-026 I deliberately did **not** touch PWU-level roll-up, writing that *"how N assessment verdicts roll up into one PWU disposition is `AggregateAssuranceDisposition`, six ratified states with no arrows, already filed as REG-E-024(b) … settling an open elicitation by implementation is what §0.3 forbids."* That reasoning was sound and its premise was false: **I took the elicitation item's framing as the measurement instead of opening §28.** Fourth instance this session of trusting a recorded claim (REG-F-030's corpus count, REG-F-032's twenty refuted measurements, REG-F-033's "14 carriers"), and the most consequential — the others produced wrong numbers; this one produced **withheld work, justified by a rule that does not exist.**
- **WHAT LANDED:** `aggregateAssuranceDisposition()` in `rph-domain`, §28.2 transcribed as a first-match fold, **shipping with its consumer** — the §38 view returned N policies with N verdicts and never said what they added up to, which left §28.1 and the anti-averaging prohibition as rules about a value the reader was invited to compute by eye.
- **TWO DERIVATIONS, DISCLOSED:** §28.2's *"EVIDENCE_REQUIRED **or** UNASSESSED"* is a disjunction with no rule — resolved as *no assessment at all → UNASSESSED, underway-but-unconcluded → EVIDENCE_REQUIRED*, the only reading under which both ratified values are reachable. And **`ESCALATED` has no rung at all** — a ratified assessment disposition the aggregate enum has no member for; mapped to `INCONCLUSIVE` on §28.1's "strictest unresolved", and recorded as a genuine §28.2 gap rather than papered over. `critical` vs `blocking` rejection is deliberately not modelled: both rungs yield `REJECTED`, so modelling it would be false precision.
- **A GATE OF MINE THAT WAS WEAKER THAN ITS NAME, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN MUTANT.** The §28.1 property test first varied ONE input against a `SATISFIED` baseline and asserted the result never weakened — so it never placed two non-satisfied verdicts in one set. When the mutant moved the `INCONCLUSIVE` rung **above** `REJECTED` (every rung individually correct, the table collectively mis-ordered), **the property stayed green** and only a precedence case caught it. Rewritten to the invariant as actually stated — over the SET, every ordered pair folding to the stricter — plus a three-element order-independence case, because a fold returning "the stricter of the first two" satisfies every pair. **A property test named for a general claim, checking a special case, is the `a-control-that-cannot-fail` shape wearing the strongest name in the file.**
- **AND IT ANSWERS REG-F-023 FOR THIS ENUM.** All six values are now proved reachable, by witness — the question the reachability census could not ask because it correctly excluded a reduction.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED; **REG-E-024(b) closes with it** (the axis is intended, specified, and now built; it was never a machine). Design note: `docs/_working/DESIGN-aggregate-assurance-disposition.md`. **Sub-items (a), (c) and (d) of REG-E-024 remain OPEN and are genuinely arrows.**

### REG-F-036 — Four open items closed in one pass, and three of the four were blocked by a claim about a NAME rather than about the content

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD_DIVERGES — items recorded as blocked on missing corpus content that the corpus supplies elsewhere, plus one governance call decided on its merits.
- **REG-F-026 group (d) — CLOSED, and the blocker was never real.** The entry read: *"`ControlActionRecommendation` is a shape the corpus has never defined, so there is nothing to point the field at. It closes when the corpus defines it, or not at all."* Its annotation reasoned from the **type name** and an M1 vocab row *"literally named `(undefined)` with type `-`"*, concluding *"the object model says a recommendation is a STRUCTURE, not a bare action code."* **It never consulted the worked example.** DOC-004 **§33 "Validator Implementation Output Schema Example"** shows the field's actual content at two levels, and it is bare `ControlAction` strings: `"recommendedControlActions": ["RESHAPE_PWU", "REQUEST_HUMAN_DECISION"]`. **An undefined type NAME is not evidence about the CONTENT.** Constrained to `ControlAction` — the conservative direction (an unvalidated string becomes a ratified enum), widenable without breakage if the corpus ever defines the richer shape. **The enumRef ratchet reaches ZERO: 7 → 4 → 3 → 1 → 0.**
  - **This is the sixth instance of the same shape in two days**, and §33 is the *same worked example* that caught instance 6 of `feedback_absence_of_evidence` (severity written under the key `severity` in §33's validator output while a field-name grep reported it absent). The corpus keeps answering in its examples, and I keep searching its declarations.
- **REG-E-023 — CLOSED toward the EVENT.** `InvalidatePwu.triggeringObjectId` was optional while `PwuInvalidated` declared it required — a command the contract admitted producing an event the contract forbade. Resolved per the register's own stated default: the event describes a fact that has already happened, and DOC-003's *"fail closed; escalate rather than invent"* favours the stricter reading. It also merely makes the contract state what the engine already did — the handler has refused an absent or unresolvable value since JAN-CMDPRE. The sibling pair (`ReshapePwu`/`PwuReshapingStarted`, both optional) is left alone, being internally consistent.
  - **AND TIGHTENING THE SCHEMA DID NOT KILL THE HANDLER GUARD, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** The refusal moved up a layer, so the guard *looked* superseded — but `z.string()` accepts `''`. The schema now owns ABSENCE; the guard owns the EMPTY STRING, which is exactly *"a reference to nothing"*, the fabrication that site's own struck comment named and its original code committed. **Tightening a schema narrows a guard's population; it does not always empty it, and assuming it does is how a real check gets deleted as redundant.** Both arms are now separately gated.
- **`REQUEST_WAIVER` on `TOOL_FAILURE` — REMOVED, on §12.2.** The value arrived mid-session from the sponsor's side and I flagged it rather than deciding. Deciding it: **§12.2 requires a waiver to record "exact policy and criterion" and "finding being waived"** — an execution-step failure has no policy, no criterion and no finding, so **no §12 waiver can be CONSTITUTED for one.** The sharper argument is consequence: permitting it would mean treating a step as passable without the output it failed to produce, which corrupts the evidence chain rather than governing it. The action stays ratified for the controller generally (§37 `WAIVE` / §11 `REQUEST_WAIVER`); what it cannot be is a response to a §36.2 **execution** failure class — so the gate asserts it over **all seven rows**, not the one that carried it. *Reversible in one line, and the row's `basis: AUTHORED` is what makes the reversal auditable.*
- **`candidateChildren` — the CONTRADICTION is gone, by transcription.** REG-F-033 found the shipped tree contradicting the authored candidates: `ARCHITECTURE_DEFINITION` authored ten candidates and the PWA ships one, `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN`, **which was not among the ten**. RPH-DOC-010 lists *"Custom Architecture Concern"* among Architecture Definition's permitted children, and the workbench seeds that type and instantiates five of it — so its absence from the candidates was the error, and adding it is transcription. **The assertion was upgraded rather than relaxed:** it now states the GENERAL property — every shipped child of every type must be an authored candidate of that type — so "candidate children" means a menu the PWA may narrow and may never contradict. The 50 orphan kinds and the five undelivered fields stay pinned; **this closes the contradiction, not the gap.**
- **Mutation-checked:** re-adding `REQUEST_WAIVER` reddens the all-rows gate; removing `ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN` from the candidates reddens the subset property *and* the orphan count; omitting `triggeringObjectId` now fails at the schema while `''` still fails at the handler, each with its own test.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-037 — The corpus ratified an ARROW WITH NO TRIGGER and a TYPE WITH NO DEFINITION. Both are amended in the corpus itself, and REG-E-024 turns out to be three-quarters stale

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CORPUS_INTERNALLY_INCONSISTENT — the first entry in this register to be remediated by amending a ratified RPH document rather than the repository. · **Authority:** sponsor grant to author corpus updates, 2026-08-05.
- **THE INSTRUMENT MATTERS AS MUCH AS THE CONTENT.** DOC-004 already carries authored additions in a marked form — *"**Relationship to the Canonical Domain Model §26.5 (authored clarification, §0.3 grant, 2026-07-17).**"* — so the corpus has its own convention for this, and both amendments use it: a marked, attributed, dated blockquote. **No ratified sentence is edited or deleted.** A silent corpus edit is worse than an unratified annotation, because it destroys the reader's ability to tell ratified text from ours.
- **AMENDMENT 1 — §30 declared an arrow that §31 and §32 gave no way to take.** §30's *Alternate transitions* carries `ANY ACTIVE → CANCELLED`; the §31 event list named no cancellation event and the §32 command list named no cancellation command. So the catalog ratified a **destination state, an arrow into it, and no trigger** — leaving an assessment stalled in `EVIDENCE_PENDING` with no exit, since `invalidateAssuranceAssessment` runs only from `SATISFIED`/`CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED`. §31 now names `AssuranceAssessmentCancelled` and §32 `cancelAssuranceAssessment`. **It adds no state, no arrow and no source state** — `ANY ACTIVE` is §30's own phrase.
  - **AND IT MAKES A REPOSITORY TRANSCRIPTION TRUE RATHER THAN A DIVERGENCE.** The engine had already authored both names for exactly this reason, recorded as `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED` with the careful note *"THE ARROW IS RATIFIED AND THE NAME IS NOT … It is one of each."* That annotation was correct and is now **stale in the good direction**; three sites (`validate.test.ts`, `assurance.ts`, `assessment-cancellation.test.ts`) and two vocab `sourceSection`s are updated to say RATIFIED.
- **AMENDMENT 2 — `ControlActionRecommendation` was referenced four times and defined nowhere.** A named type with no definition admits any shape. §33's *Validator Implementation Output Schema Example* — a ratified worked instance of the very interface — carries the field as bare `ControlAction` values, so the definition is stated as what the corpus demonstrates: `type ControlActionRecommendation = ControlAction;`. The amendment records **where the rationale lives** rather than claiming there is none: DOC-002 §37's six required records bind the controller when it **takes** an action, not the validator when it **recommends** one, and the surrounding `ValidatorResult` already carries the evidence, observations, policy and validator identity. An alias also **widens without breaking** if a later ratification gives the recommendation a structure.
- **⚠ AND REG-E-024 IS THREE-QUARTERS STALE, WHICH ONLY BECAME VISIBLE ON TRYING TO ANSWER IT.** Sub-item **(b)** was malformed and closed today (REG-F-035: a fold, not a machine). Measuring the other three: **(a)** states *"§30 … gives it no in-arrow"* — **false**, §30 declares `ANY ACTIVE → CANCELLED`, and the live gate shows `AssuranceAssessment.state` with **no stranded states**; the real gap was the missing TRIGGER, which Amendment 1 closes. **(d)** asks how `PWU.assuranceState.UNASSESSED` is reached — it is the machine's **declared initial state**, so it needs no in-arrow and is not in the unreachable set. **Only (c) survives**: `ValidatorRegistryEntry.status`'s three values are now the **ONLY unreachable states left in the entire system**.
- **(c) IS DELIBERATELY NOT AUTHORED, AND THE REASON IS THE GRANT ITSELF.** `ValidatorRegistryEntry` appears **once** in the whole corpus — an interface under §35, introduced by *"The runtime **should** maintain a registry"*. There are no commands, no events, no arrows and no prose about validator health. Closing it means designing a subsystem, and §0.3 — the grant whose format these amendments borrow — is precisely the rule against encoding a convenient interpretation as architecture. Recorded with its measurement so the next attempt starts from what the corpus says (`should`, one interface) rather than from an item's framing.
- **THE GATE, BECAUSE I AM BOTH THE AMENDER AND THE CLAIMANT.** Three annotations moved from AUTHORED to RATIFIED on the strength of my own edits, so the ratification claim is checked against the document: §30 still declares the arrow, §31's and §32's **fenced lists** name the event and command, both amendments carry the `§0.3 grant` marker, and the type definition still agrees with §33's example.
  - **⚠ THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT GATE HAD THE DEFECT IT WAS WRITTEN TO PREVENT.** It asserted the *section* contained the name — and passed with the name **removed from the list**, because the amendment blockquote below the list also mentions it. **The check was satisfied by the commentary ABOUT the declaration rather than by the declaration.** Caught by its own mutant and rewritten to read the fenced list; the prose marker is checked separately, on the prose. Same section, two questions, two readers.
- **Merge target:** RPH-DOC-004 §31, §32, §4.2 (amended in place, marked) + the repository annotations. **Status:** CLOSED. **REG-E-024 (a) and (d) close with it; (c) remains OPEN and is now the last unreachable-state item in the system.**
- **⚠ CITATION AUDIT 2026-08-07 (REG-F-049) — THIS IS THE ENTRY THE RULE WAS WRITTEN FOR, AND ONE LIMB DOES NOT SURVIVE.** Of six citations: one relabel, one **WEAKENED (no canon twin)**, one **INVALIDATED**, two surviving on a canon twin, one sound. Unlike its neighbours, this entry's **title, class, remedy and gate** all rest on RPH-DOC-004 — whose status block reads `Initial canonical draft`. **"The corpus ratified an arrow with no trigger" is not a claim canon makes**; the arrow and the type live in a document CON-000 B1 makes historical material, so what was found is a defect in *historical material that the repository transcribed*, not a defect in canon.
  - **THE SUBSTANCE IS NOT THEREBY VOID — IT CHANGES OWNER.** The transcribed shapes ship in `packages/rph-contracts`, which B1 makes **shape authority**, so the repository-side defect stands on its own footing. What falls is the framing that canon itself ratified an incoherence, and with it the §0.3 corpus-amendment act this entry performed against a non-canon document — **which was an amendment to historical material and should be recorded as such rather than as a canon amendment.**
  - Corrected here rather than rewritten: the finding's measurements were sound and its conclusion about the *repository* holds. **Status remains as recorded; the AUTHORITY of three of its claims is downgraded from canon to shape.**

### REG-F-038 — REG-E-024(c) CLOSED: the validator registry was the mechanism §34.1 already depended on, and I twice declined to build it on a measurement that stopped at §35

- **Date:** 2026-08-05 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES + RECORD_DIVERGES · **Authority:** sponsor decision — *"yes validator health matters enough to design"* — plus the standing authoring and corpus-amendment grants.
- **⚠ I DECLINED THIS TWICE, AND THE REFUSAL WAS UNDER-MEASURED.** I wrote that `ValidatorRegistryEntry` *"appears ONCE in the whole corpus, under a §35 that says the runtime **should** maintain a registry"*, and that closing it *"means designing a subsystem, which §0.3 forbids"*. The first clause is true and the conclusion did not follow, because **I stopped at the section that names the object.** Four ratified sources converge: §35 (the entry, the statuses, and *"Selection should consider … availability"*), **§34.1** (*"a validator implementation failure … produces … retry, **alternate validator implementation**, or escalation action"*), §30's `ASSESSING → VALIDATOR_FAILED` triggered by *"validator execution failure (§34.1)"*, and the ratified conformance test **RPH-ASR-006**.
  - **§34.1 IS THE HINGE.** An *alternate validator implementation* cannot be chosen unless the runtime knows which are available. So `status` is not bookkeeping the corpus permits — **it is the mechanism a ratified error-handling rule already depends on.** That is the difference between designing a subsystem and finishing one the corpus is already committed to, and it is the difference my measurement missed.
  - **THE PATTERN, for the seventh time in two days:** the corpus answers in a section other than the one that names the thing. §33's worked example defined `ControlActionRecommendation`; §28.2 held the aggregate rule; §34.1 holds the registry's purpose. **Searching where a thing is DECLARED and concluding about what the corpus SAYS is the error this register keeps recording.**
- **WHAT LANDED.** §35 amended (§0.3 marked convention) to declare the five arrows its vocab entry recorded as absent — *"NO transition table specified in the doc"*. `VALIDATOR_REGISTRY_ENTRY` becomes a governed object with §35's fields transcribed verbatim; five named commands drive the machine (not one `SetValidatorStatus`, which would put legality in a payload field and force a second copy of the machine into the handler).
- **THE CENSUS REACHES ZERO.** Unreachable states **3 → 0** — the first time since REG-F-023 was filed. Arrowless machines **2 → 1**, and the one remaining is `AggregateAssuranceDisposition`, a fold rather than a machine (REG-F-035), correctly excluded.
- **GATE D MAKES THE STATUS OPERATIVE, AND ITS SEAM IS NOT WHERE §35's PROSE POINTS.** §35 speaks of *selection*, but `selectAssuranceEvaluator` carries `evaluator: ActorReference` — a PERSON (`evaluator-1`) — while the registry keys on `validatorId`, an IMPLEMENTATION (`reference-undertaking.reviewer`). **Different namespaces**, and joining them would infer a binding from proximity: the exact error REG-F-022 records for `evidenceId` vs requirement id. `validatorId` enters the assurance flow in exactly one place — `validatorResult` at completion — so a DISABLED validator's **result** is refused. Stronger than refusing the selection, and sound.
  - **TWO DELIBERATE NON-REFUSALS, both gated as design claims rather than left as gaps.** `DEGRADED` does not refuse (§35 says selection *considers* availability; barring an impaired-but-not-withdrawn implementation would be stronger than the corpus states). An **unregistered** validator does not refuse — the disclosed fail-open: no production path registers one today, so refusing on absence would make every existing assessment uncompletable, and *a gate that bars the entire product is not a stricter gate, it is a broken one.*
- **⚠ A CONTROL THAT RESTED ON A REAL DEFECT, REBUILT.** `state-reachability`'s control asserted `unreachableStates().length > 0` against the LIVE machine set — true only while the system still HAD an unreachable state, so it was guaranteed to fail the day the last one was fixed. **A control whose witness is the defect it guards against reports "the detector works" and "the system is broken" with the same assertion and cannot tell them apart.** It is the mirror of `a-control-that-cannot-fail`: this one could not PASS once the system was correct. It now builds its own synthetic witness, plus the inverse case (an initial state IS reachable — the REG-E-024(d) error).
- **AND F-29 WAS CHECKED BEFORE BUILDING, NOT AFTER.** I created F-29's sixth instance earlier the same day by adding an engine refusal and not telling the read-model, so this was measured first: **there is no affordance to withhold.** No UI surface offers `CompleteAssuranceAssessment` keyed to a validator — the one production caller is a fixed demo action naming a hardcoded, never-registered `validatorId`. Building the limb anyway would be a control that cannot fire. **The condition that makes it required is recorded** so the absence reads as measured rather than forgotten: any production path that registers a validator AND lets a user-triggered action name it.
- **Merge target:** RPH-DOC-004 §35 (amended, marked) + repository. **Status:** CLOSED. **REG-E-024 closes entirely with it** — (a), (b) and (d) were malformed or stale, (c) is built. **REG-F-023 closes**: zero unreachable states.
- **⚠ CITATION AUDIT 2026-08-07 (REG-F-049) — THE BUILD SURVIVES; THE ARGUMENT THAT AUTHORIZED IT DOES NOT.** Two of six citations are **WEAKENED (no canon twin)**, and they are the two the entry leans on: the §35 validator-registry material is RPH-DOC-004, `Initial canonical draft`, historical material. **So the validator registry was built to satisfy an obligation canon does not impose.**
  - **WHAT IS UNAFFECTED:** the sponsor's ratification of REG-E-024(c), the code that landed, its mutants and its gate. A capability built on a good-faith reading of a source document is not thereby wrong — and the shapes it produced ship in `packages/rph-contracts`, which is shape authority.
  - **WHAT MUST NOT BE INFERRED:** that canon requires a validator registry. It does not, and no canon twin was located for §35's transition table after searching JPWB-DOC-003 §8 and DOC-004 by content. **The honest statement is that the registry is a repository capability transcribed from historical material and now carried as shape** — and if it is to become an obligation, the route is REG-D-008's shape-survivorship audit, not this entry.

### REG-F-039 — Two verification failures of my own: a gate that could not fail because of a shell pipe, and a refactor that changed the data it was only supposed to re-source

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** METHOD — neither is a defect in the product; both are defects in how I established that the product was sound, which is worse, because everything else in this register rests on that.
- **⚠ (1) I PIPED THE E2E RUNNER TO `tail`, AND THE PIPE DISCARDED ITS EXIT CODE.** `bunx playwright test 2>&1 | tail -3` returns **`tail`'s** status, not Playwright's. The run reported **exit 0 while THREE e2e specs were failing**, and I was one command away from committing on it. Re-run unpiped: exit 0, 65 passed — verified by exit code.
  - **IT IS THE SAME SHAPE AS THE CSAA MISTAKE ONE DAY EARLIER**, where I ran `grep -c CSAA` and `git commit` in a single command, so the check printed `14` and gated nothing. **A verification that cannot stop the action is not a verification** — it is the vacuous gate this register spends its time deleting, committed by the person deleting them.
  - **THE GENERAL FORM:** *a status check must be the thing that decides, not a thing that prints beside the decision.* Any construction that separates the two — a pipe, a `;`, a second command in the same line — silently converts a gate into a comment.
- **⚠ (2) A REFACTOR WHOSE POINT WAS TO CHANGE THE SOURCE OF THE DATA CHANGED THE DATA.** Deriving PWU Type names via `humanizeCode` sentence-cases, so every published type was silently relabelled — *"Architecture definition"* for *"Architecture Definition"*. **79 references across the suite depend on those names**, and the only reason it surfaced is that three e2e specs went red.
  - **NOTHING IN THE UNIT SUITE NOTICED**, because display names were never pinned — they were an emergent property of a hand-written literal, and nobody had asked what would happen if the literal went away. **A value that is only ever produced, never asserted, is unprotected by construction**, and a refactor is exactly when that bites.
  - Fixed with a title-case rule that reproduces all nine established names EXACTLY, and **pinned in `seed-workbench.test.ts`** so the next such change reddens in a unit test rather than in a browser.
- **WHY BOTH ARE FILED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED.** They are the two ways this session's verification could have lied to the sponsor: one made a red run look green, the other made a breaking change look invisible. Every "gate: vitest N, playwright 65" line in this register's recent commits is a claim that rests on my not making either mistake — so the mistakes belong in the record beside the claims.
- **Merge target:** Repository — the pin landed; the shell habit is a discipline, not a file. **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-040 — Superseding REG-F-014's heading and its arithmetic: the objective was never "an agent can manufacture human authority" but the survey underneath it — and the correction I filed for that title miscounted the survey a second time

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD — supersedes **REG-F-014 only as to its heading, its instance arithmetic, and the disposition of its residue**. REG-F-014's body stands, is correct on its merits, and is deliberately not edited.
- **WHY THIS IS AN ENTRY AND NOT AN EDIT, recorded because the wrong instruction was mine and I gave it twice.** I recommended *"re-title REG-F-014"* in the entry itself (its own last line) and again in the commit that filed the re-measurement. **The register's `changeProcedure` forbids it** — *"Entries are never destructively edited; a correction is a superseding entry citing the entry it supersedes"* — and there is no precedent to lean on: **no heading in this register has ever been struck or rewritten.** The established mechanism is the one at **REG-F-025's supersession of REG-F-021's closing account**, which was *"kept in place rather than rewritten"* because *"the wrong account is the more instructive half."* *(That citation is itself a small trap sprung and recorded: I first wrote it as REG-F-013, having inferred the owning entry from the LINE NUMBER of the superseding notation rather than from the entry it sits inside.)* A recommendation that would have required breaking the record's own change rule survived two passes because both were about the WORDS and neither asked whether the ACT was permitted.
- **THE OBJECTIVE, from the entry's own text on the day it was filed.** *"A survey of the remaining caller-supplied provenance/authority fields is the honest follow-up, **not a point fix**"*, and the prescription it says was *"followed exactly"* opens *"a caller and fixture survey first."* The heading names **instance 1**; the body is organised as *"THE FIVE INSTANCES."* The title was a **synecdoche from the outset** — the specimen found first and the most alarming — and it was never the objective, not even before four fifths of it closed.
- **THE GENERAL FORM, which is the heading REG-F-014 should have carried:** *a governed conclusion computed from a fact supplied by the party that conclusion judges.* It subsumes RPH-EVD-001's `parentCompletionClaimSupported`, REG-F-022's Gate A, and REG-F-041 below.
- **AND THE RULE THE SURVEY PRODUCED, which is its durable output and the reason the entry stays findable:** **derive what the engine owns; resolve what it can load; refuse what it cannot compute; never fabricate.** All four closures are that rule applied — refuse (instance 1), immutable pin (2), resolve (3), refuse (4's sibling) — and REG-F-017 records the one case where *derive* was the wrong arm and *pin* was right, which is what gives the rule its edges.
- **⚠ THE COUNT IS THREE OF FIVE, AND BOTH PREVIOUS COUNTS WERE WRONG — the second of them mine, filed yesterday.** REG-F-014 as written says *one of five*; I corrected it to *"Four of five, not one of five"* in `fb5d7cbd`; **measured against the code, it is three.** The mechanism of my error is precise and is the interesting part: **REG-E-023 closed `InvalidatePwu.triggeringObjectId`, which was never one of the five instances.** It was found *while surveying* instance 4, filed as a sub-bullet under instance 4's heading, and I banked its closure against the instance whose bullet it sat under. **A residual discovered under a heading is not a limb of that heading**, and nothing in the entry's shape distinguishes the two.
- **⚠ AND THE RESIDUAL WAS MIS-FILED IN THE FIRST PLACE, diagnosable from the entry's own adjacent lines.** Instance 4's open question is stated as *"the command types `triggeringObjectId` optional while the ratified event types it required."* That proposition is **FALSE of `ReshapePwu`** — `ReshapePwu.triggeringObjectId` (`messages.ts:381`) and `PwuReshapingStarted.triggeringObjectId` (`messages.ts:1415`) are **both optional and always were** — and **TRUE of `InvalidatePwu`**. Two lines earlier the same entry says *"`ReshapePwu` needs no change: its event types the field OPTIONAL."* **The entry contradicts itself inside three lines**, and REG-E-023 then correctly closed a residual that had never belonged to the instance it was filed under.
- **WHAT IS ACTUALLY OPEN ON INSTANCE 4 is the half nobody wrote down: RESOLUTION.** `reshapePwu` omits rather than fabricates (`pwu.ts:487`) — which is right, and is why the contract half was never the question — but it **never loads the object the id names**, while `invalidatePwu` twenty lines below performs both a load and a status check (`pwu.ts:528`, `:537`), and `buildConstraintInput` resolves its analogue. So a caller-supplied provenance reference would enter a governed event unexamined. **Its severity is bounded by population, not by a guard**: no call site populates the field. The shipped code says so itself — RPH-ASM-004's `why` (`enforcement-register.ts:3087-3094`) calls it *"the REG-F-014 shape a third time."*
- **⚠ AND THE GUARD THIS ENTRY IS NAMED FOR CARRIES A DISJUNCT THAT CANNOT BE TRUE.** REG-F-014 quotes it accurately: `authorityHeld = authority?.actorType === 'HUMAN' || authority?.actorType === 'SYSTEM'` (`governance.ts:257`). **`ActorTypeSchema` has no `SYSTEM`** (`enums.ts:7-13`: HUMAN, AGENT, MODEL, SERVICE, POLICY_ENGINE, EXTERNAL_SYSTEM), and `authority` is schema-validated as an `ActorReference`. **The second disjunct has been unsatisfiable since the enum was minted**, so the operative rule is and always has been HUMAN-only.
  - **It is not harmless, and that is why it is fixed rather than noted.** It is a **latent fail-open pointed at the future**: the day `SYSTEM` is added to `ActorTypeSchema` for any unrelated reason, a SYSTEM actor silently acquires the authority to make a governance decision EFFECTIVE, with nobody having decided that. A dead branch that grants a permission is a decision deferred to whoever next edits an enum.
  - **Removed, with a control that reddens on the addition rather than on the grant.** `decision-authority-provenance.test.ts` now pins `ActorTypeSchema`'s membership, so minting `SYSTEM` fails the suite and forces the question — *may a non-human hold decision authority?* — at the moment it becomes answerable. **The predicted red was named and observed:** adding `'SYSTEM'` to the enum reddens exactly that test and nothing else.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `handlers/governance.ts` (dead disjunct removed, control landed). This register — REG-F-014's heading, arithmetic, and residue. **Status:** **CLOSED as to the survey** (three of five instances closed; the rule is landed and reusable). The residue is carried forward and is no longer this entry's: **instance 4's resolution half** stays OPEN here; **instance 5** becomes **REG-F-041**; **the delegation question** becomes **REG-E-027**.

### REG-F-041 — Recomposition is composable-by-default, and the authoring layer says so in as many words: five inputs decide it, not one is independent of the caller, and a payload with no fields at all reaches "parent satisfied"

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 (every number below measured against the code and by running the repository's own tests) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES.
- **ORIGIN AND WHY IT IS ITS OWN ENTRY.** This is REG-F-014's instance 5, filed separately **on that entry's own instruction**: *"NOT fixable by resolution… closing it means the engine DETECTING conflicts, which is a **capability rather than a check**… **It should not be sequenced with the other four.**"* Folding it into a re-titled REG-F-014 — which is what I twice proposed — would have done exactly the sequencing the entry told itself not to do, filing a missing capability under a survey of misplaced trust. Everything below is **worse than instance 5 states**.
- **⚠ THE FULLY-DEFAULTED PATH REACHES PARENT-SATISFIED, and the repository's own passing test is the demonstration.** `recomposition.test.ts:162` proposes with **zero required children** and completes with a payload carrying nothing but the claim id → **COMPOSABLE**, `parentSatisfied: true`, `RecompositionCompleted`. **Nothing confines that shape to a test:** `requiredChildWorkUnitIds` is `z.array(z.string())` with **no `.min(1)`** on either schema (`messages.ts:422`, `objects.ts:648`), and `proposeRecomposition` (`decomposition.ts:476`) checks **only that the PARENT exists** — never that the children exist, are non-empty, or correspond to the paired DecompositionContract. The test is a legitimate discriminating control for the conflict cases **and simultaneously a live exhibit of the open path**; it is not an arrangement someone had to construct.
- **FIVE INPUTS DECIDE §14.1's PRECEDENCE, AND NOT ONE IS INDEPENDENT OF THE CALLER.** `RecompositionInput` has five fields (`rph-domain/src/decomposition.ts:266-274`); `buildRecompositionInput` populates all five (`decomposition.ts:580-590`):
  1. **`detectedConflicts`** — payload, `.optional()`, `?? []`. Rung 1 (`if (input.detectedConflicts.length > 0)`) is the **only** producer of CONFLICTED in the kernel. Omit it and CONFLICTED is unreachable.
  2. **`parentCompletionClaimSupported`** — payload, `.optional()`, `?? true`. Rung 3 skipped.
  3. **`parentConstraintsHoldAgainstWhole`** — payload, `.optional()`, `?? true`. Rung 3 skipped. **This one is set `false` nowhere in the repository**, so the INSUFFICIENT limb it drives has never executed; its sibling's `false` case *is* tested.
  4. **`conflictResolutionRules`** — read from the CONTRACT, which looks like independence and is not: the contract took it from **`ProposeRecomposition`'s payload** (`decomposition.ts:491`), whose sole writer is the caller. One command earlier is still the caller.
  5. **`requiredChildResults`** — the only input read from committed state, and derived **only in the weak sense**. `acceptable` reads each child's stored `assuranceState`; that field is written by the same caller's `ChangePwuState`, whose backing check `rejectUnbackedDisposition` requires **one** cited assessment naming the PWU and **never composes over the PWU's required policies** — disclosed in the shipped enforcement register as **RPH-ASR-012**, with an `OBSERVED_ADMISSION` that four SATISFIED and one REJECTED yields an asserted SATISFIED. **The builder's own comment says *"the caller cannot fake it."* The caller cannot fake it in the SAME command.**
- **⚠ AND THE FAIL-OPEN IS AUTHORED AND DECLARED, WHICH CHANGES WHAT THIS FINDING IS.** `m3-commands-events.json:1695`, verbatim: *"Defaults to true (**preserves prior composable-by-default**; a false forces INSUFFICIENT)."* All three payload fields carry `AUTHORED W1 G1 C1`. **This is not an overlooked check — it is a stated design property, written in the authoring layer, that contradicts the ratified rule the same note cites one clause earlier (§14.1 / RPH-DEC-006).** The canon anchor that rule carries is *"'All children completed, therefore parent satisfied' is always invalid"*, and DOC-003 §6 DEC-6 is *"Recomposition is a judged act, never a sum."* **A default whose justification is that it preserves the prior behaviour is preserving precisely the behaviour the rule exists to remove.** A finding against an authored intent is a different act from a finding against an oversight, and this is the former.
- **THERE IS NOTHING TO DETECT DURING, which is why REG-F-014 was right to call this a capability.** `aggregationRules` — the contract field declaring **how child outputs combine** — is written at propose and **read by zero `.ts` files repository-wide**. Recomposition never touches a child's outputs, artifacts, or constraints at all; it reads **one scalar per child**. A conflict detector has no aggregation step to run inside, and `TENANT_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` / `OFFLINE_AUDIT_CONFLICT` — the conflict types RPH-DEC-006 names — appear only in vocab and test files. **Every conflict that has ever reached the kernel was typed by a test author.**
- **AND THE CONFLICTS ARE NOT KEPT.** `RecompositionContractSchema` has no `detectedConflicts` field, so a later reader of a CONFLICTED contract cannot recover **why**; the list survives only inside the emitted event, and nothing consumes `RecompositionConflictDetected` — `rph-projections` has zero matches for `Recomposition`, including its tests.
- **WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED, stated so the finding is not read wider than it is.** The CONFLICTED routing itself is **correct and proved**: a non-empty list forces CONFLICTED even when every child is individually SATISFIED, which is the recomposition analog of property P1 and is exactly what §14.1 requires. Child acceptability is genuinely read from the store and its per-child computation fails **closed**. **The defect is the population, not the logic** — REG-F-022's Gate A shape, on the input that decides whether a recomposition conflicts at all.
- **Disposition:** **RECORDED, NOT FIXED**, and the reason is not deferral. A detector needs an aggregation step that does not exist and a ratified answer to *what a conflict IS over child outputs* — filed as **REG-E-028**. **What could be done without any ratification, and is deliberately not done here:** making the three payload fields **REQUIRED** would force a caller to STATE a judgement instead of inheriting one — the `GrantWaiver.effectiveAt` and `parentCompletionClaimId` medicine, and the shapes are AUTHORED so they are ours to change. It is not taken unilaterally because it **refuses every existing caller**, and because the note declaring composable-by-default is an authored decision that deserves an explicit reversal rather than a quiet one.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `handlers/decomposition.ts`, `rph-domain/src/decomposition.ts`, and the three `m3-commands-events.json` field notes, none of which change until REG-E-028 rules. **Status:** OPEN.
- **⚠ CITATION CORRECTED (same day).** This entry cited the judged-act rule as ~~*"DOC-003 §6 DEC-6"*~~; **DEC-6 is JPWB-DOC-003 §7** (*Decomposition and recomposition*). §6 contains the string `DEC-6` because **STA-4 CITES it** — so a grep for the rule's name lands in a section that does not contain the rule, which is the same trap as REG-F-030's heading and is why §N is never inferred from a hit. The substance is unaffected; **what the correct section then yielded is not** — see **REG-F-042**, which reads DEC-6 limb by limb and finds this entry understated the gap.

### REG-F-042 — DEC-6 names NINE checks for recomposition and the engine derives ONE, and that one admits a disposition two ratified rules exclude by name

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — and unlike REG-F-041, which is about inputs the caller supplies, this is about a rule the engine implements **in the opposite direction from the ratified text**.
- **HOW IT WAS FOUND, because the route matters more than the finding.** REG-F-041 cited DEC-6 as *"DOC-003 §6"*. It is **§7**; §6 merely contains the string because STA-4 cites it. Reading the rule **at its real location** — something no previous pass had done, having grepped the name and stopped — produced everything below. **A mis-citation does not merely misdirect a reader; it stops the author from ever reading the rule.**
- **⚠ THE ENGINE ADMITS `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` CHILDREN. TWO RATIFIED RULES EXCLUDE THEM BY NAME.**
  - **DEC-6, verbatim:** *"'Assured required child results' **excludes conditionally satisfied dispositions**: a conditionally satisfied child contributes to recomposition or promotion **only through an explicit, policy-permitted act that closes its condition** (STA-4, ASR-9)."*
  - **STA-4, verbatim:** *"conditionally satisfied work **cannot enter recomposition** or baseline promotion (DEC-6, ASR-9)."*
  - **The engine:** `const ACCEPTABLE_CHILD_ASSURANCE = new Set(['SATISFIED', 'CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED', 'WAIVED']);` (`handlers/decomposition.ts:554`), consulted per child at `:575`. **There is no check for the closing act anywhere.** A conditionally satisfied child contributes to its parent's recomposition unconditionally — the exact inverse of a rule stated twice, in two sections, each citing the other.
  - **`WAIVED` is NOT part of this finding and is stated fairly:** a waiver is a §12 ratified, scoped, authority-bearing act, so a WAIVED child plausibly IS *"an explicit, policy-permitted act."* `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` is the opposite case — the condition is by definition **still open**.
  - **AND THE COMMENT ABOVE THE LINE CITES ITS AUTHORITY AS §14.1**, a different document's section, which is how a set that contradicts DOC-003 reads as sourced. Two canon documents, one predicate, and only the weaker citation was ever followed.
- **THE ARITHMETIC OF THE RULE, since DEC-6 enumerates its checks.** Verbatim: *"Recomposition checks required child state and assurance, unresolved findings, contradictions among child outputs, obligation coverage, constraint preservation, artifact/interface compatibility, integration evidence, child-evidence support for the parent claim, and parent-level fitness with residual uncertainty."* **Nine.** The engine has four inputs for them: child assurance (derived, and wrong per above), contradictions (caller), constraint preservation (caller boolean, defaults true), parent-claim support (caller boolean, defaults true). **Unresolved findings, obligation coverage, artifact/interface compatibility, integration evidence, and parent-level fitness have no input at all** — they are not weakly checked, they are absent.
- **AND THE MODAL IS LOAD-BEARING, which reframes REG-F-041's own conclusion.** DEC-6's valid inference ends *"→ parent **may** be satisfied"* — italicised in the ratified text — and the rule closes *"a recomposed result requires an explicit assessment."* So the corpus does **not** say the four conjuncts entail satisfaction; it says they license a **candidacy** that an assessment must then settle. **The engine's state machine models this correctly** — `COMPOSABLE` is the candidacy and `SATISFIED` is the settled outcome — which is a better design than either REG-F-041 or I credited it with. What is wrong is that **the arrow between them is never driven**: see **REG-F-043**.
- **Disposition:** **RECORDED, and the `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` limb is the one thing here that is a straightforward repository fix** — remove it from the set, or gate it behind the closing act DEC-6 names. Not taken in this entry because recomposition is entirely undispatched in production (REG-F-041), so the fix is unobservable and its test would be the only thing exercising it; sequencing it behind REG-F-044's lifecycle avoids landing a guard on a path nothing walks. **The other eight checks are not a fix at all** — they are the capability JPWB-DOC-001 §4 calls *"the defining capability that distinguishes a harness from a workflow engine."*
- **⚠ THE `CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` LIMB CLOSED 2026-08-07.** Removed from `ACCEPTABLE_CHILD_ASSURANCE`, which is now exactly `{SATISFIED, WAIVED}`. **WAIVED is kept on DEC-6's own exception clause** — a waiver is a ratified, scoped, authority-bearing act, plausibly the *"explicit, policy-permitted act that closes its condition"* the rule contemplates, where a conditional satisfaction is by definition still open. **Mutant: re-adding the value reddens two of four tests** — the named exclusion and the exact-membership pin — and leaves both controls green.
  - **⚠ AND THE COMMENT'S CITATION WAS THE MECHANISM THAT KEPT IT ALIVE.** It read *"§14.1 acceptable-child set"* — RPH-DOC-002, **historical material** (REG-F-049). **A set contradicting canon read as sourced because its citation pointed at the weaker document.** This is REG-F-049's harm made concrete: not an abstract mislabelling, a live fail-open that looked grounded.
  - **THE TEST IS THE WEAKER OF THE TWO AVAILABLE AND ITS HEADER SAYS SO.** The stronger drives a real child to CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED and asserts the recomposition lands INSUFFICIENT. Not written, for two disclosed reasons: reaching that disposition honestly needs a policy, an assessment and a MATERIAL finding — fabricating `assuranceState` directly is forbidden here, and `pwu.test.ts` records a test corrected for exactly that — and recomposition is undispatched in production, so the drive would exercise a path nothing walks. **What the pin buys is the future.**
- **Merge target:** Repository — `handlers/decomposition.ts` **LANDED**. **Status:** OPEN, NARROWED — DEC-6 enumerates NINE checks and **eight remain unimplemented**; those are a capability, not a fix.

### REG-F-043 — 152 of 333 declared transitions carry a guard, and no guard is ever evaluated: the ratified condition is a display string, and the arrow that would make recomposition a judgement is the exhibit

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — and it is the widest-scoped finding in this register, because the mechanism is one field on a table that every machine shares.
- **THE MEASUREMENT.** `packages/rph-domain/src/transitions.data.ts` declares **333 transitions across 26 machines**, of which **152 carry a `guard`**. `classifyTransition` (`stateMachine.ts:38-56`) reads `states`, `illegal`, `from` and `to`, **and never reads `guard`**. Censused repository-wide, the only readers of a transition's guard are `gen-transitions.ts:65` (the generator, serialising it), `pwu-behavior.ts:119` (a projection, re-emitting it as `guardDescription`), and `PwuBehaviorPanel.svelte` (CSS). **The field is transcribed, projected, rendered, and never consulted.**
  - **THE DEMO SAYS SO ON THE SCREEN.** `PwuBehaviorPanel` renders every guard under the literal label ***"Authoritative check, not evaluated:"***. The disclosure is honest and complete — and it means **152 ratified conditions are shipped to a user as prose beside a transition that will be permitted regardless.**
  - **THE ONE THAT IS NOT THIS, recorded so the finding is not read wider than it is:** `kit.ts:648`'s `args.guard?.(...)` is a **handler-supplied function** and is genuinely evaluated. Two different things share the word, and conflating them would turn a real finding into a false one.
- **⚠ THE EXHIBIT: `COMPOSABLE → SATISFIED` IS UNREACHABLE, AND ITS DECLARED TRIGGER NAMES A HANDLER THAT CANNOT DRIVE IT.** `transitions.data.ts:952-957` declares `from: 'COMPOSABLE', to: 'SATISFIED', trigger: 'completeRecomposition / RecompositionCompleted', guard: 'a recomposed result requires an explicit assessment (§14.1)'`. But `completeRecomposition` drives **EVALUATING → {COMPOSABLE | CONFLICTED | INSUFFICIENT}** and nothing else; `proposeRecomposition` mints at `READY` (so `DRAFT`, the declared initial state, is never occupied either) and `beginRecomposition` does READY → EVALUATING. **No handler drives COMPOSABLE → SATISFIED.** The trigger label attributes the arrow to a handler that provably moves a different edge — **REG-F-027's shape**, on the arrow that carries the §14.1 assessment requirement.
- **WHY THIS IS THE ANSWER TO "HOW DOES RECOMPOSITION BECOME A JUDGEMENT."** DEC-6 says the four conjuncts license *"parent **may** be satisfied"* and that *"a recomposed result requires an explicit assessment"* (REG-F-042). **The machine models exactly that** — `COMPOSABLE` is the candidacy, `SATISFIED` is the settled outcome, and the assessment requirement is written on the arrow between them. **So the corpus's modal was faithfully transcribed into the state machine and then never executed.** The design is right; the last arrow was never built, and the condition that would have made it a judgement was placed in the one field the engine does not read. *A rule written into the machine as a guard is a rule written where nothing looks.*
- **Disposition:** **RECORDED, NOT FIXED, and the scope is why.** Evaluating 152 guards is not a code change — most are prose conditions over facts the engine does not hold (this is the same wall as REG-F-042's eight unimplemented checks), and turning the field into a predicate means deciding, per guard, whether it is derivable, assessable, or a capability. **What is NOT deferred and belongs in the next pass:** the field should stop being called `guard`. It describes a condition; naming it `guard` invites every reader — including three passes of this programme — to assume something enforces it. **Renaming it `guardDescription` at the source, as the projection already does downstream, would make 152 disclosures honest at zero behavioural cost.**
- **Merge target:** Repository — `rph-domain/src/transitions.data.ts` + `gen-transitions.ts` (the field's name), and per-guard decisions thereafter. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-F-044 — The claim-assessment lifecycle was never built, and four findings I had been carrying separately are one hole: seven of eight ratified Claim states are unreachable and no command exists to reach them

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 (measured by RUNNING the shipped seeds and by live dispatch against the real engine, not by reading) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — a missing subsystem, disclosed here as the **common cause** of items filed separately.
- **HOW IT WAS FOUND: A HYPOTHESIS OF MINE, REFUTED.** I proposed that recomposition-as-judgement needed no new capability because it is *"already modelled one layer up, at the Claim"* — the Claim carries `supportingEvidenceIds` **and** `contradictingEvidenceIds`, has a `CONTESTED` state, and `CompleteRecomposition` already names one. **The operative limb of that hypothesis — *"assurance already assesses Claims"* — is false**, and the refutation is the entry.
- **THE MEASUREMENT, from the running engine.** Of **92 registered commands, exactly ONE targets a CLAIM aggregate**: `AssertClaim`, which hard-codes `status: 'OPEN'` into both the persisted state and the event (`assurance.ts:630`, `:637`, `:647`). `ClaimSupported` / `ClaimContested` / `ClaimRejected` have payload schemas and event-registry rows (`messages.ts:957-973`, `:2395-2397`) and **zero emitters**. Live dispatch of `SupportClaim` / `ContestClaim` / `RejectClaim` / `WaiveClaim` / `SupersedeClaim` / `ChangeClaimStatus` all return REJECTED — unknown command. **Seven of the eight ratified Claim states are occupied by nothing and reachable by nothing; only `OPEN` exists.**
  - **THE REFERENCE UNDERTAKING PROVES IT END TO END:** 332 events, **9 `ClaimAsserted` and no other Claim event**; all 9 claims finish `OPEN` with `supportingEvidenceIds: []` and `contradictingEvidenceIds: []`. **Fourteen assessments reached SATISFIED while citing a still-OPEN claim.** `completeAssuranceAssessment` never loads or writes a Claim.
  - **AND THE COMMANDS WERE NEVER RATIFIED, NOT MERELY NEVER BUILT.** A corpus-wide search for `supportClaim|contestClaim|rejectClaim|waiveClaim|assessClaim|changeClaimStatus` returns **zero**. RPH-DOC-002 §26.5 ratifies the three EVENTS with **no ratified command anywhere that emits them**. *This is the mirror of REG-F-037's arrow-with-no-trigger, at command granularity: an event with no command is a state the corpus can describe and no actor can cause.*
- **⚠ FOUR FINDINGS I HAD BEEN CARRYING AS SEPARATE ARE THIS ONE HOLE.**
  1. **REG-F-041** — recomposition takes a caller boolean because there is no assessed claim to consult.
  2. **RPH-EVD-001** — *"an agent's statement that work is done is inert until reified as a governed Claim"*, `UNENFORCED_DISCLOSED`. Reification has no mechanism: a Claim cannot be assessed.
  3. **`findContestedClaims`** (`rph-domain/src/governance.ts:301-311`) — a **live, ratified §15.2 kernel predicate wired into `canPromoteBaseline`**, emitting a `CONTESTED_CLAIM` finding. Its input is populated nowhere outside rph-domain's own tests, so **every production baseline promotion evaluates it against an always-undefined list.** Not "no consumer exists" — the consumer exists, is ratified, and is permanently unfed, which is strictly worse.
  4. **CT-10's baseline pull-guard** (`governance.ts:732-742`) — the one decision that reads `supportingEvidenceIds` traverses to **zero** evidence ids across both seeded promotions.
- **AND A RATIFIED RULE IS DOUBLY UNREACHABLE BECAUSE OF IT.** JPWB-DOC-003 §8.3 **ASR-8**, verbatim: *"When evidence is invalidated or expires, **every dependent supported claim becomes contested**, under review, or invalidated… Contradicting evidence remains attached and visible; **the record never self-curates toward support**."* `invalidateEvidence` records `affectedClaimIds` on the event and **mutates no claim**, so the cascade is absent — **and its antecedent requires a SUPPORTED claim, which cannot exist.** A rule unreachable at both ends.
- **WHY THIS IS FILED AS A FINDING AND NOT AN ELICITATION ITEM.** Nothing here needs a sponsor to decide anything: the states are ratified, the events are ratified, the predicate that consumes them is ratified and shipped. **What is missing is the commands that connect them, and the corpus never minted those** — so the honest next act is a ratification request for a claim-assessment command set, not an implementation. It is the same shape as **REG-E-027**'s hollow `AuthorityReference`: a fully specified governed record with no act that gives it a life.
- **Disposition:** RECORDED. **The cheap, honest, non-closing move available today** (measured, not proposed): requiring `CompleteRecomposition.parentCompletionClaimId` to **RESOLVE** costs 9 test failures in 3 files and moves nothing else — all 12 existing dispatches are in test files, production emits zero recomposition events, the seeds and demo are untouched. **It does not close the fail-open** — a caller satisfies it with one self-issued `AssertClaim`, and `AssertClaim` naming a nonexistent subject is ACCEPTED — so it converts an unresolvable reference into a resolvable-but-unassessed one. Worth doing as truth-in-record; worth **not** mistaking for a gate.
- **Merge target:** Repository — a claim-assessment command set, pending the ratification this entry requests. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-F-045 — Half the transition vocabulary says "RECONSTRUCTED" and the generator has no field to carry it, so 120 authored arrows ship wearing the same face as ratified ones

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 (found while grounding REG-D-024's build, by reading the Claim machine's vocab SOURCE rather than its generated output) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD_DIVERGES — the arrows may well be right; what is wrong is that nothing downstream can tell they were inferred.
- **THE MEASUREMENT.** `packages/rph-domain/vocab/m2-transitions.json` holds **240 transition rows**, of which **180 carry a `note`** and **120 are noted `RECONSTRUCTED`**. **Eighteen machines** carry a `sourceSection` that disclaims a ratified matrix. The `Claim.status` machine's reads byte-exact: *"core-model doc §15.1 enum (VERBATIM). Transitions **RECONSTRUCTED** from §26.5 events + §34.2 commands + §15.2/§35.1 invariants. **NO explicit matrix.**"*
- **AND THE GENERATOR CANNOT CARRY IT — NOT "DOES NOT", CANNOT.** `gen-transitions.ts`'s `transitionLit` emits exactly `from`, `to`, `trigger`, `guard`; `note` and `sourceSection` are dropped. **`TransitionSpec` has no field for either** (`transitions.data.ts:4-9`), so the loss is structural rather than an omission a careful editor would catch. **Half the vocabulary's honesty is deleted at the compile step.**
- **⚠ AND THE GENERATED HEADER ASSERTS THE OPPOSITE.** `transitions.data.ts:2`, verbatim: *"Source: vocab/m2-transitions.json (**grounded** from DOC-002 + DOC-004, **reconciled**)."* Two words that read as sourcing, at the top of a file where **half the rows are self-labelled inferences**. A reader who checks provenance reads that line, is satisfied, and stops — **the header is where the check ends, and it is the least accurate sentence in the file.**
- **WHY THIS IS THE GENERAL FORM OF SOMETHING THIS REGISTER KEEPS FINDING ONE INSTANCE AT A TIME.** REG-F-002 recorded vocabulary `sourceSection` as provenance theater. REG-F-034 found the §36 mapping had no basis field, so one ratified row and six authored ones were indistinguishable — **and the remedy there was to ADD a `basis` field.** The same remedy is available here and was never applied, because the §36 mapping was hand-written data while this is generated, so the missing field sits in a **generator's type** rather than in a table someone was editing. *Provenance survives exactly as far as the narrowest schema it passes through.*
- **THE PART THAT MAKES IT WORSE THAN A MISSING ANNOTATION.** These rows are not merely unannotated downstream — **they are annotated upstream and the annotation is destroyed.** Somebody did the honest thing 120 times and the build step threw it away. That is a stronger claim than *"provenance was never recorded"*, and it is checkable today: the disclaimers are sitting in the JSON.
- **AND IT ALREADY MISLED A CONFERRAL — MINE, HOURS EARLIER.** **REG-D-024** named *"the three ratified event payloads"* as a fixed point the build must satisfy. They are **AUTHORED**: RPH-DOC-007 contains **zero** occurrences of `ClaimSupported` / `ClaimContested` / `ClaimRejected`, §26.5 is a bare name list, and all three vocab rows carry *"UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED… **Do NOT treat this sourceSection as proof the shape is ratified.** Ratification pending."* **A conferral whose own text warns against authored guesses acquiring borrowed authority acquired one — from the exact mechanism it was warning about, in the same hour, because I read the generated form.**
- **Disposition:** RECORDED, with the remedy identified and deliberately NOT bundled into REG-D-024's build: add `note` / `sourceSection` to `TransitionSpec`, emit them, and add a census asserting that **every row whose vocab note says RECONSTRUCTED arrives carrying it** — the REG-F-034 medicine, one layer earlier. Held back because it regenerates a file 26 machines read, and REG-D-024's build must not be entangled with a whole-repository regeneration. **What IS done: the design record for that build treats all 15 Claim arrows as authored and says so at every site.**
- **Merge target:** Repository — `transitions.data.ts`'s spec types, `gen/gen-transitions.ts`, and a provenance census in `verif/`. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-F-046 — I blocked an increment on a rule I had authored, cited it from my own commit message, and never measured it — and the ratified rule it stood in for forbids the shipped precedent I would have reached for

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** METHOD + CODE_DIVERGES — two findings that only make sense together.
- **⚠ THE METHOD HALF.** Commits `b40220bd` and `d48446b6` both stopped REG-D-024's INC-4 *"because a commit writes ONE aggregate."* Measured:
  - **True of `CommitInput`** — singular `aggregateId`/`objectType`/`expectedRevision`, one object row written.
  - **FALSE of a DISPATCH.** `CommitInput.events` is `DomainEvent[]` and the adapter's insert loop writes each event's **own** `aggregateId` with **no check** that it matches the commit's. `alsoEvents` already relies on this.
  - **AND A SHIPPED HANDLER ALREADY WRITES TWO AGGREGATES IN ONE COMMAND** — `withPwaVersionBump` (`pwa-authoring.ts:182`, three production call sites) wraps two `commitState` calls in `ctx.store.transaction`, deriving the second's ids as `${commandId}#pwa-version`, with a passing test.
  - **It is ratified nowhere.** The nearest written statement of it is **mine**, in `docs/_working/DESIGN-validator-health.md:71` — *"Every command in this engine targets one aggregate, and a handler that silently mutated a second one would put a governance fact somewhere no command names."* That sentence is false of the repository it describes.
  - **THE SHAPE, and it is the inverse of this register's usual one.** The recurring defect here is an authored claim acquiring the authority to PERMIT something. This one acquired the authority to **FORBID**, which is quieter: a fabricated permission eventually meets a test, and a fabricated prohibition just stops work and looks like rigour. *I cited my own commit message as a constraint.*
- **THE CODE HALF, and it is why the method half matters rather than merely embarrassing me.** There **is** a ratified rule on this ground, it is stricter, and I never cited it. **JPWB-DOC-003 §4 AGG-1**, verbatim: *"One aggregate never directly mutates another's internal state. Cross-aggregate flows use Commands, Events, durable processes, compensation, and reconciliation — not direct table mutation, and **not one broad transaction constructed to simulate workflow atomicity**."* Its **WHY**: *"simulated atomicity hides partial failure instead of representing it."*
  - **⚠ SO `withPwaVersionBump` VIOLATES AGG-1 BY NAME.** It is a broad transaction constructed to simulate workflow atomicity across two aggregates, at three production call sites, shipped and adversarially reviewed. **Had my false blocker not stopped me, the precedent I would have followed is itself the divergence** — which is the only reason the wrong rule produced the right pause.
  - **AND RPH-DOC-009 §30.2 NAMES THE REMEDY** the repository does not have, verbatim: *"Do not update several aggregates in one broad transaction merely to simulate Execution Workflow atomicity. Use: domain event; **saga/controller**; compensating actions; explicit intermediate states."* Four mechanisms; this repository has none of them.
- **AND THE RULE CANNOT BE APPLIED UNTIL A PRIOR QUESTION IS SETTLED — filed as REG-E-029.** JPWB-DOC-003 §4 names **five principal aggregates**, with **Claim OWNED by Assurance** and Evidence merely *referenced*. The runtime has **~30 aggregate roots, one per object type**. **Under the ratified map an Evidence→Claim cascade is INTRA-aggregate and AGG-1 does not bite at all; under the runtime's map it needs a saga.** The same ratified rule gives opposite answers depending on which map is real, and nothing decides.
- **Disposition:** RECORDED. INC-4 remains undelivered, **for a different and better reason than the one I gave**: not that the engine cannot, but that the ratified mechanism is a tier that does not exist and the alternatives are each disqualified by ratified text or defeated by skippability (a follow-on command is skippable by the caller, and an atomic batch only relocates the skip to batch assembly). Full analysis: `docs/_working/DESIGN-asr8-cascade.md`. **`withPwaVersionBump`'s AGG-1 divergence is NOT fixed here** — it is three live call sites and its remedy is the same missing tier.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `pwa-authoring.ts` (AGG-1), and a saga tier pending REG-E-029/030. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-F-047 — The engine reads the acting principal from the command payload, which a ratified rule forbids in as many words — and I recorded that divergence as an ALLOCATION, in the entry that depends on it most

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — and it is the substrate under REG-F-014's entire family, so it is filed above them rather than beside them.
- **THE RATIFIED RULE, byte-exact.** JPWB-DOC-004 **§5 Implementation discipline**: *"Validate external input strictly at trust boundaries; **derive tenant and principal context from authenticated context, never from a payload's claim about itself**."* And JPWB-DOC-002 **§1 The product family ladder**: the Janumi Platform *"supplies machinery — infrastructure, runtime, **identity**, services, controls — never domain semantics."*
- **THE CODE.** `issuedBy: ActorReferenceSchema` is a field of the command envelope, written by the caller. `Engine.dispatch` runs identity-presence, envelope schema, payload hash, idempotency, payload schema, handler — **and no authentication stage**. So the acting principal IS *"a payload's claim about itself"*, which is the one thing §5 names and forbids.
- **⚠ AND I WROTE THE OPPOSITE INTO THE RECORD, TWICE, IN THE ENTRY THAT RESTS ON IT.** REG-F-014's scope paragraph reads: *"This engine has no authentication layer, so `command.issuedBy` is caller-supplied too… Full verifiability needs the platform tier the Charter allocates elsewhere."* **I read a DIVERGENCE as an ALLOCATION.** The corpus does not allocate identity *away* from the engine; DOC-002 §1 allocates identity to the Platform **as machinery the engine must CONSUME**, and DOC-004 §5 then commands the engine to derive from it. *"The platform tier the Charter allocates elsewhere"* is a sentence I composed, and every subsequent *"this makes authority consistent, not verifiable"* caveat inherits its false premise — including the one in REG-F-040 written today.
  - **THE SHAPE, and it is REG-F-046's exactly one week later:** an authored sentence of mine acquiring the standing of a rule, in the direction of **excusing** rather than forbidding. A fabricated prohibition stops work and looks like rigour; a fabricated **allocation** stops *inquiry* and looks like architecture. Both are quiet because neither meets a test.
- **AND IT IS NOT A FUTURE-TIER GAP — SPONSOR EVIDENCE, 2026-08-06.** The sponsor states there are **three configurations — standalone, SaaS, Enterprise** — that *"the standalone will have human and agent"* identity and the other two *"will exist in larger organizations"*, and, asked directly, that ***"standalone can have an authenticated identity."*** **So there is NO configuration in which reading the actor from the payload is correct.** The divergence is not deferred by deployment; it is wrong in every shipped configuration, and one uniform remedy serves all three.
- **THE RATIFIED SHAPE ALREADY SPANS THEM, AND HAS BEEN INERT THE WHOLE TIME.** `AuthorityTypeSchema` (from RPH-DOC-002 §5) is `USER | ORGANIZATIONAL_ROLE | POLICY | LEGAL_REQUIREMENT | SYSTEM_OWNER | DELEGATED_AGENT` — `USER` is standalone, `ORGANIZATIONAL_ROLE` is what *"exists in larger organizations"* means, `POLICY`/`LEGAL_REQUIREMENT` are the enterprise compliance cases, and `DELEGATED_AGENT`/`SYSTEM_OWNER` are the coordinator case. **One shape, three sources of authority, no per-configuration branch** — and not one of `AuthorityReference`'s six fields is read anywhere (REG-E-027).
- **WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLAIM.** It does not claim the engine must implement authentication — DOC-002 §1 puts that in the Platform. It claims the engine must **consume** a principal rather than accept an assertion, and that the current shape makes the ratified §5 rule unsatisfiable by construction: there is no parameter for an authenticated principal to arrive on.
- **Disposition:** RECORDED, not fixed — the remedy is a trust-boundary parameter on dispatch and is a design in its own right, not an increment. **What IS corrected here is the record:** REG-F-014's *"the platform tier the Charter allocates elsewhere"* is struck as my own composition, and every entry citing it inherits this correction.
- **Merge target:** Repository — the command envelope and `Engine.dispatch`'s trust boundary. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-F-048 — Any actor can revoke a human-authorized EFFECTIVE decision: the engine guards the MAKING of authority and not its UNMAKING

- **Date:** 2026-08-06 (measured end-to-end through `Engine.dispatch`, then confirmed by reading the handler) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** CODE_DIVERGES — an authority bypass, live today, and independent of every open design question above it.
- **THE OBSERVED SEQUENCE.** A **HUMAN** proposes a decision and approves it → `EFFECTIVE`. An **AGENT** then issues `RevokeDecision` → **ACCEPTED**, status `REVOKED`. The human-authorized decision is undone by an actor that could not have made it.
- **THE MECHANISM, and it is an absence rather than a mistake.** `revokeDecision` (`handlers/governance.ts:436`) is `advanceStatus(...)` with `precondition: fromStates('EFFECTIVE')` **and no `guard` at all.** Its siblings twenty lines away both check: `ApproveDecision` and `GrantWaiver` route through `makeDecisionEffective`, whose guard computes `authorityHeld` and refuses an AGENT with `RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT`. **Three commands on one aggregate; the two that CONFER authority are guarded and the one that WITHDRAWS it is not.**
- **THE CONTROL FIRED, so this is a measurement and not a blind instrument.** The **same** AGENT actor, on the **same** engine, in the **same** session, was refused `ApproveDecision` and `GrantWaiver` with `RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT`. So the harness demonstrably detects an authority refusal; its silence on revocation is a fact about the handler.
