- **AND CANON TREATS REVOCATION AS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL OF THE THREE.** ASR-15 names revoking a decision as an act that **triggers impact analysis** — the one governance act whose effects the corpus says must propagate. **So the act canon treats as consequential enough to cascade is the one act issuable by anybody** — and *the cascade does not happen either*: this register already records that `assessDecisionRevocation` ignores its argument and is a constant function. **Unguarded going in, inert coming out.**
- **WHY IT SURVIVED, stated because it generalises.** REG-F-014's survey enumerated the sites where an agent could **manufacture** authority and closed them one by one. It never asked the mirror question — where an agent can **destroy** authority — because the finding's own title framed the family as manufacture. *A survey inherits the blind spot of the sentence that names it.*
- **Disposition:** RECORDED, not fixed in this entry, because the right guard is a ratification question and not a repository one: canon says an approval requires authority and says nothing about whether **revoking** one requires the same authority, a **greater** one, or the authority of the original approver. Guessing would encode a convenient interpretation on a governance act. **Filed as REG-E-031.** *The interim asymmetry is disclosed rather than patched: a system that refuses an agent's approval and accepts its revocation is not half-governed, it is differently governed, and the difference favours the agent.*
- **Merge target:** Repository — `handlers/governance.ts` (`revokeDecision`). **Status:** **CLOSED 2026-08-06.** REG-E-031 was dispositioned SYMMETRIC and the guard landed the same day: `revokeDecision` now refuses a non-HUMAN issuer with `RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT`. **Two mutants, each isolating one test:** deleting the guard reddens only *"an AGENT cannot revoke a decision a HUMAN authorized"* — i.e. it reproduces exactly what shipped until today — and making it unconditional reddens only the CONTROL. A third test asserts the symmetry as ONE proposition, so neither half can quietly re-open without the other noticing. **The refusal message names the escalation path** (contest the claim, record a blocking observation, move the work to AT_RISK), because a refusal that does not say what the refused actor may do instead invites the workaround it was written to prevent.

### REG-F-049 — The RPH source documents are HISTORICAL MATERIAL by the Constitution's own registry, and I have cited them as ratified all week

- **Date:** 2026-08-07 (found while grounding INC-B; every citation below verified against the constitution's registry clause) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** RECORD_DIVERGES — the findings mostly survive; **their stated authority does not.**
- **THE REGISTRY CLAUSE, byte-exact.** JPWB-CON-000 **B1 — The canon**: *"The recognized corpus is exactly: JPWB-CON-000, JPWB-DOC-001, JPWB-DOC-002, JPWB-DOC-003, JPWB-DOC-004, JPWB-REG-005; the **JPWB-SPEC-nnn series**…; **program working references**…; plus the repository's generated contracts, schemas, migrations, and conformance tests as shape authority. **Nothing else governs. A document not in this registry — whatever its title or voice — is historical material.**"* And **REG-D-010**: *"from this moment every document outside the registry is historical material — the pre-canon corpus is **quarantined by recognition**."*
- **AND THE RPH DOCUMENTS SAY IT THEMSELVES.** RPH-DOC-007's own status block reads **`Status: Initial implementation baseline`**, **`Contract version: 0.1.0`**. It never claimed ratification; I supplied that.
- **⚠ WHAT I CITED AS RATIFIED THIS WEEK, AND WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS.** RPH-DOC-002 §27.2 (the ten command-handler obligations, *"authenticate actor"* first), RPH-DOC-002 §14.1 and §26.5, RPH-DOC-002 §5 (`AuthorityReference`), RPH-DOC-004 §4.1 (`ValidatorContract`), RPH-DOC-008 §13 RPH-EVD-002 (*"the command is rejected"*), RPH-DOC-009 §30.2 (*"domain event; saga/controller; compensating actions"*). **Every one of these I introduced with the word "ratified", in register entries, design records, commit messages and code comments.** They are historical material.
- **THE FINDINGS SURVIVE; MOST OF THEM HAVE A CANON TWIN AND I CITED THE WRONG ONE.** ASR-8 is **JPWB-DOC-003 §8.3**, not RPH prose. AGG-1 is **§4**. DEC-6 is **§7**. And the obligation INC-B rests on is **PER-9** — *"Every material act in the stream retains identity, scope, actor and provenance, **correlation and causation**…"* — with **PER-2** requiring events preserve *"causal relationships"*. **So the substance was ratified all along and I reached for the weaker citation**, which is the more embarrassing error: not inventing authority, but failing to find the real one while standing next to it.
- **WHAT IS GENUINELY WEAKENED, and it must be said plainly.** Rules with **no** canon twin now rest on historical material: §27.2's ten-step sequence, §30.2's four-mechanism list, and RPH-EVD-002's *"the command is rejected"* — which is the text I used to settle the COMMANDED-vs-DERIVED question in REG-D-024. **That ruling's second leg (PER-3, canon) still carries it**, so the conclusion holds on one leg rather than two; recorded rather than quietly re-justified.
- **THE PRECISE STATUS, because it is not simply "worthless".** B1 makes *"the repository's generated contracts, schemas, migrations, and conformance tests"* **shape authority**. So a field transcribed from an RPH document into `packages/rph-contracts` **is** authoritative — as shape. **The FIELD `causationId` is authoritative; the SENTENCE defining it is historical.** REG-D-008 separately holds RPH-DOC-007 for a *"shape-survivorship audit"*, which is the mechanism for promoting those shapes rather than assuming them.
- **THIS IS THE SECOND RECORDED INSTANCE OF EXACTLY THIS ERROR, AND THE REGISTER ALREADY HELD THE FIRST.** REG-D-024 named *"the three ratified event payloads"* and they were `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED` — corrected on 2026-08-06 with the observation that *"an authored guess acquires borrowed authority."* **One day later I did it again at a whole-document scale.** The first instance was a shape inside a document; this is the document. *Correcting an instance does not correct the habit, and a register that catches the same error twice in two days is describing a method problem, not two accidents.*
- **Disposition:** RECORDED. **A citation audit is OWED and is not attempted here** — it spans roughly a dozen entries filed 2026-08-05 to 2026-08-07 — because doing it hastily is how the error propagates a third time. **The rule adopted going forward, and applied in INC-B's code and tests already: cite canon or cite the repository's shape authority; if the only source is an RPH document, say "historical material" in the same sentence.**
- **⚠ AUDIT COMPLETED 2026-08-07, AND IT CORRECTS THIS ENTRY'S OWN FRAMING.** 42 citations across ten entries, each dispositioned rather than blanket-relabelled: **16 SOUND AS WRITTEN · 12 SUBSTANCE SURVIVES ON A CANON TWIN · 10 RELABEL ONLY · 3 WEAKENED (no twin) · 1 FINDING INVALIDATED.** **38 of 42 survive.** The alarm was proportionate to the habit and disproportionate to the damage.
  - **⚠ THE FRAMING ERROR, AND IT IS MINE: CANON DELIBERATELY DOES NOT CARRY SHAPES, SO "NO CANON TWIN FOR A SHAPE" IS THE DESIGN AND NOT A WEAKNESS.** JPWB-CON-000 **AX-10**: *"Exact shapes belong to the repository (Part B, clause 3); the canon states meaning and defers."* JPWB-DOC-004 §10 item 6: *"**Never restate shapes.** State the semantic requirement and cede the exact shape to the repository."* This entry treated a missing twin as a demotion in every case; **for shapes it is a ratified cession.**
  - **AND REG-D-008 CANONICALLY AUTHORIZES THE PRACTICE I RAISED THE ALARM ABOUT — NAMING THE FINDING THAT MOTIVATED IT.** Byte-exact: *"Because the source documents' field-level schemas are what made implementation impoverishment detectable (**REG-F-005**), the cession of shapes to the repository is **conditional and verified**."* **Reading an RPH schema as the independent "expected" against which the implementation is measured is sponsor-directed method, not unauthorized reliance.**
  - **SO THE RULE IS SHARPER THAN THIS ENTRY STATED IT.** Not *"RPH citations are suspect."* It is: **an RPH SCHEMA used as an expected shape is canon-authorized (REG-D-008 + AX-10); an RPH SENTENCE used as a ratified obligation is the error.** Shape versus prose — and I collapsed the two.
  - **THE EXEMPLAR OF DOING IT RIGHT WAS ALREADY IN THE REGISTER: REG-F-045**, where every RPH citation is a demotion or an absence proof and none is an authority claim. It scored SOUND AS WRITTEN on all five.
  - **THE REAL DAMAGE IS TWO ENTRIES, NOT TEN** — REG-F-037 and REG-F-038, corrected in their own entries below.
- **Merge target:** This register — **audit COMPLETE**; the design record was corrected at `3f70bcfe` (9/9 citations labelled). **Status:** **CLOSED**, superseding its own over-broad framing per the shape/prose distinction above.

### REG-F-050 — My remedy for the concurrency gap was a gate that could not fail, and canon had already ruled the opposite arrangement — naming the obligation, the check, the census and the mutant

- **Date:** 2026-08-07 (found by grounding INC-C before building it, per REG-D-025) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** METHOD — the diagnosis was right and the remedy was its opposite, in the same document, four lines apart.
- **⚠ THE INCOHERENCE.** `DESIGN-accountability-substrate.md` §1.4 diagnoses correctly and **caller-side**: *"the check runs only `if (command.expectedRevision !== undefined)`; **zero of the production command-producing sites set it.** So the ratified concurrency contract is inert by default — a caller opts IN to being protected."* Its sequence item 3 then prescribes: *"**`expectedRevision` required** where **the engine** can supply it."* **The diagnosis says callers do not set it; the remedy says let the engine set it. Those are opposites.**
- **AND THE REMEDY WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A GATE THAT CANNOT FAIL.** `loadOrReject` reads the object once (`kit.ts:126`) and the handler-layer check compares `command.expectedRevision` against **that same read** (`:143`). An engine that filled the field from the store would be filling it from the only read available to it, so the conjunct becomes `existing.revision !== existing.revision` — **`x !== x`.** This is REG-F-015's shape exactly (*"the conjunct compares a thing with itself… nothing can ask it a question it could get wrong"*), one layer lower and **without** the redundant second enforcement that saved the original. It would have closed the finding and protected nothing — CON-000 B7's *"no assurance represented by an unread flag."*
- **PER-4 SAYS WHO, AND I QUOTED ONE SENTENCE OF FOUR.** Byte-exact, JPWB-DOC-003 §9 (heading-verified): *"Updates to existing aggregates declare the revision they believe current. **On conflict, the command is rejected; the caller reloads current state and re-forms intent;** the system never silently overwrites and never silently retries with stale business assumptions."* **SCOPE/NON-EXAMPLE:** *"Command types explicitly exempted by contract (**pure creations**) need no expected revision."* The remedy names **the caller**, and the create exemption I would have had to invent is already ratified.
- **⚠ AND CANON HAS ALREADY RULED THE WHOLE INCREMENT, INCLUDING ITS VERIFICATION.** **JPWB-SPEC-001 §11.4.22 FORK-22** — ratified by **REG-D-023**, which states *"every `SHALL` in the document is now an obligation rather than a proposal"* — binds the **surface**: *"**SHALL** set `expectedRevision` on every state-changing Command issued from a Projection whose `freshness` is not `FRESH`, to the `derivedAtSubjectRevision` the Projection recorded for that subject. **This obligation is not satisfied today and is the operative content of this ruling.**"* It further names the check **`SPEC-001-FX-FRESH-18`**, states the assertion so it does not rest on an identifier (dispatch from a projection derived at *r* against a subject at *r+1* ⇒ `CONFLICT` / `RPH_REVISION_CONFLICT`), requires a **census** over `apps/rph-demo/src/lib/server/workbench.ts`, and requires a **red-proof mutant** removing `expectedRevision` from the envelope literal.
  - `derivedAtSubjectRevision` is a **caller-side** value captured when the projection was derived — *the revision the professional believed current*. **That is PER-4's phrase performed**, and it is the one value an engine cannot fabricate.
  - **MEASURED: zero implementation.** `freshness`, `derivedAtSubjectRevision` and `FX-FRESH-18` return no hits across `apps/rph-demo/src` and `packages/rph-projections/src`. SPEC-001's self-assessment is accurate.
- **AND THE STORE-LEVEL CAS CANNOT FIRE EITHER, for a different reason worth recording.** `commit()` re-reads the revision inside its own transaction, so a read-to-commit window formally exists — but nothing can enter it: handlers are synchronous (`CommandHandler` returns `CommandResult`, no Promise; zero `async` in `kit.ts`), the driver is synchronous better-sqlite3, and the only file-backed opener is a per-process singleton in the demo. **So the protection that exists is unreachable, and the protection that is ratified is unbuilt.**
- **THE GENERAL FORM, and it is the third distinct variety of the same failure this week.** REG-F-046: an authored **prohibition** acquired the standing of a rule and stopped work. REG-F-049: authored **authority** — historical documents cited as ratified. **This is an authored REMEDY**, which is the most dangerous of the three, because a wrong prohibition merely blocks and a wrong citation merely misattributes, while *a wrong remedy ships*. **It would have been green, gated, committed, and inert.**
- **Disposition:** RECORDED; the design record is corrected in the same commit and **INC-C is re-scoped to implementing SPEC-001 §11.4.22 FORK-22** rather than to anything of mine. The increment is not attempted here: it requires a projection freshness model the surface does not have, and SPEC-001 already specifies it — so it earns its own design pass rather than being improvised at the end of one.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `apps/rph-demo` (projection freshness + `expectedRevision` on non-FRESH dispatch), `SPEC-001-FX-FRESH-18`, and the census over `workbench.ts`. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-F-051 — The dist-trap remedy covers `@janumipwb/*` and not a relative `./index.js`, so a mutant can still report a false green — and one did, in my hands, hours after I cited the remedy approvingly

- **Date:** 2026-08-07 (found by running a mutant that should have reddened and did not) · **Type:** DIVERGENCE FINDING · **Class:** METHOD — a gap in an existing, working remedy, not an absent one.
- **THE REMEDY THIS REPOSITORY ALREADY BUILT, and it works.** `vitest.config.ts` documents the trap in as many words — *"Cross-package tests exercise the BUILT `dist`, never `src`… **The dist trap was STRUCTURAL. Any cross-package mutant is silently meaningless without a rebuild**"* — and fixes it by aliasing every `@janumipwb/*` specifier to `src`. `scripts/mutants/run.ts` relies on exactly that: *"so no rebuild is needed and **the trap cannot recur here BY CONSTRUCTION** rather than by discipline."* Both statements are true.
- **⚠ THE HOLE: THE ALIAS IS KEYED ON THE PACKAGE SPECIFIER, AND A TEST INSIDE A PACKAGE DOES NOT USE ONE.** `queries-revision.test.ts` imported its subject as `./index.js` — a RELATIVE path through the package's own barrel. No `@janumipwb/*` specifier, so no alias, so the import resolved to built output. **Reverting `listByType` to the exact defective form the test was written against left all three tests PASSING.** Re-pointed at `./engine.js` and `./queries.js` directly, the same mutant reddens all three.
- **THE EXPOSURE, MEASURED: 38 test files under `packages/*/src` import via a relative `./index.js`.** Every ad-hoc mutant run against one of those is subject to this unless a build ran in between. (134 more import via `@janumipwb/*` and are protected by the alias; the DECLARED-mutant runner is safe by construction, as its own comment claims.)
- **AND THE TRAP IS INTERMITTENT, WHICH IS WHY IT IS HARD TO NOTICE.** `bun run check-types` runs a build. So a mutant run that happens to follow a typecheck sees fresh output and behaves correctly; one that does not, does not. **The same mutation can be honest or meaningless depending on what command preceded it** — and nothing in the workflow marks which happened.
- **WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT INVALIDATE, stated precisely because the temptation is to over-claim in both directions.** A mutant that **REDDENED** is trustworthy: the mutation was demonstrably observed, whatever the resolution path. Only a mutant that **DID NOT redden** is suspect, and only if no build preceded it. So the week's mutation evidence stands, with one exception worth naming:
  - **REG-F-040 is the sole place I used ABSENCE of a red as evidence** — *"removing the disjunct reddened NOTHING, which is the positive evidence it was dead."* **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-07 with an explicit rebuild between edit and run: restoring the dead `|| SYSTEM` disjunct changes nothing — 2475 passed either way.** The claim holds; it was true evidence and not a trap artifact. Recorded because a claim of that shape must be re-earned once the instrument is in doubt, not assumed.
- **THE LESSON, and it is not "be careful".** *A predicted red that does not fire is only evidence if the harness could have shown it.* I had the discipline exactly right — name the red, run the mutant — and the instrument was lying. **Discipline does not survive an instrument nobody validates**, which is the same sentence this register has written about gates, guards and censuses, now aimed at the mutation harness itself.
- **Disposition:** RECORDED. The narrow remedy is to alias the relative barrel too, or to forbid tests importing their own package's `./index.js` (a lint rule reaching 38 files). **Neither is done here**: both are repository-wide changes that would land unmeasured at the end of a long session, and the immediate exposure is closed — the one affected test now imports its modules directly, and the one absence-of-red claim is re-verified.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `vitest.config.ts` (alias the relative barrel) or a lint rule over `packages/*/src/**/*.test.ts`. **Status:** OPEN.

Each entry is an OPEN QUESTION directed to the sponsor, with the drafting agent's strongest candidate answer as its safe default. The register — not the sidecars — is the carrier: an elicitation item not filed here does not survive ratification. Sponsor dispositions are collected on the Ratify Sheet; each disposition closes here by merge.

**Repository (raised 2026-08-03 from REG-F-010 group 3):**
- **REG-E-022** — Which ratified error code the **stale-decision-version** promotion refusal should carry. `promoteBaseline` refuses a promotion whose decision bound subject versions that are no longer current, and reports the generic `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` with `STALE_DECISION_VERSION` in the message prose. The sibling refusal beside it was moved to `RPH_EVIDENCE_INVALIDATED` on 2026-08-03 because a ratified code named that condition exactly; **no ratified code names this one**. `RPH_SUBJECT_VERSION_MISMATCH` is the obvious candidate and is deliberately not taken: it is the code `RPH-ASR-010`'s ratified statement claims for a **different** subject (an assessment's version binding), that rule is enforced nowhere, and its register row is disclosed on the recorded census that no handler refuses with the code — a census that adopting it here would silently make untrue, since the row is guarded by observation rather than by a grep. `RPH_BASELINE_VERSION_MISMATCH` (the other unused candidate, categorised INVARIANT) is about baseline versions, not a decision's bound subject versions. *Default: the generic code STANDS, and the condition keeps travelling in the message, until either a code is assigned to this meaning or `RPH-ASR-010` is enforced and its code thereby claimed. Recorded as a decision rather than left as silence, because "the codes are ratified and one is obviously close" is exactly the reasoning §0.3 calls a convenient interpretation encoded as architecture.*

**Repository (raised 2026-08-05 from REG-F-023):**
- **REG-E-024 — CLOSED IN FULL 2026-08-05.** (a) and (d) rested on premises measurement contradicted, (b) was malformed (a fold, not a machine), (c) is built. See REG-F-035, REG-F-037, REG-F-038. ~~**Which arrow closes an assurance assessment that cannot gather its required evidence, and what becomes of the eleven unreachable states?**~~ The §30 machine declares `CANCELLED` **terminal** and gives it no in-arrow, so an assessment stalled in `EVIDENCE_PENDING` has no exit at all. Four sub-questions, each a ratification not an implementation: ~~**(a)** declare an arrow into `CANCELLED` (from which states, on which command — §32's `invalidateAssuranceAssessment` is the obvious candidate but its ratified arrows today run only `SATISFIED`/`CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED` → `INVALIDATED`), or retire the state;~~ **— CLOSED 2026-08-05 (REG-F-037), AND THE PREMISE WAS FALSE: §30 DOES declare `ANY ACTIVE → CANCELLED`.** The gap was the missing TRIGGER, not the missing arrow — §31 named no event and §32 no command — and DOC-004 is amended to name both. ~~**(b)** `AggregateAssuranceDisposition` — six states, no arrows: is the aggregate roll-up axis intended and unbuilt, or superseded?~~ **— CLOSED 2026-08-05, AND THE QUESTION WAS MALFORMED: see REG-F-035.** Both branches presuppose a state machine; it has no arrows because it is a computed REDUCTION, and its rule is ratified in full at DOC-004 §28.2 (with §28.1's "strictest unresolved disposition" invariant). Intended, specified, and now built as a fold with its consumer. Sub-items (a), (c) and (d) stand and are genuinely arrows. ~~**(c)** `ValidatorRegistryEntry.status` — three states, no arrows: validator health is declared and unrunnable, so the assurance system cannot record that one of its own validators is failing;~~ **— CLOSED 2026-08-05 (REG-F-038), sponsor decision "yes validator health matters enough to design". §35's transition table is declared, the machine is built and driven, and Gate D refuses a DISABLED validator's result. The registry turned out to be the mechanism §34.1 ALREADY depended on — "retry, alternate validator implementation, or escalation action" — not a subsystem needing invention.** ~~**(d)** `PWU.assuranceState.UNASSESSED` has no in-arrow, so a PWU that needs assurance cannot be marked as awaiting it — is `UNASSESSED` reachable by an arrow nobody declared, or is `NOT_REQUIRED` doing its work?~~ **— CLOSED 2026-08-05 (REG-F-037): `UNASSESSED` is the machine's DECLARED INITIAL STATE, so it needs no in-arrow and is not in the unreachable set.** **DISPOSITION 2026-08-05: (a), (b) and (d) are CLOSED — (b) as malformed (a fold, not a machine; REG-F-035), (a) and (d) on measurements that contradict their own premises (REG-F-037). ONLY (c) SURVIVES, and it is now the last unreachable-state item in the system: `ValidatorRegistryEntry.status`'s three values. It is deliberately NOT authored — the corpus mentions the entry ONCE, under a §35 that says the runtime "should" maintain a registry, with no commands, events, arrows or health prose. Designing that subsystem is what §0.3 forbids.** *Default as filed: none of the five is invented in the repository; the gate holds the count and the states stay unreachable until ratified.*

**Repository (raised 2026-08-04 from the JAN-CMDPRE `InvalidatePwu` residue):**
- **REG-E-023 — CLOSED 2026-08-05, the stated default taken (see REG-F-036).** Whether `InvalidatePwu`'s **`triggeringObjectId`** is REQUIRED or OPTIONAL, because the command contract and the event contract disagree and only a ratification can settle which is wrong. `InvalidatePwuPayloadSchema` declares it **optional**; `PwuInvalidatedPayloadSchema` declares it **required** (`z.string()`). So a command the contract admits produces an event the contract forbids, and the two cannot both be right.
  - **THE SIBLING PAIR AGREES, which is what makes this an inconsistency rather than a convention.** `ReshapePwuPayloadSchema` and `PwuReshapingStartedPayloadSchema` carry the same field and **both** declare it optional. So the corpus is not applying a rule "commands relax what events require" — it did the consistent thing next door and something else here.
  - **WHAT THE REPOSITORY DID, and why it is not the answer.** The handler now REFUSES an `InvalidatePwu` whose `triggeringObjectId` is absent or does not resolve to a real object. That is the safe reading under EITHER resolution — if the command is meant to require it, refusing is correct; if the event is meant to relax, refusing is merely stricter than necessary and loses nothing, because an invalidation that cannot name what triggered it is an assertion with no subject. It is deliberately a HANDLER guard rather than a schema edit: tightening the command schema, or loosening the event schema, would pick a winner between two ratified documents, which is a sponsor act and not a repository one.
  - **WHY IT IS FILED RATHER THAN QUIETLY LEFT.** The gap is currently invisible from either contract alone — each is internally consistent, and only a dispatch reveals that they disagree. A future author reading `InvalidatePwuPayloadSchema` would reasonably conclude the field is optional and be surprised by the refusal. **DISPOSITION: the command is TIGHTENED**, which also makes the contract state what the handler already enforced. And tightening did NOT make that guard redundant — `z.string()` accepts `''`, so the schema owns ABSENCE and the guard owns the EMPTY STRING ("a reference to nothing"), each separately gated. *Default as filed: TIGHTEN THE COMMAND to match the event. The event contract is the one describing a fact that has already happened, and DOC-003's "fail closed; escalate rather than invent" favours the stricter reading — but this is exactly the choice §0.3 says must not be made by whoever happens to be editing the file.*

- **REG-E-025** — **What vocabulary is an execution `failureClass`, given that the corpus names one, the runtime uses another, and the rule binding it to anything is unimplemented?** Raised from REG-F-026 group (c), where two payload fields (`FailExecutionStep.failureClass`, `ExecutionStepFailed.failureClass`) declare an enum that does not exist and therefore accept any string. Constraining them requires a ratification, and here is the whole picture rather than the two obvious options:
  - **The corpus names seven kinds, in PROSE, and never enumerated them.** DOC-004 §36.2 lists *tool failure; model failure; timeout; sandbox failure; dependency unavailable; retry exhaustion; invalid output schema.* No canonical enum was ever minted from that list, nor from the four sibling taxonomies in §36.1/§36.3/§36.4/§36.5.
  - **The runtime uses a DIFFERENT vocabulary, and only in tests.** The sole value ever passed is `TRANSIENT`, which appears in no §36 list and in no enum anywhere. **It has no production producer at all** — every occurrence is a test fixture.
  - **AND THE FIELD IS INERT.** `execution.ts` only ever passes it through (`...(p.failureClass ? { failureClass: p.failureClass } : {})`). Nothing reads it, branches on it, or projects it. So today it is a string carried from a caller to an event and never consulted.
  - **WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE §36 CLOSES WITH A REQUIREMENT NOBODY HAS MET.** After the five taxonomies, verbatim: *"Each failure class must map to permitted control actions."* That is a §36-wide rule — it sits after §36.5 and governs all five lists — and **the mapping exists nowhere.** So this is not merely a choice between two vocabularies: whichever is adopted becomes the domain of a ratified mapping that has never been built, and adopting the runtime's `TRANSIENT` would make the ratified sentence unsatisfiable as written, since `TRANSIENT` is not a §36 failure class.
  - *Default: the two fields STAY unconstrained and annotated, and the census holds them at a count that may only fall. Constraining to §36.2's seven would refuse the only value in flight; constraining to `TRANSIENT` would ratify a vocabulary the corpus does not contain and orphan the §36 mapping rule. Neither is a repository decision, and picking the nearer one is exactly the "convenient interpretation encoded as architecture" §0.3 forbids.*

- **REG-E-026** — **What are the catalog's evidence requirements, given that §6.1 makes prose MANDATORY and the corpus supplies none for ten of the twelve policies?** Raised from REG-F-022, which is the last thing making the restored §30 lifecycle *look* like it gates evidence: `EVIDENCE_PENDING → READY`, Gate A, and §38's "missing evidence" fold all read an **empty required set** on every policy that ships. The logic is right and its input is empty.
  - **THE SHAPE IS RATIFIED AND THE CONTENT IS NOT.** DOC-004 §6.1 defines nine fields. Of them, `id` is derivable, `admissibilityRules: []` and an absent `freshnessRule` are honest, and **five must be invented per requirement**: `cardinality`, `requiredForDispositions`, `mayBeWaived`, `description`, `purpose`. Only **2 of 12** policies have a required-evidence section, and both give a **prose list of item names** with none of those five. The dataset's `requiredEvidenceTypes` does not help: its own provenance marks it **AUTHORED (no ratified source)**.
  - **§6.1 IS WHAT MAKES THIS UNAVOIDABLE.** `description` and `purpose` are **required strings**, so there is no form of an `EvidenceRequirement` that can be stated without authoring prose. A sponsor who wants this gate live must either author it or ratify a relaxation of §6.1. That is the actual decision, and it cannot be dissolved by a cleverer derivation.
  - **THE BEHAVIOURAL FORK IS `requiredForDispositions`.** `ALL` gates assessing — every assessment sits in `EVIDENCE_PENDING`, the canonical drive submits no evidence, and **the product ships unable to complete an assessment**. `SATISFIED_ONLY` gates a positive conclusion — Gate A becomes real, the lifecycle still flows, and an assessor may REJECT work without evidence only a SATISFIED verdict would need. The corpus states neither.
  - *Default: NOTHING IS INVENTED. The requirements stay unauthored, the census pin stays at zero with its "delete, do not extend" instruction unexecuted, and the vacuity is disclosed rather than papered over. Landing five invented fields across twelve policies to make a gate report non-empty would be the exact disease REG-F-022's own entry names — a system that had stopped looking empty rather than one that had started checking.* Full measurement: `docs/_working/DESIGN-evidence-requirements.md`.

- **REG-E-027** — **Does the governance plane adopt `AuthorityReference`, and what act mints a delegation?** Raised from REG-F-014's third residual, and the item is filed with its own premise **corrected**: the entry says this repository *"has no object for"* delegated authority, which is **false on the code**.
  - **THE RATIFIED SHAPE EXISTS AND IS INERT.** `AuthorityReferenceSchema` (`objects.ts:143-150`) carries `authorityId`, `authorityType`, `grantedBy`, `scope`, `validFrom`, `validUntil` — transcribed verbatim from RPH-DOC-002 §5 — and `AuthorityTypeSchema` (`enums.ts:153-161`) includes **`DELEGATED_AGENT`**. It is REQUIRED on three persisted object types and carried on seven payloads. **Not one of its six fields is read anywhere in the repository.** So the answer to *"is delegation representable?"* is yes; the answer to *"does anything mint, amend, revoke, or consult one?"* is no. **This is a hollow, not a vacuum**, and the distinction matters because a vacuum needs a new ratified shape while a hollow needs a command and a reader for a shape already ratified.
  - **AND THE GOVERNANCE PLANE USES THE WEAKER OF TWO AUTHORITY MODELS THAT ALREADY SHIP.** `ProposeDecision.authority` is a bare `ActorReference` — actorId/actorType/displayName (`messages.ts:356`) — while `AssertObligation`, `AssertConstraint` and `ProposeHarness` use `AuthorityReference`. **Two authority models on adjacent commands, and the one that decides governance got the model with no scope, no validity window, and no grantor.** A delegation cannot be expressed on a Decision even if one were minted.
  - **WHY IT IS A SPONSOR QUESTION AND NOT A REPOSITORY ONE.** `proposeDecision` now REFUSES a caller declaring an authority it is not, which is the safe reading **precisely because delegation is unrepresentable there** — the refusal is what stands in for a delegation check that has nothing to check. Admitting delegated authority means deciding who may grant it, over what scope, for how long, and what evidence records the grant. *Default: the refusal stands and the hollow is disclosed rather than filled. Nothing mints an `AuthorityReference` until the grant act is ratified; inventing one would put a forgeable delegation underneath the refusal that currently makes forgery impossible.*

- **REG-E-028** — **What IS a recomposition conflict, and over what is it computed?** Raised from **REG-F-041**, which measured that §14.1's conflict rung has no engine-side detector and cannot acquire one without this answer.
  - **THE OBSTACLE IS NOT A MISSING FUNCTION, IT IS A MISSING SUBJECT.** `aggregationRules` — the contract field declaring **how child outputs combine** — is written at propose and read by **zero** `.ts` files. Recomposition never touches child outputs, artifacts, or constraints; it reads one `assuranceState` scalar per child. **There is no aggregation step for a detector to run inside**, so "add a conflict check" is not a smaller ask than "define the aggregation."
  - **THE CORPUS NAMES CONFLICTS BUT DOES NOT MAKE THEM COMPUTABLE.** RPH-DEC-006 gives *"incompatible tenant-identity models"* as its example, and `TENANT_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` / `OFFLINE_AUDIT_CONFLICT` exist as vocabulary — but nothing says over WHICH child artifacts, by what comparison, at what granularity. **Every conflict that has ever reached the kernel was typed by a test author.**
  - **THE INTERIM DECISION IS SEPARABLE AND IS THE REAL ASK.** Whether to make `detectedConflicts`, `parentCompletionClaimSupported` and `parentConstraintsHoldAgainstWhole` **REQUIRED** does not wait on the detector: it forces the recomposing party to STATE a judgement rather than inherit `composable-by-default`, converting a silent fail-open into a recorded assertion — the `GrantWaiver.effectiveAt` medicine, on AUTHORED shapes that are ours to change. It refuses every existing caller, which is why it is not taken unilaterally. *Default: nothing changes; the fail-open stays disclosed in REG-F-041 rather than papered over by a detector that would have to invent what it detects. **A conflict predicate invented here would be the "convenient interpretation encoded as architecture" §0.3 forbids, on the input that decides whether recomposition is a judgement or a sum.***

- **REG-E-029** — **Which aggregate map is authoritative: JPWB-DOC-003 §4's FIVE principal aggregates, or the runtime's ~30 roots?** Raised from REG-F-046, and it is prior to every cross-aggregate design question in this repository rather than specific to one.
  - **§4 names five**, with **Claim OWNED by an Assurance aggregate** (*"rooted at an Assurance Assessment: claims, evidence references…"*) and Evidence **referenced**, never *"mutated through the containing aggregate."* The runtime instead roots one aggregate per object type — ~30 of them, Claim and Evidence among them.
  - **THE CONSEQUENCE IS NOT COSMETIC.** AGG-1 governs *"semantic writes across aggregate boundaries."* Under the ratified map, ASR-8's Evidence→Claim propagation is **INSIDE one aggregate** and needs no saga, no compensation, and no new tier. Under the runtime's map it is a cross-aggregate flow and needs all three. **One ratified rule, two opposite verdicts, decided by a map nobody has reconciled.**
  - **AND IT REACHES FURTHER THAN ASR-8:** every future "should this handler touch that object" question has been answered ad hoc against an unstated map — the register already holds one cross-aggregate write that shipped (`withPwaVersionBump`) and one that was refused on the same ground (`execution.ts:392`), with no rule distinguishing them. *Default: NOTHING IS BUILT ON EITHER READING. The two are disclosed as inconsistent and no design assumes one — because picking the map that makes the current work easier is exactly the convenient interpretation §0.3 forbids, on the boundary that defines where invariants are enforced.*

- **REG-E-030** — **Is a saga / process-controller tier ratified, and what are its shapes?** Raised from REG-F-046. **RPH-DOC-009 §30.2 names four mechanisms** — *"domain event; saga/controller; compensating actions; explicit intermediate states"* — and **the repository implements none of them.**
  - Without it, every ratified cascade in the corpus is unimplementable by a route the corpus permits: ASR-8's evidence-invalidation propagation, DEC-2's *"triggers impact analysis"*, and baseline-readiness recalculation all describe an effect with no actor and no act.
  - **WHAT MAKES IT A RATIFICATION RATHER THAN A BUILD:** a saga is a governed actor. It issues commands nobody typed, so **`issuedBy` for a saga-issued command is a question this repository has no answer to** — REG-F-014 refuses a caller that declares an authority it is not, and a process controller declares one by construction. Compensation is a second: an act that UNDOES a governed act needs its own record, and DOC-002 ratifies no compensating-action object.
  - *Default: nothing is built. The cascades stay disclosed as unimplemented rather than delivered by a mechanism the corpus does not name — and the interim mitigation stays labelled as mitigation: hardening the DECIDING READS to derive freshness stops a stale status misleading a decision, and does not make the status correct.*

- **REG-E-031** — **Does revoking a governance decision require authority, and whose?** Raised from **REG-F-048**, where an AGENT revoked a HUMAN-authorized EFFECTIVE decision and the command was ACCEPTED.
  - **The corpus is silent in a specific way.** ASR-15 and RPH-GOV-001/002 govern *making* a decision effective — *"an agent may recommend a decision but cannot exercise authority unless delegated"* — and no ratified sentence addresses withdrawal. ASR-15 does say revocation **triggers impact analysis**, which is evidence that the corpus regards it as consequential, not as a lesser act.
  - **Three candidate rules, and they are genuinely different:** (a) revocation requires the **same** authority as approval (symmetric — simplest, and it makes the guard a one-line reuse of `makeDecisionEffective`'s); (b) it requires the authority of the **original approver or greater** (protects a decision from being undone by a peer, and needs an ordering over actors this repository deliberately does not have — REG-F-014 refused to invent one); (c) it is deliberately **easier** than approval, on the reasoning that withdrawing a permission is the safe direction and should never be blocked in an emergency.
  - **(c) IS NOT OBVIOUSLY WRONG AND SHOULD NOT BE DISMISSED**, which is why this is a sponsor question: in safety-critical practice, *stopping* is usually the act you do not gate. But this engine's revocation is not a stop — it retracts the authorization under work already performed, and the impact analysis that would make that safe is itself unimplemented.
  - *Default: the asymmetry is DISCLOSED, not patched. Adding a symmetric guard is one line and is deliberately not taken, because the shape of the rule decides whether this is a defect or a design, and a one-line guess on a governance act is exactly how an authored interpretation acquires the standing of canon.*

- **REG-E-006 — SPONSOR EVIDENCE RECORDED (2026-08-06), item remains OPEN.** The sponsor stated, in their own voice, the deployment configurations this item's *"editions, trust tiers"* clause has carried unresolved since filing: **"There are three configurations: standalone, SaaS and Enterprise. The standalone will have human and agent; the other two will exist in larger organizations."** And, asked whether standalone identity is authenticated or merely declared: **"Standalone can have an authenticated identity."**
  - **RECORDED AS EVIDENCE, NOT AS A DISPOSITION.** The sponsor stated facts about the product; they did not rule on this item's question, which is the *disposition of Executive-Overview-only platform claims*. Following the REG-E-001 precedent, sponsor voice is recorded as primary source while the item stays open. **The three configurations are named nowhere in the ratified corpus** — searched and confirmed — so this is their first authoritative statement.
  - **WHAT IT ALREADY SETTLES FOR THE REPOSITORY, and this is why it is recorded here rather than held:** it removes the reading that caller-asserted identity is acceptable in some configuration. All three have human and agent identity; standalone can authenticate. **REG-F-047's divergence therefore has no deployment in which it is correct**, and the remedy is uniform rather than tier-conditional — which is a materially different design from the one that was forming an hour earlier.

- **REG-E-031 — DISPOSITIONED (sponsor ruling, 2026-08-06): SYMMETRIC.** Revoking a governance decision requires the same authority as approving one. Of the three candidates the item put — symmetric, greater-than-the-original-approver, or deliberately easier — the sponsor chose the first, which is also the only one needing no ordering over actors (the ordering REG-F-014 refused to invent). **The consequence, recorded so it is not discovered later:** an agent that finds the assumption under an approval falsified cannot revoke; it must escalate. That is survivable because the agent retains every other lever — it can contest the claim, record a blocking observation, and move the work to AT_RISK — so it can make a situation visible and unpromotable without holding the authority to undo. Closes **REG-F-048**'s ruling dependency; the guard itself is a one-line reuse of `makeDecisionEffective`'s.

- **REG-E-030 — DISPOSITIONED (sponsor ruling, 2026-08-06): the coordinator tier is authorized, and a coordinator-issued command acts as THE ACTOR WHOSE ACT TRIGGERED IT.** Of the candidates — the triggering actor, a distinct system actor, a delegated coordinator, or one citing the compelling rule — the sponsor chose the first.
  - **IT AVOIDS A TRAP THE ENGINEER'S PREFERRED OPTION WALKED INTO.** Measured before the ruling: the de minimis assurance floor keys on whether the acting actor is AI (HUMAN/SERVICE/POLICY_ENGINE/EXTERNAL_SYSTEM pass, AGENT/MODEL are held), so a coordinator declaring itself `SERVICE` would have **evaded** the floor rather than satisfied it. Under this ruling a cascade triggered by an agent runs as that agent and the floor still applies. *The sponsor's answer is safer than the one that was recommended to them.*
  - **⚠ AND THE ENGINEER'S RESIDUAL CONCERN IS RESOLVED BY THE STANDING DIRECTIVE (REG-D-025), NOT BY THIS RULING.** Acting as the triggering actor, with `issuedBy` the only provenance field, would make the governed record assert that a human performed acts they did not — REG-F-014's shape by another door. The directive's own standard — *"who did what, when, why, where, how"* — forecloses that: a record naming Alice as the contester of a claim she never contested fails *"who did what"* on its face. **So derived acts carry explicit derivation provenance, distinct from `issuedBy`, and the resolution is recorded as authored under delegated authority rather than as something the sponsor adjudicated.** A second bound follows from REG-E-031: a derived act may move work toward caution and may not approve or revoke, or the cascade would silently inherit the authority the symmetric ruling just protected.

- **REG-E-027 — DISPOSITIONED (sponsor ruling, 2026-08-06): the delegation record becomes real.** `AuthorityReference` — ratified from RPH-DOC-002 §5, carrying `authorityId`, `authorityType` (incl. `ORGANIZATIONAL_ROLE` and `DELEGATED_AGENT`), `grantedBy`, `scope`, `validFrom`, `validUntil`, required on three persisted object types and with **not one of its six fields read anywhere** — acquires a minting act and readers. It composes with REG-E-030: because a coordinator acts as its triggering actor, delegation stays a **human-to-deputy** mechanism and does not become a back door for machine authority.

- **REG-E-029 — DISPOSITIONED (sponsor ruling, 2026-08-06): the DOCUMENTS' map is authoritative** — JPWB-DOC-003 §4's five principal aggregates, not the runtime's twenty-three roots.
  - **THE READING PROCEEDED ON, stated because the two readings differ by an order of magnitude in cost and risk.** Taken as an **ownership rule** — who may write which object — it is cheap and safe: the assurance cluster is 11 of 93 command types and 13 write-sites, all 13 in one file, and `targetAggregateType` is constructed 287 times and read semantically once. Taken as a **storage re-rooting** it is not: a probe re-rooting `aggregateId` did not re-key but silently OVERWROTE, destroying an assessment's state row. **The ownership reading is adopted first; re-rooting is treated as a separate question that must earn its own design.**
  - **AND A CORRECTION THE SPONSOR WAS GIVEN BEFORE RULING:** the engineer had told them this map makes the evidence→claim cascade intra-aggregate and *"the whole problem evaporates."* It does not. §4 owns **claims** inside the assurance aggregate but only **references** evidence, and a referenced object *"is never mutated through the containing aggregate"* — so invalidating evidence remains a write outside the boundary and contesting a claim a write inside. **The cascade stays cross-aggregate and still needs the coordinator.** What the ruling does buy is that claim/assessment/observation interactions become internal.

**Section E dispositions (append-only notations):**
- **⚠ REG-E-028 — WITHDRAWN THE SAME DAY IT WAS FILED: it is not a sponsor question, because the ratified corpus answers it.** I asked *"what IS a recomposition conflict, and over what is it computed?"* and recorded a safe default warning that inventing a predicate would be the *"convenient interpretation encoded as architecture"* §0.3 forbids. **The corpus had already answered every limb, and I had not read the rule at its own location** — REG-F-041 cited DEC-6 as §6 and it is §7 (REG-F-042).
  - **"OVER WHAT?"** — RPH-DOC-002 §14.1 states it as its own ratified bullet: *"**Child outputs** must be checked for contradiction"*, separate from and adjacent to *"Child evidence must support the parent claim, not merely child claims."* JPWB-DOC-003 §7 DEC-6 lists *"contradictions among child outputs"*, *"artifact/interface compatibility"* and *"child-evidence support for the parent claim"* as **three distinct items in one sentence**. The subject is artifacts, and the corpus says so.
  - **"WHO COMPUTES IT?"** — **not the engine.** RPH-DOC-003 §36 assigns contradiction detection to the **Verifier role**, and RPH-DOC-004 §4.1 puts it in Assurance Policies with **replaceable Validators whose `implementationType` may be HUMAN**. So the analysis was never the kernel's to perform; the engine's part is to **require the assessment and refuse without it**.
  - **AND THE ANSWER I ALMOST ADOPTED WOULD HAVE DELETED A RATIFIED RULE.** I proposed re-reading "a conflict" as *contradicting evidence against the parent completion Claim*. That **collapses §14.1's two bullets into one** and leaves the child-output contradiction rung with no reader — a re-interpretation of ratified text dressed as a design simplification. The elegance of the reframing is exactly what should have made it suspect.
  - **THIS IS THE FOURTH TIME IN THIS PROGRAMME THAT A BLOCKING QUESTION DISSOLVED ON READING THE SOURCE PROPERLY** (REG-F-030, REG-F-036, REG-F-037's third limb, and now this). Every one was a claim about MY SEARCH presented as a claim about the corpus. **The safe default I attached — "nothing is invented" — was protecting the corpus from an invention the corpus had already ruled out**, which is the specific way this failure mode is self-concealing: it produces cautious, correct-sounding restraint about a question that was never open. **Superseded by REG-F-042, REG-F-043 and REG-F-044**, which are findings, not questions. The one genuine ratification request extracted from it lives in REG-F-044: a **claim-assessment command set**, for which the corpus ratifies EVENTS and no commands.
- **REG-E-026 — CLOSED (sponsor ratification, 2026-08-05).** The sponsor ratified and delegated the authoring. Executing it **overturned the measurement the item was raised on** — see **REG-F-030**: 11 of 12 policies carry an evidence list, not 2, so the item's premise ("ten policies have no textual basis whatsoever") was false and its safe default ("NOTHING IS INVENTED") was answering a question that did not exist. **What landed:** DOC-004's 89 items authored as §6.1 `EvidenceRequirement`s and delivered to the policy objects. `description` is the corpus item **verbatim**, machine-checked against the markdown; `id` and `cardinality` follow stated rules; `requiredForDispositions = SATISFIED_ONLY` on §15.9's own words, so **nothing gets `ALL`** and the item's feared arm — *"the product ships unable to complete an assessment"* — is refuted by ratified text rather than dodged. `evidenceType` and `purpose` are AUTHORED and are named as such in every policy's provenance. **The tier is the corpus's own word:** §15.5/§16.4 say *"Required evidence"* → `requiredEvidence` (13, gating); §17.4–§26.4 say *"Evidence"* → `optionalEvidence` (76, carried). §20 gets `[]` for both and the census asserts it, so *"all twelve carry evidence"* can never become the passing condition. **The one call that could be wrong** is flagged in the design note and enumerated in the gate: five items are `ZERO_OR_MORE` by authored judgement, because under a blanket `AT_LEAST_ONE` an intent captured from one clear expression could never be assured, and POL-INTENT-COMPLETENESS could never be satisfied by **any** undertaking — this system has no `AMBIGUITY` or `STAKEHOLDER` object type at all. *A requirement whose referent cannot be produced is not a strict gate; it is a dead policy.* Closing this also closed **REG-F-022** and surfaced **REG-F-031**.
- **REG-E-025 — CLOSED (sponsor ratification, 2026-08-05).** Ratified, and executing it corrected the item itself on two counts. **(1) The citation was wrong:** the entry and both field annotations said *"DOC-004 §36.2"*; RPH-DOC-004 §36 is **Assurance Profiles** and contains no failure taxonomy. The taxonomy is **RPH-DOC-002 §36**. Recorded rather than quietly fixed — a mis-cited ratification is how an authored claim acquires borrowed authority, because the reader checks §36.2 of the document they were pointed at, finds a §36.2, and stops. **(2) The item saw one family of five:** §36 has 32 classes across five families. Only §36.2's seven are minted, because only execution failures have a field to travel in; minting the other 25 would create four more declared-and-unreachable vocabularies (REG-F-023's finding). **The §36-wide rule is now MET for that family** — *"Each failure class must map to permitted control actions"* had never been built; `EXECUTION_FAILURE_CONTROL_ACTIONS` is total over the enum by derivation and every action is a ratified §37 `ControlAction`. **And it is ENFORCED**, which is the part that distinguishes it from a table satisfying a rule on paper: `retryExecutionStep` refuses a retry whose last failure class does not permit `RETRY`, with `RETRY_EXHAUSTION` the case that gives it teeth — a genuinely different check from the retry cap, proved by refusing on attempt ONE with `maxAttempts: 9`. `TRANSIENT` is removed, not aliased: it had **no production producer**, so constraining the field refuses nothing any shipped path emits. **A blind spot the item did not see:** there were **four** `failureClass` fields, not two — the plan-level pair was declared plain `string` with no `enumRef` and was therefore **invisible to the enumRef census**, which only watches fields that *name* an enum. *A field is not safe because the census is quiet about it; it may be below the instrument's floor.* All four are constrained; the census now asserts all four. Ratchet: 7 → 4 → 3 → **1**.
- **REG-E-003 — CLOSED (REG-D-015, 2026-07-24):** the sponsor restated the chain-of-thought ruling in their own voice; merged into DOC-003 PER-12 v1.2.0. The reconstruction is no longer load-bearing.
- **REG-E-001 — sponsor evidence recorded (2026-07-24), amendment pending confirmation:** the sponsor's substantive input on the thesis sentence — "recursively composable" reads against the more familiar reductionist "recursively decomposable" and needs explanation — is recorded as primary-source voice. A proposed amendment (pairing decomposable/recomposable, with the anti-reductionist asymmetry glossed per Part A §2 and AX-4) is before the sponsor; the item remains OPEN until the sponsor confirms exact wording (CONSTITUTIONAL layer; B5).
- **REG-E-001 — second sponsor statement (2026-07-24, same session):** the pairing "is coming into view because of the larger discussion and vision of Janumi managing work and complexity — where complexity is differentiated from complicated." This grounds the amendment in the Complex Systems Management thread (source discussion, historical: the irreversibility-of-reductionism as the falsifiable test of complexity — "decompose, understand every part, recompose, still missing something; that remainder is the complexity"; the producing-system-vs-artifact distinction; sponsor's prior wholesale endorsement at that discussion's L1262, scoped near-term to Shape Engineering). Revised proposal package before the sponsor: thesis wording variants + complexity-grounded gloss (CON-000), a "complex, not merely complicated" doctrine passage (DOC-001 §2), and a **Complex ≠ Complicated** guard (DOC-002 §3/DOC-001 §4 inequality family).
- **REG-E-001 — third sponsor statement (2026-07-24, same session):** "complexity was in part captured by the Governed Stream and Narrative Memories concepts in terms of being able to answer questions like 'How did we get to this point?' and 'Where can we credibly go from here?'" — the **temporal axis** of the complexity stance, in sponsor voice: complex systems are path-dependent; the present state does not explain itself (retrospective question → the governed professional stream, AX-7) and futures are *credible* rather than arbitrary — constrained by accumulated commitments, assumptions, and evidence (prospective question → Narrative Memory, reconciliation, assurance). This supplies the design rationale, at doctrine altitude, for why the stream and Narrative Memory exist — and gives Narrative Memory its defining question, unifying the source corpus's two competing readings (scenario-evolution vs capability-evolution: both serve the prospective question). Package updated accordingly; item remains OPEN pending sponsor confirmation.
- **REG-E-001 — CLOSED (REG-D-016, 2026-07-24):** the sponsor confirmed variant B verbatim. The thesis now rests on sponsor voice, traced through three recorded evidence statements to the confirmation act. No sponsor-voice elicitation items remain open.

*(This section is the only part of the register the synthesis program may rewrite before ratification; thereafter, append-only discipline applies to it as to all sections.)*

## 7. Post-ratification program grants and commission questions

### REG-Q-051 — CSAA program-working-reference grant and Wave 1 commission

- **Date:** 2026-07-25 · **Type:** OPEN QUESTION
- **Statement:** The `docs/ASTs and Code Analysis/README.md` proposal requests establishment of Codebase Semantic Analysis and Assurance (`JAN-CSAA`) as a bounded program working-reference series for conventional TypeScript/Svelte codebase analysis and software assurance. Direction has been given to proceed with Wave 0 preparation, but no itemized sponsor act yet decides the working name/product standing, program-versus-`JPWB-SPEC-nnn` authority form, exact program scope, first supported repository subject, dirty-worktree inventory mode, permanent prefix and `000`–`011` allocations, lifecycle convention, Wave 1 commission, or adoption/oracle authority. The full judgment surface, verified evidence, strongest opposing considerations, consequences, recommendations, and candidate grant text are recorded in `docs/ASTs and Code Analysis/Wave 0 Sponsor Decision Instrument.md`. Does the accountable sponsor confer the proposed program grant, with what item-by-item dispositions and amendments?
- **Safe default:** No CSAA authority is conferred. `JAN-CSAA-000` remains a Draft proposal with a provisional ID; `JAN-CSAA-001` through `JAN-CSAA-011` remain unauthored and blocked. Wave 0 may prepare the decision instrument, requirement-ledger/review templates, and read-only evidence. It may not claim a permanent prefix, author governed Wave 1 members, select tools, change implementation or oracle artifacts, or treat a general direction to proceed as a sponsor conferral.
- **Disposition:** Await the accountable sponsor's separate disposition of W0-01 through W0-09. Before recording the result, recheck the next available `REG-D-nnn`; at filing time it appears to be `REG-D-017`.
- **Merge target:** A future sponsor-conferral entry in this register; administrative carriage into the CSAA `README.md` status/manifest and every authorized member status block. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-Q-052 — CSAA two-stage program grant, charter adoption, and Wave 1 activation

- **Date:** 2026-07-25 · **Type:** OPEN QUESTION
- **Statement:** Independent review of the `REG-Q-051` decision surface found that its nine-item carrier bundled independently contestable semantic baselines and could allow an unconditional grant to contradict a `REJECT` or `DEFER` disposition. The revised `CSAA-W0-INSTRUMENT-2026-07-25@0.2.0` therefore separates W0-01 through W0-16 and requires two sponsor acts: Stage A may establish the bounded program and authorize preparation/review of an exact `JAN-CSAA-000` candidate; Stage B (W0-17) may adopt only an identified version and SHA-256 digest and then activate the three-document Wave 1 commission. Per-member Normative status requires its own `JPWB-REG-005` sponsor conferral and synchronized member/manifest carriage. This entry supersedes `REG-Q-051` only as the requested disposition procedure; its no-authority safe default remains consistent and in force.
- **Safe default:** No CSAA grant, charter adoption, permanent identifier authority, Normative member status, or Wave 1 commission is effective. Preparation of the itemized instrument, templates, exact evidence, and OPEN register records may continue. No sponsor response to an unseen W0-17 candidate may be inferred.
- **Disposition:** First await individual sponsor dispositions for W0-01 through W0-16. File no Stage A grant unless every prerequisite is `RATIFY` or a mutually compatible `AMEND`; any `REJECT`, `DEFER`, or unresolved incompatibility preserves the safe default. If Stage A is conferred, prepare and independently review the exact W0-17 candidate, then seek a separate version/digest-bound sponsor disposition. Recheck all identifiers and live evidence before either filing.
- **Merge target:** A compatible Stage A sponsor-conferral entry, followed by a distinct `JAN-CSAA-000` adoption/Wave 1 activation conferral if W0-17 is later ratified; synchronized administrative carriage into the CSAA member and manifest status blocks. **Status:** OPEN.

### REG-D-017 — JAN-CSAA program-working-reference grant and charter-preparation commission

- **Date:** 2026-07-25 · **Type:** DECISION (sponsor conferral; itemized disposition of `CSAA-W0-INSTRUMENT-2026-07-25@0.2.0`)
- **Statement:** The accountable sponsor establishes **Codebase Semantic Analysis and Assurance** (`JAN-CSAA`) as a JPWB program working-reference series for the design, specification, fixture, implementation, qualification, operation, and validation of a revision-bound semantic-analysis and conventional software-assurance capability for the root-manifest-resolved TypeScript, JavaScript, and TypeScript-bearing Svelte implementation in `JanumiCode/janumiprofessionalworkbench`. The program is an engineering capability, not a separate Janumi product, PWA, PWU, Undertaking, canonical role, or professional-semantic object. It holds no more than HYPOTHESIS-grade authority within the granted program scope, is subordinate to every canon artifact by concern, and defers exact governed shapes to enforced repository reference artifacts. The permanent, non-reusable `JAN-CSAA-000` through `JAN-CSAA-011` identifiers and the Draft / Proposed / Normative / Deprecated / Superseded lifecycle are confirmed; identifier allocation does not confer member authority. The first supported subject is the root-workspace-resolved JPWB package and `apps/rph-demo` source/configuration perimeter ruled by W0-04, with Playwright and other undeclared-project surfaces inventory-only and with documentation prototypes, workflow harness code, derived/build output, third-party source, live network/agent execution, production traces, and sibling repositories excluded as ruled there. Dirty-worktree inventory is revision/change-set bound and read-only by default; installation, generation, builds, cache-writing commands, live/network tests, external scanners, and production traces require separate execution authorization. Compiler-confirmed TypeScript facts are source/configuration/toolchain bound and provider outputs remain derived evidence; coverage proves identified execution rather than correctness; runtime traces support only their captured execution; findings and dispositions preserve append-only logical history; remediation and source mutation remain outside the analysis core; and unsupported, failed, stale, excluded, partial, or incomplete analysis may never report an unqualified green result. Concrete analyzer providers, graph composition and persistence, coverage provider/thresholds, production trace ingestion, gate/severity profiles, licensing/deployment profiles, and executable topology remain deferred. The W0-08 documentation-only commission for `JAN-CSAA-001`, `JAN-CSAA-002`, and `JAN-CSAA-005` is approved in principle but **is not active** until the exact W0-17 `JAN-CSAA-000` adoption conferral is recorded. Each CSAA member requires its own exact-version/digest `JPWB-REG-005` sponsor conferral and synchronized member/manifest carriage. This Stage A grant authorizes only administrative incorporation of these dispositions into a Draft or Proposed `JAN-CSAA-000`, completion of its requirement ledger and independent review, and preparation of the exact W0-17 adoption package. No CSAA member is Normative; `JAN-CSAA-000` is not adopted; Wave 1 is not active; no provider, implementation, procurement, oracle change, professional-work semantic, infrastructure-assurance rule, or unauthored content receives authority.
- **Disposition:** `W0-01 RATIFY; W0-02 RATIFY; W0-03 RATIFY; W0-04 RATIFY; W0-05 RATIFY; W0-06 RATIFY; W0-07 RATIFY; W0-08 RATIFY; W0-09 RATIFY; W0-10 RATIFY; W0-11 RATIFY; W0-12 RATIFY; W0-13 RATIFY; W0-14 RATIFY; W0-15 RATIFY; W0-16 RATIFY.` The sponsor expressly made no disposition on W0-17 and directed Stage A only. The live pre-recording evidence was rechecked at `2026-07-25T12:52:23.7902772-04:00`: branch `main`, HEAD `92f30710dd559120af608f84018c33ac5d846f0b`, 35 tracked/untracked porcelain entries, root workspaces `packages/*` and `apps/*`, ten package build configurations, eleven authored `.svelte` files, and no repository-wide coverage-configuration hit in the recorded inspection. Effective on recording as the `JPWB-CON-000 B1` program sponsor grant and charter-preparation commission; confers neither canon nor `JPWB-SPEC-nnn` status.
- **Merge target:** The conferral itself has no substantive merge target. Administrative carriage remains pending in the Stage A decision instrument, the Draft/Proposed CSAA `README.md` manifest, and the exact W0-17 adoption package. `REG-Q-052` remains OPEN only for Stage B adoption and Wave 1 activation; its former no-grant safe default is superseded by this act, while its no-member-adoption and no-Wave-1 defaults remain in force.
- **Status:** EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING.

**Append-only Stage A progress notation — 2026-07-25 (ministerial carriage of REG-D-017):**

- **REG-Q-051 — SUPERSEDED / CLOSED FOR STAGE A:** `REG-Q-052` superseded its nine-item disposition procedure, and `REG-D-017` now resolves the program-grant question. Its former no-grant safe default no longer controls.
- **REG-Q-052 — STAGE A SATISFIED; OPEN FOR STAGE B ONLY:** W0-17 remains undisposed. Until a later exact-version/digest sponsor conferral, `JAN-CSAA-000` is not adopted, no CSAA member is Normative, and the W0-08 Wave 1 commission is inactive.
- **REG-D-017 Draft/Proposed sequencing clarification:** the phrase “Draft or Proposed `JAN-CSAA-000`” names the permitted preparation lifecycle range; it does not permit skipping ratified W0-16. The charter remains Draft through disposition incorporation, requirement-ledger closure, and self-review. Only then may the author advance the exact candidate to Proposed for independent review. This notation creates no new decision.

### REG-D-021 — JAN-CSAA standing documentation-corpus construction commission and deferred final sponsor review

- **Date:** 2026-07-28T08:58:53.646-04:00 · **Type:** DECISION
  (accountable-sponsor standing direction, program commission, and bounded
  process delegation)
- **Sponsor:** Marshall Hendricks, Architect and accountable sponsor
- **Sponsor-originated direction:** “We need to remove me from the manual
  authorization of these documents because it's taking way longer to generate
  than I had expected. Granted, we do want very high quality documentation so
  the process itself is fine. But my authorization as sponsor will have to be
  understood as me reviewing the full corpus of documents once all the waves,
  etc. have been finished.”
- **Direction evidence:** `JAN-CSAA-SPONSOR-DIRECTION-001@0.1.0`;
  `docs/ASTs and Code Analysis/records/JAN-CSAA - Sponsor Standing Direction
  for Autonomous Corpus Preparation and Final Review.md`; 15,802 bytes;
  SHA-256
  `6aae01e189386352a5fc693faa9379ec44c0faffc52bbf0badef9efccb1c6484`;
  UTF-8 without BOM, CRLF-only, one terminal CRLF.
- **Register preimage:** 107,854 bytes; SHA-256
  `d516e7068eae1a2a19fa1259420518f63833af070cf5642a9d95fb4bf2f09872`;
  UTF-8 without BOM, LF-only, one terminal LF; endpoint
  `REG-D-020 / EFFECTIVE — MERGED`.
- **Statement:** The accountable sponsor activates a standing,
  documentation-only construction commission for the complete reserved
  `JAN-CSAA-001` through `JAN-CSAA-011` corpus, its requirement ledgers,
  evidence and review records, cross-corpus closure records, repository-specific
  design, and detailed implementation roadmap. Documentation agents may author,
  revise, reconcile, validate, complete objective wave-exit checks, and advance
  a member from Draft to Proposed after its ledger and author self-review close.
  Proposed members require independently recorded adversarial review. No
  intermediate sponsor response, concern-owner appointment or disposition,
  per-member adoption package, register decision, or README-carriage decision
  is required merely to continue in-scope documentation work or enter the next
  documentation wave.
- **Scope:** The commission completes Wave 1 documentation and activates the
  documentation-authoring portions of Waves 2, 3, and 4. It permits read-only
  repository inspection and non-authoritative working-state records. It permits
  proposed specifications of schemas, fixtures, tests, provider criteria, and
  implementation work, but it does not perform or confer those artifacts.
  `JAN-CSAA-000@0.3.0` remains the active Normative charter and adopted-manifest
  baseline; its final corpus successor may be prepared only as Draft or
  Proposed before the final sponsor act.
- **Duration:** Effective on recording until the earliest of the final
  completed-corpus sponsor disposition, explicit sponsor revocation or
  supersession, a material authority incompatibility that cannot safely remain
  an explicit Draft/Proposed assumption or alternative, or recorded program
  cancellation. There is no per-document or per-wave expiry.
- **Reviewability:** Permanent IDs, versions, exact candidate digests,
  provenance, requirement ledgers, self-reviews, independent adversarial
  reviews, unresolved findings and alternatives, strongest opposing cases,
  horizontal-closure evidence, and a current working-status record must remain
  inspectable. The sponsor may inspect or redirect at any time, but intermediate
  sponsor review is not a continuation predicate and its absence is never
  approval.
- **Recording mechanism:** This entry is the single standing commission.
  Member metadata, ledgers, review records, and a separate non-authoritative
  working-corpus status record carry preparation state. The adopted README
  manifest is an authority/adoption baseline and need not churn for every Draft
  revision or wave transition. Interim sponsor presentations, sponsor-response
  records, concern-owner authority/determination records, and state-only
  manifest-ratification packages are not required. At completion, one exact
  corpus manifest binds every proposed member and its assurance evidence for
  one sponsor-review event; the accepted set and any exceptions are then
  recorded and synchronized in one controlled final-carriage procedure.
- **Separation of duties:** The authoring identity may not independently review
  the same exact candidate. A separately identified reviewer invocation records
  method, evidence, findings, and candidate identity; if that reviewer edits
  candidate bytes, another reviewer must review the affected result. Authors,
  reviewers, validators, recorders, and program-local records may not confer
  Normative status, fabricate sponsor voice, waive a requirement, accept risk,
  weaken a pre-existing oracle, or expand this delegation. Material
  cross-document semantics require horizontal-closure and single-owner review.
- **Final sponsor boundary:** Every newly authored member remains Draft or
  Proposed and non-authoritative until the completed corpus is presented. The
  sponsor's next required authorization for this documentation program is one
  final exact-corpus review event. That package preserves the full-judgment
  evidence required by `REG-D-013`, including individually visible material
  forks and exceptions, but does not require separate intermediate
  authorization events. One exact manifest-bound sponsor act may confer all
  accepted members together while expressly excluding or deferring others.
- **Transition:** The unexecuted
  `JAN-CSAA-W1-MANIFEST-002@0.2.0`/`JAN-CSAA-W1-VALIDATION-004@0.1.0`/
  `INSTRUMENT-002`/`FREEZE-002`/`INTAKE-002` chain is withdrawn from active
  solicitation and retained unchanged as historical preparation evidence. No
  Part A assignment, Part B `W1M2-CO-01` response, sponsor presentation,
  sponsor response, README carriage, archive, completion, or confirmation is
  authorized for that package. This entry consumes `REG-D-021`, which that
  proposal expected but did not reserve, and therefore independently defeats
  its exact pre-recording predicate.
- **No-expansion boundary:** This decision authorizes no canon amendment,
  Normative member conferral, application/source/test/configuration/dependency
  mutation, installation, procurement, provider selection or qualification
  execution, external scan, network or live-agent execution, production trace,
  prototype, executable schema/type/fixture/conformance-suite/oracle creation
  or change, analyzer implementation, deployment topology, retirement, or
  authority transfer. Each remains subject to its existing separate authority.
- **Disposition:** The per-member sponsor-conferral procedure in
  `REG-D-017`, the Wave-1-only commission in `REG-D-018`, and the
  separately-authorized Wave 2–4 preparation gates in
  `JAN-CSAA-000@0.3.0` are superseded prospectively only to the extent necessary
  to implement this standing documentation-preparation model. Their historical
  acts, substantive semantic baselines, lifecycle distinctions, evidence rules,
  implementation prohibitions, and final sponsor-conferral requirement remain
  in force.
- **Merge target:** Administrative consolidation into the completed
  `JAN-CSAA-000` successor, final controlled manifest, accepted member metadata,
  and final corpus-review records. Interim carriage is deliberately deferred to
  avoid turning working-state changes into repeated sponsor gates.
- **Status:** EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING.

### REG-D-022 — Correction and assurance clarification of the JAN-CSAA standing documentation commission

- **Date:** 2026-07-28T09:18:50.1250030-04:00 · **Type:** DECISION

  `REG-D-021`; no new sponsor judgment)
- **Recorder:** Codex documentation recorder and assurance auditor, acting
