- **Correction evidence:**
  `JAN-CSAA-STANDING-DIRECTION-CORRECTION-001@0.1.0`;
  `docs/ASTs and Code Analysis/records/JAN-CSAA - Standing Direction
  Interpretation Correction and Assurance Clarification.md`; 10,000 bytes;
  SHA-256
  `1ac465b6b072808b178827ad1012310070941de794635c7fd0c82a4b8d7eccd4`;
  UTF-8 without BOM, CRLF-only, one terminal CRLF.
- **Register preimage:** 115,401 bytes; SHA-256
  `eb8c26b66981f95e708c3baa4327700591731b440f373e1c1f7b7886cc83464b`;
  UTF-8 without BOM, LF-only, one terminal LF; endpoint
  `REG-D-021 / EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING`.
- **Correction 1 — final judgment grain:** One final corpus-review interaction
  does not authorize a bulk or undifferentiated disposition. The final package
  must provide one full-judgment surface and individual sponsor response field
  for every candidate member and every independently contestable material fork,
  exception, residual-risk acceptance, or amendment. All fields may be answered
  in one itemized payload. Each accepted member receives a distinct exact-member
  `JPWB-REG-005` conferral decision within one controlled final transaction.
  This preserves `REG-D-013` while moving sponsor attention to one final event.
- **Correction 2 — phase terminology:** Authorization to complete objective
  “wave” checks under `REG-D-021` means documentation-subphase entry, readiness,
  transition, and completion only. It does not satisfy or authorize the full
  executable exit criteria in `JAN-CSAA-000@0.3.0` §15. Wave 2 fixture-oracle
  evidence, Wave 3 executable schemas/types/fixtures/tests, and Wave 4 provider
  qualification execution remain unperformed and separately unauthorized. The
  final documentation corpus must report those predicates as explicit non-pass,
  later-execution allocations.
- **Correction 3 — MANIFEST-002 incompatibility:** The statement that consuming
  `REG-D-021` independently defeated the package's register-identifier
  predicate is withdrawn. `JAN-CSAA-W1-MANIFEST-002@0.2.0` required the actual
  next unreserved identifiers. It cannot execute because `REG-D-021` changed
  its exact frozen register preimage and authority state and expressly withdrew
  it from solicitation. The complete package remains immutable, historical,
  non-conferring, and unexecuted.
- **Assurance clarification 1 — reviewed bytes:** Every candidate-byte change
  after an exact review freeze triggers affected re-review. The sole exception
  is an exact pre-frozen administrative substitution set with enumerated
  operations, source and result digests, no semantic or judgment change,
  independent replay, independent result validation, and ministerial recording
  against exact predicates. Undefined “ministerial corrections without
  re-review” are prohibited.
- **Assurance clarification 2 — role separation:** For the same exact judgment
  surface, author/integrator, adversarial reviewer, integrity/provenance
  validator, final decision authority, and ministerial recorder are distinct
  identities. Editing candidate bytes makes that identity an author for the
  result. The recorder may not supply or reinterpret judgment, and the decision
  authority may not record its own disposition as ministerial recorder.
- **Template clarification:** `REG-D-017` and W0-16 are modified prospectively
  only within the exact intermediate-authorization and documentation-wave scope
  of `REG-D-021`; they are not superseded wholesale. Active review and ledger
  templates must preserve that wording, the five-role separation, individually
  dispositionable final surfaces, and exact reviewed-byte continuity.
- **Disposition:** This entry supersedes `REG-D-021` only where the earlier
  entry or its interpretation record conflicts with the corrections above.
  `REG-D-021` otherwise remains effective: no intermediate sponsor gate is
  restored, all documentation subphases remain commissioned, every member
  remains Draft or Proposed until final conferral, and every no-expansion
  boundary remains in force.
- **Merge target:** Active CSAA review and ledger templates, successor
  non-authoritative working-status records, the completed `JAN-CSAA-000`
  successor, final controlled manifest, exact member-review records, and final
  corpus-review/carriage records.
- **Status:** EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING.

### REG-D-023 — Ratification of JPWB-SPEC-001 v0.1.0
- **Date:** 2026-07-29 · **Type:** DECISION (sponsor conferral) · **Governs:** `JPWB-SPEC-001 Professional Projection and Workbench Surface — Deep Reference Specification`, **version `0.1.0` exactly**.
- **Decision:** **RATIFIED.** `JPWB-SPEC-001` v0.1.0 is recognized as a member of the `JPWB-SPEC-nnn` deep-reference tier established by REG-D-009 and recognized at CON-000 B1. Its obligations bind the surfaces named in its `Applies to` block. This entry is the individual ratification B1 requires, and it discharges the specification's own **D-12** ("Register entry filed"): it names the specification, its authoring grant of 2026-07-28 (the Constitution-Discussion ruling), its twenty-seven §11.4 forks — all ruled under delegated authority and recorded there — and an exact version.
- **The precondition, and how it was established.** §12.3 set the bar at **zero unbound authored obligations in §§0–10**. That criterion is met: every authored obligation in scope names a check in its own paragraph, re-derivable by `bun run spec:obligations`. The path to it is recorded in §12.5 and is itself part of what is being ratified: the residual was reported as 509, then 397, and was in the end **zero** — the figures were successive defects in the measuring script (a matcher blind to four of the document's own fixture namespaces; then a ±1-sentence window that could not reach a trailing `*Verification:*` clause and simultaneously credited each obligation with the *previous* one's fixture). The script now carries selftests for the matcher, the window, and each exclusion strip, and prints both windows so they bracket rather than assert.
- **Ratified WITH its recorded residue, not despite it.** §12.5.3 declares ten remaining detections, hand-classified as non-obligations (multi-line quotations of JCUX/RIWS/CPM master text, a fork-option continuation line, scope disclaimers, a recorded demotion, a Decision record, a table cell, and a record statement). They are deliberately NOT excluded: three further strips would drive the count to zero, and a count driven to zero by adding exclusions measures the exclusions. §12.5's other recorded residues — eleven §11.4 citation slips, two fixture ids with no §10 body (`SPEC-001-NF-76`, `-NF-77`), five unbound disclosure codes, and two §§1–10 corrections owed (§3.7's "thirty e2e specs" against 28 measured; §2.8.4's claim that DOC-003 carries no Question object, contradicted by `JPWB-DOC-003:84`) — are carried forward as editorial debt against v0.1.1. **None is a defect of an obligation**; ratifying with them declared is preferred to ratifying a version that claims to have none.
- **Consequences.** (a) The Reader's note is replaced and the status block reads `RATIFIED`; every `SHALL` in the document is now an obligation rather than a proposal. (b) `SPEC-001-CHK-DRAFT-CITATION` is **RETIRED by this conferral** — it exists solely to require citing sentences to carry the not-ratified status, and that requirement is now false. Its retirement is recorded rather than silent, because a check deleted without a record is indistinguishable from a check that was never run. (c) REG-D-009's pairing requirement stands: this specification is paired with the enforced repository artifacts its `*Verification:*` clauses name, and `JPWB-SPEC-001-DR-001` / `-DR-002` are the roadmaps that produced them.
- **What this does NOT confer.** It does not ratify `JPWB-SPEC-001-DR-001` or `-DR-002` (working references, not canon), does not settle REG-F-006's open DOCS_STRONGER component, and does not confer status on the Constitution-Discussion source documents (`JCUX`, `RIWS`, `CPM`), which remain outside the authority ladder pending the conferral sheet.
- **Merge target:** `JPWB-SPEC-001` §1 status block + Reader's note (landed with this entry). **Status:** EFFECTIVE.

### REG-D-024 — Ratification of the claim-assessment capability (REG-F-044)
- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DECISION (sponsor conferral) · **Governs:** the missing claim-assessment lifecycle measured in **REG-F-044** — the commands that move a `CLAIM` off `OPEN`, and the mechanism that makes the ratified `ClaimSupported` / `ClaimContested` / `ClaimRejected` events reachable.
- **Decision:** **RATIFIED.** The sponsor ratified REG-F-044 on 2026-08-06 with the instruction *"Ratify REG-F-044."* The finding requested a ratification rather than an implementation, on the ground that **the states, the events, and the consuming predicate are all already ratified and only the causing acts were never minted**. That request is granted, and this entry is the conferral.
- **WHAT IS CONFERRED, stated narrowly.** Authority to mint the acts that make the ratified Claim machine reachable, and to make its existing ratified consumers live. **Not** authority to alter `ClaimStatusSchema`'s eight states, the three ratified event payloads, `findContestedClaims`, ASR-8, or §14.1 — those are the fixed points the build must satisfy, not material it may adjust to fit.
- **WHAT IS EXPLICITLY *NOT* CONFERRED, because this is where a ratification of a *capability* differs from a ratification of a *design*.** The conferral does not decide **whether claim status is COMMANDED or DERIVED**, and that question is load-bearing rather than stylistic: the corpus ratified **events with no commands**, which is at least as consistent with status being a **consequence the engine computes** as with an unbuilt command set. Choosing wrongly here would rebuild REG-F-014's shape one layer up — *a governed conclusion computed from a fact supplied by the party it judges* — on the field that is supposed to close it. **The choice is therefore measured, not assumed**, and whatever must be authored is disclosed as authored under the standing delegated authority, in the design record, before anything is built.
- **THE STANDING TRAP THIS BUILD IS MOST EXPOSED TO, recorded in advance so its absence is checkable later.** REG-F-044 exists because a fully specified governed record had no act that gave it a life. **The failure mode of curing that is to mint an act that is trivially self-satisfiable** — the register already carries two: `AuthorityReference`, hollow (REG-E-027), and `AssertClaim` itself, which accepts a claim naming a subject the store has never held. *A command that lets a caller move its own claim to `SUPPORTED` would not close REG-F-044; it would close the register entry while leaving the hole, which is worse than leaving both open.* The design must state plainly whether the gate it produces can be satisfied unilaterally, and if it can, say so rather than shipping it as a gate.
- **Sequence, per standing practice:** design record → implementation roadmap → build. No code precedes the design record; the ratification is authority to proceed, not a shortcut past it.
- **⚠ PREMISE CORRECTED (same day, before any code) — this entry asserted a ratification that does not exist.** *"WHAT IS CONFERRED"* names ~~*"the three ratified event payloads"*~~ among the fixed points the build *"must satisfy, not material it may adjust."* **They are AUTHORED.** RPH-DOC-007 contains **zero** occurrences of `ClaimSupported` / `ClaimContested` / `ClaimRejected`; RPH-DOC-002 §26.5 is a bare name **list**; and all three vocab rows carry, in as many words, *"UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED… **Do NOT treat this sourceSection as proof the shape is ratified.** Ratification pending."* **What IS ratified and does bind the build:** the eight `ClaimStatus` values (§15.1 verbatim), the four event NAMES (§26.5), `ClaimAsserted`'s payload (RPH-DOC-007 §13.1), RPH-EVD-002, ASR-8, §15.2, and PER-3 / PER-7. The conferral of the **capability** stands unchanged; only this premise falls.
  - **HOW IT HAPPENED IS ITSELF THE FINDING — see REG-F-045.** I read the machine's *generated* form, where provenance cannot survive: **120 of 240 vocab rows are self-labelled `RECONSTRUCTED`, 18 machines disclaim a ratified matrix, and `TransitionSpec` has no field to carry either.** *An entry written to warn against authored guesses acquiring borrowed authority acquired one, from the exact mechanism it was warning about, in the same hour.*
- **AND THE WITHHELD QUESTION IS ANSWERED: COMMANDED.** This entry refused to decide COMMANDED vs DERIVED. Measurement decides it, on ratified text I had not found. **RPH-DOC-008 §13 RPH-EVD-002**, verbatim: *"**Given** a claim with no admissible evidence. **When** status is changed to `SUPPORTED`. **Then** the command is rejected."* The corpus ratifies a claim-status-changing **command by presupposition**; only its name was never minted. **JPWB-DOC-003 §9 PER-3** forecloses the alternative — canonical state is mutated *"only through authenticated, authorized, semantically named commands"* — and **PER-7** shuts the projection escape. **REG-F-044's *"corpus-wide grep returns zero"* was true of the IDENTIFIERS and false of the CONCEPT** — the both-directions check it did not run, on a finding whose entire subject was absence.
- **⚠ AND THE TRAP THIS ENTRY RECORDED IN ADVANCE IS SPRUNG: NEITHER CANDIDATE DESIGN PRODUCES A GATE.** Measured by dispatch, ONE actor issuing all nine commands: `CreateAssurancePolicy` (independenceRequirement **DIFFERENT_AGENT**) → `Activate` → `AssertClaim` (subject the store has never held) → `ProposeEvidence` (`producedBy` a HUMAN it is not) → `AdmitEvidence` (its own evidence) → `Request` / `Begin` / `CompleteAssuranceAssessment(SATISFIED)` — **all nine ACCEPTED.** `dispatch` has no authentication stage; `validatorRole` / `evaluatorRole` have **4 writes and 0 reads**; `assertedBy` is honestly bound and read by nothing. **So the build proceeds under exactly the disclosure this entry demanded: it makes the machine REACHABLE and its ratified consumers LIVE, and it does NOT make claim status unforgeable.** Its one non-forgeable refusal is RPH-EVD-002's, because admissibility is a fact the engine holds.
- **Merge target:** Repository — a claim-assessment command set and its consumers; `docs/_working/DESIGN-claim-assessment.md` and its roadmap carry the reasoning. REG-F-044 closes when the build lands, not when this entry is recorded. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING.


### REG-D-025 — Standing directive: the accountability substrate, delegated authoring authority, and the corpus-first method
- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DECISION (sponsor conferral) · **Governs:** how work proceeds on this repository until superseded — the standard it is held to, the authority under which solutions are authored, the method required before authoring, and the act that constitutes acceptance.
- **THE STANDARD, in the sponsor's words:** *"This system we are building needs to be very high assurance in terms of things like accountability, auditability, etc. — who did what, when, why, where, how, etc."* The enterprise scenarios put to the sponsor (a scoped, time-bounded delegation an auditor can reconstruct; a human-authorized approval undone by an agent; a claim left supported on retracted evidence) are recorded as **instructive of the intended bar**, in the sponsor's assessment.
- **THE ALLOCATION — what JPWB builds and what it does not.** *"JPWB is expected to be pluggable into tenancy, org roles, IdP integration, etc. So building the integration points (e.g., by having the ability to track identities and roles, etc.) so that the future Janumi Platform work — which is really responsible for the SaaS and Enterprise products — will just 'plug in'."* And, asked directly whether standalone identity is authenticated or merely declared: ***"Standalone can have an authenticated identity."***
  - **THIS IS A CONFERRAL OF SCOPE AS MUCH AS OF AUTHORITY, AND IT NARROWS THE WORK.** JPWB does not build authentication, tenancy, or an IdP. It builds the **sockets**: identities, roles and authority as first-class governed facts, and a trust boundary at which an externally authenticated principal arrives. That is the allocation JPWB-DOC-002 §1 already ratifies — Platform *"supplies machinery — infrastructure, runtime, identity, services, controls — never domain semantics."* **The directive therefore instructs the implementation of a stated allocation, not the invention of one**, and it settles the reading under which **REG-F-047** is a divergence in every configuration rather than a deferred tier gap.
- **THE METHOD, mandated rather than suggested:** *"it requires adversarial sub-agents to study the document corpus in depth to truly understand what has been specified versus what truly needs authoring and implementation."* The sponsor's ground for it: *"Often it seems that upon closer inspection the document corpus has thought through a lot of these scenarios and details, but it's just that the implementation has not followed as closely."*
  - **THAT HYPOTHESIS IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** In the two days preceding this directive, each of the following was found **already ratified and simply unbuilt**, in every case after the engineer's first instinct was that it needed inventing: ASR-8's evidence-invalidation cascade; the `AuthorityReference` delegation record; RPH-DOC-009 §30.2's saga/controller tier; JPWB-DOC-004 §5's *"derive… from authenticated context, never from a payload's claim about itself"*; RPH-DOC-008 §13 RPH-EVD-002's claim-status command; and the eight-state Claim lifecycle. **The corpus-first default is therefore adopted as a rule of work: assume the answer is in the corpus until it is proved absent, and prove absence by search that is stated and auditable.**
- **THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT, named by the sponsor:** *"Race conditions are an example of something that we should make every attempt to avoid when it is foreseeable that a design path might cause such a situation to occur."* Recorded as a **design-time obligation**, not a testing one: a foreseeable interleaving must be designed out rather than detected later.
- **CORPUS AMENDMENT IS IN SCOPE, BY AMENDMENT AND NOT DELETION.** *"The document corpus while fairly well thought out is not infallible and as such there is a process for making updates to the document corpus when that is the best choice… it's more amending rather than simply deleting from the corpus."* The established mechanism stands: a marked **§0.3 authored clarification** blockquote that preserves the original text, with the amendment recorded here. Never a silent edit of ratified prose.
- **AUTHORIZATION.** *"You will consider my committing the changes to the git repo as my formal authorization and acceptance of the valid changes."* Consistent with the standing arrangement: the build agent generates local commits; the sponsor's commit is concurrence. **Work is therefore to be sequenced in small, individually acceptable increments** — a single large change would force acceptance or rejection of a bundle, which is not what a conferral by commit can meaningfully express.
- **WHAT THIS DOES NOT CONFER**, stated because a broad grant is exactly where an authored guess acquires borrowed authority. It does not license inventing a shape the corpus settles, ratifying by implementation, or closing an elicitation item by building past it. **An authored solution that contradicts ratified text the author did not find is the specific failure this directive exists to prevent** — it occurred three times in the preceding two days and the adversarial pass caught it each time. The pass is therefore a **gate**, not a courtesy: no authored solution lands without it.
- **Merge target:** This entry is the conferral and is complete on recording; the work it authorizes merges into the repository and, where a corpus amendment is the right answer, into the owning artifact under §0.3. **Status:** EFFECTIVE.


### REG-F-052 — I published "3 of 33 wired" in a commit message that named the fourth two paragraphs later, because I counted the routes I had touched instead of measuring which sites declare a revision
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (self-reported; accounting) · **Governs:** how the surface-wiring programme's remaining work is counted, and what counts as satisfying PER-4.
- **THE CONTRADICTION IS INSIDE ONE COMMIT MESSAGE.** `0b21d752` states *"3 of 33 in-scope actions"* and *"REMAINING AND COUNTABLE: 30 of 33"*, and then, describing the batch routes, names **`acceptAgentCandidate` as "the working precedent"** for revision-guarded dispatch. A site cannot be the working precedent for the protection and also be outside the set of sites that have it. **The true figures are 4 wired and 29 remaining.**
- **THE MECHANISM — a count derived from my own activity rather than from the property being counted.** I enumerated "wired" as *"routes I have edited in this increment"*. The property is *"sites that declare a revision they did not re-fetch"*. Those two sets coincide only by accident, and here they did not. This is **[feedback: findings before counts]** in its exact shape: the number was quoted before the population it summarises had been written down.
- **AND THE FOURTH SITE USES A DIFFERENT, STRONGER SHAPE — which is the part worth keeping.** Verified directly rather than accepted from the survey that raised it. `acceptAgentCandidate` (`pwa/[id]/+page.server.ts:316`) calls `commitAuthoringTurn`, which builds a guard vector from `current.base.revisions` — captured by `snapshotRevisions` (`authoring-turn.ts:124-145`) at **turn start**, over the PWA, every PWU-Type, every assurance policy and the conversation — and passes it to `dispatchBatchGuarded` with `mustNotExist` for aggregates that did not yet exist, plus the base event count and per-object content hashes.
  - **This is not a re-fetched tautology**, which was the only reason it might not have counted: the capture point is turn start and the commit happens after an entire agent conversation, so a concurrent write during the turn genuinely conflicts.
  - **So PER-4 has TWO legitimate surface shapes in this repository, not one:** a **form round-trip** for a single act on a single aggregate, and a **session-scoped revision vector** for a multi-aggregate turn whose duration is the protection's whole point. The roadmap must name both, because a wiring survey that recognises only the form shape will misclassify every batch site — and would have reported this one as unwired forever.
- **WHAT THIS DOES NOT EXCUSE.** The per-file split published alongside the count was also wrong and in the same direction — asserted as `pwa/[id]` ~15 and `undertakings/[id]` ~12; measured, the two are **transposed** (12 and 15). Both numbers were estimates presented without the enumeration that would have falsified them.
- **Merge target:** Repository — the roadmap at `docs/_working/ROADMAP-fork22-surface-wiring.md` carries the enumerated population and both shapes. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — CORRECTION RECORDED.


### REG-F-053 — The mutant I offered as proof for /decisions could not have tested the branch I said it tested: deleting an input posts NO key, not an empty string — and extracting the parser then exposed two more defects in it
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (self-reported; verification) · **Governs:** what the `/decisions` red-proof established, and the standard for a red whose mechanism is unconfirmed.
- **THE CAVEAT I RECORDED IS NOW RESOLVED, AND IT RESOLVES AGAINST ME.** Shipping `0b21d752` I noted that a grounding agent had *predicted* one mutant would stay green on a revision-0 argument, that it went red, and that I therefore refused to call the guard "proved for the predicted reason". **The reason is now measured: the prediction was about a wire shape that mutant never produced.**
  - **Deleting a `<input type="hidden">` removes the KEY from the submission entirely**, so `form.get('expectedRevision')` returns **`null`** and the `typeof raw !== 'string'` branch fires. The empty-string branch requires the element to be **present with an empty value** — the shape a broken interpolation produces, not the shape a deleted element produces.
  - So the mutant reddened through the missing-key branch, and **the empty-string guard — the branch I described as "the entire defence" — was never executed by the test I offered as its proof.**
- **THE BRANCH IS UNREACHABLE FROM ANY END-TO-END TEST, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED REVIEW.** Every rendered page supplies the field, so no e2e can produce an empty value without being mutated into producing one. A guard whose only home was a route file therefore had no assertable form. **That, and not reuse, is the argument that moved it** to `$lib/server/optimistic-concurrency.ts`.
- **MEASURED PROPERLY, THE GUARD IS LOAD-BEARING AND THE DEFECT IS A SILENT PASS — worse than the failure I had imagined.** Two mutants on `/baselines`, predicted by line number in advance and confirmed:
  - *empty value + strict parser* → RED at **`baselines.e2e.ts:27`**, the `submit` step — fail-closed, as designed.
  - *empty value + lax parser* (`\d+`→`\d*`) → **`submit` stays GREEN** and RED arrives only at **:31**. Because `createObject` commits `newRevision: alsoEvents.length` (`kit.ts:563`), a freshly created aggregate sits at **revision 0** — and `Number('')` is **0**. The first act on any new aggregate therefore *matches*, and a form that round-tripped nothing is accepted. **The defect hides behind the first step of the only test that drives it.**
- **AND EXTRACTING THE FUNCTION IMMEDIATELY EXPOSED TWO DEFECTS IN IT THAT NO ROUTE-LEVEL TEST COULD HAVE REACHED.** Both were mine, both shipped in `0b21d752`:
  1. **The parser accepted spellings no template can emit.** `Number('0x2')` is 2 and `Number('1e3')` is 1000, and both satisfy `Number.isInteger(n) && n >= 0`. The first run of the new unit test caught it. *No consequence is claimed* — a forged revision only acts if it matches, and a value that matches is the true one — but a parser whose accepted language is wider than its producer's output is where a future divergence between the two hides silently.
  2. **⚠ AND MY FIRST FIX CREATED A GUARD THAT COULD NOT FAIL.** Tightening to `/^\d+$/` made the explicit `if (trimmed === '') return null;` **dead**: the regex already rejects the empty string, so deleting that line changed no behaviour and no test could have told. **I only found it by asking which mutant would redden it — which is the practice working, one increment after [feedback: a control that cannot fail] was recorded.** The line is gone and the rule is stated once, which puts the defect exactly **one character** away and makes the mutant precise.
- **A THIRD HOLLOW, ALSO MINE, ALSO FROM THE SAME COMMIT.** `refuse()` returned an RPH `code` alongside the message *so a CONFLICT would be distinguishable from a state-machine refusal* — and **nothing rendered it**. The distinction existed only in the comment claiming it existed. Both surfaces now render a stale-page instruction on `RPH_REVISION_CONFLICT`, because *"someone changed this while you were looking at it"* and *"this cannot be done"* are different instructions to a professional.
- **THE GENERAL FORM, per [feedback: ask the general form].** A red whose **mechanism** is unconfirmed is evidence that *something* is load-bearing, never evidence of *which thing*. Three of the four defects above sat behind a mutant that was genuinely red. **The rule adopted: when a mutant's predicted mechanism and its observed mechanism are not shown to coincide, the guard is UNPROVEN — and the way to close it is a prediction stated at the granularity of the failing assertion, not of the suite.**
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed with this increment. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT.


### REG-F-054 — "Who did what" is unanswerable by construction: every human act in the workbench is one hardcoded person who does not exist — and the finding I set out to write was false
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (self-reported; accountability) · **Governs:** the trust boundary, and the strength at which its absence may be stated.
- **⚠ THE FINDING I INTENDED WAS "AN AGENT CAN FORGE HUMAN AUTHORITY," AND MEASURED, THAT IS NOT TRUE TODAY.** The authoring broker defaults to `actorType: 'AGENT'` (`broker.ts:241`) and `makeAuthoringBroker` never overrides it; the surface stamps `HUMAN`. **No in-process caller forges.** The forgeability is **architectural — nothing prevents it — not live.** Recorded because I was one commit from filing the stronger claim, and it would have been false in exactly the way [feedback: absence of evidence] warns about, in reverse: a *presence* asserted from a capability rather than measured from a caller.
- **WHAT IS THE CASE NEEDS NO EXPLOIT, AND IS WORSE.** `issuedBy: { actorId: 'ui-user', actorType: 'HUMAN', displayName: 'Workbench User' }` is **hardcoded at five sites** — the command envelope (`workbench.ts:171`), decision authority (`decisions:52`), execution provenance (`undertakings/[id]:718`, `:828`), and the assurance evaluator (`:863`). **There is one name for every human act.** The sponsor's standard opens *"who did what"*; the record cannot answer it — not because someone lied, but because nothing ever asked.
- **AND THE RATIFIED GATE'S ABSENCE IS ITSELF THE DIVERGENCE.** **PER-3** (DOC-003 §9, canon): *"Canonical state is mutated only through **authenticated, authorized**, semantically named commands…"*, whose SCOPE clause makes *"the **existence and completeness** of the gate"* the semantic requirement. `Engine.dispatch` (`command-bus.ts:96-260`) runs six stages — identity presence, envelope schema, payload hash, idempotency, payload schema, route. **Authenticate and authorize are not among them**, and no parameter exists on which an authenticated principal could arrive. So the divergence is established without any exploit, which is the correct footing for it.
- **GUARDS BUILT ON AN UNVERIFIED CLAIM INHERIT ITS WEAKNESS — INCLUDING ONE I WROTE THE DAY BEFORE.** The REG-E-031 symmetric revoke guard (`governance.ts:466`) turns entirely on `command.issuedBy.actorType === 'HUMAN'`, with no redundancy. **Measured:** the same `actorId: 'bot-1'` is **UNAUTHORIZED / RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT** declaring `AGENT`, and **ACCEPTED** declaring `HUMAN`; the record then reads `{"actorId":"bot-1","actorType":"HUMAN"}`. *(My first probe's control was vacuous — `AI_AGENT` is not an `ActorType` member, so it was refused by envelope schema validation and never reached the guard. Re-run with `AGENT`. A control that fails for the wrong reason proves nothing, which is the anatomy this register keeps recording.)* **The guard is not wrong; its input is untrusted.** Same for REG-F-014's remedy: making the declared authority agree with the issuer does not make either true when both come from one envelope.
- **NOT TIER-CONDITIONAL, ON THE SPONSOR'S OWN EVIDENCE.** REG-E-006: *"All three have human and agent identity; standalone can authenticate. **REG-F-047's divergence therefore has no deployment in which it is correct**, and the remedy is uniform."*
- **Merge target:** Repository — `docs/_working/DESIGN-trust-boundary.md` carries the design (D-0..D-5). **Status:** EFFECTIVE — DESIGN LANDED, BUILD PENDING.


### REG-F-055 — The strongest rung of the independence ladder does not compare identities, so a professional evaluating their own work is "independent"
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (measured) · **Governs:** ASR-13's `HUMAN` rung and its relationship to INV-5.
- **MEASURED against `checkIndependence` (`packages/rph-assurance/src/assurance-rules.ts:184-216`):**
  | requirement | producer | evaluator | result |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `HUMAN` | `ui-user` | `ui-user` | **`{ independent: true }`** |
  | `DIFFERENT_AGENT` | `ui-user` | `ui-user` | `false — same agent identity` |
  | `DIFFERENT_AGENT` | ids absent | ids absent | `false` (fail-closed) |
- **THE IDENTITY-COMPARISON RUNGS ARE CORRECT.** `differs(k)` requires both sides present *and* unequal, so an absent id fails closed rather than passing vacuously — the right direction, and worth recording as working.
- **THE `HUMAN` RUNG NEVER COMPARES.** `return evaluator.actorType === 'HUMAN' ? { independent: true } : fail('evaluator is not human')` (`:205-208`). It asks whether the evaluator is *human*, not whether they are *someone else*. Combined with REG-F-054's single hardcoded `ui-user` on both sides, the top of the ladder is satisfied by self-evaluation.
- **⚠ FILED AS AN ELICITATION ITEM, NOT PATCHED, AND THE RESTRAINT IS THE POINT.** ASR-13's ladder reads *"separate invocation → different agent → different model/provider → **human or organizational independence**"*. One defensible reading is that this rung asserts the evaluator's **nature** (a human rather than a machine) while `ORGANIZATIONALLY_INDEPENDENT` carries **distinctness** — on which the implementation is correct as written. The other is that "independence" means what it says on every other rung. **INV-5 — exec ≠ assurance — is the invariant that makes this consequential**, and a one-line guess on the assurance separation is precisely how an authored interpretation acquires the standing of canon. See **REG-E-032**.
- **Merge target:** Repository, after ruling. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — DISCLOSED, AWAITING REG-E-032.


### REG-E-032 — Does ASR-13's `HUMAN` independence rung require a DIFFERENT human, or only a human?
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** ELICITATION · **Raised from:** REG-F-055.
- **THE QUESTION.** `ASR-13` (DOC-003 §8.5, canon) graduates independence *"separate invocation → different agent → different model/provider → human or organizational independence, scaling with claim materiality"*, and requires the runtime to verify *"**actual** independence"*. The implemented `HUMAN` rung checks only `evaluator.actorType === 'HUMAN'`. **Does satisfying the ladder's top rung require the evaluator to be a different person from the producer?**
- **WHY IT IS NOT OBVIOUS.** Read as a NATURE claim — *a human, not a machine, must look at this* — the implementation is exactly right, and distinctness is `ORGANIZATIONALLY_INDEPENDENT`'s job. Read as an INDEPENDENCE claim, a rung named `HUMAN` sitting at the top of a ladder whose every other rung compares two identities ought to compare two identities. **Both readings are defensible on the ratified text**, which is why this is a sponsor question.
- **WHAT MAKES IT CONSEQUENTIAL.** `INV-5` — execution ≠ assurance — is a founding invariant of this engine, and the highest-materiality claims are exactly the ones that select the top rung. Under the first reading, the most consequential work in the system is the work whose reviewer may be its author.
- ***Default: the implementation stands unchanged and the gap is disclosed (REG-F-055).*** Adding a producer/evaluator comparison is one line and is deliberately not taken. It would refuse every currently-passing HUMAN assessment, and it would encode a reading of the assurance separation that the corpus does not compel.
- **Status:** OPEN.


### REG-F-056 — The trust boundary's shape is decided by REG-Q-004's binding safe default, not by the clause I framed the question around — and the increment as I scoped it would have made forgeability LIVE
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (design; corpus-first) · **Governs:** the D-1 API shape and its scope.
- **I ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION, AND THE CONCESSION IS WHAT MAKES THE ANSWER TRUSTWORTHY.** I framed it as *"does a credential carried on the command envelope violate JPWB-DOC-004 §5?"* **It does not.** §5 prohibits a **source of derivation** — *"never from a payload's claim about itself"* — not a location of bytes: under that design the engine derives the principal from the port's **verification result**, and the token is an input to authentication rather than principal context. **PER-3 is neutral too**: its SCOPE clause cedes *"the exact pipeline ordering **and envelope**"* as repository shapes and mandates only the gate's existence. Both anchors I leaned on decline to decide.
- **WHAT DECIDES IT BINDS TODAY AND I HAD NOT CONSULTED IT.** **REG-005 §1**: an `OPEN` entry is *"live; **any recorded safe default binds**"*, and §3 makes every Section B entry OPEN unless stated. **REG-Q-004**'s safe default: *"Serialize the repository's generated envelopes exactly. **Enforce tenant/principal scoping through authenticated transport**, repository, and RLS context. **A public-envelope addition requires a new schema version and coordinated code/storage/test change**; never create an unscoped path."* **JPWB-SPEC-001 §11.4.2 restates it as a ratified SHALL.** The enforcement locus is named — the transport, expressly not the envelope. **A binding safe default on an OPEN elicitation item is as governing as a ruling, and I have been treating OPEN items as absences.**
- **AND THE BLAST-RADIUS ARGUMENT INVERTS UNDER MEASUREMENT.** `DomainCommand` is a closed versioned wire contract (`additionalProperties: false`, `$id …DomainCommand:1`) and therefore **CON-000 B1 shape authority**, so an envelope credential is a `DomainCommand:2` **contract change** — larger than an in-process signature change, not smaller, and DOC-004 §5 says an agent adding a field ad hoc to an envelope *"has left its delegation"*. It would also version a wire with **no traffic**: measured, **no command envelope crosses a process boundary anywhere in this repository** — every page route reads `request.formData()` and the envelope is built server-side. The real trust boundary is the HTTP request into the SvelteKit server action, exactly where REG-Q-004 puts it.
- **⚠ AND THE INCREMENT AS SCOPED WOULD HAVE INVERTED ITS OWN FINDING.** `broker.ts:241` defaults the authoring agent to `actorType: 'AGENT'` and nothing overrides it. **Once the engine stamps `issuedBy` from the session principal, `this.actor` (`broker.ts:792`) becomes dead and every agent-authored command silently acquires the HUMAN session identity.** REG-F-054 established that forgeability is *architectural, not live*; **D-1 without a distinct AGENT credential for the broker would have made it live**, in the increment written to close it — and it is the exact back door REG-E-027 keeps shut by making delegation human-to-deputy. D-1's scope is enlarged accordingly.
- **THREE MORE HAZARDS RECORDED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED.** (1) `verif/unread-refusal-guard.ts` monkey-patches `Engine.prototype.dispatch` with a **one-argument** signature in `setupFiles` for **every** project — an OPTIONAL credential would be dropped suite-wide, leaving every test passing and the gate exercising nothing. **The credential must be required positional with no default.** (2) Seed paths run before any session exists and are **80.7% of all dispatches**; they need an explicit SYSTEM principal, and handing them `ui-user` would defeat D-2 at the largest site by volume. (3) `dispatchBatchGuarded` already has four positional parameters — the credential goes **first**, or it reproduces the *"accepted and dropped on the floor"* defect `dispatch-expected-revision.test.ts` exists to catch.
- **THE MIGRATION IS MECHANICAL, AND THE NUMBER I HAD BEEN QUOTING WAS THE WRONG UNIT.** **284 executed dispatch sites in 102 files** is the planning figure — not 30,123 dispatches (75.9% of which come from one line), and not the 16,609 I quoted, which was neither. 91.1% of construction sites are reused builders; **88 of 102 files need one edit each**; 14 files hold 190 sites; ~8 need real thought.
- **AND D-1 DOES NOT CLOSE THE EVALUATOR=EXECUTOR HOLE.** `checkIndependence` reads producer and evaluator from **state and payload**, never from `command.issuedBy`. Authenticating the *dispatcher* does not authenticate the *evaluator named in a payload*. REG-F-055 / REG-E-032 stay open, and this is recorded here so *"the trust boundary landed"* is never cited as having closed them.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `docs/_working/DESIGN-trust-boundary.md` §2/§4. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — DESIGN SETTLED, BUILD PENDING.


### REG-E-033 — What error code does an authentication refusal carry, and does `issuedBy` become optional on the command envelope?
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** ELICITATION · **Raised from:** REG-F-056. Two questions, because both are sponsor acts and neither can be taken under delegated authority.
- **(a) THE CODE.** `packages/rph-contracts/src/errors.ts` ratifies **fifteen** codes and **none** means *unauthenticated*. `RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT` is category AUTHORIZATION and fits D-5's authorize stage, not D-1's authenticate stage. **The precedent is recorded verbatim in the code**: `command-bus.ts:234-238` says of `RPH_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` that it *"is NOT among the ratified fifteen — minting one is a sponsor act"*, and the remedy taken there was to use a ratified code and carry the label in the message. *Default: follow that precedent — refuse with a ratified code and name the condition in the message. Do not quietly extend the enum.*
- **(b) THE ENVELOPE FIELD.** `issuedBy` is REQUIRED in `domainCommandSchema` on a `z.strictObject`, and that schema is enforced in production since REG-F-011. **While it stays required, 100 test files keep authoring an issuer**, and the laundering shape — a port that echoes the caller's claim — stays one keystroke away in every one of them. Making it `.optional()` is a generated-contract change under **B1 shape authority** (`bun run gen` + a record here), not a code edit. *Default: it stays REQUIRED, and D-1 ships with the engine's stamp overwriting it and a standing guard as the only thing preventing the echo — **the disclosed posture, not a discovery**.*
- **WHY THEY ARE ASKED TOGETHER.** Answering (b) *yes* removes the laundering surface structurally and makes (a) more urgent, because a command with no issuer that fails authentication must refuse with something legible. Answering (b) *no* is survivable and is the recorded default.
- **Status:** **DISPOSITIONED 2026-08-07 by REG-D-027** (build agent, under delegated authority; the sponsor re-conferred these two questions expressly). (a) `RPH_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` minted, category `AUTHORIZATION` — and the premise of the question, that minting is a sponsor act, is itself refuted by ratified text: see **REG-F-057**. (b) `issuedBy` becomes optional, in the increment that stamps it and not before.


### REG-D-027 — REG-E-033 DISPOSITIONED under delegated authority: a distinct authentication code is minted, and `issuedBy` becomes optional when — and only when — the engine can stamp it
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** DECISION (build agent, under the delegated authority conferred by REG-D-025 and re-conferred expressly for these two questions; the sponsor's commit is concurrence) · **Governs:** REG-E-033 (a) and (b). **Closes REG-E-033.**
- **THE SPONSOR'S ADDED CONSTRAINT, verbatim:** *"whatever solution should be consistent with the three tiers of product currently being implemented: standalone, SaaS, and Enterprise customers. And I recall that we said standalone would have some sort of identity established as well."* Both rulings below are uniform across the three; **neither introduces a tier-conditional branch**, which CON-000 **AX-10** forbids in substance — *"Deployment topology MUST NOT change professional meaning; a runtime is conformant only if it preserves the semantics, whatever its stack."*
- **(a) RULING — `RPH_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` IS MINTED, category `AUTHORIZATION`.** The refusal when the acting principal cannot be established from authenticated context.
  - **WHY A DISTINCT CODE RATHER THAN REUSING `RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT`, and the reason is the sponsor's own standard.** *"I do not know who you are"* and *"I know who you are and you may not"* are different facts, and a governed record that cannot distinguish them fails *"who did what"* at the first clause. **It is also the distinction each tier routes differently** — in SaaS and Enterprise an unauthenticated command is a security event; in standalone it is a misconfiguration — so a distinct code is exactly what lets any host route correctly **without string-matching a message**. The alternative (the WP-11 discipline: reuse a ratified code, carry the label in the message) was the precedent and is rejected here *because* it forces every tier's tooling to parse prose.
  - **NO NEW CATEGORY.** `RphErrorCategorySchema` is generated and canon-facing; `AUTHORIZATION` carries this honestly, and a second, larger shape change buys nothing the code itself does not already carry.
  - **DISCLOSED AS AUTHORED**, in the enum, in the vocabulary entry and here — *not* blended in beside the fifteen as though DOC-007 §25.1 had named it.
- **(b) RULING — `issuedBy` BECOMES OPTIONAL, IN THE INCREMENT THAT STAMPS IT AND NOT BEFORE.** Absent ⇒ the engine stamps from the resolved principal. Present ⇒ treated as a **checked claim**: a declared issuer that disagrees with the principal is **REFUSED, not silently corrected**, so a forgery attempt is a recorded refusal rather than an invisible overwrite.
  - **WHY OPTIONAL BEATS BOTH NEIGHBOURS.** Leaving it required and overwriting silently keeps the laundering shape mandatory in all 100 test files that author an issuer. Removing it outright is the only structurally-unlaunderable option, but the issuer is read *before* a principal is known — the REG-F-011 identity check, the logger, the idempotency receipt — so removal is not currently coherent. **Optional makes the laundering surface OPT-IN, and turns every existing declaration from a SOURCE into an ASSERTION THE ENGINE CHECKS** — which is strictly better than deleting them.
  - **⚠ AND THE ORDER IS PART OF THE RULING, not an implementation detail.** Widening the field before the engine can stamp would make every handler read of `command.issuedBy` optional while the only correct replacement does not exist. **Stamp first, then widen.** Implementing (b) ahead of D-1 would soften the field with nothing standing behind it.
- **TIER-UNIFORMITY, stated concretely so a later reader can check it.** JPWB never authenticates — DOC-002 §1 allocates identity machinery to Platform and JPWB *consumes a verified identity*. One `Principal` shape, one opaque branded `Credential`, per-tier **adapters** and no per-tier engine branch. **Standalone gets a real authenticated local identity, not a bypass** (the sponsor: *"Standalone can have an authenticated identity"*), and REG-D-026's scope axes are *present and singular* there, never absent.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `packages/rph-contracts` (landed with this entry: vocabulary, enum, category map, regenerated schemas); `docs/_working/DESIGN-trust-boundary.md`. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — (a) LANDED, (b) DECIDED AND SEQUENCED INTO D-1.


### REG-F-057 — "Minting an error code is a sponsor act" is an authored constraint contradicted by ratified text, and the codebase has been obeying it
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (self-reported; authored-claim class) · **Governs:** who may extend `RphErrorCodeSchema`.
- **THE CLAIM, verbatim, in production code** (`packages/rph-application/src/command-bus.ts:234-235`): *"`RPH_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` would be the natural code and is **NOT among the ratified fifteen — minting one is a sponsor act**."* Its consequence shipped: that refusal carries a semantically wrong code with its real meaning in a message string.
- **IT IS CONTRADICTED BY RATIFIED TEXT, IN THREE PLACES.** **REG-D-004** — a sponsor-participated DECISION — states: *"The repository — generated contracts, schemas, migrations, conformance tests — is authoritative for exact shapes: wire envelopes, JSON schemas, enum spellings, ID prefixes, **error codes**."* **DOC-004 §2.3** routes the reader to the repository *"When a task needs… **error codes**"*. **DOC-004 §5** routes the change itself: *"extending an enum — is a contract change: new schema version, coordinated code/storage/fixture/test change, **through the repository's contract procedure**."* And **DOC-004 §8.2** enumerates what IS reserved — Constitutional, Vocabulary, Hypothesis layer, Canon text — granting *"**Code** — full agency within this protocol."* **Shapes are not among the reserved layers.**
- **AND THE PREMISE — "the ratified fifteen" — TRACES TO HISTORICAL MATERIAL.** `errors.ts` cited *"(DOC-007 §25.1)"*, and RPH-DOC-007 is historical by CON-000 B1. **Measured:** `RPH_` occurs **zero** times in CON-000, DOC-001, DOC-002, DOC-003 and DOC-004. The two canon artifacts that do name codes (REG-005, SPEC-001) cite `errors.ts` — **they read the repository, they do not confer on it.** This is REG-F-049's shape once more, and it produced an authored prohibition rather than an authored permission, which is the harder direction to notice.
- **⚠ A NEAR-MISS OF MY OWN, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME ERROR IN REVERSE.** Mid-investigation I found SPEC-001 naming **33** distinct `RPH_*` codes and concluded *"canon names 18 beyond the enum, so the set is not closed."* **That was wrong.** SPEC-001 names them as **non-members** — *"`RPH_BINDING_NOT_AUTHORIZED` is not a member. `RPH_PRECONDITION_UNSATISFIED` is not a member"* — in a census of kernel MARKERS, an open vocabulary it deliberately refuses to close (*"Six is a sample, not the set"*). I inferred ratification from an occurrence count without reading the context, which is the trap this register exists to catch, committed while investigating an instance of it.
- **WHAT SETTLES IT BEYOND ARGUMENT: canon already made this exact choice under delegation.** SPEC-001 §11.4.15 (FORK-15) enumerated option (B) — *"Promote the markers into `RphErrorCodeSchema`, growing the ratified enum from fifteen to twenty-one"* — and **ruled under delegated authority**, rejecting it on **cost and arithmetic**, never on reservation; its own register relation says *"Option (B) would have been a repository shape change requiring the migration discipline REG-D-004 implies."* And SPEC-001 §6.1's `SHALL NOT` binds **that specification** (*"**This specification** SHALL NOT add members to… `RphErrorCode`"*), a self-limitation of the Surface, not a reservation to the sponsor.
- **REMEDY LANDED:** the count is out of the `errors.ts` header (it was load-bearing in three places), the authority is stated there instead, and `RPH_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` is minted through the contract procedure — vocabulary, enum, total category map, regenerated schemas, with the JSON-schema drift test enforcing the coupling. **`RPH_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` is NOT minted here**; that refusal is a separate question and is left as it stands rather than swept along.
- **Merge target:** Repository — landed. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — CLOSED.


### REG-F-058 — REG-D-026 will trip a ratified reopening trigger, and the check that is supposed to fire it does not exist
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (ratified obligation, unmet) · **Governs:** the sequencing of D-1/D-3 against JPWB-SPEC-001 FORK-2.
- **THE RATIFIED INSTRUCTION.** SPEC-001 §11.4.2 (FORK-2, ratified via REG-D-023, under which *"every `SHALL` in the document is now an obligation rather than a proposal"*) records: *"**An exit condition is recorded, and this ruling is not a permanent answer.** The ruling rests on a measured absence — `organizationId` at zero occurrences — not on a principle that URLs must be flat. **The fork SHALL be revisited when a tenancy model is commissioned**, and the reopening trigger is the first non-zero result of `SPEC-001-NF-69`'s tenancy-absence limb."* And the limb: *"assert that `grep -rn "organizationId" apps packages` … returns **0**; a non-zero result SHALL NOT fail the build — it SHALL **reopen this fork**."*
- **REG-D-026 REQUIRES `organizationId` AS A REQUIRED ENVELOPE FIELD.** So the moment D-1/D-3 lands, that grep goes non-zero and **FORK-2 reopens by ratified instruction.** Re-measured today: still **0** across `packages` and `apps`. N-9 itself carries the same 0-occurrence measurement in its own text, and §11.2's deliberately-unspecified table has an `organizationId` row whose stated basis is that the segment has zero occurrences. **Three ratified premises fall together, on a change this programme has already ruled to make.**
- **⚠ AND `SPEC-001-NF-69` DOES NOT EXIST.** It occurs only inside SPEC-001's own prose; no fixture, no check. SPEC-001 makes defining it *"a carriage obligation"*. **So the trigger is narrative, not mechanical** — which means the reopening would depend on someone remembering, and a ratified SHALL discharged silently is indistinguishable from one nobody ran.
- **NOT A REASON TO DEFER REG-D-026.** The ruling's own argument stands: a scope that does not exist cannot be plugged into, and no backfill can invent which organization a past act belonged to. **The obligation is to reopen the fork deliberately, not to avoid tripping it.**
- **WHAT D-3 MUST THEREFORE CARRY, or it discharges a ratified SHALL by accident:** (1) a recorded act reopening FORK-2 with its measured premise restated; (2) `SPEC-001-NF-69` **defined and executable**, so the trigger fires by measurement rather than by memory; (3) an entry here recording both.
- **Merge target:** Repository (the check) + this register (the reopening) + SPEC-001 §0.3 amendment if the fork's ruling changes. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — OPEN, BLOCKING D-3.


### REG-F-059 — I stopped myself committing the exact defect class I spent the week closing: a port with no consumer
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (self-reported; process) · **Governs:** what may land ahead of its consumer.
- **WHAT HAPPENED.** Building D-1 I wrote `packages/rph-ports/src/ports/authentication.ts` — `Credential`, `Principal`, `AuthenticationOutcome`, `AuthenticationPort`, fully commented and correct — and then could not migrate its 296 call sites within the increment. The tempting move was to land the port alone, *"disclosed as awaiting its consumer."*
- **THAT IS A HOLLOW, AND I HAVE FILED FOUR OF THEM THIS WEEK.** `causationId` was declared on both envelopes and persisted to the events table with **zero writers**. `ActorReference.roleId` ships with zero writes and zero reads. `AuthorityReference` is required on three persisted object types with **not one of its six fields read anywhere** (REG-E-027). `ObjectRow.revision` was mine, declared with a comment claiming every helper supplied it while one supplied nothing. **Every one of them was landed by someone who intended the consumer to follow.** *"Disclosed"* is what each of them would have said.
- **THE RULE ADOPTED, and it is narrower than "never land an interface early":** an artifact may land ahead of its consumer only when **something exercises it in the same commit**. A port with a reference implementation and a test that drives it is a contract. A port that only compiles is a hollow with better comments. The distinction is whether a mutant in it can be killed.
- **WHAT I DID INSTEAD:** deleted the file and landed the design. The 100 lines cost minutes to rewrite; a fifth hollow in the register would have cost the register's credibility on the exact axis this programme is about.
- **⚠ AND THE ATTEMPT PAID FOR ITSELF ANYWAY** — it produced the session-receiver refinement (see below), which the positional design would not have surfaced. **Recorded because "I could not finish, so I landed the part that compiles" is a decision shape that will recur**, and the answer is now written down.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `docs/_working/DESIGN-trust-boundary.md`. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — RULE ADOPTED.


### REG-D-028 — D-1's dispatch seam is a SESSION RECEIVER, not a positional credential (delegated refinement under REG-D-025)
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** DECISION (build agent, under delegated authority; the sponsor's commit is concurrence) · **Governs:** the shape REG-D-027 and REG-F-056 settled in principle. **Refines, does not supersede:** the credential still never touches the envelope.
- **THE SHAPE.** `engine.as(credential)` returns a dispatcher; `dispatch`, `dispatchBatch` and `dispatchBatchGuarded` move onto it and are **removed from `EngineHandle`**. A caller cannot reach a dispatch without first presenting a credential.
- **WHY IT IS BETTER, AND SIZE IS NOT THE ARGUMENT.** It closes by construction the two hazards the positional form left as things to remember:
  - **The broker inversion** (REG-F-056's showstopper): the broker holds `engine.as(agentCredential)`, so its AGENT identity is what it *is* rather than a default field that falls dead the moment the engine starts stamping.
  - **The commit replay**: a turn's recorded commands replay through **the session that recorded them**. Under the positional form `commitAuthoringTurn` would have re-stamped every agent-authored command with the approving human's principal — attributing an agent's work to a person who only pressed *accept*, which is REG-F-014's shape arriving through the door built to close it.
  - It also retires the `dispatchBatchGuarded` argument-ordering hazard outright: there is no positional argument to append or drop.
- **THE MEASUREMENT, and it is a consequence rather than a reason:** 296 call sites across 104 files. Rebinding the receiver once per file leaves the call sites untouched — `pwu.test.ts`'s 87 dispatches need **one** edit.
- **⚠ ONE HOLE, NAMED SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED:** `ontology-delivery-census.test.ts` builds partial handles with `as unknown as EngineHandle` double casts, which defeat the type checker. Removing `dispatch` from the handle makes every *other* unmigrated site a compile error; **those two stay silent and must be migrated by hand.** A design whose claim is "no silent bypass" must name its own.
- **Merge target:** Repository — D-1. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING.


### REG-F-060 — The blast-radius census counted CALLS and CONSTRUCTIONS, and the dominant cost turned out to be TYPE ANNOTATIONS, which no census took
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (measured; estimation) · **Governs:** the D-1 migration's cost, and how a census is scoped.
- **THE GATE ITSELF WORKS, MEASURED.** A scratch pass added `Engine.as(credential)`, made the three dispatch methods private and required the port at construction. **Every unmigrated site became a compile error**, which is the property REG-D-028 claims. The tree was then reverted — the work could not finish in one increment and a partly-migrated tree cannot be committed.
- **THE COUNT THAT WAS WRONG IS THE ONE THAT MATTERED.** The blast-radius analysis (REG-F-056) reported *"88 of 102 files need ONE edit each"*, having enumerated **296 dispatch call sites** and **137 construction sites**. A codemod over the constructions rewrote **117 files** and left **473 type errors in `rph-application` alone**:
  - **83** — the missing `./testing` subpath export. One line in one `package.json`.
  - **240 + 84 = 324** — variables **annotated** `: Engine` now holding an `AuthedEngine`.
  - **28** — a local `DispatchLike` helper parameter type. **2** — hand fixes.
- **⚠ SO ~324 OF 473, THE CLEAR MAJORITY, COME FROM A POPULATION NOBODY ENUMERATED.** Both censuses asked *"where is the engine CALLED"* and *"where is the engine CONSTRUCTED"*. Neither asked ***"where is the engine's TYPE WRITTEN DOWN"*** — and changing a type's surface is paid for at every place that type is named, not at every place it is used. **The general form: a census scoped to call sites prices a behaviour change; a type change is priced by declaration sites, and those are a different population.** This is [feedback: findings before counts] one level up — not a number quoted before its findings, but a number quoted over the wrong *set*, which no amount of care about the arithmetic would have caught.
- **NOT A REASON TO CHANGE THE DESIGN.** The annotation edits are mechanical and the recipe is now measured and written into `docs/_working/DESIGN-trust-boundary.md` §4, in the order that stops the 83 module errors masking the rest. D-1 becomes a mostly-deterministic increment with known counts instead of an exploratory one.
- **AND THE SECOND REVERT IN TWO INCREMENTS IS RECORDED AS SUCH.** REG-F-059 declined to land a consumer-less port; this one declines to land a half-migrated tree. Both refusals are correct and both cost an increment. **The lesson is about SEQUENCING, not about caution:** an all-or-nothing change whose cost is not yet measured should have its cost measured in a throwaway pass *first* — which is exactly what this pass now provides, and what the next one will spend.
- **Merge target:** Repository — D-1. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — RECIPE RECORDED.


### REG-D-026 — Tenant and organization scope: carried, not governed (delegated ruling under REG-D-025)
- **Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Type:** DECISION (build agent, under the delegated authority conferred by REG-D-025; sponsor's commit is concurrence) · **Governs:** the apparent conflict between two ratified texts over whether tenant and organization scope belongs in this repository.
- **⚠ CITATION CORRECTED 2026-08-07 — N-9 IS §1.4, NOT §11.2, AND I INHERITED THE ERROR RATHER THAN CHECKING IT.** This entry cites *"JPWB-SPEC-001 §11.2 N-9"* below. **N-9 is a row of §1.4 Non-goals** (heading at `:392`; N-9 at `:412`); §11 is *"Forks and deliberately unspecified"*, and `grep -n "N-9"` over the document returns exactly three lines, none of them inside §11.2's body. **The substance of this ruling is unaffected** — the quoted text is byte-exact and the reading stands. **How it happened is the finding:** SPEC-001 itself refers to *"§11.2's N-9"* twice (`:7522`, `:7596`), and I took the ratified document's own back-reference as the section rather than reading the heading above the rule. That is [feedback: never infer a section from a line] in a form the rule does not literally cover — I inferred a section from *another citation* — and the corrected rule is: **the only authority for a section number is the heading above the text.** The document's two self-references are a corpus defect and are filed as such; they are not amended here, because amending SPEC-001 is a §0.3 act and not this entry's business.
- **THE APPARENT CONFLICT.** **JPWB-DOC-003 §3** (Common object contract) requires that every authoritative semantic object carry *"…creating/updating actor, time, and provenance; **tenant and organization scope**; authority or an authority reference where professional effect requires it…"* **JPWB-SPEC-001 §11.2 N-9** (ratified) states: *"**N-9 · Multi-tenancy and the organization segment.** Organization identity, tenancy boundaries, and cross-organization coordination are **not governed**."* The code satisfies neither — `tenantId`/`organizationId` occur **zero** times.
- **THE RULING: THEY DO NOT CONFLICT, AND THE APPEARANCE OF CONFLICT IS A CATEGORY ERROR I MADE IN RAISING IT.** N-9 declines to govern three *semantic* questions — **what an organization is**, **where a tenancy boundary falls**, and **how organizations coordinate**. §3 requires objects to **carry a scope**. *Carrying a scope is not defining the semantics of the scope*, any more than carrying an `actor` field commits this repository to governing what a person is. Both texts stand unamended.
- **THEREFORE: CARRY THE FIELD, GOVERN NONE OF THE SEMANTICS.**
  - `tenantId` and `organizationId` join the **object envelope** as **required** fields, derived from authenticated context and never from a payload (JPWB-DOC-004 §5), exactly as `issuedBy` must be (REG-F-047).
  - In a **single-organization deployment they take a well-known constant** — *present and singular*, not absent. **That distinction is the whole ruling:** "one organization" and "no notion of organization" are indistinguishable in a record that has no field, and only the first can later be plugged into.
  - This repository continues to govern **none** of N-9's three questions. Cross-organization semantics remain uninvented, and REG-Q-048's safe default — *"single-organization scope holds"* — is honoured rather than overridden: it now simply has an id.
- **WHY THIS READING AND NOT THE OTHER, stated because the alternative is defensible on its face.** One could read N-9 as making §3's clause vacuous for now and defer the field. **Rejected on the directive's own terms.** REG-D-025 confers scope to build *"the integration points… so that the future Janumi Platform work will just plug in."* **A scope that does not exist cannot be plugged into**, and retrofitting scope onto a governed record is the most invasive retrofit available: every historical object would lack it, and **no backfill can invent which organization a past act belonged to.** Deferring the field does not defer the cost; it converts a schema addition today into an unanswerable provenance question later. *"Who did what, when, **where**"* — the sponsor's own standard — is the axis this field carries, and it is the one axis that cannot be reconstructed after the fact.
- **AND IT IS THE CHEAP DIRECTION IF WRONG.** A carried-but-constant scope in a standalone deployment costs two columns and nothing else; the semantics stay unauthored, so nothing has been decided that a later ratification must undo. The reverse error — omitting it — is uncorrectable for every record written in the interim.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `objectEnvelopeShape` and the trust boundary that populates it (INC-E of `docs/_working/DESIGN-accountability-substrate.md`). **JPWB-DOC-003 §3 and JPWB-SPEC-001 N-9 are both unamended**; this entry records that they were read as consistent, and why. **Status:** EFFECTIVE — MERGE PENDING.

---

### REG-F-061 — `ui-user` was never five sites, and the four that survived my fix are the ones that name an actor in a PAYLOAD
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** the D-1 e2e leg — `decisions.e2e.ts` refused with the workbench's own guard.
- **What I recorded earlier:** REG-F-054 named `ui-user` as the hardcoded identity behind "who did what", and the D-1 migration set out to remove it. `workbench.ts`'s `uiCommand` was found and fixed during the fan-out and its comment calls it *"the SIXTH such site"*. That comment is accurate about envelopes and **silent about the population it never looked at.**
- **What was actually still there:** FOUR more, in `decisions/+page.server.ts` (`authority`) and `undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts` (`executedBy` ×2, `evaluator`). Every one of them is a **payload** field, not the command envelope. My census enumerated `issuedBy:` — so it enumerated the envelope, concluded, and reported a count.
- **THIS IS THE SAME ERROR AS f8bcd7c6, AND THAT MAKES IT THE FIFTH INSTANCE THIS WEEK.** That commit stripped `issuedBy` from the seed builders' envelope and left `authority: owner-1` in the payload — *the field the guard actually reads*. I recorded it, named the class ("I looked at a field name, not the concept"), and then **ran a census by field name again eight commits later.** Naming a defect class is not the same as changing the method that produces it.
- **THE GUARD I WROTE IS WHAT CAUGHT IT, AND IT CAUGHT IT BY BREAKING THE PRODUCT.** `proposeDecision` refuses a payload `authority` that is not the stamped issuer (REG-F-014's disposition, deliberately refusing rather than correcting). With the session now `local-professional` and the payload still `ui-user`, **every governance Decision the workbench tried to propose was refused.** The guard was right, the surface was lying, and the lie had been invisible for as long as both sides were fictional together.
- **Disposition — derive, do not declare.** `AuthedEngine` now exposes the resolved `principal`, and `actingActor(session)` projects it onto the ratified `ActorReference`. That is JPWB-DOC-004 §5 performed — *"derive tenant and principal context from authenticated context, never from a payload's claim about itself"* — rather than quoted. It **throws** on an unresolved session (§13.3 fail-closed): a surface that cannot say who is acting has nothing it is entitled to write.
- **What is NOT derived, on purpose:** `completeStep`'s AI arm still declares `{ actorId: 'agent-x', actorType: 'AGENT' }`. That actor is genuinely not the operator pressing the button; deriving it would make the demo assert a human produced AI output. Only the arm that claims to BE the operator was changed.
- **Merge target:** Repository. **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-062 — The floor was healthy; RECORDING it was refused — and the product reported the exact opposite
- **Date:** 2026-08-07 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** nine red e2e specs on `wip/d1-trust-boundary`.
- **The defect:** `recordAssuranceRecordingPlan` declared `issuedBy: opts.actor`, and every caller passed the literal `{ actorId: 'assurance-svc', actorType: 'SERVICE' }`. Under REG-D-027(b) a declared issuer that disagrees with the authenticated principal is REFUSED; the recorder throws on any non-ACCEPTED result. So **every authoring turn that reached assurance died at the first `RequestAssuranceAssessment`.**
- **THE MESSAGE POINTED AT AN INNOCENT DEPENDENCY.** The throw was caught by `runFloorAfterTurn` and classified as `externalDetail`, which the state machine maps to `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL`, which the banner renders as ***"The reviewer call failed (external/operational)."*** The reviewer never failed. Nine specs went red and the product's own diagnosis sent every reader to the assessor configuration. **A catch-all that classifies by WHERE it caught rather than WHAT it caught will name the wrong component every time the cause is internal.**
- **`assurance-svc` WAS NEVER AN IDENTITY.** No authenticator issues it, no credential resolves to it, and its doc comment named it *"the Assurance Service acting on behalf of the host"* — a **delegation asserted in a field and backed by nothing**, which is precisely what `proposeDecision`'s guard refuses one layer up. It was the last self-declared issuer in a production builder; the option is now gone, not merely defaulted.
- **THE OBVIOUS FIX IS WRONG AND THE REASON IS WORTH KEEPING.** Minting an `ASSURANCE_CREDENTIAL` so the record could still say `assurance-svc` was my first design. It breaks the commit: a staged authoring turn replays its recorded commands through **one** session, so a command dispatched into the fork under a different principal yields a different `createdBy` on replay and fails the guarded batch's content-hash postcondition. Per-command principals need a mechanism this repository does not have, and **inventing one to produce a more flattering `createdBy` is not a trade worth making.** The issuer is the caller's session; the identity that carries assurance meaning — the independent evaluator §8.12 checks — was never this field and rides in `validatorResult.executionProvenance`.
- **THE COVERAGE HOLE IS THE REAL FINDING.** `floor.test.ts` sits beside `floor.ts` and tests it thoroughly — every branch of `classifyFloorRemediation`, every derived fact in `identityProvenanceFactsOf`. **Both are pure functions.** It never calls `runPwaFloor`. The module's only engine-facing surface — the one that writes canonical objects — had no unit test at all, which is how a 2,508-test suite stayed green over a floor that could not complete a single run. **A file can be well covered and still leave its only integration seam untouched, and the count will not say so.** `floor-recording.test.ts` now drives the seam under BOTH sessions (human and agent), with mutants measured in both directions: restoring the declared issuer reddens the two recording cases and leaves the control green; making the authenticator permissive reddens ONLY the control.
- **Merge target:** Repository. **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-063 — The coordinator AGG-1 requires and REG-E-029 says the cascade needs is ALREADY DECLARED, with nine states and FIFTEEN arrows that no command can traverse
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, adversarial survey) · **Source:** the completeness critic of the coordination survey; every limb re-verified by hand.
- **What was believed:** REG-E-029's disposition says the evidence→claim cascade *"stays cross-aggregate and still needs the coordinator"*, and the corpus survey concluded the coordinator's *"AUTHORIZATION exists … but its SHAPE does not."* **The shape exists.**
- **`transitions.data.ts:1725` declares `Harness.status`:** `FRAMING, PLANNING, COORDINATING, WAITING, SYNTHESIZING, COMPLETED, ESCALATED, SUSPENDED, SUPERSEDED`, initial `FRAMING`, terminal `COMPLETED`/`SUPERSEDED`, with arrows including `COORDINATING→WAITING` (*"durable wait on a dependency/callback"*), `WAITING→COORDINATING` (*"wait resolved / restart recovery resumes"*), `COORDINATING→SYNTHESIZING`, `SYNTHESIZING→COORDINATING`, `COORDINATING→ESCALATED`. **That is a saga** — a durable process with explicit intermediate states, a durable wait, a restart-recovery resume, and an escalation exit: four of AGG-1's five permitted mechanisms, already modelled. Backed in prose by DOC-003 §2 layer 4 (L60): *"**Coordination** — allocation, supervision, synchronization, reconciliation, synthesis, and escalation, represented by RPH behavior."*
- **AND IT CANNOT MOVE.** `handlers/harness.ts` is **49 lines** with **one** handler, `proposeHarness`, which writes `status: 'FRAMING'`. `registry.ts:200` registers exactly one command. **One state of nine is reachable; ZERO of FIFTEEN arrows are traversable.** ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-08, and the error is the register's own recurring one: this entry first said "eight", counted from the arrows visible in an excerpt I had printed rather than from the machine. The C-0 census, reading `transitions.data.ts` itself, reports fifteen. **A count quoted from a partial view of the evidence, in an entry whose subject is a control that read the wrong artifact.** The finding is unchanged and was understated. The fifth hollow of the programme, and the one that matters most: the mechanism the cascade needs is declared and immovable.
- **⚠ THE CONTROL I BUILT FOR THIS EXACT DEFECT CLASS CANNOT SEE IT, BY CONSTRUCTION.** `state-reachability.test.ts:74-89` `strandedFrom` walks **`m.transitions` — the machine's own declared arrows** — from `initialState`. `Harness.status` is fully connected in its data, so it reports **zero stranded**: correctly, and uselessly. I authored that control days ago (c1173a1b, dacabc0d) for the PWU assurance axis, where ten of eleven states were stranded. It asks *"is this state reachable in the diagram?"*; the question that matters is *"is this arrow traversable by a registered command?"* **A machine can be perfectly connected on paper and immovable at runtime.**
- **THIS IS THE GENERAL FORM I WAS SUPPOSED TO ASK FOR WHEN I CLOSED THE PWU FINDING AND DID NOT.** The standing lesson is that on closing a finding one asks its general form; the general form here is **declared-arrow reachability ≠ command reachability**, and asking it would have caught the Harness on the day it was authored. **A control derived from the same artifact as the defect will agree with it.** The remedy is C-0 of `DESIGN-coordination-and-read-dependencies.md`: every declared arrow × the command registry, failures listed rather than counted.
- **Merge target:** Repository — C-0 then C-4. **Status:** OPEN. **Safe default:** no design may cite the Harness as an available coordinator until an arrow is traversable; and no new state machine is authored without the arrow×command check.

### REG-F-064 — I ran a survey with the absence-of-evidence rule printed in its preamble and it produced four false absences, one of which is the canon anchor for the whole design
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** the completeness critic, checking the corpus agent's `absent[]` claims with different search terms.
- **THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** The corpus agent searched `read dependency, dependency revision, version vector, revision vector, snapshot, stale read, expectedRevision, derivedAtSubjectRevision, preconditions` and concluded the general read-invalidation rule *"is nowhere in canon."* It is **JPWB-DOC-003 §8.4 L289, ASR-12**: *"Assurance binds to exact versions and never floats. Every assessment references an **active** policy version and its subject's **exact semantic version**… **a semantic change to the subject invalidates or forces review of prior assessments**."* A ratified rule that a derived verdict is invalidated by a revision change in an aggregate it merely READ. **Not one of the nine searched terms could match *"binds to exact versions and never floats"*.**
- **The other three:** (a) the coordinator's shape, reported absent, is REG-F-063; (b) *"zero hits for at-least-once"* — `command-bus.ts:619` says *"Delivery is therefore at-least-once; subscribers **SHALL** be idempotent"*, two normative SHALLs in a file the survey quotes six times elsewhere, with global ordering in `schema.ts:91` (`global_sequence … AUTOINCREMENT`); (c) compensation, searched as `compensat/undo/reverse/rollback`, is called a **control action** in canon (DOC-002 §4 L168) and `permittedControlActions` is already a required policy field.
- **THE METHOD FAILURE IS THE FINDING, NOT THE FOUR MISSES.** This is instance seven and eight of a pattern the register has recorded since REG-F-030: *"unspecified" is a claim about MY SEARCH.* What is new and worse is that **the rule was printed verbatim in the agent preamble I wrote** — *"search for the CONTENT (the concept, its synonyms, the mechanism) and not just the field name"* — and the agent searched field names anyway, reported the terms it used exactly as instructed, and I did not check them until the critic did. **Writing a rule into a prompt is not a control; the critic that re-searches with different terms IS the control**, and it earned its slot on this run alone.
- **AND IT CHANGES THE DESIGN, NOT JUST THE RECORD.** Under ASR-12 the assurance-cluster reads that were verdicted `NOT_REACHABLE` as races are simultaneously **ASR-12 non-conformances with a canon anchor**. Not being a race does not make them conformant — and a design that had accepted the absence would have authored a read-dependency rule that already exists in stronger form.
- **Merge target:** the survey method (a re-search critic is mandatory, not optional) and `DESIGN-coordination-and-read-dependencies.md` §2. **Status:** CLOSED as a finding; its remedy is standing.
