### REG-F-065 — I cited ASR-8 as forbidding the exact state ASR-8 requires, and repeated it for a day
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, self) · **Source:** the refuter of `claims-with-admissible-evidence`; verified against DOC-003 L271/L273 by hand.
- **What I wrote** in `DESIGN-accountability-substrate.md` §2: a claim recorded SUPPORTED on evidence no longer admissible is *"the precise state ASR-8 exists to forbid."*
- **ASR-8 requires the link to persist.** L271: *"Contradicting evidence remains attached and visible; **the record never self-curates toward support**."* L273: *"**Retention is dependency-driven:** evidence is retained while any active or historical claim, assessment, decision, or baseline depends on it."* **A claim that DROPPED the invalidated evidence id would be the self-curating record ASR-8 forbids and would destroy the retention predicate the same clause defines.** I borrowed a ratified rule's authority for its opposite — which is worse than an uncited claim, because a reader who trusts the citation stops.
- **WHAT ASR-8 DOES REQUIRE IS UNMET, AND THE INVERSION HID IT.** Its first sentence: *"every dependent supported claim becomes contested, under review, or invalidated."* Canonical state never does this — `invalidateEvidence` records `affectedClaimIds` on the EVENT and its own header says *"It does not mutate the claims."* The **projection** does it (`assurance-view.ts:342-363`), and the **gates read canonical** (`pwaFloorGate`→`floorGateBlock`, `loadPwaFloor`). **So the UI shows an assessment invalidated while `PublishPwa` passes** — the divergence is shipped and points the dangerous way.
- **THE RACE FRAMING WAS STANDING IN FRONT OF THE REAL DEFECT.** Chasing an interleaving that serializability refutes cost a pass; the unbuilt ASR-8 cascade was underneath it the whole time, is not a concurrency bug, and needs the coordinator of REG-F-063.
- **Merge target:** `DESIGN-accountability-substrate.md` §2 (STRUCK in place, superseded) and `DESIGN-coordination-and-read-dependencies.md` §4. **Status:** CLOSED as a correction; the cascade it uncovered is OPEN as C-4.

### REG-F-066 — I scheduled an increment from a TRUE observation without tracing it to a consequence, and it was worthless
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, self) · **Source:** verifying C-1's premise before building it.
- **The observation, and it is entirely correct:** the authoring turn's `snapshotRevisions` pins the PWA, every PWU_TYPE, every ASSURANCE_POLICY and the conversation, and pins **no DECISION** — while `hasEffectiveWaiver` reads DECISIONs. A waiver granted or revoked mid-turn is a read dependency the revision vector does not carry. The survey critic found it; I wrote it into `ROADMAP-coordination.md` as increment C-1 with a predicted red.
- **AND NOTHING DEPENDS ON IT.** Traced before building: `FloorView.waived` has exactly two consumers, both in `pwa/[id]/+page.svelte` — **it is display-only**. The floor recording is built from a `ValidatorContext` no waiver enters. The turn's staging decision reads `satisfied`, never `waived`. The publication gate DOES honour an EFFECTIVE waiver, but at `PublishPwa`, against live state, where a staged turn cannot make it stale.
- **So the vector is COMPLETE for everything that affects a write** — the mechanism is right and its input is sufficient, which is the opposite of what both the critic and I asserted.
- **THE ERROR IS NOT THE OBSERVATION, IT IS THE PROMOTION.** A true statement about a missing pin became a scheduled increment with a gate and a predicted red, and the one question that would have killed it — *what reads this value?* — costs a single grep. **Plausibility carried it all the way onto the roadmap.** This is the "verify the gap first" lesson applying to a gap I found myself rather than one I inherited: a `Source TBD` is not a gap, and neither is an unpinned read nothing consumes.
- **SECOND INSTANCE IN THIS PROGRAMME, AND BOTH ARE MINE.** REG-F-065 was a race framing standing in front of nothing; this is a read-dependency framing standing in front of nothing. The common shape: **a hazard stated at the level of MECHANISM ("X is not pinned", "Y is read outside a transaction") and never carried down to EFFECT.** Mechanism-level hazards are cheap to generate and read as rigour.
- **Standing correction to the method:** an increment may not enter a roadmap until its premise names the WRITE it corrupts. "This read is unprotected" is not a premise; "this read is unprotected AND its value reaches this command's payload" is. C-2, C-3 and C-4 were re-checked against that bar the same day — C-3's both halves verified in code (`drainOutbox` marks every pending row PUBLISHED after a subscriber loop that is empty in production; the only production `.subscribe(` is an unrelated SSE session), so it stands.
- **Merge target:** `ROADMAP-coordination.md` C-1 (STRUCK in place). **Status:** CLOSED — the increment is withdrawn, not deferred.

### REG-F-067 — C-0's baseline is 107 false positives out of 185 triaged, and the triage cleared the ONE arrow it should not have
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, twelve-agent triage + critic) · **Source:** triage of the C-0 census output; every claim below re-verified by hand.
- **The census reported 216 uncovered arrows. Triaged verdicts over the twelve largest machines (185 arrows): BLIND_SPOT 107, UNBUILT_CAPABILITY 67, DEFECT 2, DEAD_ARROW 9.** So **58% of what C-0 printed is its own known blind spot**, concentrated in one mechanism: `pwu.ts` has ZERO `advanceStatus` sites and drives all four PWU axes through a private `advancePwuLifecycle` helper plus the generic `ChangePwuState` setter, neither of which the AST extractor can see.
- **THE CLEARS HOLD, AND THEY WERE NOT TAKEN ON TRUST.** The critic built a throwaway live probe on the real `Engine` + `SqliteStorageAdapter` and drove **twelve** `PWU.workLifecycleState` arrows end to end — including `SHAPING→BLOCKED`, `SATISFIED→RECOMPOSING`, `RECOMPOSED→ABANDONED` — with a **negative control** asserting that a declared-`illegal` arrow (`SHAPING→SATISFIED`) and an undeclared pair are both REJECTED and leave the PWU unmoved. It also closed the one avenue that could have refuted the family silently: `lifecycleStatus` is `z.string()` on the envelope, so schema validation imposes no enum ceiling on a `ChangePwuState` write.
- **⚠ AND ONE CLEAR IS WRONG, IN THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION.** `AssuranceAssessment.state REQUESTED → EVIDENCE_PENDING` was cleared BLIND_SPOT. **No command performs it, because no command ever puts an assessment in REQUESTED.** `requestAssuranceAssessment` computes `landingState = evidenceOutstanding ? 'EVIDENCE_PENDING' : 'READY'` (assurance.ts:1116) and hands it to `createObject` — a **birth**, which runs no `checkTransition`. The triage agent's own mechanism note said exactly this and cleared the arrow anyway.
- **SO THE SAME MACHINE IS BOTH OVER- AND UNDER-REPORTED.** `initialState: 'REQUESTED'` (transitions.data.ts:1177) is **never occupied**, and `REQUESTED → CANCELLED` is scored **COVERED** by the census because `cancelAssuranceAssessment` names REQUESTED in its `fromStates` — a command that can never fire from a state nothing writes.
- **THIS IS THE THIRD INSTANCE OF ONE PATTERN AND IT NAMES C-0's OWN NEXT GAP.** `dacabc0d` fixed it on the PWU assurance axis: *"the declared initial state was the first enum member, and the engine never used it."* C-0 asks whether a COMMAND can perform an arrow; `state-reachability.test.ts` asks whether a STATE is reachable in the DIAGRAM. **Neither asks whether a state is ever actually OCCUPIED at runtime**, and an arrow out of an unoccupied source is dead however many commands declare it. The missing check is small and mechanical: *for every machine, does some creation path write its `initialState`?*
- **Merge target:** Repository — C-0 gains the initial-state-occupancy check and a declarative PWU spec table (see REG-F-068's extractor note). **Status:** OPEN. **Safe default:** the C-0 baseline is a WORKING LIST, not a defect count; no total from it may be quoted as "N missing capabilities" until the blind spot is closed declaratively.

### REG-F-068 — `AssuranceAssessment.disposition` is a nine-arrow state machine over a field the aggregate does not have
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** arrow triage; verified against the generated schema.
- **The ratified `AssuranceAssessment` object schema has no `disposition` property.** Checked directly against `packages/rph-contracts/schemas/objects/AssuranceAssessment.json`: `assessmentState` is present, `disposition` is absent. The object schema is a `z.strictObject` and `commitState` validates the PRODUCED state against it, so **the axis is mechanically un-writable** — not unbuilt, impossible.
- All **nine** of its arrows are therefore DEAD, and the live axis `AssuranceAssessment.state` already performs six of the nine transitions the dead machine describes (`assurance.ts:1826`, `:1895`) — which the C-0 extractor sees and attributes correctly.
- **THE REPAIR IS RETIREMENT, NOT AN EXEMPTION.** An `EXEMPT` entry would record "deliberately unbuilt", which is false: this cannot be built without adding a field the ratified schema forbids. It is retired declaratively (struck in the machine table with the successor axis named), the way this repository retires a mutant whose target legitimately moved.
- **AND THE THREE `WAIVED` ARROWS DO NOT SMUGGLE A DEFECT THROUGH.** Waiving is a Decision act (`grantWaiver`, governance.ts:627, operating on the DECISION aggregate); the repository states that position explicitly at pwu.ts:579; and no canon rule obliges the assessment object's own state to become WAIVED. Recorded because the tempting reading is the other one.
- **⚠ ADDENDUM, SAME DAY — THE VOCAB CARRIES THE ERROR TOO, AND IT MAKES THIS BIGGER THAN A DELETE.** Two ratified event rows declare `drivesMachine: 'AssuranceAssessment.disposition'` (`vocab/m3-commands-events.json:590, 664`, generated into `messages.ts:2821, 2828`), so the dead axis is not an orphan in the data — it is **cited as the machine two commands drive**. And both citations are wrong twice over: `RequestAssuranceAssessment` is recorded as `(initial) -> PENDING` when the handler BIRTHS the object in `READY`/`EVIDENCE_PENDING` on `assessmentState` and `PENDING` is not a state of the live axis at all; `CompleteAssuranceAssessment` is recorded on the dead axis when `assurance.ts:1898` names `machine: 'AssuranceAssessment.state'`. **A third artifact disagreeing with the other two**, and it joins REG-F-067's finding that the live axis's declared `initialState` is never occupied — both are the same object's lifecycle being described by records nothing reconciles.
- **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE INCREMENT:** retirement is not a deletion. It is (a) a declarative RETIRED marker on the machine naming `AssuranceAssessment.state` as successor, (b) correcting two vocab rows, (c) a regeneration, and (d) a DELIBERATE C-0 baseline edit — the arrow total drops 304 → 295, which the total pin will refuse until a human moves it. That refusal is the pin working, not an obstacle.
- **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-08, in two commits, and the second one corrected a claim the first one made.**
  - **First half** (`ba51214d`): the machine retired declaratively in a SHARED `packages/rph-domain/src/machine-exclusions.ts` — shared because the list had been a private const inside `state-reachability.test.ts` while a second control (C-0) read the same table, so the two censuses disagreed about what the population was. C-0's baseline moved deliberately: arrows 304 → 295, uncovered 211 → 202, orphans 13 → 11.
  - **⚠ CORRECTION — the (d) above is wrong for the SECOND half, and that commit's message repeated it.** It said correcting the vocab rows *"moves the arrow total again, which is another deliberate edit."* **It does not.** The arrow total is computed over `STATE_MACHINES`, generated from `rph-domain/vocab/m2-transitions.json`; the corrected rows are in `rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json`, which feeds `messages.ts` and nothing else. Verified after the fact: the regeneration diff touched `messages.ts` alone, `transitions.data.ts` was byte-identical, and C-0's baseline needed no edit. The error was assuming a second edit to "the vocab" hit the same generator as the first.
  - **Second half:** nine locations corrected — five binding rows, four command entries — plus the superseded `conflicts` ruling that had elected the dead axis as *"the transition authority"*, struck in place with why it was decidable at the time. **And asking the general form found two more wrong rows in the same family and produced a control: see REG-F-074.**
- **Merge target:** Repository — `transitions.data.ts`. **Status:** CLOSED.

### REG-F-069 — Assumption falsification is canon-obliged, contract-ratified, kernel-implemented, and has no command
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, triage critic) · **Source:** the critic overturning a triage clear; every limb re-verified.
- **The triage agent cleared all thirteen `Assumption.status` arrows as UNBUILT_CAPABILITY**, reporting it had searched OBJ-7, §6 L171, SPEC-001 N-6 and *"assumption superseded"*, and concluding *"no canon rule obliges any of the 13."* **It did not search OBJ-4.**
- **JPWB-DOC-003 §3 OBJ-4 (CANON, L117), byte-exact:** *"**Material assumptions are reified.** … A critical assumption must be verified or explicitly accepted by authority before dependent irreversible work proceeds; **falsification triggers impact analysis**; expired assumptions stop authorizing work. Human acceptance yields ACCEPTED status, never VERIFIED."* Reinforced by **§6 STA-7 (L199):** *"**Falsified material assumptions**, violated conditions, upstream semantic change, and post-recomposition sibling conflict route satisfied work into reshaping or invalidation."*
- **THREE INDEPENDENT CORROBORATIONS THAT THIS IS RATIFIED RATHER THAN CONVENIENT:**
  1. `AssumptionFalsifiedPayloadSchema` — carrying `impactAnalysisRequired: z.literal(true)` — is a member of `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS`, whose membership rule is *"events the corpus actually SCHEMATIZES (vocab sourceSection present and not UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED)"*. **Of all 21 members it is the only one no handler emits by any idiom.**
  2. The kernel already computes the act: `assessFalsification` (`rph-domain/src/decomposition.ts:451`) returns `newStatus: 'FALSIFIED'`, `impactAnalysisRequired: true`, `event: 'AssumptionFalsified'` — **and has no caller.** `enforcement-register.ts:3101` already records that fact and nobody joined it to the missing command.
  3. The clearing premise was itself false: the agent rested on *"the UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED labels on the five orphan events"*, and that label does not apply to this one.
- **THE METHOD LESSON, WHICH IS THE SAME ONE AGAIN.** A canon obligation was reported absent after a search of four identifiers. The critic found it in one pass using CONCEPT terms — *material assumption*, *falsif*, *accepted by authority*. **Instance nine.** The rule against this was in the triage preamble, verbatim, for the second run in a row.
- **Merge target:** Repository — a falsification command joining OBJ-4, the ratified event, and the existing kernel function. **Status:** ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-08. `falsifyAssumption` (`handlers/assurance.ts:985`) calls the existing kernel `assessFalsification` rather than reimplementing it, and `discloseAssumption` (`:916`) was authored alongside because without it `FALSIFIED` is unreachable — a `FalsifyAssumption` registered on its own would have been scored COVERED by C-0 while nothing could produce any of its four source states. Both are REGISTERED (`handlers/registry.ts:168, :172`), which is the check that separates this from a hollow landing. Four censuses moved deliberately.

### REG-F-070 — Abandonment is a governance act that any controller can perform, and canon names it explicitly
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, arrow triage) · **Source:** the `PWU.workLifecycleState` triage; the enforcement absence re-checked by grep over production sources.
- **JPWB-DOC-001 §5.2 (CANON), byte-exact:** *"**Governance is an authority function outside the six disciplines.** It alone authorizes waiver, risk acceptance, rejection or **abandonment** of governed work, and promotion. No discipline may absorb it."*
- **All seventeen `→ ABANDONED` arrows declare the guard** *"Authorized decision (Decision.decisionType=ABANDON)"* (transitions.data.ts:253 and sixteen siblings). **Nothing enforces it.** `changePwuState` runs exactly three "may not be asserted" guards — disposition (pwu.ts:621), baselining (pwu.ts:664), execution success (pwu.ts:716) — and abandonment is not among them. A grep for `ABANDON` across `packages/rph-application/src` excluding tests returns **one** hit, an unrelated comment.
- **AND IT IS DEMONSTRATED, NOT INFERRED:** `execrem-wp12-authority.test.ts:378` abandons a PWU with `reasonCode: 'fixture'` and `supportingObjectIds: []`, and the dispatch is ACCEPTED.
- **⚠ THIS IS THE OPPOSITE FAILURE FROM THE ONE C-0 HUNTS, AND C-0 IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT.** The census asks whether an arrow can be performed; this arrow can be performed **by too many actors**. An over-permissive arrow is invisible to a coverage census by construction, and would have stayed invisible if the triage had answered only the question it was asked. The general form: **a declared guard on an arrow is a claim, and no control in this repository checks that arrow guards are enforced.**
- **Related, same family:** the ratified events `PwuAbandoned` and `PwuBlocked` are declared (messages.ts:1361, 1372) and registered (2543, 2545) and **emitted by no handler** — all 32 generic-setter arrows emit `PwuStateChanged` instead.
- **Merge target:** Repository — an abandonment precondition citing an EFFECTIVE ABANDON Decision, plus the two ratified events. **Status:** ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-08 for the precondition; **the two ratified events remain OPEN** (see below). The prior safe default — *"until enforced, no document may describe abandonment as governance-controlled"* — is DISCHARGED for the enforcement half.
- **WHAT SHIPPED.** `resolveAbandonAuthorization` (`handlers/abandon-authorization.ts`), seven ordered checks mirroring `resolveSkipAuthorization`, wired as the fourth limb of `changePwuState`'s guard chain (`pwu.ts`). Exists · is a DECISION · parses · `decisionType === 'ABANDON'` · `status === 'EFFECTIVE'` · names this PWU (RPH-GOV-005) · **binds this PWU at its current semantic version.** The three fixtures that abandoned on `reasonCode: 'fixture'` now propose and approve a real ABANDON Decision through the bus.
- **⚠ THE SEVENTH CONJUNCT IS THE ONE I WOULD HAVE OMITTED, AND AN ADVERSARIAL PASS FOUND IT.** I had reasoned that version-pinning an abandonment would be *invented*, since only promotion is version-pinned in the corpus. **DOC-003 §8.7 ASR-15 (L307) settles it and I had not read it:** *"Every material Decision binds exact subjects **and semantic versions** … **A decision approving version n never authorizes version n+1.** … An approval whose actor, subject, **subject version**, type, and time cannot be identified is not authority — it is provenance at best."* Its WHY (L308) is the design rationale, not merely permission: *"authority that floats across versions … is indistinguishable from no governance at all."* Instance ten of the absence-of-evidence class — I concluded a requirement was absent after searching the wrong document.
- **THE VERSION-DRIFT ARM IS DEFENDED AHEAD OF A CAPABILITY THAT DOES NOT EXIST, and that is recorded rather than hidden.** No PWU command bumps `semanticVersion` — `advanceStatus`'s `bumpSemanticVersion` is used only by `reviseIntent` and `reviseDecomposition`, and `changePwuState` commits `loaded.semanticVersion` unchanged — so genuine n→n+1 drift cannot be driven through the bus. That arm is tested at the resolver, against a decision built through the real store rather than a hand-written object. The *absent-pin* arm IS bus-reachable (a blanket decision taken before the work existed) and is tested there.
- **SIX MUTANTS, EACH PREDICTED BEFORE IT RAN, AND TWO FOUND DEFECTS IN MY OWN TESTS.** Unwiring the guard reddened 8 of 12 (every bus reject plus the discriminator control) and left the accept case green. Accepting `APPROVAL` reddened exactly one test; dropping subject scope exactly one; dropping the version bind exactly two. **The two catches:** (a) dropping the version bind left the "pins NO version" test GREEN — because that test cited an unpinned subject alongside a pinned one and then asserted the abandonment SUCCEEDED, an accept case wearing a "REJECTS" title; it now closes the unpinned PWU itself. (b) The scope mutant reddened all eleven tests including the one labelled CONTROL, because `beforeEach` performs non-abandon transitions — **so that control had no failure mode of its own and was demoted out of the control count**, replaced by the already-abandoned limb, which reddens alone.
- **STILL OPEN, and named so it is not mistaken for done:** `PwuAbandoned` and `PwuBlocked` remain declared (`messages.ts:1367, :1378`), registered (`:2561, :2563`) and emitted by NO handler — all thirty-two generic-setter arrows still emit `PwuStateChanged`. The `PwuAbandoned` payload REQUIRES `abandonmentDecisionId` (`vocab/m3-commands-events.json:5741`, noted *"id-ref; Decision decisionType=ABANDON"*) — **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-08: this entry twice called `PwuAbandoned`/`PwuBlocked` "ratified". They are NOT.** Neither is in `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS`; both carry `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED` in their vocab `sourceSection`, which I had read and quoted while analysing them and then overstated in the write-up. The distinction is the one this repository uses to decide how much weight a record carries, so overstating it is not cosmetic. **The fix itself is unaffected** — it rests on JPWB-DOC-001 §5.2 and ASR-15, both genuinely canon; only this supporting citation was inflated, so the contract has been asking for exactly the id this guard now demands, with no emitter to supply it. **Emitting it is a separate increment**, because the generic setter emits one event for all thirty-two arrows and splitting that is REG-F-072's territory, not this one's.
- **AND THE GUARD IS UNREACHABLE IN GOOD FAITH FROM THE DEMO — see REG-F-077.** The only surface that creates Decisions hardcodes `subjectObjectIds: []`, so no UI-created decision can satisfy the scope conjunct of this guard, of promotion, or of skip authorization.

### REG-F-071 — Four state machines declare an initial state the engine never writes, and the reachability control walks from that fiction
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** building C-0a's occupancy check; measured, then each limb re-verified by grep against production handlers.
- **The generalisation of REG-F-067, which found one instance and predicted the class.** Measured across all 26 machines:

| machine | declared `initialState` | what creation actually writes |
|---|---|---|
| `DecompositionContract.status` | `DRAFT` | `UNDER_REVIEW` (`decomposition.ts:66`) |
| `RecompositionContract.status` | `DRAFT` | `READY` (`decomposition.ts:498`) |
| `ExecutionPlan.status` | `PROPOSED` | `UNDER_REVIEW` (`execution.ts:264`) |
| `AssuranceAssessment.state` | `REQUESTED` | `READY` / `EVIDENCE_PENDING` (`assurance.ts:1263`) |

  **In every case the declared initial state is written NOWHERE in production** — verified by grepping for the literal in the owning handler, which returns nothing.
- **⚠ SO `state-reachability.test.ts` COMPUTES ITS ANSWER FROM A ROOT THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** Its `strandedFrom` walks the arrow graph outward from `m.initialState`. For these four machines that root is never occupied, so *"reachable from the initial state"* is a claim about a diagram whose entry point the engine does not use. The control is not merely blind here (REG-F-063); its premise is false. **Both controls over these machines are now known to be measuring something other than what they say.**
- **AND IT MAKES C-0 OVER-REPORT COVERAGE.** A covered arrow out of an unoccupiable source is dead. Measured: **18 covered arrows leave a non-initial state with no covered in-arrow.** Fourteen are FALSE positives — the state is BORN outside the declared initial (exactly the four rows above) — which is why the occupancy check cannot be built from the arrow graph alone. The residue is genuinely dead, and three of them I created hours ago: `UNDER_VERIFICATION|ACCEPTED|VERIFIED -> FALSIFIED`, scored covered by `FalsifyAssumption` while nothing can produce those three sources.
- **WHAT THIS COSTS THE NEXT INCREMENT, STATED SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED LATE.** C-0a's occupancy check needs BIRTH STATES, and they are not derivable from the arrow graph or from `initialState` — both lie. They must be DECLARED at the 22 `createObject` sites and checked at runtime, the same shape as `targetStates` (REG-F-069's increment), so a birth that drifts from its declaration fails a command rather than only a census.
- **AND THE DECLARED `initialState` FIELDS ARE THEMSELVES SUSPECT.** Once births are declared, four `initialState` values are provably wrong and must be corrected or struck — which moves C-0's arrow total and is therefore a deliberate, reviewed edit, not a silent one.
- **Merge target:** Repository — C-0a. **Status:** OPEN. **Safe default:** no document or control may treat `initialState` as the state an object is created in until births are declared; `state-reachability.test.ts` carries a note saying so.

### REG-F-072 — 152 arrows declare a guard, nothing checks that any is enforced, and 33 are not — 18 of those canon-cited
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, eight-agent census + critic) · **Source:** the general form of REG-F-070, asked on closing it.
- **`transitions.data.ts` declares `guard:` on 152 arrows — 86 distinct texts — and no control in this repository has ever checked one.** Censused, grouped by guard text, each resolved by reading the handler that performs the arrow: **UNENFORCED 33 · ARROW_UNREACHABLE 26 · ENFORCED 21 · NOT_MECHANICALLY_CHECKABLE 4 · REDUNDANT_WITH_MACHINE 2.** Eighteen of the 33 carry a ratified citation.
- **⚠ THE PATTERN UNDER THE COUNT: A GENERIC SETTER IS A BYPASS AROUND EVERY SPECIFIC COMMAND'S GUARD.** `markPwuReady` calls `checkPwuShapeReadiness` — eight limbs, fails closed. `changePwuState` performs the SAME `SHAPING -> READY` arrow and checks none of it: its only object loads live inside three "may not be asserted" guards, none of which fires on that hop, and `WORK_LIFECYCLE_CROSS_AXIS_GUARDS` has exactly three keys. **Demonstrated by a SHIPPED TEST, not inferred** — `execrem-wp12-authority.test.ts:163` flips a PWU to READY with empty `inScope`/`outOfScope`/`expectedOutputs` and a RAW intent: four limbs `markPwuReady` would have refused. Canon names the failure mode exactly (JPWB-DOC-003 §6 STA-5): *"readiness-as-status-flip is how unbounded work acquires the authority of bounded work."*
- **THIS IS WHY A COVERAGE CENSUS CANNOT SEE IT.** C-0 asks whether an arrow can be performed; every one of these CAN. An over-permissive arrow is invisible to coverage by construction, which is the whole reason this second census had to exist.
- **AND THE CENSUS ITSELF OVER-CLEARED FOUR TIMES — the direction that matters.** The critic drove each through the real command bus:
  - `Baseline APPROVED -> AUTHORITATIVE` — see REG-F-073.
  - `PWU SATISFIED -> BASELINED` is `ENFORCED_BY_DELEGATION` to that same weaker check, not enforced.
  - *"Intent exists"* is really *"some object exists"*: `proposePwu` calls `loadObject` and never checks `objectType`. **Probed: `ProposePwu` with a PWU id as its `intentId` was ACCEPTED.** Two functions earlier, `rejectInvalidOwnershipBinding` DOES check `objectType !== 'UNDERTAKING'` — same file, same shape, one checked and one not.
  - `RecompositionContract EVALUATING -> COMPOSABLE` — `parentCompletionClaimSupported ?? true` and `parentConstraintsHoldAgainstWhole ?? true`: a caller that omits both reaches COMPOSABLE unconditionally. **That is the `!!waiverOrRevisionId` shape JAN-EXECREM WP-12 removed from `skipExecutionStep`, reintroduced and then certified ENFORCED.**
- **ONE FALSE ABSENCE, THE TENTH.** `Decision PROPOSED -> EFFECTIVE` was reported UNENFORCED and is enforced twice over — `authorizeDecisionEffective` refuses `RPH_AUTHORITY_INSUFFICIENT` on a non-HUMAN authority, and `proposeDecision` refuses a declared authority that is not the issuer. There is a pinned suite asserting it. Instance ten, in a run whose preamble named the pattern and said nine had been recorded.
- **Merge target:** Repository — C-0b turns this census into a standing control; the individual guards are separate increments. **Status:** ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-08 — the generic-setter bypass is closed structurally AND C-0b has landed. **Safe default DISCHARGED and replaced by a mechanism:** the ledger now records, per guard text, whether it is enforced, so "is this declared guard a control?" is answered by a file rather than by a prohibition.
- **C-0b — `verif/guard-enforcement-ledger.{ts,data.ts,test.ts}`.** The population is derived at RUNTIME from `STATE_MACHINES`, sharing `machine-exclusions.ts` with C-0 and C-0c: **146 guarded arrows in 82 distinct texts.** Every text carries a disposition and its evidence; the arrow total, the text total and the disposition census are pinned; an ENFORCED row must name the line that REFUSES; an unclassified text fails; a stale row fails.
- **⚠ THE FIRST READER SILENTLY DROPPED 30% OF ITS OWN POPULATION, and it nearly "corrected" a correct record.** A line-based parse of `transitions.data.ts` found 108 of the file's 154 raw `guard:` occurrences — guards also live in the `guarded` and `illegal` sections and some span lines — and reported 51 distinct texts against this entry's recorded 86. **The register was right and the new instrument was wrong.** It was caught only by comparing against a raw `grep -c`. Reading the values off the exported `STATE_MACHINES` instead of re-parsing the file removes the class, the same way C-0c reads `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS`.
- **THE CENSUS, NOW PERSISTED AND REPRODUCIBLE:** `UNENFORCED 44 · ARROW_UNREACHABLE 22 · ENFORCED 14 · REDUNDANT_WITH_MACHINE 2`. These differ from this entry's original figures because the population is scoped to the two-machine exclusion list and keyed by text; the original was never persisted, so the two cannot be reconciled row by row — **which is precisely the defect C-0b exists to end.** From here the numbers are checkable.
- **⚠ THE ADVERSARIAL PASS OVERTURNED TWO OF SIXTEEN ENFORCED CLAIMS, and only ENFORCED was attacked — deliberately.** An UNENFORCED row understates the system and is safe; a false ENFORCED puts a hole in the ledger while making it read as covered.
  1. *"a skipped MANDATORY step requires an authorized plan revision or waiver (§21.1)"* — **the worst kind of false ENFORCED.** The refusal at `execution.ts:1582` is real and its mutants do redden, but it only runs when the caller SUPPLIES `waiverOrRevisionId`, and `mandatory` comes from the COMMAND PAYLOAD (`p.mandatory ?? true`). **The actor the rule constrains disables it with one boolean**, using a payload shape 15 of this repository's own fixtures already use.
  2. *"privilege expansion requires a NEW authorization event (§22.1)"* — the cited site refuses on GRANT CONTAINMENT, which is verbatim the guard of the OTHER arrow on that machine. **The row had reused a sibling arrow's enforcing site.** No value corresponding to "a new authorization event occurred" is ever computed, so nothing can refuse on it.
- **THREE MUTANTS, EACH PREDICTED.** Making `unclassified` unconditionally empty reddened CONTROL 2 alone; making `enforcedWithoutSite` unconditionally empty reddened CONTROL 3 alone; narrowing the reader to one machine reddened the pins AND CONTROL 1 — a narrowed reader is caught several ways, which is the property the first reader lacked.
- **WHAT C-0b DOES NOT DO, said plainly:** it does not prove the guards are enforced. Forty-four of eighty-two are recorded UNENFORCED — that is the finding, not a failure of the file. What it proves is that every declared guard has been read, that the reading is written down, that a new guard cannot arrive unclassified, and that the ledger cannot be improved by deleting guards from the machine table. **`UNENFORCED: 44` is not a score to optimise; the only honest way to move it is to enforce a guard and re-drive its row.**
- **⚠ THE HEADLINE OVERSTATED IT, AND MEASUREMENT SAYS SO. "A bypass around EVERY specific command's guard" is false:** of the six semantic PWU lifecycle commands, only `markPwuReady` and `invalidatePwu` add ANY refusal over the shared `advancePwuLifecycle`. `beginPwuShaping`, `challengePwu`, `reshapePwu` and `supersedePwu` are bare `advancePwuLifecycle` calls. A runtime probe drove all 46 guard-declaring `PWU.workLifecycleState` arrows through the real bus: the bypass surface is **19 arrows raw and exactly ONE differentially** — `SHAPING -> READY`. The other five commands' delta is not a guard but the **EVENT**: `PwuChallenged`, `PwuReshapingStarted`, `PwuSuperseded`, `PwuInvalidated` carry fields (`challengeReason`, `reason`, `supersedingWorkUnitId`, `triggeringObjectId`) that `ChangePwuStatePayload` cannot express, so the setter silently substituted a generic `PwuStateChanged` for a semantic record. **Two distinct losses were being reported as one.**
- **WHAT SHIPPED — (c) SCOPE, not a copied guard.** `PWU_SEMANTIC_LIFECYCLE_COMMANDS` maps the six owned target states to their commands, and `rejectArrowOwnedBySemanticCommand` refuses any `ChangePwuState` that would MOVE into one, naming the command to dispatch instead. **`advancePwuLifecycle`'s `target` is narrowed to `keyof` that table**, so a seventh semantic command cannot be added without adding its row — `check-types` fails before its arrow could quietly become reachable through the setter too. A HOLD (`newState === current`) performs no arrow and is untouched; the guard runs LAST so an illegal hop is reported as illegal rather than redirected to a command that would refuse it for a different reason.
- **⚠ I BUILT THE WRONG FIX FIRST, AND THE PREMISE CAME FROM THE FILE I WAS REPAIRING.** I wrote a fifth "may not be asserted" guard copying `checkPwuShapeReadiness` into `changePwuState`, justified by a comment in `pwu.ts` asserting that `ChangePwuState` is the vehicle for DOC-002 §8.2 EXCEPTION transitions while §8.1 PRIMARY transitions keep their own commands. **That comment's own derivation admits it is invention** — `docs/_working/HARMONIZATION-LOG.md` closes the argument *"(That §8.2 is its referent is my inference; the corpus never links them.)"* Three facts refute it: the machine's triggers name `challengePwu`/`reshapePwu`/`invalidatePwu` AS the exception commands, so §8.2 is where the NAMED commands already are; every production `ChangePwuState` call performs a §8.1 primary or a hold, so scoping the setter TO §8.2 would have deleted its entire real use; and `ChangePwuState` is `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED`, the vocab noting *"DOC-007-only generic transition; DOC-002 has no ChangePwuState (uses named per-transition commands)"*. **A preference laundered as a rule, believed because it was written down nearby, and quoted back to me as decisive by my own design.** The comment is corrected in place.
- **THE REAL CANON ANCHOR, WHICH I HAD NOT FOUND — JPWB-DOC-003 §9 PER-3 (L343), byte-exact:** *"**One authoritative write path.** Canonical state is mutated only through authenticated, authorized, **semantically named commands** that check expected revision, **validate preconditions and invariants**, enforce required assurance, and atomically persist state, version history, events, outbox, and command receipt. **No generic CRUD/PATCH path**, UI local state, RPH worker, validator, projection worker, broker message, agent output, or informal approval bypasses this pipeline."* It legislates the WRITE PATH, which is why closing the route is the repair and copying the predicate is not.
- **THE STRUCTURAL FIX COST ONE TEST MORE THAN THE SINGLE-ARROW ONE — 49 red vs 48 — AND ZERO PRODUCTION CALLERS.** The reference undertaking, the demo route and every e2e already reach all six states through the named commands; greps for `newState: '(CHALLENGED|RESHAPING|INVALIDATED|SUPERSEDED)'` find no `ChangePwuState` site anywhere. **Five fixture files were repaired rather than exempted**, and the repair is the point: each had walked a PWU with empty `inScope`, empty `outOfScope`, no expected output and a RAW intent to READY — four limbs `markPwuReady` refuses. They now shape the PWU and mature its Intent, so the arrangement finally matches production. `execrem-wp16-enforcement-observed.test.ts`'s local `chg` helper became **unused and was deleted** — the tell that the file no longer dispatches the setter for a PWU at all. Sharpest instance: **RPH-PWU-004's own enforcement probe** — the readiness rule's — reached the state it observes from by way of the bypass that rule was being escaped through.
- **FOUR MUTANTS, EACH PREDICTED BEFORE IT RAN.** Unwiring the guard reddened the five owned-target rejects and left both controls green. Dropping the hold exemption reddened ONLY the hold test. Moving the check ahead of the legality check reddened ONLY the ordering control (plus the INVALIDATED case, which asserts the same ordering) — invisible to the other eight, which all use legal arrows. The control that the OWNING commands still perform the identical arrows is what separates "route closed" from "capability removed".
- **RESIDUE, NAMED SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR DONE.** (1) `MarkPwuReady`'s `expectedSemanticVersion` staleness check is NOT portable — it reads a field the setter's payload lacks — so the two routes are not equivalent and never can be. (2) **Twelve lifecycle arrows still have no named command at all**: six §8.1 middles (`READY->PLANNED`, `PLANNED->EXECUTING`, `EXECUTING->EVIDENCE_PENDING`, `EVIDENCE_PENDING->UNDER_ASSURANCE`, `UNDER_ASSURANCE->CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED`, `UNDER_ASSURANCE->SATISFIED`) and six exception arrows (`->BLOCKED` x3, `->ESCALATED`, `->REJECTED`, `Any active->ABANDONED`). PER-3 wants those named too; this increment closes the arrows that HAVE an owner and mints no new commands. (3) **`workLifecycleState -> REJECTED`** — CLOSED as REG-F-078, and **two of the four limbs this note originally offered are WITHDRAWN as evidence**: "the arrow declares no guard, unlike the ABANDONED arrows" (core-model §8.2 has three columns and no conditions column at all, so no §8.2 row could carry a guard — guardlessness there is a reconciliation gap, not a canonical statement) and "its payload requires blockingObservationIds and no decision id" (both payloads are UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED, so that contrasts two same-day guesses). The head claim survived on ratified text alone. (4) `seedPwuWorkLifecycleState_FIXTURE` writes lifecycle state straight to the store, around every guard; used three times, never for READY — the hole one refactor away.

### REG-F-073 — Any effective promotion decision authorizes any promotion: the one act canon reserves to Governance has no subject scope
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, guard-census critic) · **Source:** verifying an `ENFORCED` verdict; both halves re-read by hand.
- **`canPromoteBaseline` (`rph-domain/src/governance.ts:364-368`), byte-exact:**
  ```
  const decisionOk = !!d && d.status === 'EFFECTIVE' && d.authorityHeld &&
      (d.decisionType === 'PROMOTE_BASELINE' || d.decisionType === 'APPROVAL');
  ```
  **Kind, status, authority — and no subject scope.** The call site builds `promotionDecision` from `p.promotionDecisionId` and never compares the decision's `subjectObjectIds` against the baseline id or its `itemObjectVersions`. `decisionAuthorizesVersions` iterates the DECISION'S OWN `subjectSemanticVersions`, so a decision about unrelated subjects satisfies it vacuously.
- **DRIVEN THROUGH THE REAL BUS, not argued:** a `PROMOTE_BASELINE` decision whose only subject was an unrelated PWU, approved, then used to promote a baseline of a DIFFERENT PWU — **ACCEPTED, baseline AUTHORITATIVE.**
- **AND IT CASCADES.** `rejectUnbackedBaselining` gates `PWU SATISFIED -> BASELINED` on a cited baseline being AUTHORITATIVE — a status this hole hands out on an off-topic decision. So the PWU guard is enforced exactly as strongly as this one, which is not at all.
- **THE FIX IS NOT A NEW PATTERN; THE PATTERN IS FORTY LINES AWAY AND CITES THE RULE.** `resolveSkipAuthorization` (`skip-authorization.ts:99-113`) checks `decision.subjectObjectIds.includes(query.planId)`, then plan-exactness, then step-exactness, refusing with *"an authorization does not bleed to another step (RPH-GOV-005)"* — and its header quotes RPH-GOV-005 in full: *"a waiver does not bleed to another criterion, another object, or another version."* **The scope discipline is already in this repository, already ratified, and absent from the one act JPWB-DOC-001 §5.2 reserves to Governance by name** — *"It alone authorizes waiver, risk acceptance, rejection or abandonment of governed work, **and promotion**."*
- **Merge target:** Repository — `canPromoteBaseline` + its call site. **Status:** ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-08 (`7afba73f`) — `BaselinePromotionInput` gained `baselineId`, the finding code `PROMOTION_DECISION_OUT_OF_SCOPE` was added (`rph-domain/src/governance.ts:239, :403`), and 21 fixtures were scoped so the decision names the baseline it authorizes. **The prior safe default — *"until scoped, no document may describe baseline promotion as decision-authorized; it is decision-accompanied"* — is DISCHARGED and no longer binds.**
- **⚠ RECORD DEFECT, CORRECTED 2026-08-08 AND RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED.** This entry sat at `Status: OPEN` for a full day after its fix landed, carrying a safe default that had become FALSE — it forbade describing promotion as decision-authorized, which by then it was. Found while reading this entry for an unrelated reason, not by any control. **The general form is unaddressed: nothing checks that a register entry's Status agrees with the repository.** A `CLOSED` claim is verifiable (the code exists or it does not); an `OPEN` one that is stale silently keeps a discharged prohibition in force, which is the direction that misleads. Cf. the standing observation that fixes hold while records rot.
- **⚠ AND ONE LIMB OF THIS FIX IS NARROWER THAN ITS SIBLING, WHICH IS AN OPEN QUESTION NOT A DEFECT.** `canPromoteBaseline` still accepts `decisionType === 'APPROVAL'` as well as `PROMOTE_BASELINE`, while `resolveSkipAuthorization` (`skip-authorization.ts:77-83`) explicitly refuses it — *"an APPROVAL or a PROMOTE_BASELINE authorizes something else entirely"*. Two governance gates in one repository disagree about whether a generic approval may stand in for a specific authorization. Recorded here so the disagreement is visible; settling it needs a canon reading, not a preference.

---

### REG-F-074 — The command→transition binding table is 151 unchecked claims, and the copy carrying most of the errors is not generated at all
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** the general form of REG-F-068, asked on closing it. Measured, then every flagged row re-verified against the owning handler.
- **THE GENERAL FORM: a binding row is a CLAIM about what a command does, and the only control over it compares it to a DRAWING.** `rph-domain/src/binding.test.ts` requires every concrete `from -> to` to classify LEGAL or NOOP against `STATE_MACHINES`. All five REG-F-068 rows passed it on every run, correctly — they *are* legal edges of the diagram. **Legality is not truth.** A row may name a machine the aggregate has no field for, a source state nothing ever occupies, and an arrow no handler performs, and the check is silent on all three.
- **AND THE CONTROL SHARED THE DEFECT'S SOURCE.** Both the binding rows and the machine tables are generated from the reconciled vocab, so the comparison is an artifact against itself — the recurring shape *a control derived from the same artifact as the defect will agree with it*.
- **⚠ THE SECOND COPY HAD NEVER BEEN READ BY ANYTHING.** The same claim is recorded twice in `vocab/m3-commands-events.json`: once as a `bindings[]` row, once as `drivesMachine`/`drivesFrom`/`drivesTo` on the command entry. **`gen-messages.ts` emits only the first** (`BINDINGS`), so the command-entry copy reaches no generated artifact and no test. It carried **four of the five** REG-F-068 defects. A control auditing only the generated file would have reported the contracts clean while the source they are generated from stayed wrong.
- **MEASURED, and the false-positive rate is what makes it a control rather than a working list — 151 claims audited (74 binding rows + 77 command entries), 5 distinct defects flagged, 0 false positives:**

| claim | recorded | what the handler does |
|---|---|---|
| `RequestAssuranceAssessment` | `AssuranceAssessment.disposition (initial) -> PENDING` | births `assessmentState` at `EVIDENCE_PENDING`/`READY` (`assurance.ts:1295`) |
| `CompleteAssuranceAssessment` | `AssuranceAssessment.disposition ASSESSING -> …` | `AssuranceAssessment.state` (`assurance.ts:1898`) |
| `RequestAssuranceAssessment` (2nd row) | `AssuranceAssessment.state REQUESTED -> EVIDENCE_PENDING` | nothing writes `REQUESTED`; event is co-emitted at birth and moves no state |
| `CreateBaseline` | `Baseline.status (initial) -> DRAFT` | births at `CANDIDATE` (`governance.ts:732`) |
| `ProposeDecomposition` | `DecompositionContract.status (initial) -> DRAFT` | births at `UNDER_REVIEW` (`decomposition.ts:72`) |

- **THE LAST TWO ARE THE PLAINEST, AND NEITHER REQUIRED PICKING A SIDE.** Both recorded their machine's declared `initialState` as the state the command *produces* — and in both cases the m2 machine table and the handler disagree with the row and **agree with each other** (`DRAFT -> CANDIDATE` triggered by `BaselineCreated`; `DRAFT -> UNDER_REVIEW` triggered by `proposeDecomposition`). The row had transcribed the arrow's SOURCE as its TARGET. This is REG-F-071's safe default coming due: *"no document or control may treat `initialState` as the state an object is created in until births are declared."* Births are now declared, so the reading is settled.
- **REPAIR — C-0c (`verif/binding-row-truth.ts` + `.test.ts`), whose witness is the HANDLER SOURCE, not the diagram.** Occupancy comes from `occupiable()`, seeded by the `births` declarations `createObject` checks at runtime. Six checks: no excluded machine · no undeclared machine · every `from`/`to` token names declared states or is a deliberate `(marker)` · no landing in an unoccupiable state · **every `(initial) -> X` lands where a handler declares a birth** · and no dead transition beyond what C-0 already found.
- **THE DEAD-SOURCE CHECK IS A COMPARISON, NOT A SECOND BASELINE.** C-0's `deadCovered()` already owns *"an arrow out of an unoccupiable source is dead"* and names five `Assumption.status` arrows (REG-F-071). Pinning them here too would mean fixing one dead arrow required editing two baselines with neither being authority — the split-population defect `machine-exclusions.ts` was created to end. C-0c instead asserts its set is a SUBSET of C-0's: the question C-0 cannot ask is whether the *vocab* claims a dead transition the *handlers* do not declare. Residue today: zero.
- **MUTATION-MEASURED, five mutants, each with its predicted red named before it was run.** M1 reverted `CreateBaseline` to `DRAFT` through regen and build → the two occupancy checks reddened. M2 reverted the command-entry copy ONLY, with **no regeneration** → `excluded` reddened naming `vocab.commands:`, proving the unread copy is audited. M3 loosened the token resolver's alternation from `.every` to `.some` → **only CONTROL 2** reddened. M4 disabled the birth check → the main birth assertion went green-and-vacuous and **only CONTROL 3** reddened. M5 pointed the vocab reader at `BINDINGS` → **only CONTROL 1** reddened. Each control has its own distinct failure mode, and three of the five mutants are invisible to every main assertion.
- **⚠ HALF THE SURFACE IS STILL UNCHECKABLE, AND THE NUMBER IS PINNED RATHER THAN BURIED.** 13 of 27 machines have no declared birth, so no claim naming them can be occupancy-checked — including `PWU.workLifecycleState`, `Decision.status` and `ExecutionPlan.status`, three of the busiest. The list is an assertion, so it shrinks deliberately as births are declared.
- **Merge target:** Repository — C-0c, landed with this entry. **Status:** CLOSED for the five rows and the control; **the 13-machine unanalysed set remains OPEN** and is the natural next increment of C-0a. **Safe default:** no count derived from `BINDINGS` or from the command-entry `drives*` fields may be described as verified for a machine in the unanalysed list.

### REG-F-075 — Two of the three findings closed in one session were still recorded OPEN, and an OPEN entry's safe default BINDS
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** noticed while reading REG-F-073 for an unrelated reason. **Found by eye, by accident, and by no control** — which is the part worth recording.
- **MEASURED, not impressions.** Three findings were closed in this session's work. **REG-F-073** (`7afba73f`) and **REG-F-069** (`DiscloseAssumption`/`FalsifyAssumption`) both still read `**Status:** OPEN` a full day later; only REG-F-068 was marked correctly, and only because closing it was the increment being written up. Both fixes verified landed before the status was changed — REG-F-073 by `PROMOTION_DECISION_OUT_OF_SCOPE` at `rph-domain/src/governance.ts:239, :403`; REG-F-069 by the handlers at `handlers/assurance.ts:916, :985` **and their registration at `handlers/registry.ts:168, :172`**, the check that separates a landing from a hollow one.
- **⚠ WHY A STALE `OPEN` IS THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION, AND IT IS NOT SYMMETRIC.** JPWB-REG-005 §1: an `OPEN` entry's recorded **safe default binds**. REG-F-073's safe default read *"until scoped, no document may describe baseline promotion as decision-authorized; it is decision-*accompanied*."* By the time it was read, promotion **was** scoped — so the register was actively enforcing a prohibition its own repository had discharged, against a fact that had become true. A stale `CLOSED` would be a claim someone can check and falsify against the code; a stale `OPEN` is a live constraint nobody has any reason to re-examine.
- **THE GENERAL FORM: nothing in this repository checks that a register entry's `Status` agrees with the repository.** Every other claim here has been made checkable — arrows against handlers (C-0), binding rows against births (C-0c), guards against enforcement (C-0b, owed) — while the register's own status field, which is the field that decides whether a prohibition binds, is maintained entirely by hand.
- **AND THE OBVIOUS DETECTOR WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED, rather than built and left to rot.** Cross-referencing entry ids against `git log` flags **11 of the 32 OPEN entries** as "mentioned in a commit". That is a working list, not a control: a commit mentions an entry while *working* it at least as often as while *closing* it (REG-F-014 and REG-F-021 are each mentioned in four commits and each legitimately still open). A control shipped at that false-positive rate would be read as noise within a week and then ignored — the allowlist-rot shape one step earlier. **Recorded as measured-and-not-built so the next reader does not rebuild it.**
- **WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY WORK is structural rather than textual, and is the recommended increment:** make closure a mechanical act instead of an edit. An entry's `Status` should be derived from whether its **discharge condition** — a named symbol, test, or control that exists only once the fix lands — is satisfiable, the same way C-0c derives occupancy from declared births rather than from `initialState`. Entries authored before that convention would need discharge conditions retrofitted, which is the real cost and the reason this is registered rather than done inside another increment.
- **Merge target:** Repository — a discharge-condition field on register entries plus the control that evaluates it. **Status:** OPEN. **Safe default:** an `OPEN` status is EVIDENCE OF NOTHING about whether the work landed; before relying on any entry's safe default, verify against the repository that the fix has not already shipped.

### REG-F-076 — Two governance gates in one repository disagree about whether a generic APPROVAL substitutes for a specific authorization
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent) · **Source:** choosing the decisionType conjunct for REG-F-070's guard; both predicates read directly.
- **`resolveSkipAuthorization` REFUSES it, in terms** (`skip-authorization.ts:77-83`): *"3. The right KIND of decision. §21.1 names an authorized plan revision (REPLAN) or a waiver (WAIVER); **an APPROVAL or a PROMOTE_BASELINE authorizes something else entirely.**"*
- **`canPromoteBaseline` ACCEPTS it** (`rph-domain/src/governance.ts:373-377`): `d.decisionType === 'PROMOTE_BASELINE' || d.decisionType === 'APPROVAL'`.
- **NEITHER IS CANON-GROUNDED, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** No text in DOC-001/003/004 or CON-000 licenses one `decisionType` standing in for another, and none forbids it in those words either — so the two gates are each expressing a *preference* while presenting it as a rule. **CON-000 AX-2 (L73) breaks the tie in one direction only:** *"approval and exception authority default to denied; no scenario, agent, validator, or registry grants itself authority."* A disjunct that widens who may authorize is precisely a grant, and it has no ratified source.
- **REG-F-073 AUDITED THAT EXACT PREDICATE AND LEFT THE DISJUNCT STANDING.** It added the subject-scope conjunct and did not question the type disjunct beside it — a reminder that fixing one limb of a predicate is not evidence about its siblings.
- **DECIDED FOR THE NEW GUARD, NOT FOR THE OLD ONE.** `resolveAbandonAuthorization` requires `decisionType === 'ABANDON'` exactly, and says why at the call site. Inheriting promotion's generosity would have spread an unratified permission; changing promotion's behaviour inside an abandonment increment would have been an unreviewed widening or narrowing of a *different* governed act. **Recorded so the divergence is visible rather than silently normalised in either direction.**
- **Merge target:** Repository — one reading of whether `APPROVAL` is a generic authorizer, applied to both gates. **Status:** OPEN. **Safe default:** no document may state that this system has a single rule for what authorizes a governance act; it has two, and they disagree.

---

### REG-F-077 — The only surface that can create a Decision hardcodes `subjectObjectIds: []`, so no UI-created decision can authorize anything
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent + adversarial verification) · **Source:** blast-radius analysis for REG-F-070; the route read directly.
- **`apps/rph-demo/src/routes/decisions/+page.server.ts:48-50`, byte-exact:**
  ```
  const r = dispatch('ProposeDecision', 'DECISION', id, {
      decisionType,
      subjectObjectIds: [],
  ```
  **A hardcoded empty list with no branch** — the form collects `decisionType`, `selectedOption` and `rationale`, and nothing else. `'ABANDON'` is among the offerable types (`+page.svelte:28`).
- **⚠ CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS NOT SPECIFIC TO ABANDONMENT.** `proposeDecision` derives `subjectSemanticVersions` from `subjectObjectIds` (`governance.ts:42`), so an empty subject list yields an empty version pin as well. **Every subject-scope check in the system therefore refuses every UI-created decision, by construction:** REG-F-073's promotion scope conjunct, `resolveSkipAuthorization`'s plan/step scope, and REG-F-070's abandonment scope and version bind. The demo can create decisions that look governed in the list view and can authorize nothing.
- **AND ASR-15 ALREADY SAYS WHAT SUCH A RECORD IS** (DOC-003 L307): *"An approval whose actor, subject, subject version, type, and time cannot be identified is not authority — it is provenance at best, and re-decision is required."* The route produces provenance, and the UI does not say so.
- **THIS IS THE HOLLOW SHAPE ONE LAYER OUT.** The usual instance is a field declared and never read; here the field is read by four gates and never *written* by the only writer. Same defect, opposite end — and it means REG-F-073's fix, which this session recorded as closed, is closed at the engine and unreachable from the surface.
- **NOT FIXED HERE, DELIBERATELY.** Choosing subjects needs a UI affordance and a decision about which objects are selectable in which context — a design question, not a guard. Fixing it inside an abandonment increment would have shipped an unreviewed surface change.
- **Merge target:** `apps/rph-demo` — subject selection on the decision form, plus version pinning at propose time. **Status:** OPEN. **Safe default:** no document or demo narration may describe the decisions surface as producing authorizations; until subjects are selectable it produces records that every scope gate refuses.

### REG-F-078 — Rejecting governed work needed neither authority nor a finding, and the corpus had already refused the excuse
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** FINDING (build agent, four-lane investigation + three-lens adversarial panel) · **Source:** REG-F-072's residue, re-opened because the lead doubted his own framing.
- **MEASURED FIRST, THROUGH THE REAL BUS.** `ChangePwuState` drove `UNDER_ASSURANCE -> REJECTED` to ACCEPTED with `supportingObjectIds: []`, `reasonCode: 'CONTROLLER'`, **no decision, no observation, and no assessment object in existence** — while the assurance axis sat untouched. The PWU landed in a state whose only exits are ABANDONED (governance-guarded since REG-F-070) and SUPERSEDED. One unbacked command permanently disposed of governed work.
- **⚠ AND THE EXISTING GUARD WAS ON THE WRONG AXIS TO STOP IT.** `rejectUnbackedDisposition` guards `assuranceState`; asking the same command to ALSO move the assurance axis to REJECTED is refused `RPH_EVIDENCE_MISSING`. **Declining to update the assurance axis was sufficient to evade it.** A guard that a caller escapes by doing LESS is the sharpest form of the vacuous gate.
- **THE DISSOLVING RE-READING WAS PUT UP HONESTLY AND THEN REFUTED BY THE SOURCE OF THE CLAUSE IT REINTERPRETS.** The tempting reading: REJECTED merely mirrors an assurance verdict, so authority was settled upstream. DOC-001's own provenance file records *"Governance outside the six: Guide L336"*, and Guide L336 says both things **in one sentence**, byte-exact: *"**Assurance may record a `REJECTED` Assessment disposition under policy;** Governance is an authority function outside the six engineering disciplines and **alone authorizes waiver, risk acceptance, rejection or abandonment of governed work**, and promotion."* The corpus anticipated the objection and carved the disposition OUT of the reserved act. DOC-002 states the same split operationally — *"Assurance evaluates; governance decides. The two vocabularies never substitute for each other."*
- **VERDICT: BOTH — authority AND fact, each with its own citation.** JPWB-DOC-001 §5.2 (L168) reserves the act to Governance; DOC-001 §6 requires of governance dispositions that they be *"version-bound, **evidence-backed**"*; and the arrow's VERBATIM corpus trigger is **"Blocking finding"** (`PWU.workLifecycleState`'s vocab records §8.2 as VERBATIM, unlike the assurance machine's RECONSTRUCTED rows — which is why enforcing THIS declared trigger is not the error `pwu.ts` warns against elsewhere).
- **WHAT SHIPPED.** `resolveRejectAuthorization` (`handlers/reject-authorization.ts`) — the same seven ordered checks as the abandonment gate, differing only in the decision type, because the two acts sit in the same §5.2 sentence and giving them different authority tests would invent a distinction the clause does not make. Plus `rejectUnauthorizedRejection` in `pwu.ts`, requiring BOTH: an EFFECTIVE `REJECTION` Decision naming this PWU at its exact semantic version, AND a `BLOCKING`/`CRITICAL` `ASSURANCE_OBSERVATION` whose `subjectObjectIds` include it. **Severity is read off the STORED observation, never from the payload.**
- **⚠ THE DECISION TYPE IS `REJECTION`, NOT `REJECT`.** `DecisionTypeSchema` spells it `REJECTION`; `REJECT` is the ControlAction spelling from the §37 controller menu. A guard copied from the abandonment module and left on the action name would match no Decision that can exist and would **deny everything, silently** — a gate that refuses for the wrong reason is not a working gate.
- **PLACED AFTER THE LEGALITY AND OWNERSHIP CHECKS, correcting a defect in its own first draft.** It was first written into the `unearned` chain, which runs BEFORE `rejectIllegalWorkLifecycleMove` — so REJECTED requested from any of the nineteen states that have no such arrow would have been told "no governance decision" instead of "not an arrow". Its three siblings in that chain do mis-message this way; that is precedent, not justification, and REJECTED is the sharpest case because it is legal from exactly one state of twenty.
- **NOT DONE, AND EACH FOR A REASON.** No `RejectPwu` command was minted: the Guide's instruction for exactly this case is *"when the required semantic Command is absent, **stop for a contract Decision rather than inventing one**"*, and `PwuRejectedPayloadSchema` is a `z.strictObject` with no decision field, so minting would force a contract amendment on an already-UNRATIFIED shape — authoring atop authoring. `REJECTED` was NOT added to `PWU_SEMANTIC_LIFECYCLE_COMMANDS`: with no handler, the ownership guard would redirect callers to a command that does not exist, **deleting the capability instead of closing the route**. BLOCKED and ESCALATED are NOT guarded: their triggers are an absence of evidence and a missing dependency, their carriers' id lists are OPTIONAL, and §5.2 reserves rejection but not escalation or blocking — and all three states have zero recovery arrows, so an entry guard on BLOCKED would harden a dead end.
- **THREE MUTANTS, AND ONE PREDICTION WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING.** Unwiring the guard reddened all eight reject rows plus the discriminator control, leaving the accept case and the already-rejected control green. Dropping the already-rejected disjunct reddened that control ALONE. Turning the two conjuncts from AND into OR reddened **seven** rows — I predicted two. The prediction was too narrow because every row except "nothing cited" carries one VALID artifact and one bad one, so an OR passes them all. The mutant is caught, and caught more broadly than expected; the error was in my model of my own battery, not in the battery.
- **BLAST RADIUS: ZERO.** Across the whole repository not one site — test, fixture, seed, route or e2e — put a PWU into `workLifecycleState` REJECTED. The hole was total and unexercised, which is why nothing had ever noticed it.
- **Merge target:** Repository — `reject-authorization.ts` + the `changePwuState` guard. **Status:** ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-08. **Safe default retired with it.** OPEN residue: `PwuRejected` remains declared, registered and emitted by nothing, and the act still travels on the generic setter rather than a named command — both awaiting the contract Decision the Guide directs us to stop for.

### REG-F-079 — Twelve lifecycle arrows have no named command, PER-3 wants them named, and the Guide forbids me to name them
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** BLOCKED ITEM (build agent) · **Source:** REG-F-072's residue, taken up and then STOPPED on canon rather than deferred for convenience.
- **THE GAP IS REAL AND MEASURED.** Twelve `PWU.workLifecycleState` arrows are performable only through the generic setter because no semantic command exists for them: six §8.1 middles — `READY->PLANNED`, `PLANNED->EXECUTING`, `EXECUTING->EVIDENCE_PENDING`, `EVIDENCE_PENDING->UNDER_ASSURANCE`, `UNDER_ASSURANCE->CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED`, `UNDER_ASSURANCE->SATISFIED` — and six exception arrows: `->BLOCKED` (×3), `->ESCALATED`, `->REJECTED`, `Any active->ABANDONED`.
- **AND CANON WANTS THEM NAMED.** JPWB-DOC-003 §9 PER-3 (L343): *"Canonical state is mutated only through authenticated, authorized, **semantically named commands** … **No generic CRUD/PATCH path** … bypasses this pipeline."* On PER-3's own terms these twelve are the remaining bypass.
- **⚠ AND THE SAME CORPUS FORBIDS ME TO MINT THEM. Coding Agent Guide L1097, byte-exact:** *"Acceptance, rejection or abandonment of governed work, risk acceptance, waiver, and Baseline promotion require a valid Governance Decision and exact contracted Commands. Use Section 9.5's current registry; **when the required semantic Command is absent, stop for a contract Decision rather than inventing one.**"* That instruction is what stopped `RejectPwu` being minted in REG-F-078, and it applies with equal force here — more so, because minting twelve commands means authoring twelve payload contracts and (for most) twelve events, atop shapes that are already `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED`.
- **SO THIS IS RECORDED AS BLOCKED, NOT AS BACKLOG, and the distinction matters.** A backlog item is work nobody has got to; this is work the corpus says an agent may not do alone. Proceeding would be the failure mode CON-000 AX-2 names — *"no scenario, agent, validator, or registry grants itself authority"* — expressed as contract authorship rather than as approval.
- **WHAT UNBLOCKS IT:** a sponsor contract Decision settling (a) which of the twelve get named commands, (b) their payload shapes, and (c) whether the §8.1 middles should instead be DERIVED — computed from the execution and assurance axes rather than commanded at all, which is what `workLifecycleState` being described as *"a computed rollup of the independently-commanded sub-axes"* (`pwu.ts` header, DOC-002 §5/§7) would imply. **(c) is a live possibility that would make (a) and (b) mostly moot**, and settling it is exactly the kind of question the instruction reserves.
- **INTERIM POSITION, so nothing reads as safer than it is:** the six §8.1 middles are *unguarded but not dangerous* — they are scheduling and progress facts, and the two that matter (`->SATISFIED`, `->CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED`) already carry cross-axis guards requiring the assurance axis to agree. Of the exception arrows, `->ABANDONED` and `->REJECTED` are now authority-guarded (REG-F-070, REG-F-078); `->BLOCKED` and `->ESCALATED` remain unguarded and are the residue.
- **Merge target:** Sponsor — a contract Decision. **Status:** BLOCKED. **Safe default:** no document may describe the PWU lifecycle as fully command-driven; twelve of its arrows are reachable only through a generic setter that PER-3 does not sanction.

### REG-D-029 — Sponsor grant: delegated authority to AUTHOR the PWU write-path solution, discharging Guide L1097 for this program
- **Date:** 2026-08-08 · **Type:** DECISION (sponsor grant, recorded by the build agent) · **Discharges:** the contract Decision REG-F-079 was blocked pending.
- **THE GRANT, in the sponsor's words:** *"You will need to use your vast intelligence combined with your extensive expertise with the document corpus and codebase to use your delegated authority to author a solution whether it's a documentation or codebase or both to author with rigor a valid solution(s)."*
- **WHAT IT DISCHARGES.** Coding Agent Guide L1097 — *"when the required semantic Command is absent, **stop for a contract Decision rather than inventing one**"* — is the clause that stopped `RejectPwu` under REG-F-078 and put REG-F-079 in BLOCKED. **This grant is that Decision**, for the PWU write path specifically: the missing lifecycle commands and their payload/event shapes may be AUTHORED.
- **⚠ WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, and the limits are the sponsor's own standing ones (REG-D-025).** It does not RATIFY anything. Every shape authored under it is `UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED` and must be annotated so, exactly as the corpus's existing authored shapes are. It does not license inventing a shape the corpus already settles, ratifying by implementation, or closing an elicitation item by building past it. **A design document is not a ratification**, and neither is a passing test.
- **SCOPE:** the PWU write path — `ChangePwuState`, the PWU lifecycle commands, and the four PWU axes. It does not extend to the assurance plane's own contracts, the demo surface, or the four infrastructure races.
- **THE DESIGN AUTHORED UNDER IT:** `docs/_working/DESIGN-pwu-write-path.md`, which answers REG-F-079's question (a)/(b)/(c) — **COMMAND, not derive** — and was then put through a four-lens adversarial review that **overturned its central argument** and blocked six of its nine increments. Both the original argument and its refutation are recorded in the document rather than edited away; see its §1.0 and §8.
- **⚠ AND A NOTE ON WHY THIS ENTRY EXISTS AT ALL, since the grant was verbal and the work could have proceeded without it.** REG-F-079's *WHAT UNBLOCKS IT* asked for a sponsor contract Decision. An earlier draft of the design cited **REG-D-026** as its discharge — an entry about **tenant and organization scope**, which discharges nothing here. The mis-citation was found by the adversarial pass, not by me. Had it stood, the program's one canonical blocker would have been recorded as cleared by an entry about something else. **The grant is real; the record of it was not, until this entry.**
- **Merge target:** Register — this entry. **Status:** ✅ RECORDED. **Consequence for REG-F-079:** its BLOCKED status is lifted for authoring; its substantive question is answered by the design, whose own §8 records what remains unsafe to build.
